# Open Questions: Colorado Volume

Interpretation questions the authoring pass could not settle from the text, each with the assumption the draft uses so the codex stays evaluable. Items: lists the items that carry the assumption.

### OQ-1. Partly resolved: the Cuban/Haitian entrant half is settled; open: the date from which FNS applies Pub. L. 119-21 sec. 10108's narrowed non-citizen categories to current participants

Body: The amended 7 U.S.C. 2015(f) (S8, S9) names only four eligible categories: citizens/nationals, LPRs, Cuban/Haitian entrants, and individuals lawfully residing under a Compact of Free Association. Refugees, asylees, parolees, and the other "qualified alien" categories of 8 U.S.C. 1641 are no longer eligible under the statute. 10 CCR 2506-1 4.305 (S10-S17) and 7 CFR 273.4 (S18) still describe the pre-H.R.1 scheme and list COFA migrants as not qualified. The draft follows the amended statute for the category list (refugees and asylees ineligible; COFA migrants eligible) and 8 U.S.C. 1612-1613 (S21-S24) for the five-year bar and its exceptions, which sec. 10108 did not amend and which Colorado restates at 4.305,B,3. Two points remain open: whether the five-year bar reaches Cuban/Haitian entrants (they are qualified aliens under 8 U.S.C. 1641(b)(7), not fetched, and 1613(b) exempts refugees and asylees but not entrants; the draft treats them as eligible without a wait, following the amended 2015(f) list), and the date from which FNS treats sec. 10108 as effective for current participants (not stated in D-5; the draft applies it as of the determination date). A future revision should replace this with the CDHS guidance implementing sec. 10108 once issued. Items: CO-306, CO-307, CO-308, CO-309, CO-310.

Phase 8: the version of 10 CCR 2506-1 effective 2026-10-01 (D-82) still lists citizens of Compact of Free Association nations among the non-citizens ineligible for SNAP (4.305.1.H, S751), and its rule history shows the October 1, 2026 amendments touched only definitions, the COLA tables and the utility allowances (S752); Colorado's rule has not implemented sec. 10108. The draft continues to follow the amended statute.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the entrant half is resolved and its premise was wrong. 8 U.S.C. 1613(b)(1)(D) (D-25, S1150) exempts "a Cuban and Haitian entrant as defined in section 501(e) of the Refugee Education Assistance Act of 1980" from the five-year limitation, so 1613(b) does exempt entrants, and the draft's treatment (entrants eligible without a wait) rests on that text; snap_is_eligible_non_citizen (CO-308) now cites it, and CO-306 and CO-307 no longer carry this question. Still open: the date FNS applies sec. 10108 to current participants. FNS's "SNAP Implementation of the One Big Beautiful Bill Act of 2025 - Alien SNAP Eligibility - Revised" memo and its Question and Answer #1 would settle it, but their text is served only from www.usda.gov, which refused the request (Access Denied); no Federal Register rule amends 7 CFR 273.4, and the 12/30/2025 version of 10 CCR 2506-1 (D-133) records no 4.305 amendment. Items: CO-308.

CDHS stakeholder review fix round (2026-11-21): the CDHS program representative's position (review section 5, marked [PK], program knowledge not yet tied to a published text) is to apply sec. 10108 to new applications from CDHS's implementation date and to ongoing households at their next recertification or an earlier acted-on change, not by determination date. The text does not say so: Pub. L. 119-21 sec. 10108 (D-5) has no effective-date or transition clause, so it took effect on enactment, and 7 CFR 272.1(g) leaves an implementation schedule to the amendment itself, which states none; the FNS memo that would set the treatment of current participants is still served only from www.usda.gov (refused again this session). snap_is_eligible_non_citizen (CO-308) now carries an effective range from July 4, 2025 and a county-facing statute_override note; the ongoing-case treatment stays as of the determination date and is tracked in OQ-533. Items: CO-308.

SNAP non-financial coverage round (2026-11-22; coverage review P2-10): how sec. 10108 sits with 8 U.S.C. 1611(a) was checked. 1611(a) (S1911) makes "an alien who is not a qualified alien" ineligible for any Federal public benefit "Notwithstanding any other provision of law"; the reviewer read 1641(b)(8) as making Compact of Free Association residents qualified aliens only for Medicaid. That limit is gone: Pub. L. 118-42 (March 9, 2024) "struck out ', but only with respect to the designated Federal program defined in section 1612(b)(3)(C) of this title (relating to the Medicaid program)'" (D-285, S3074), so a COFA resident is a qualified alien for every Federal public benefit and 1611(a) does not bar one; the amended 7 U.S.C. 2015(f)(2)(D) (S8) then makes the resident an eligible non-citizen. The categories are settled on the texts; only the date from which FNS applies sec. 10108 to current participants remains open, as above.

Class: blocked on a named document
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: The narrower list of non-citizens who may receive SNAP applies from the day the 2025 Act was enacted, to every determination made after that day, for new and ongoing households alike.
Blocked on: FNS memorandum "SNAP Implementation of the One Big Beautiful Bill Act of 2025 - Alien SNAP Eligibility - Revised" and its Question and Answer #1 (www.usda.gov refused, HTTP 403 on 2026-11-22; fns.usda.gov links to it)

Checked 2026-11-22: Pub. L. 119-21 sec. 10108 (D-5) has no effective-date or transition clause; 10 CCR 2506-1 4.305 (D-82, effective 2026-10-01) still lists the pre-Act categories, so it has not adopted the change; FNS's implementation page on fns.usda.gov links to the memorandum on www.usda.gov, which refused it again. Only that memorandum states the date FNS applies the change to current participants. Also checked 2026-11-22: 8 U.S.C. 1611(a) (S1911) and 1641(b)(8) with its 2024 amendment (D-285, law.cornell.edu, S3074): a Compact of Free Association resident is a qualified alien for every Federal public benefit, so 1611(a) does not conflict with the amended 2015(f)(2)(D). Re-checked 2026-11-22 (coverage pass 3 N3-P3-1): www.fns.usda.gov/snap/obbb-alien-eligibility now answers 301 and redirects to www.fna.usda.gov/working-families-tax-cuts/snap/alien-eligibility, which is not an allowed host and, per the reviewer, carries only the memorandum's title and a link to www.usda.gov; the classification stands.

### OQ-2. Resolved: the elderly and the blind-or-disabled waiting-period exceptions are separate; CO-306 now carries the blind-or-disabled one

Body: The brief describes an additional-condition exemption for "elderly/disabled who were lawfully residing on 8/22/1996." The only text found in the fetched Colorado or federal documents matching that date is 10 CCR 2506-1 4.305,B,3,e (S15), which is framed as a date-of-birth cutoff (born on or before August 22, 1931, i.e., age 65 or older as of that date) combined with lawful residence on that date, not a general elderly-or-disabled exemption. CO-306 (snap_is_exempt_from_immigrant_waiting_period; the fact snap_eligible_household_size (CO-313) is not the item with this logic - N-3 fix-round correction) models S15 literally (date_of_birth on or before 1931-08-22 and qualified_status_date on or before 1996-08-22, as a proxy for "lawfully resided ... on August 22, 1996" since no supplied fact captures residence on a specific historical date directly), rather than the broader "elderly or disabled" reading from the brief. Items: CO-306.

Resolution (open-question sweep, 2026-11-21): the brief merged two exceptions. 10 CCR 2506-1 4.305.B.3.d (D-82, S1305) exempts "A non-citizen who is blind or a person with a disability if the non-citizen is receiving benefits or assistance for their condition regardless of entry date", following 8 U.S.C. 1612(a)(2)(F)(ii) (D-24, S1306: "is receiving benefits or assistance for blindness or disability (within the meaning of section 3(j) of the Food Stamp Act of 1977"), which has no August 22, 1996 condition; 4.305.B.3.e is the separate date-of-birth test CO-306 already modeled. snap_is_exempt_from_immigrant_waiting_period (CO-306) gains the disjunct snap_is_disabled_member (CO-302, the 7 CFR 271.2 benefit-receipt list that 7 U.S.C. 2012 defines) and new tests CO-306-T3 (an LPR receiving SSI two years after obtaining status is exempt) and CO-306-T4 (an SSA disability finding without a benefit is not). No household expectation changed. Items: CO-306.

### OQ-3. Resolved: snap_meets_citizenship_requirement reads Colorado residency for 7 U.S.C. 2015(f)(1)'s residency prong

Body: The amended 7 U.S.C. 2015(f)(1) (S8) requires "a resident of the United States" in addition to the status prong. No supplied fact in this volume's interface captures residency as distinct from `state_of_residence` (a text field naming the state). The draft treats a known `state_of_residence` as sufficient evidence of U.S. residency and does not add a further supplied fact; residency verification itself is out of this volume's scope per docs/design-colorado.md. Items: CO-307.

Resolution (open-question sweep, 2026-11-21): 10 CCR 2506-1 4.303.A (D-82, S1311): "Applicants shall live in the county or district in which they make application for the Program". A person who meets Colorado's residency requirement lives in a Colorado county and so is "a resident of the United States" (7 U.S.C. 2015(f)(1)); no separate fact is needed. snap_meets_citizenship_requirement (CO-309) now requires is_colorado_resident (CO-505, the shared state-residence test CCCAP and LEAP also read) beside the status prong, with a new test CO-309-T4 (a citizen living in Wyoming does not meet it). Twenty-seven SNAP rule tests whose givens stated citizenship without a state of residence now state state_of_residence CO (CO-310-T2 through CO-371-T2); their expected values are unchanged. Every household case already states state_of_residence for its SNAP members; no case expectation changed. The county-of-application half of 4.303.A has no county fact (OQ-393). Items: CO-309.

### OQ-11. Resolved (fix-round C-6): BCE's OAP/AND/AB prong now has its own supplied fact

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.206,C,1,a (S47) lists SSI, Colorado Works (CW/TANF), Old Age Pension (OAP), Aid to the Needy Disabled (AND), and Aid to the Blind (AB) as the benefits that confer BCE when every household member receives or is authorized to receive one of them. This question originally noted that `snap_household_all_members_receive_categorical_assistance` (CO-336) read `receives_disability_based_general_assistance` as a stand-in for OAP/AND/AB. On review (C-6) that substitution was found to be wrong, not merely approximate: `receives_disability_based_general_assistance`'s own stated meaning is general assistance "based on disability or blindness criteria" (the 271.2 elderly-or-disabled prong CO-302 needs), and OAP is an age-based program (65 and over, or 60 with AND), so a household of pure OAP recipients -- the commonest BCE household in Colorado -- was not BCE under the model as written. Fixed by adding `receives_state_cash_assistance` (CO-073) for the OAP/AND/AB prong and having CO-336 read it instead; `receives_disability_based_general_assistance` is left to CO-302 alone. Items: CO-336, CO-338, CO-340, CO-073.

### OQ-12. Whether the TANF/MOE non-cash-service notice condition of ECE (S48) is modeled as always met

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.206,C,2,a,2) (S48) makes ECE conditional on the household having "been authorized to receive" a non-cash TANF/MOE-funded service, and states that notice of the service "shall be provided on the application ... and/or the statement of facts" — i.e., every Colorado SNAP applicant is given the opportunity to receive that non-cash service as part of applying. No supplied fact in this volume's interface captures whether a household affirmatively took up the non-cash service. The draft's `snap_meets_ece_eligibility` (CO-339) therefore tests only the 200% gross income limit and the IPV/drug-felony exclusion, treating the TANF/MOE notice-and-authorization condition as met whenever a household applies (consistent with Colorado's practice of extending the non-cash service to every applicant through the application form itself). A future revision should add a supplied fact if a case needs to model a household that was not actually authorized for the service. Items: CO-339, CO-340.

Checked 2026-11-21: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.206.C.2.a.2 (D-82) and the 12/30/2025 version (D-133) require households "who have been authorized to receive" the non-cash TANF/MOE service and only that "Language regarding the non-cash TANF/MOE funded program shall be provided" on the forms; neither says that providing the language is the authorization, so no published rule settles it. Watch: FNS's proposed rule "SNAP: Reforming Categorical Eligibility" (91 FR 72454, Nov. 10, 2026; D-132, S1308) would "limit categorical eligibility to only households that receive TANF assistance, as defined at 45 CFR 260.31" and remove 7 CFR 273.2(j)(2)(i)(B)-(C) and (2)(ii), which would end ECE; it is a proposal (comments close 2026-12-10) and changes nothing today. snap_meets_ece_eligibility (CO-340) notes it.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: Every SNAP applicant is treated as authorized for the non-cash TANF-funded service, because the notice of it is printed on the application.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.206.C.2.a.2 (D-82, S48) and the 12/30/2025 version (D-133) require households "who have been authorized to receive" the non-cash TANF/MOE service and only that its language be on the application or statement of facts; neither says that giving the language is the authorization, and the proposed federal rule on categorical eligibility (D-132) changes nothing yet. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-13. Resolved: the elderly/disabled SE household is not subject to the gross income test at all

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.206,C,3,b,1) (S49) and 4.401,A,3,b (S50) each state only a net-income (100% FPL) and resource requirement for an SE household with an elderly or disabled member, with no gross income figure given for that household type. This was originally treated as an assumption drawn from silence. It is not: 7 CFR 273.10(e)(2)(i)(B) (S103) states in terms that "In addition to meeting the net income eligibility standards, households which do not contain an elderly or disabled member shall have their gross income ... compared to the gross monthly income standards" -- i.e., a household that does contain an elderly or disabled member is not subject to the gross income test. snap_meets_gross_income_test (CO-347) is correct as written (deeming the test met via snap_household_has_elderly_or_disabled_member) and now cites S103 alongside S41, S49, S50. Items: CO-347.

### OQ-14. Resolved: a destitute migrant or seasonal farmworker household is defined by 10 CCR 2506-1 4.406

Body: 7 CFR 273.2(i)(1)(ii) (S58) grants expedited service to a "[m]igrant or seasonal farmworker household[] who [is] destitute as defined in § 273.10(e)(3)," which this batch's document set does not include (273.10 is not among D-1..D-25). The draft adds a supplied fact, `is_destitute_migrant_or_seasonal_farmworker` (CO-055), for a county worker's determination of that status under the unfetched 273.10(e)(3) standard, rather than trying to reconstruct the destitution test from memory. A future batch that fetches 273.10 should replace this supplied fact's `meaning` with a derived rule citing that text, if the composition of "destitute" income and resources is itself decomposable into facts this volume already has. Items: CO-055, CO-347.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): answered by the current Colorado text. 10 CCR 2506-1 4.406.A-C (S1018, D-82) defines the destitute household: a migrant or seasonal farm worker household whose only income for the month of application was received from a terminated source before the date of application, or is more than $25 from a new source that will not be received by the tenth calendar day after filing, or a combination of the two, considered destitute only for the first month of each certification period; the grower is the source (B). The supplied fact stays supplied with that definition in its meaning (is_destitute_migrant_or_seasonal_farmworker, CO-055): deriving it would need three new income-source facts (terminated source, new source over $25, received by the tenth day) that no other rule reads. Items: CO-055, CO-347.

### OQ-15. Resolved: Colorado's 200% BBCE table now has its FY2026 version (2025-10-01 to 2026-10-01)

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.401.1 in the version effective 2026-10-01 (D-82, S739) states the 130%, 200% and 165% gross income levels "Effective October 1, 2026" (200%: $2,660 for one person through $9,288 for eight, +$948 each additional). snap_bbce_gross_income_limit_table (CO-122) and snap_bbce_gross_income_limit_each_additional (CO-123) carry it as a version from 2026-10-01, and the 2024-10-01 version now ends there. Determinations dated 2025-10-01 through 2026-09-30 still use the 2024 table, because the FY2026 version of the rule (emergency rules effective 10/03/2025 and 12/30/2025 per the rule history, S752) was not fetched; an archived SOS version would supply it. Items: CO-122, CO-123.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): the phase-8 review (S-7) found this question overclaimed as Resolved: the 2026-10-01 version is answered (S739), but every determination from 2025-10-01 through 2026-09-30 is still tested against the FY2025 200% table carried by the 2024-10-01 version (CO-339-T1/T2 among them). The FY2026 version of 10 CCR 2506-1 (rule history S752: emergency rules effective 10/03/2025 and 12/30/2025) was not fetched in this round, which had no document range; the question stays open until it is. Items: CO-122, CO-123.

Resolution (open-question sweep, 2026-11-21): the FY2026 version of 10 CCR 2506-1 is fetched from the Secretary of State's archive (rule version 12299, effective 12/30/2025, D-133; the 10/03/2025 emergency version states the same table). Its 4.401.1 (S1304) states the 200% gross income levels "Effective October 1, 2025": $2,610 (1), $3,526, $4,442, $5,360, $6,276, $7,192, $8,110, $9,026 (8), +$918 each additional. snap_bbce_gross_income_limit_table (CO-122) and snap_bbce_gross_income_limit_each_additional (CO-123) carry it as a version from 2025-10-01 to 2026-10-01, and the 2024-10-01 version now ends at 2025-10-01. The 130% and 100% tables already had FY2026 versions from FNS (CO-110 to CO-113). Changed rule tests: CO-339-T1 (one person, as of 2026-09-30) 2,510 to 2,610 and CO-339-T2 (two persons) 3,408 to 3,526, the FY2026 figures their titles named. No household case expectation changed. Items: CO-122, CO-123, CO-339.

### OQ-21. Resolved: FY2027 maximum allotments fetched

Body: FNS's FY2027 Maximum Allotments and Deductions table (D-83, S730), effective October 1, 2026 through September 30, 2027, states the 48-States-and-DC maximum allotments ($306 for one person through $1,841 for eight, $225 per additional person); 10 CCR 2506-1 4.207.3.D effective 2026-10-01 (D-82, S738) states the same figures. snap_maximum_allotment_table (CO-130) and snap_maximum_allotment_each_additional (CO-131) carry them as versions from 2026-10-01, and the FY2026 versions now end there. The same documents supply the FY2027 standard deduction, shelter cap, homeless shelter deduction, minimum allotment, income limits and the elderly/disabled asset limit (CO-110-CO-114, CO-119, CO-132-CO-135). Items: CO-130, CO-131.

### OQ-22. Resolved: SNAP's child support exclusion averages three or more months of payments (4.407.5.D) or uses the estimate (4.407.5.E)

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.407.5,A (S99) excludes legally binding child support paid to or for a non-household member from the household's total countable gross income before any gross income test, Colorado's election under 7 U.S.C. 2014(e)(4); 4.411.1,B,2,c says the payments are deducted before an ineligible member's income is prorated. Resolved in the fix round: snap_person_gross_income subtracts child_support_paid_legally_obligated (floored at zero per person), so the exclusion reaches the gross income test and the prorated share alike, and the separate child support deduction item was removed. What remains open is 4.407.5,D's three-month averaging of the payments, which the supplied monthly amount is assumed to already reflect. Items: CO-332.

Resolution (open-question sweep, 2026-11-21): 10 CCR 2506-1 4.407.5.D-E (D-82, S1314): households "with a history of three (3) or more months of paying child support shall have the support payments averaged ... and shall use that average as the household's support exclusion"; with "less than a three (3) month record, the local office shall estimate the anticipated payments". The new supplied snap_child_support_payment_history_months (CO-2002) states the length of the record, and the new snap_child_support_exclusion (CO-2003) averages child_support_paid_legally_obligated over the three months before the budget month when the record is three months or longer, and otherwise takes the month's anticipated payment; snap_person_gross_income (CO-332) subtracts it instead of the month's raw payment, so the shared fact (which CCCAP also reads) keeps its plain monthly meaning. An unstated history is read as none (rule 12 of docs/conventions.md), which is the old behavior, so no household expectation changed. 4.407.5.D's adjustment for an anticipated change in the obligation is stated through the monthly figures (CO-2003's precision). Items: CO-332, CO-2002, CO-2003.

### OQ-23. Resolved: Colorado uses option (2): round the allotment down after subtracting the unrounded 30% of net income

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.207.3.A (D-82, S737): "1. Multiply the net monthly income by thirty percent (30%) 2. Subtract the result from the maximum benefit allowed for the appropriate household size ... 3. Round the result down to the nearest dollar." This is 7 CFR 273.10(e)(2)(ii)(A)'s option (2). snap_benefit_reduction (CO-384) no longer rounds, and snap_allotment (CO-385) rounds the difference down (written as the negated ceiling of the negated difference). CO-384's tests now expect unrounded figures. Items: CO-384, CO-385.

### OQ-24. Resolved: the homeless shelter estimate is the default and actual costs replace it only when higher and verified - the larger of the two, as modeled

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.407.3.C effective 2026-10-01 (D-82, S742): "All households experiencing homelessness that incur, or reasonably expect to incur, shelter costs during a month shall be eligible for the estimate, unless higher shelter costs are verified, at which point the household may use actual shelter costs rather than the estimate. Households experiencing homelessness that incur no shelter costs during the month shall not be eligible for the homeless shelter deduction." The household receives the estimate unless verified actual costs are higher, which is the greater-of reading snap_homeless_shelter_deduction (CO-380) and snap_household_net_income (CO-381) already implement, and CO-380 already requires shelter costs above zero. The deduction amount is $205.66 from October 1, 2026 (S732, S742; CO-134). Items: CO-380, CO-381.

### OQ-25. Resolved: Colorado's standard deduction table is current from October 1, 2026

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.407.1 effective 2026-10-01 (D-82, S741) states $217 (1-3), $229 (4), $268 (5), $308 (6+) "Effective October 1, 2026", identical to FNS's FY2027 Table 2 (D-83, S731). snap_standard_deduction_table (CO-132) carries them as a version from 2026-10-01. Items: CO-132.

### OQ-26. Resolved: a zero-benefit household of three or more is denied unless it is categorically eligible

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.207.3.B (D-82, S737): "Except for households that are eligible under BCE or ECE, households with three or more members who are entitled to zero benefits shall have their SNAP application denied." Colorado takes the denial option for a Standard Eligibility household and certifies a BCE or ECE household with zero benefits. snap_is_eligible (CO-386) now accepts a categorically eligible household with a $0 allotment, and snap_eligibility_status (CO-387) reports zero_allotment only for a household that is not categorically eligible. The initial-month and proration exceptions in the same paragraph are not modeled (OQ-38). Items: CO-385, CO-386, CO-387.

### OQ-27. Resolved: the earned income deduction applies to a disqualified member's prorated earnings, as CO-371/CO-372 build it

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.411.1,B,2,c (S107) applies the earned income deduction to "the prorated income earned by the disqualified and/or sanctioned member that is attributed to the household," in addition to the household's own eligible members' earned income. Batch 2's snap_household_earned_income (CO-335) sums only snap_eligible_household_members' own earned income and does not include any share of an ineligible member's earned income (by design, per CO-335's own rationale). This batch cannot edit CO-335, so snap_counted_earned_income_of_ineligible_member (CO-371) and snap_household_earned_income_for_deduction (CO-372) build the earned-income-deduction base as snap_household_earned_income plus the same pro rata (or full, for a fraud/work-sanction/fleeing-felon disqualification) share of each ineligible member's own earned income that snap_counted_income_of_ineligible_member (CO-333) already computes for gross income, mirrored onto the earned-income component alone. Items: CO-371, CO-372.

Resolution (open-question sweep, 2026-11-21): 10 CCR 2506-1 4.407.2.A (D-82, S1312): "The twenty percent (20%) deduction shall also apply to prorated income earned by the disqualified member and attributed to the household." snap_counted_earned_income_of_ineligible_member (CO-371) and snap_household_earned_income_for_deduction (CO-372) implement exactly this and now cite S1312; the open point was only why the base is a new item, which the rationale of each states. No change. Items: CO-371, CO-372.

### OQ-28. Resolved: a disqualified member's billed shelter and dependent care expenses are prorated or counted in full by the member's disqualification

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.411.1,B,2,c (S107) also states that shelter and dependent care expenses paid by or billed to a disqualified or sanctioned member (other than the standard utility allowance, which is added in full) are prorated evenly among all household members including the disqualified member, with only the remaining members' share deductible. This volume's supplied-fact interface does not distinguish which household member a shelter or dependent care expense is billed to; snap_shelter_costs (CO-377) and snap_dependent_care_deduction (CO-374) sum shelter_cost_monthly/utility_allowance_amount and dependent_care_expense over every household member's stated value without prorating out a disqualified member's own share. This is a simplification a future revision should replace once the supplied-fact interface can attribute an expense to the member who is billed for it. Items: CO-377, CO-374.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the rule is clear (4.411.1.B.2.c, S107); what is missing is a fact attributing a shelter or dependent care expense to the member billed for it, and a per-member split of those household expenses in CO-374 and CO-377. Not modeled in this sweep: the attribution changes how every case states shelter costs (OQ-29), so it is left with that question.

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep, supplied-facts round): 10 CCR 2506-1 4.407.3.A (S1600) allows shelter expenses "as billed to a household member or as paid or billed to a disqualified individual", in their entirety for fraud and divided evenly for an ineligible non-citizen or missing SSN; 4.407.4.A (S1601) says the same of dependent care; 4.411.1.B.1.a and B.2.c (S1602) extend the full and prorated treatment to the other disqualifications; 4.411.2.A and C (S1603) leave a non-household member's (an ineligible student's) share out. shelter_cost_monthly (CO-035) and dependent_care_expense (CO-032) now state the amount billed to or paid by each member. snap_counted_shelter_cost_of_member (CO-2300) and snap_counted_dependent_care_expense_of_member (CO-2301) count it in full for an eligible member and a 4.411.1.B.1 member (snap_is_disqualified_member_counted_in_full, CO-2302), less the member's pro rata share for an ineligible non-citizen, and not at all for an ineligible student; snap_shelter_costs (CO-377) and snap_dependent_care_deduction (CO-374) sum them, adding the standard utility allowance once in full. New tests CO-2300-T1 to T5, CO-2301-T1 to T4, CO-377-T4, CO-374-T3. In every household case the shelter cost is billed to an eligible member (CO-01/CO-02: p2; CO-03: p1), so no expectation changed.

### OQ-29. Resolved: shelter costs are stated per member as billed or paid, and an unstated member makes the total unknown

Body: shelter_cost_monthly and utility_allowance_amount are described in this volume's supplied-fact interface as "stated once per unit, carried on the head," but no fact or relationship identifies which member is the head, and the P39 sum pattern (docs/conventions.md) makes a sum over a group unknown if any member's value of the summed fact is unknown. snap_shelter_costs (CO-377) instead sums only over the household members whose shelter_cost_monthly/utility_allowance_amount is actually known (filtering out members for whom the fact was never asked), so a value stated on any one member (typically the head) is picked up and members who were never asked are treated as contributing zero rather than making the whole sum unknown. This is a reasonable reading given the interface, but it means a household member's shelter_cost_monthly of $0 (asked and answered) is indistinguishable in this rule from a member the interface never asked. Items: CO-377.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): the standard utility allowance no longer uses this device: snap_utility_allowance_amount (CO-1500, which replaces CO-036) is household-level - the highest tier any member's stated utility facts reach, with unstated facts read as false or zero - and snap_shelter_costs (CO-377) adds it once rather than summing a per-member allowance, so two members stating heating costs no longer give two allowances (S-3; 4.407.31, S743). shelter_cost_monthly still uses the sum over members whose value is known. Items: CO-377, CO-1500.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the rule treats shelter cost as a household figure (4.407.3, D-82); the ledger lacks a household-level (or head-of-household) shelter fact. Not modeled in this sweep: replacing shelter_cost_monthly by a household fact restates every SNAP case's shelter figures.

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep, supplied-facts round): with OQ-28, shelter_cost_monthly (CO-035) is the amount billed to or paid by the member, not a household figure carried on the head, which is how 4.407.3.A (S1600) allows the expense. snap_shelter_costs (CO-377) now sums snap_counted_shelter_cost_of_member (CO-2300) over every household member with the ordinary P39 sum, so an unasked member makes the total unknown and a member who pays nothing states 0 (new test CO-377-T5). The utility allowance was already household-level (CO-1500). Every case with a SNAP outcome already stated the fact for every member; no expectation changed.

### OQ-30. Whether the excess shelter deduction and the net income computation floor intermediate income at $0 before applying the 50% shelter share is not addressed by the fetched text

Body: 7 CFR 273.10(e)(1)(i)(H) (S101) and 10 CCR 2506-1 4.407.3,A (S96) describe the 50% shelter-income test as applied to "the household's income after all other deductions," without addressing what happens if the standard, earned income, medical, dependent care, and child support deductions computed so far exceed gross income (a negative intermediate figure). snap_adjusted_income_before_shelter (CO-378) floors this intermediate figure at $0 before the 50% shelter share is taken, on the reading that "income after deductions" cannot itself be negative for this purpose (consistent with the final net income floor at $0 that 7 CFR 273.10(e)(1) implies throughout). Items: CO-378.

Checked 2026-11-21: 7 CFR 273.10(e)(1)(i)(H) (D-14: "Subtract from total shelter costs 50 percent of the household's monthly income after all the above deductions have been subtracted") and 10 CCR 2506-1 4.407.3 (D-82, and the same text in the 12/30/2025 version, D-133); neither addresses a negative intermediate figure, so no published rule settles it.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: When the deductions taken before the shelter deduction exceed a household's income, the income used for the shelter test is zero, not a negative amount.

Checked 2026-11-22: 7 CFR 273.10(e)(1)(i)(H) (D-14: "50 percent of the household's monthly income after all the above deductions have been subtracted") and 10 CCR 2506-1 4.407.3 (D-82; the same in D-133) say what is subtracted but not what happens when that figure is below zero. No published rule settles it.

Final verification (docs/reviews/final-snap.md F-21, 2026-11-22): the choice changes no outcome. When the deductions taken before the shelter deduction exceed income, net income is $0 whether the intermediate figure is floored or not (snap_household_net_income, CO-381, floors the result); only the displayed excess shelter deduction differs. Kept as recorded.

### OQ-31. Resolved: the amended ABAWD age exception ("under 18, or over 65") leaves 18-64 subject to the time limit

Body: Pub. L. 119-21 sec. 10102 (S128), amending 7 U.S.C. 2015(o)(3)(A) (S127), exempts an individual "under 18, or over 65, years of age." Read literally, "over 65" exempts only a person who has turned 66, leaving the subject-to range at 18 through 65 inclusive. The draft (`snap_abawd_max_age`, CO-166) follows that literal reading. A common alternative reading, applied in other volumes of this codex (e.g. `mwr`'s WR-121, which cites "the reported FNS implementation"), treats the exemption as reaching age 65 itself, putting the subject-to ceiling at 64. No document in this volume's set states which reading FNS or CDHS applies to the amended text; the draft's choice should be revisited once CDHS issues implementing guidance. Items: CO-166, CO-412, CO-418.

Phase 8: the version of 10 CCR 2506-1 effective 2026-10-01 (D-82, S749) still states the pre-H.R.1 ABAWD definition - "Aged fifty-five (55) or older" is not an ABAWD, and a person residing with anyone under 18 is excluded - so no Colorado text yet settles the 18-64/18-65 reading of the amended statute.

Resolution (open-question sweep, 2026-11-21): FNS reads "over 65" as excepting a person aged 65. Its proposed rule "Modification to Work Requirements for Able-Bodied Adults" (91 FR 72439, Nov. 10, 2026; D-131) would amend 7 CFR 273.24(c)(1) to "Under 18 or 65 years of age or older" (S1302) and says the 2025 Act "increased the upper age limit for the time limit to include individuals through age 64" (S1301). The source is a proposed rule, not a final one (comments close 2026-12-10), but the proposal states that it would "codify USDA's regulatory implementation" of the Act (S1300), so it describes the reading FNS already applies, and no federal or Colorado text reads the statute the other way. snap_abawd_max_age (CO-166) is now 64, as a version from 2025-07-04 (the date of enactment; FNS's implementation memo, which would give a later implementation date if there is one, is served only from www.usda.gov, which refused it), citing S1300-S1302; snap_is_in_abawd_age_range (CO-412) gains tests at 65 (not in range) and 64 (in range). The preamble also says persons aged 60 to 64 are not excepted by age alone (proposed 273.24(c)(5) reads 7 CFR 273.7(b)(1)(ii)-(vii), not (i)); snap_is_exempt_from_abawd_time_limit (CO-414) already reads the work-registration exemptions without an age arm, and its precision says so. No household case has a person aged 65; no case expectation changed. Items: CO-166, CO-412, CO-414.

CDHS stakeholder review fix round (2026-11-21) (C-1): the review asks that no month before CDHS put the new ABAWD categories into effect, or before the person was notified, count. Colorado's fixed clock started over on July 1, 2026 (4.311.3.C.2, S1014; snap_abawd_clock_start_month, CO-1515), so for any determination from July 2026 no month before July 2026 counts, whatever the implementation date; earlier determinations are outside the volume's period. The implementation date itself and the notice requirement are program knowledge (OQ-533). CO-166 and CO-412 keep their July 4, 2025 start and now carry a county-facing statute_override note.

Audit fix round 4 (2026-11-21, legal UX walkthrough L9): the July 4, 2025 version of snap_abawd_max_age (CO-166) no longer rests on the November 10, 2026 proposed rule, which postdates determinations it applies to. It rests on the statute's own words as the federal regulation has always read them: sec. 10102 kept the form of the exception it replaced, "over ... years of age" (S127, S128); the Fiscal Responsibility Act of 2023 set "over 55 years of age" from fiscal year 2025 (Pub. L. 118-5 sec. 311(a)(1), D-203, S2113), and 7 CFR 273.24(c)(1) implements it as "Under 18 or 55 years of age or older", reverting on October 1, 2030 "from '55 years of age or older' to '50 years of age or older'", the regulation's reading of the original "over 50 years of age" (D-202, S2112). The same construction of "over 65" excepts age 65 and leaves 64 as the oldest age subject to the time limit, from enactment. The proposed rule (S1300-S1302) is cited as corroboration only: it proposes the same words, "Under 18 or 65 years of age or older". CO-166's version source is now S128; CO-412 cites S2112 and S2113. No value or case changed.

### OQ-32. Resolved: the pre-H.R.1 ABAWD exceptions for homelessness, veteran status, and former foster youth ended with the 2025 Act

Body: 7 CFR 273.24(c)(7)-(9) (S131) and 10 CCR 2506-1 4.311.1.C-E (S132) still list homelessness, veteran status, and having aged out of foster care at 18 (through age 24) as ABAWD exemptions. Pub. L. 119-21 sec. 10102 (S128) struck and replaced the entire exceptions paragraph, 7 U.S.C. 2015(o)(3), with a shorter list (S127) that does not include any of the three. Per the design brief's instruction that the amended statute controls where the regulation and Colorado's rule manual have not caught up (see OQ-1's treatment of the parallel alien-eligibility conflict), the draft's `snap_is_exempt_from_abawd_time_limit` (CO-414) does not model homelessness, veteran status, or former-foster-youth status as ABAWD exemptions, even though `snap_is_homeless`, `has_military_connection`, and `was_foster_youth_at_eighteen` remain in this volume's supplied-fact interface for other rules. A future revision should replace this with CDHS guidance implementing sec. 10102 if the regulation and rule manual are updated to match, or if CDHS chooses to continue these exemptions under a state option this draft has not found documented. Items: CO-414.

Phase 8: the version of 10 CCR 2506-1 effective 2026-10-01 (D-82, S750) still lists homelessness, veterans, and former foster youth aged 24 or younger as ABAWD exemptions (4.311.1.C-E); the rule history (S752) shows no H.R.1 amendment of 4.311. The draft continues to follow the amended statute; if CDHS treats 4.311.1 as still operative under a state option, these exemptions would return.

Resolution (open-question sweep, 2026-11-21): FNS's proposed rule (91 FR 72439; D-131, S1303) says "The OBBB removes the temporary exceptions at Section 6(o)(3) of the Act for homeless individuals, veterans, and individuals 24 years of age or younger and in foster care on their 18th birthday" and that the rule "would codify USDA's regulatory implementation of these changes" by replacing 7 CFR 273.24(c)(7)-(8) with the Indian or Urban Indian and California Indian exceptions and striking (c)(9). Colorado's 4.311.1.C-E is stale text, not a state option: the statute lists the exceptions, and a State's only lever is the discretionary exemptions of 7 U.S.C. 2015(o)(6). snap_is_exempt_from_abawd_time_limit (CO-414) is right as written and now cites S1303. The California Indian exception remains unmodeled for want of a fact (CO-414's precision). Items: CO-414.

### OQ-33. Resolved: Colorado's first ABAWD exemption is the general work requirement exemption, which CO-414 reads

Body: 7 U.S.C. 2015(o)(3)(D) (S127, S128) exempts an ABAWD who is "otherwise exempt under subsection (d)(2)," which is the same work-registration exemption paragraph modeled at CO-405 (`snap_is_exempt_from_work_registration`), sourced from 7 CFR 273.7(b)(1) and 10 CCR 2506-1 4.310.3 rather than the statute's own (d)(2) text directly. The draft treats the regulatory and Colorado exemption lists as faithful implementations of (d)(2) and reads CO-414's reference to CO-405 as satisfying 2015(o)(3)(D), rather than re-deriving a separate (d)(2)-only fact from S121 alone. Items: CO-414.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): answered by the current Colorado text. 10 CCR 2506-1 4.311.1.A (S750, D-82) makes the first ABAWD exemption a person "Exempt from the general work requirements" - the 4.310.3 exemptions snap_is_exempt_from_work_registration (CO-405) carries - so the Colorado rule itself states the coextension this question assumed for 7 U.S.C. 2015(o)(3)(D). Items: CO-414.

### OQ-34. Resolved: "single parent" for the full-time-student exemption counts the child's parents in the household

Body: 7 U.S.C. 2015(e)(8) (S133) and 10 CCR 2506-1 4.306.1.D (S135) state the single-parent full-time-student exemption applies "in those situations where only one natural, adoptive, or stepparent ... is in the same SNAP household as the child." This volume's relationship facts do not let a rule count how many parents of a given child are in the household directly; the draft (`snap_is_single_parent_full_time_student_exemption`, CO-409) instead tests that the person is a parent of a child under the relevant age and has no spouse relationship recorded, which is an approximation (a household could, in principle, include a second parent who is not the person's spouse). Items: CO-409.

Resolution (open-question sweep, 2026-11-21): 7 U.S.C. 2015(e)(8) and 4.306.1.D (S133, S135) are unambiguous: "only one natural, adoptive, or stepparent ... is in the same SNAP household as the child". snap_is_single_parent_full_time_student_exemption (CO-409) now counts parents: no other parent of this person's children may be in the household (P43, the persons who share a child with this person), and no spouse, since a spouse who is not the child's parent is a stepparent. New test CO-409-T4: the child's other parent in the household, not married to the student, defeats the exemption (the old derivation granted it). The count is per person rather than per child (CO-409's precision). No household case has a full-time student parent; no case expectation changed. Items: CO-409.

### OQ-35. Resolved: the Federal minimum wage is $7.25 from July 24, 2009 (29 U.S.C. 206(a)(1)(C))

Body: The work-registration employment exemption (7 U.S.C. 2015(d)(1)(E), S121; 7 CFR 273.7(b)(1)(vii), S123) and the student employment exemption (7 U.S.C. 2015(e)(4), S133; 7 CFR 273.5(b)(5), S134) both reference "the minimum hourly rate under the Fair Labor Standards Act of 1938 [29 U.S.C. 206(a)(1)]" without stating the dollar amount, and no other document in this volume's set (D-1 through D-25) quotes it either. The draft's `federal_minimum_wage` parameter (CO-160) uses $7.25, the rate in effect since 2009-07-24 under 29 U.S.C. 206(a)(1), without a document citation for the number itself, consistent with the mwr volume's WR-101 parameter of the same name. A future revision should replace this with a fetched excerpt of 29 U.S.C. 206(a)(1) if the number needs a primary citation. Items: CO-160.

Resolution (open-question sweep, 2026-11-21): 29 U.S.C. 206(a)(1) is fetched (D-134, S1309): "(C) $7.25 an hour, beginning 24 months after that 60th day", the 60th day after May 25, 2007, so from July 24, 2009. federal_minimum_wage (CO-160) cites it; the value is unchanged. Items: CO-160.

### OQ-36. Resolved: the standard utility allowance is a derived rule over Colorado's four tiers

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.407.31 effective 2026-10-01 (D-82, S743-S746) states Colorado's four-tiered mandatory SUA - Heating and Cooling Utility Allowance $616, Basic Utility Allowance $390, One Utility Allowance $73, telephone allowance $100, all effective October 1, 2026 - with each tier's conditions; FNS's FY2026 SUA table (D-85, S747) gives the prior year's Colorado figures ($594, $377, $71, $97). The four amounts are parameters (CO-1235-CO-1238), and the former supplied fact utility_allowance_amount is now the derived snap_utility_allowance_amount (CO-036, renamed for the SNAP prefix), computed from the household's utility facts (incurs_heating_cooling_cost, received_energy_assistance_within_twelve_months, non_heating_utility_cost_count, incurs_telephone_cost; CO-1239-CO-1242) through snap_entitled_to_heating_cooling_allowance (CO-1243); snap_shelter_costs (CO-377) and expedited service (CO-348) read it under the new name. Sec. 10103's limit of the energy-assistance route to households with an elderly or disabled member is applied (the Colorado rule has not adopted it; OQ-251), and internet fees are excluded by the rule itself (4.407.3.E.5, S753). Cases that state the amount directly keep their stated figure. Items: CO-036, CO-1235, CO-1236, CO-1237, CO-1238, CO-1243.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): the derived rule now carries its own ID, CO-1500 (S-6): phase 8 had reused CO-036, the ID of the deleted supplied fact, which docs/conventions.md forbids; CO-036 is retired and the identifier snap_utility_allowance_amount moves to CO-1500 unchanged. Items: CO-1500.

### OQ-37. Resolved: the elderly and disabled separate-household election is wired into household composition

Body: P-5 of the fix-round review found that the 273.1(b)(2) separate-household election (S20) had unused parameters (CO-105, CO-114) and an unused supplied fact (CO-019, is_elderly_disabled_unable_to_prepare_meals). This fix round adds `snap_meets_elderly_disabled_separate_household_election` (CO-419), which computes the income test the election turns on. It does not, however, change `snap_household_members` (CO-305) to actually carve the electing member (and spouse) out of the unit they live with: doing so needs a two-pass or fixed-point composition rule, because CO-305's buys_prepares_with/spouse/parent-child/caretaker-dependent closure would need to already know whether the election succeeds (which depends on "the others'" income, which in turn depends on who "the others" are, i.e., on composition) before it can decide whether to include or exclude the electing member. A future batch should either (a) compute CO-419 from the physical residence-sharing group (as it does now, which is composition-independent) and then have CO-305 read it as one more edge-exclusion condition (removing the buys_prepares_with/spouse edge to an electing member's non-spouse housemates when CO-419 is true for that member), or (b) restructure CO-305 as an explicit two-pass rule. Items: CO-419, CO-305.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): 7 CFR 273.1(b)(2) (S20) is clear; wiring the election into snap_household_members (CO-305) needs a two-pass composition rule or an edge-exclusion read, as the question describes. Not done in this sweep.

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep, supplied-facts round): 7 CFR 273.1(b)(2) (S1604) and 10 CCR 2506-1 4.304.3.A (S1605) make the separate household an option for a member sixty or older unable to purchase and prepare meals. The new supplied snap_requests_elderly_disabled_separate_household (CO-2303, false when unstated) records the request; snap_meets_elderly_disabled_separate_household_election (CO-419) now also requires the request and age sixty (snap_is_elderly). The new snap_is_in_elderly_disabled_separate_household (CO-2304) marks the electing person and spouse for the determination month, and snap_household_members (CO-305) no longer crosses a buys_prepares_with edge into or out of that couple; spouse and mandatory parent/child edges still cross (4.304.1). This is the edge-exclusion reading the question proposed: CO-419 is computed from the physical residence group, so there is no cycle. New tests CO-305-T7 to T9, CO-419-T5 and T6, CO-2304-T1 to T3. Colorado's 4.304.3.A.2 counts the spouse in the others' income test when the spouse is the one who purchases and prepares meals; no fact says who does, so CO-419 keeps 273.1(b)(2)'s exclusion of the spouse (recorded on CO-419). Every case person states is_elderly_disabled_unable_to_prepare_meals false or leaves the request unstated; no expectation changed.

### OQ-38. Resolved: initial-month proration, the ABAWD regaining of eligibility and additional three months, and voluntary quit

Body: The fix-round review (P-4, P-7, P-8) identified three provisions that determine eligibility or amount and are not modeled in this chapter, and this fix round does not model them (out of scope for this phase; recorded here so a future phase note can point to it):

- P-4: initial-month proration of the allotment from the date of application (7 CFR 273.10(a)(1)(ii)-(iii)) and the $10 minimum-issuance floor in an initial month, plus the initial-month carve-out from the ordinary minimum allotment (7 CFR 273.10(e)(2)(ii)(C), "[e]xcept during an initial month"). This chapter has no fact distinguishing an application date from the determination date, and no notion of an "initial month" at all.

- P-7: the ABAWD second set of up to three additional countable months (7 CFR 273.24(b), (e)) and regaining eligibility by working 80 hours in a 30-day period (7 CFR 273.24(d)). `snap_abawd_time_limit_status` (CO-418)'s enum (not_subject | within_limit | limit_reached) has no branch for either.

- P-8: voluntary quit and reduction of work effort below 30 hours a week without good cause (7 CFR 273.7(a)(1)(vii)) as an independent ground of ineligibility, distinct from the sanction-procedure fact `snap_is_disqualified_for_work_requirement_noncompliance` (CO-040) this chapter already carries.

Each is a genuine gap for a household that applies mid-month, accrues ABAWD countable months, or has a work-quit history, but building any of the three correctly needs new facts and, for P-7, a redesign of the ABAWD counting-months items (CO-416/CO-417/CO-418) this fix round did not have the scope to redo alongside the correctness fixes (C-1..C-10) and the household-composition and case work. A future batch should model each from the citations above. Items: CO-040, CO-418.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): current citation. 10 CCR 2506-1 (D-82, effective 2026-10-01) states each provision: initial-month proration and the initial-month threshold, which in Colorado is the minimum allotment rather than the federal $10 (4.207.2.A-C, 4.207.3.B, `documents/D-82.md:2553-2589`); regaining eligibility and the single additional three months (4.311.3.D, `D-82.md:5575-5596`); voluntary quit (4.308, 4.310.5). They stay open only because they are not modeled. The month of the eighteenth birthday and Colorado's fixed 36-month clock (4.311.3.A.2, C.2, S1014) are now modeled in CO-416 and CO-417.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): all three provisions are stated in D-82 (cited above); each needs facts the ledger lacks (an initial-month marker distinct from the determination month, countable-month history for the additional three months and regaining eligibility, and a voluntary-quit history). Not modeled in this sweep.

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep, supplied-facts round). (P-4) 10 CCR 2506-1 4.000.1 (S1614) defines the initial month; 4.207.2.A-C (S1615) prorate it from the date of application over the exact days of the month, except a migrant job-stream household after a break of thirty days or less; 4.207.3.B-C (S1616) pay nothing for an initial month under the minimum allotment and apply the one- and two-person minimum only outside it; 7 CFR 273.10(a)(1)(iii)(C) (S1617) rounds down. New: snap_is_initial_month (CO-2305, from snap_application_date and snap_determination_is_recertification, CO-1746 and CO-1567), snap_is_migrant_in_job_stream_with_short_break (CO-2306), snap_full_month_allotment (CO-2307, the former CO-385 computation), snap_prorated_initial_month_allotment (CO-2308); snap_allotment (CO-385) prorates in the initial month; snap_is_eligible (CO-386) and snap_eligibility_status (CO-387) read the full month, since 4.207.3.B certifies a household whose initial-month zero is due to proration. The engine gains P47 (the number of days from a date to a date; docs/conventions.md). (P-7) 4.311.3.D (S1613): snap_abawd_eligibility_regained (CO-2309), snap_abawd_additional_months_start_month (CO-2310), snap_abawd_additional_months (CO-2311, 3) and snap_abawd_is_in_additional_months (CO-2312); snap_abawd_time_limit_status (CO-418) gains regained and additional_months. (P-8) 4.000.1, 4.308.A, B, E (S1611) and 4.310.8.A-B (S1612): snap_voluntary_quit_date (CO-2313), snap_work_requirement_violation_number (CO-2314), snap_voluntary_quit_disqualification_start_month (CO-2315), the one, three and six months (CO-2316, CO-2317, CO-2319), the sixty-day look-back (CO-2320) and snap_is_disqualified_for_voluntary_quit (CO-2318), which makes the person an ineligible member (CO-311) counted in full (CO-2302, read by CO-333 and CO-371). Household cases: CO-03 now states that the couple recertifies (snap_determination_is_recertification true, application 2026-09-15), so October is not an initial month and its $25 minimum allotment stands; CO-01 applies on October 1, so its prorated October allotment equals the full month ($336); no expectation changed. Not modeled: 4.207.2.A's release-date proration for residents of a public institution applying jointly for SSI and SNAP, which has no fact (recorded on CO-2305).

Final verification (docs/reviews/final-snap.md F-1, 2026-11-22): the threshold for an initial month is the statute's $10, not the minimum allotment. 7 U.S.C. 2017(c)(1) (D-4, S2500) prorates the initial month and withholds the allotment only "if the value of the allotment which such household would otherwise be eligible to receive under this subsection is less than $10", as 7 CFR 273.10(a)(1)(iii)(C) and (e)(2)(ii)(B) (S1617, S2501) also say; 10 CCR 2506-1 4.207.3.B (S737) names "the federal minimum allotment" ($25 from October 1, 2026). The statute governs: snap_allotment (CO-385) now reads snap_initial_month_minimum_issuance (CO-3700, $10) and carries a statute_override naming 4.207.3.B. Rule test CO-385-T6 changes from 0 to 18 (an $18 prorated benefit is issued); no household case has an initial-month benefit between $10 and $24, so no case expectation changed.

### OQ-41. Resolved: MAGI non-filer household: siblings under 19 are counted through shared_relative (batch 6)

Body: 42 CFR 435.603(f)(3)(iii) and 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.4.E.1.d.iv include, for an applicant under 19, "the applicant's ... siblings under age 19" living with them. Batch 5 could not derive this: testing whether two different persons (the applicant and a candidate sibling) share a common parent looked like a two-hop join the pattern catalog's group operators could not express within one filter pass, and the one operator that walks parent/child edges, `reachable`, reapplies the same crossing condition at every hop with no notion of the original subject, over-including an intermediate minor parent's own parent when tested against p5 (whose only parent, p4, is herself under 19).

Resolution: the engine already implements a pattern batch 5 did not find - `shared_relative` (P43 in docs/conventions.md, "the persons who share a [relationship] with this person"), added for this phase and confirmed present in `src/engine/evaluate.ts`/`check.ts`/`render.ts`. It computes siblings directly (the persons, other than this person, who have a person in the named role - `parent` - in common), without walking a chain at all, so it does not over-include. Batch 6 added a third disjunct to ma_magi_household_members' (CO-500) non-filer branch: `[all, [<, age, ma_magi_child_age_limit], [in_group, [P], [shared_relative, parent]], [<, [of, [P], age], ma_magi_child_age_limit]]`, and added a rule test (CO-500-T6) with two true siblings under 19 to confirm it. For the target household this still makes no numeric difference (p5's only parent, p4, has no other children), but a household with true minor siblings is now counted correctly. Items: CO-500, CO-501, CO-503, CO-504.

### OQ-42. Resolved: MAGI household: the joint-filing and non-custodial-parent exceptions (435.603(f)(2)(ii)-(iii)) are not modeled

Body: 42 CFR 435.603(f)(2)(ii)-(iii) and 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.4.E.1.c.ii-iii route a child under 19 to the non-filer household rules when (ii) the child is claimed by one parent, lives with both parents, but the parents do not expect to file jointly, or (iii) the child is claimed by a non-custodial parent. This batch created files_jointly_with_spouse (CO-077) toward testing (ii), but modeling (ii) correctly needs "living with both parents" (a count of parent relationships) and "the claiming parent's spouse is the child's other parent," and modeling (iii) needs a custodial-parent fact this batch's supplied-fact interface does not include. ma_magi_household_members (CO-500) models only the 435.603(f)(2)(i) exception (claimed by someone other than a spouse or parent), which is what the target household's p5 needs. Items: CO-500.

Phase 7 sweep: the current § 8.100.4.E (D-65) states the same joint-filing and non-custodial-parent routes; they need custodial-parent and living-with-both-parents facts, a modeling gap rather than an unanswered question of rule, so this stays open.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): modeled. 42 CFR 435.603(f)(2)(ii)-(iii) (S1226) and the current § 8.100.4.E.1.c and E.5 (S1227) route a claimed child under 19 to the non-filer rules when (ii) claimed by one parent while living with both parents who do not expect to file jointly, (iii) claimed by a non-custodial parent (custody per a court order or agreement, else most nights), or (E.5) claimed by married parents filing jointly while one lives outside the household on separation or pending divorce. New facts: lives_with (CO-1925, shared, also read by the community engagement exclusions), ma_custodial_parent (CO-1946), ma_lives_apart_due_to_separation_or_pending_divorce (CO-1947); new rule ma_dependent_is_routed_to_non_filer_rules (CO-1976). ma_magi_household_members (CO-500) routes on CO-1976 and counts in the non-filer household only the persons in this person's lives_with (where it is stated), so a non-custodial parent or a separated parent outside the home is not counted. Where lives_with or the custodial parent is not stated the earlier conventions hold (every related person lives together; the claiming parent is custodial), as the facts' meanings say. CO-04 now states p4's custodial parent and home, so its non-custodial-parent claim is decided by the rule.

### OQ-43. Resolved: Cover All Coloradans' postpartum extension is modeled (batch 6)

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.3.G.1.g.viii.1 extends the immigration-status exception to a person "within 12 months of the beginning of their postpartum period," not only a currently pregnant person. ma_meets_citizenship_requirement (CO-509) tested only is_pregnant in batch 5, because a postpartum-period fact (pregnancy_end_date) was a batch-6 identifier per the phase-3 brief's interface.

Resolution: batch 6 added pregnancy_end_date (CO-089) and ma_postpartum_coverage_end_month (CO-560, 12 months after the month the pregnancy ended, per 42 U.S.C. 1396a(e)(16)), then edited ma_meets_citizenship_requirement (CO-509) to add a postpartum disjunct: true when the determination date falls on or before the last day of ma_postpartum_coverage_end_month, and definitionally false (not unknown) when pregnancy_end_date itself is unknown, so the addition does not turn the item unknown for every person who has never been asked about a pregnancy end date. Two new tests (CO-509-T5, CO-509-T6) exercise it. Note the open question this raised about which postpartum period Colorado's own text actually elects (60 days per 8.100.4.G.5's own sentence, or 12 months per this clause and 1396a(e)(16)) is tracked separately as OQ-52, since it affects ma_postpartum_coverage_end_month (CO-560) generally, not only this citizenship test. Items: CO-509.

### OQ-44. Resolved: one immigration_status fact with the full documented category set; each program's acceptance in its own derived items

Body: The phase-3 brief offered two ways to reach 8.100.3.G.1.g.iv/.vi's broader lawfully-present categories (parolee, TPS holder, pending asylum applicant with work authorization, and so on): fold new options into citizenship_status (CO-002, batch 1, shared across every program) or add a separate supplied fact. This batch added lawful_presence_status (CO-084) rather than editing CO-002, because citizenship_status is read by SNAP's narrower "qualified alien" test (7 U.S.C. 2015(f)) and every other program chapter this volume will eventually author, and widening its ten-option enumeration to also carry Medicaid-only lawful-presence categories would change what every consumer's rules must handle for values that only ever matter to Medicaid. A reviewer could instead choose to fold the two fact spaces together once CHP+ (batch 7) and non-MAGI (phase 4) show whether they need the same distinction. Items: CO-084, CO-507, CO-509.

Phase 7 sweep: a design choice about the volume's fact interface; no published rule settles it.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): Checked 2026-11-21: 42 CFR 435.406 and § 8.100.3.G.1.g (D-65), which state the categories but not how a ledger stores them; no published rule settles it.

Resolution (audit fix round, 2026-11-21; product-owner decision at the end of docs/plan-colorado.md; docs/reviews/audit-dual-entry.md 3.1; same-word audit SW-18): Resolved as the product owner directed. citizenship_status (CO-002) is renamed immigration_status through a change set that keeps the ID and carries 32 options: the 8 U.S.C. 1641(b)-(c) qualified categories (S1900, S1901), the § 8.100.3.G.1.g.i-viii categories (S174, S655-S658, S1902-S1905), CHP+'s 10 CCR 2505-3 § 110.1.B list (S1906-S1908), LEAP's 3.753.16-17 (S1909), DACA (excluded by vii.6) and undocumented. lawful_presence_status (CO-084), federal_five_year_bar_exempt_category (CO-1724) and ma_five_year_bar_exempt_category (CO-1170) are retired; the status history is status_before_lpr_adjustment (CO-2700), is_american_indian_born_in_canada_or_tribal_member (CO-2701), is_hmong_or_highland_lao_veteran (CO-2702) and parole_end_date (CO-2703). The shared derived items read the one fact: is_us_citizen_or_national (CO-1358), is_qualified_non_citizen (CO-506, 1641), claims_lawful_immigration_status (CO-1359), is_exempt_from_five_year_bar (CO-1725, 1613(b)). Each program's acceptance is its own derived item citing its own list: SNAP snap_is_eligible_non_citizen (CO-308, 7 U.S.C. 2015(f) as amended); Medicaid ma_is_lawfully_present (CO-507), ma_is_five_year_bar_exempt (CO-1172), ma_is_in_federal_payment_status_category (CO-2400) and ma_meets_citizenship_requirement (CO-509, now with the g.ii and g.viii arms); CHP+ chp_has_qualifying_immigration_status (CO-2706) and chp_meets_status_requirement (CO-2707), new; Colorado Works, AND and AB meets_public_assistance_citizenship_requirement (CO-905, C.R.S. 26-2-111.8) and OAP af_meets_citizenship_requirement_oap (CO-929); CCCAP cccap_child_meets_citizenship_requirement (CO-998, 3.103.Z, OQ-394 resolved); LEAP leap_meets_citizenship_requirement (CO-1010, 3.753.16). SUN Bucks has no status test (OQ-551). The SAVE status list could not be fetched (OQ-550). Every rule test and household case was migrated (citizen is us_citizen; the lumped other_qualified_non_citizen became parolee_one_year_or_more or deportation_withheld, other_lawfully_present_non_citizen became temporary_protected_status, as the tests' titles say); no household expectation changed. Items: CO-002, CO-084, CO-1724, CO-1170, CO-2700 to CO-2707, CO-506, CO-507, CO-509, CO-998, CO-1010, CO-1172, CO-1358, CO-1359, CO-1725, CO-2364, CO-2386, CO-947, CO-2400, CO-308.

### OQ-49. Resolved: Parents/Caretaker Relatives category has no numeric federal-floor comparison

Body: 42 CFR 435.110(c) states the federal minimum income floor for the Parents and Caretaker Relatives group as "a State's AFDC income standard in effect as of May 1, 1988 for the applicable family size converted to a MAGI-equivalent standard" - a historical dollar figure this volume has no fetched document stating, unlike the flat 133%/185% percentages 435.118, 435.119, and 435.116 state for the Children's, Adults, and Pregnant Women groups. ma_income_limit_parent_caretaker_pct (CO-541) therefore applies Colorado's 60% (10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.4.G.3) directly, with no federal-floor comparison parallel to ma_magi_income_standard_child/_adult/_pregnant (CO-540, CO-542, CO-543). Since Colorado's own state plan already reflects an approved MAGI-equivalent conversion of its historical AFDC standard, this is unlikely to matter in practice, but a reviewer with access to CMS's 1902(e)(14) conversion guidance could add the comparison. Items: CO-541.

Phase 7 sweep: the current § 8.100.4.G.3 (D-65, S639) states 68% (MAGI-equivalent) and CMS's eligibility levels table (D-73) lists 68%, but neither states the May 1, 1988 AFDC standard converted to MAGI, so the federal-floor comparison still has no published figure.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): 42 CFR 435.110(b)-(c) (S1235) makes the May 1, 1988 AFDC standard the minimum for the income standard the State sets in its plan; it is not a second test of the individual. The individual is tested only against the State plan standard (68%, § 8.100.4.G.3), as ma_is_parent_caretaker_category (CO-547) already does. No change.

### OQ-50. Resolved: Colorado eliminated the dependent-child deprivation requirement (state plan page S25)

Body: 42 CFR 435.4's "dependent child" definition has a second criterion beyond age - deprivation of parental support by reason of a parent's death, absence, physical or mental incapacity, or unemployment (defined as working fewer than 100 hours a month) - unless the state has elected to eliminate it. Colorado's MAGI conversion at 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.4.G.3 states the Parents and Caretaker Relatives category's dependent-child requirement without restating a deprivation clause, consistent with MAGI's general elimination of AFDC-era deprivation and resource tests across the mandatory groups (8.100.4.G.2's Children's category and 8.100.4.G.4's Adults category likewise carry no deprivation language), so ma_is_dependent_child (CO-544) assumes Colorado elected to eliminate the deprivation requirement rather than silently applying a federal floor Colorado's own text does not restate. A reviewer with access to Colorado's approved 1902(e)(14) State Plan Amendment could confirm the election. Items: CO-544.

Phase 7 sweep: the current § 8.100.4.G.3 (D-65) still states no deprivation requirement; whether that is an election to eliminate it is a state plan question no fetched rule text answers.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): Checked 2026-11-21: 42 CFR 435.4 (deprivation unless the State eliminates it in its plan) and the current § 8.100.4.G.3 (D-65), which states no deprivation requirement; the election is in the State plan, which no allowed host publishes; no published rule settles it.

Resolution (coverage round, 2026-11-22; coverage review F-10): Colorado's MAGI state plan amendment CO-13-0045-MM1 (D-315, approved 12/18/2013, effective 01/01/2014), page S25 (Parents and Other Caretaker Relatives), selects "The state elects to eliminate the requirement that a dependent child must be deprived of parental support or care by reason of the death, physical or mental incapacity, or absence from the home or unemployment of at least one parent" (S3362). The assumption is confirmed: ma_is_dependent_child (CO-544) defines a dependent child by age and living with the parent or caretaker relative alone, and now cites S3362. No answer changes. Items: CO-544.

Checked 2026-11-22: Colorado State Plan Amendment CO-13-0045-MM1, page S25 (D-315, medicaid.gov, transcribed from the page image), 42 CFR 435.4 (D-35) and 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.4.G.3 (D-65).

### OQ-51. Resolved: Caretaker relative's specific relation categories not separately tested

Body: 42 CFr 435.4's "caretaker relative" definition lists specific relations (father, mother, grandfather, grandmother, brother, sister, stepfather, stepmother, stepbrother, stepsister, uncle, aunt, first cousin, nephew, niece, or a parent's/relative's spouse) rather than treating any household member who cares for a child as a caretaker relative. ma_is_parent_caretaker_category (CO-547) tests only this volume's "caretaker" relationship role (and "parent"), on the assumption that a case author would only state a caretaker edge for a relation 435.4 actually recognizes; the rule does not itself validate that the caretaker relationship named on a case is one of 435.4's listed relations. Items: CO-547.

Phase 7 sweep: a modeling choice about the caretaker relationship edge; the current rule text adds nothing that settles it.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the rule is clear (triage: NEEDS FACT) but this sweep did not model it; it needs the relation degree of each caretaker edge (a per-edge or per-person fact quoting the 435.4 list, which 42 CFR 435.554(a) repeats for community engagement). The new community engagement exclusion (CO-1962) reads the same caretaker edge and carries the same assumption.

Phase-10 needs-fact sweep (2026-11-21): resolved without a new fact. Colorado's definition takes 42 CFR 435.4's State option (3) (D-35:41): "Caretaker Relative is a person who is related to the dependent child or any adult with whom the dependent child is living and who assumes responsibility for the dependent child's care" (10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.1, S1500). Any relative, or any adult living with the child who assumes responsibility for the child's care, is a caretaker relative, so the degree of relation is not a condition and the caretaker edge (a person who lives with the child and assumes responsibility for its care) states all the rule asks. ma_is_parent_caretaker_category (CO-547) and the community engagement exclusion (CO-1962, whose 435.554(a)(iv) optional relatives follow the State's 435.110 elections) cite S1500. No expectation changed.

### OQ-52. Resolved: the postpartum coverage period is 12 months (current 8.100.4.G.5)

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.4.G.5's own sentence states pregnant-woman coverage runs "through the last day of the month following 60 days from the date the pregnancy ends," which is the pre-ARPA federal default (42 CFR 435.170(b), also 60 days). But 8.100.3.G.1.g.viii.1's Cover All Coloradans exception, cited as S175 in batch 5, refers to a person "within 12 months of the beginning of their postpartum period," and 42 U.S.C. 1396a(e)(16) is the federal statute letting a state elect a 12-month postpartum extension (which nearly every state, including Colorado, has adopted post-ARPA). ma_postpartum_coverage_end_month (CO-560) and the postpartum extension added to ma_meets_citizenship_requirement (CO-509, batch 5) both follow the 12-month reading as the more specific, more recently amended text, and treat 8.100.4.G.5's 60-day sentence as an unamended cross-reference the rule text has not caught up to; a reviewer with access to Colorado's approved 1396a(e)(16) State Plan Amendment election could confirm this is correct and that 8.100.4.G.5 needs a text correction rather than a substantive difference. Items: CO-560, CO-198, CO-509.

Resolution (phase 7): the current Secretary of State text of 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.4.G.5 (D-65, rule version effective 11/14/2026, S640) reads "through the last day of the 12th month following the date the pregnancy ends". The 60-day sentence was in the out-of-date Cornell copy (D-40). ma_postpartum_coverage_end_month (CO-560) and ma_postpartum_coverage_months (CO-198) already followed the 12-month reading; both now cite S640 and no longer list this question. Items: CO-560, CO-566, CO-198.

### OQ-53. Resolved: Continuous eligibility's non-residency exceptions not modeled

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.3.Q.1.b and 42 CFR 435.926(d) list several exceptions that end a child's continuous eligibility period early beyond ceasing to be a Colorado resident: the child's death, becoming an inmate of a public institution, no longer being part of the required household, being unable to be located, requesting a voluntary withdrawal, or the eligibility having been erroneously granted through agency error or fraud. ma_continuous_eligibility_applies (CO-555) models only the age, category, and residency conditions because this volume has no supplied fact for any of the other exceptions. A future batch could add supplied facts for these (for example is_deceased, is_inmate_of_public_institution, is_unable_to_be_located) if continuous eligibility's month-by-month status needs to be tracked precisely rather than just its starting entitlement. Items: CO-555.

Phase 7 sweep: the current § 8.100.3.Q (D-65) states the same exceptions; modeling them needs facts (death, inmate status, whereabouts unknown, withdrawal request, agency error) the volume does not carry for this purpose - a modeling gap, not an open reading of rule.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the rule is clear (triage: NEEDS FACT) but this sweep did not model it; it needs supplied facts for the child's death, inmate status, whereabouts unknown, voluntary withdrawal and an erroneous grant (42 CFR 435.926(d), § 8.100.3.Q.1.b), each ending the period in ma_continuous_eligibility_applies (CO-555).

Phase-10 needs-fact sweep (2026-11-21): resolved. § 8.100.3.Q.1.b (S1502) ends the period effective the earliest possible month on death, inmate status (moving the child to the limited incarceration benefit until release), leaving the required household, being unable to be located, a withdrawal request, failing to provide documentation during a reasonable opportunity period, or an erroneous grant. New facts is_deceased (CO-2200), ma_has_left_required_household (CO-2201), ma_is_unable_to_be_located (CO-2202), ma_has_requested_withdrawal_from_continuous_eligibility (CO-2203) and ma_eligibility_was_erroneously_granted (CO-2204); the inmate prong reuses is_inmate_of_public_institution (CO-1105) and the reasonable opportunity prong reads the volume's reasonable opportunity items. ma_continuous_eligibility_exception_applies (CO-2205) gathers them and ma_child_is_in_continuous_eligibility_period (CO-561) reads it month by month, since the exceptions end a period already open. The household cases state the facts false for the children in a period (CO-01, CO-02, CO-04, CO-05, CO-73); no expectation changed.

### OQ-54. Resolved: presumptive eligibility reads the attested income, not the computed household income

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.4.F.2, 42 CFR 435.1101's "preliminary information" language, and 42 U.S.C. 1396r-1/1396r-1a all describe presumptive eligibility as turning on a qualified entity's determination from preliminary, self-attested (not verified) income - conceptually a lighter-weight figure than the fully verified MAGI household income a regular determination uses. The phase-3 brief offered a separate attested_household_income_pct_fpl supplied fact as an option; ma_is_presumptively_eligible_pregnant (CO-558) and ma_is_presumptively_eligible_child (CO-559) instead reuse ma_magi_household_income_pct_fpl (CO-504), because this volume's underlying income facts (wages_gross and the rest) are themselves applicant-stated at the point of collection and not independently verified until later in the determination process, so a separate "attested" figure would duplicate rather than differ from the computed one in this volume's model. A reviewer could still choose to split them if a presumptive-eligibility site's income figure should be allowed to diverge from the fuller application's. Items: CO-558, CO-559.

Phase 7 sweep: a fact-interface choice; no published rule distinguishes the attested figure from the application's income in a way that settles it.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): Checked 2026-11-21: § 8.100.4.F.2 and 42 CFR 435.1101-435.1102, which describe preliminary attested income without requiring a separate figure; no published rule settles it.

HCPF fix round (2026-11-21, docs/reviews/stakeholder-hcpf.md), S-11: settled in the items. ma_is_presumptively_eligible_pregnant (CO-558) and ma_is_presumptively_eligible_child (CO-559) read attested_household_income_pct_fpl, the figure the applicant declares at the presumptive eligibility site (§ 8.100.4.F.2, S1501), and the new hospital presumptive eligibility category (CO-2439) reads the same declared figure (§ 8.100.3.R.2, S1722); the question's premise (reuse of ma_magi_household_income_pct_fpl) no longer describes any item. CO-558 and CO-559 no longer carry this question. Items: none.

Audit fix round 4 (2026-11-21, the product owner's decision that presumptive eligibility uses an attested income fact separate from the computed MAGI income; heavier-definitions audit H-7, H-9): the attested figure is now the declaration itself, not a ratio the site computed. declared_household_income_monthly (CO-092, renamed from attested_household_income_pct_fpl) and declared_household_size (CO-3150) are what the applicant declares on the presumptive eligibility or hospital site application, or on the Express Lane source application; declared_household_income_pct_fpl (CO-3151) derives the ratio from the one federal poverty table. Every presumptive route reads it and none reads ma_magi_household_income: CO-558, CO-559 and the new limited family planning route CO-3155 (§ 8.100.4.F.2), the hospital route CO-2439 (§ 8.100.3.R.2, with the applicant's own declared income, CO-2434 and CO-3153, for the employed-disabled group and the applicant counted as two for limited family planning, CO-3152, OQ-590), CHP+ presumptive eligibility CO-708 (10 CCR 2505-3 § 170.1), and Express Lane (CO-3156, § 8.100.4.J.1.a.v, J.2.a.v). Items: none.

### OQ-55. Resolved: Retroactive coverage's "whichever is later" not modeled

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.3.E.1 and 42 CFR 435.915(a) both measure retroactive coverage from "the three months preceding the date of application, or as of the date the person became eligible for Medical Assistance, whichever is later." ma_retroactive_coverage_start_month (CO-557) models only the three-months-before-application half of this rule; it does not compare that month against a separate "date the person became eligible" this volume does not track as a distinct fact (eligibility itself is evaluated month by month through ma_magi_is_eligible, CO-553, rather than as a single anchor date).
