A person retroactively eligible for fewer than three months because they were not otherwise eligible earlier in the period is not distinguished by this item alone; a reviewer evaluating ma_magi_is_eligible for each month in the three-month window can still determine the correct per-month answer. Items: CO-557.

Phase 7 sweep: the current § 8.100.3.E.1 (D-65) restates 'or as of the date the person became eligible for Medical Assistance, whichever is later'; the rule is clear and the gap is the volume's month-by-month model, not the text.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the rule is clear (triage: NEEDS FACT) but this sweep did not model it; it needs a month-by-month eligibility history (the date the person became eligible) for ma_retroactive_coverage_start_month (CO-557) to take the later of the two dates.

Phase-10 needs-fact sweep (2026-11-21): resolved. New fact ma_eligibility_start_date (CO-2206), the date the person became eligible (§ 8.100.3.E.1, S1503); CO-557 takes the later of the three-month start and the month of that date (new tests CO-557-T3, T4). CO-04 p1 and CO-05 p3 state dates at the start of the three-month period (eligible throughout), so their expectations (2026-07, 2026-01) are unchanged.

Coverage pass 7, C7-01 and C7-08 (2026-11-24): resolved differently. The supplied date (CO-2206) is retired: it restated an outcome the months' own facts decide, and left the window unknown, read as covered, where it was not stated. 42 CFR 435.915(a)(2) (S218) covers a retroactive month in which the person "Would have been eligible for Medicaid at the time he received the services", and 42 U.S.C. 1396a(a)(34) as amended (S1706) one in which the person "was (or upon application would have been) eligible for such assistance at the time such care and services were furnished": eligibility is decided month by month (ma_magi_eligibility_status, CO-554), so E.1's later date is the first month whose facts make the person eligible. CO-557 is the three (from 2027, one or two) months before the application month; CO-4101 marks the months before it.

### OQ-56. Resolved: Presumptive eligibility's citizenship/lawful-presence declaration not modeled

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.4.F.2.a-b conditions presumptive eligibility on the applicant declaring they are "a United States citizen or a documented immigrant," in addition to the income test. ma_is_presumptively_eligible_pregnant (CO-558) and ma_is_presumptively_eligible_child (CO-559) model only the age/pregnancy and income conditions, not this declaration, because this volume's citizenship_status and lawful_presence_status facts are modeled as verified (or at least applicant-stated with later verification) facts for a full determination, and 8.100.4.F.2's presumptive-eligibility declaration is explicitly unverified at the site level (435.1102(d)(2)(ii): the agency "may not... require verification of the conditions for presumptive eligibility"). A reviewer could add a lighter declared_citizen_or_documented_immigrant supplied fact if presumptive eligibility needs to be denied for a declared non-citizen/non-documented status distinctly from a full Medicaid citizenship failure. Items: CO-558, CO-559.

Phase 7 sweep: a fact-interface choice; the current presumptive eligibility text does not settle it.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the rule is clear (triage: NEEDS FACT) but this sweep did not model it; it needs a declared citizenship-or-documented-immigrant fact distinct from the verified status for ma_is_presumptively_eligible_pregnant/child (CO-558, CO-559).

Phase-10 needs-fact sweep (2026-11-21): resolved without a new fact. The current § 8.100.4.F.2.a-b (S1501) asks for the declaration "unless they meet the exceptions outlined under 8.100.3.G.1.g.viii", and § 8.100.3.G.1.g.ix.1.a (S607) says persons under 19 and pregnant persons "shall not be excluded from ... MAGI Prenatal Presumptive Eligibility, MAGI Child Presumptive Eligibility" on the basis of immigration status. Every person these two categories reach is therefore excepted, and a declared-status fact could never change the result. The cross-reference to viii (the Afghan humanitarian parolee paragraph, an exemption from a waiting period) is read as ix, which names these categories. CO-558 and CO-559 now say so in their precision and cite S1501 and S607.

### OQ-57. Resolved: the CHP+ enrollment fee section (10 CCR 2505-3 § 310) is repealed; there is no enrollment fee to model

Body: The fetched copy of 10 CCR 2505-3-300 (volumes/co/documents/D-42.md, retrieved 2026-09-18, from the Cornell/law.cornell.edu mirror of the Colorado CCR) rendered only the section heading and the amendment-history "Notes" block - no band boundary, no dollar figure, and no text distinguishing fee amounts by number of children. Every other section this chapter cites (50, 110, 120, 150, 170, 320, 330, 420, 430) rendered its substantive text normally from the same mirror, so this is a rendering gap in that one mirror's copy of that one section, not a repealed or genuinely blank section - the amendment history ends "46 CR 19, October 10, 2023, effective 10/30/2023," the same last entry as the (fully rendered) §§ 320 and 330, so the section was substantively amended as recently as 2023 and almost certainly still has operative text. Per this volume's provenance rule, no number or existence claim is stated from memory: this chapter does not assert that CHP+ currently charges an enrollment fee, does not assert that it does not, and does not model a fee item (chp_annual_enrollment_fee, formerly CO-093, is removed in the fix round rather than kept as an unread placeholder asserting a fee schedule structure - "varies by income band and number of children enrolled" - that no fetched document states).

This is a **blocking provenance gap**: 10 CCR 2505-3-300's operative text must be re-fetched - from the Colorado Secretary of State's own CCR publication (the authoritative source; the Cornell mirror is what failed here) and/or the HCPF CHP+ cost-sharing web page that both § 300 and § 320 implement - before any enrollment-fee item is authored. Two pieces of already-fetched evidence are suggestive but not sufficient on their own: § 330's enumeration of cost-sharing rules (330.1-330.4) never mentions an enrollment fee, and § 320's copayment income bands (<101%, 101-150% FPL) lie entirely below § 110.1.E's 142% CHP+ floor (OQ-63), suggesting §§ 300 and 320 may be of the same stale vintage - but that is inference from adjacent text, not the operative text of § 300 itself, and 42 CFR 457.560(a) (S250) and 42 U.S.C. 1397cc(e)(3)(B) (S252) both still contemplate an enrollment fee as a permitted CHIP charge, so the federal text does not settle whether Colorado currently charges one either. Once § 300's text is fetched, the derived rule (a `case` over ma_magi_household_income_pct_fpl bands with parameterized dollar amounts, gated by chp_is_exempt_from_cost_sharing, OQ-64) should be authored as one coherent sub-chapter with the § 320/§ 330 items OQ-64 describes. Items: (none currently - the removed CO-093 is not replaced by a placeholder).

Resolution (phase 7): the current Secretary of State text of 10 CCR 2505-3 (D-66, rule version effective 08/30/2026, S642) reads "300 ENROLLMENT FEES AND COPAYMENTS" followed by "310 [Repealed eff. 10/30/2022]" and then § 320 Copayments; no other section states a fee. The Cornell copy (D-42) rendered only the § 300 heading because the fee section under it had been repealed. No enrollment fee item is authored; the copayment schedule and the cost-sharing exemptions remain OQ-60/OQ-64's modeling gaps. Items: (none).

### OQ-58. Resolved: 10 CCR 2505-3 § 120.1.C's inmate and IMD exclusion is modeled with two new supplied facts

Body: 10 CCR 2505-3-120.1.C excludes a person who is "an inmate of a public institution or a patient in an institution for mental diseases" from CHP+ eligibility, and 42 CFR 457.310(c)(2) states the same exclusion for a targeted low-income child at the federal level. This batch's chp_child_is_eligible (CO-705) and chp_prenatal_is_eligible (CO-706) do not test it, because this volume's existing snap_resides_in_institution_providing_meals supplied fact (batch 1, shared across SNAP and long-term care Medicaid) means a different thing - entry into a nursing facility or similar level-of-care institution for long-term care purposes - not "inmate of a public institution" (a correctional or juvenile-justice context) or "patient in an institution for mental diseases" (a specific 42 CFR 435.1010 term of art). Reusing snap_resides_in_institution_providing_meals here would be wrong for the target household's grandmother (p1, a nursing-facility applicant who is not a CHP+-eligible age or pregnancy status in any case, so this makes no numeric difference to CO-01) but would be wrong in general. A future batch should add a supplied fact (for example is_inmate_of_public_institution, distinct from snap_resides_in_institution_providing_meals) and wire it into CO-705/CO-706 as an additional exclusion. Items: CO-705, CO-706, CO-707.

Resolution (phase 7): the current § 120.1.C (D-66, S653) still excludes "an inmate of a public institution or a patient in an institution for mental diseases" (with a 90-days-before-release reentry-services carve-out that is a benefit, not eligibility). Two supplied facts distinct from snap_resides_in_institution_providing_meals were added, is_inmate_of_public_institution (CO-1105) and is_patient_in_institution_for_mental_diseases (CO-1104); chp_child_is_eligible (CO-705) and chp_prenatal_is_eligible (CO-706) test both, and chp_eligibility_status (CO-707) reports ineligible_institution for a child or pregnant applicant who passes every other test. Items: CO-705, CO-706, CO-707, CO-1104, CO-1105.

### OQ-59. Resolved: The federal state-employee-dependent exclusion (42 CFR 457.310(c)) is not modeled because Colorado's own regulation does not state it

Body: 42 CFR 457.310(c) excludes a targeted low-income child from CHIP eligibility if the child could get health benefits coverage through a State health benefits plan on the basis of a family member's public-agency employment (with a "more than nominal" employer-contribution test). The phase-3 brief asked this batch to model "not a dependent of a state employee if 2505-3 says so." 10 CCR 2505-3-110 and -120 (the sections this batch fetched and cites) do not state this exclusion anywhere; it appears only in the federal regulation. Because this volume models Colorado's own program rules from Colorado's own regulation text (citing federal text for definitions and floors, not as a substitute for a missing state rule), this exclusion is not modeled as a CHP+ rule in this batch. If Colorado's CCR states it elsewhere (a section outside 50-430 this batch did not fetch) or through CBMS policy, a future batch should add the supplied fact and rule. Items: CO-705, CO-706.

Phase 7 sweep: the complete current text of 10 CCR 2505-3 (D-66) was fetched and states no state-employee-dependent exclusion; whether 42 CFR 457.310(c) applies through Colorado's state plan without a state rule is not settled by any fetched text.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the exclusion is part of the federal definition of a targeted low-income child (42 U.S.C. 1397jj(b)(2)(B), S1223; 42 CFR 457.310(c)(1), S1224) and applies to a targeted low-income pregnant woman in the same manner (1397ll(d)(2)(C), S1225), so it binds CHP+ whether or not 10 CCR 2505-3 restates it. The two statutory exceptions of 1397jj(b)(6) (the agency's maintenance of effort on dependent coverage, and the State's 5% hardship finding) are stated as applying whenever their facts hold, so they are modeled too. New facts CO-1951-CO-1953 and rule chp_is_excluded_as_public_agency_employee_family (CO-1978), read by chp_child_is_eligible (CO-705) and chp_prenatal_is_eligible (CO-706). The household cases now state chp_is_eligible_for_public_agency_state_health_benefits_plan: false wherever they state chp_has_other_health_coverage; no expectation changed.

### OQ-60. Resolved: The 320 copayment schedule and the 330.2 aggregate 5% cost-sharing cap are not modeled as enforcement rules in this batch

Body: 10 CCR 2505-3-320 states a detailed table of per-service copayment dollar amounts by FPL band (four bands, over a dozen service types each), and 330.2/42 CFR 457.560(a)/42 U.S.C. 1397cc(e)(3)(B) cap the aggregate of enrollment fee, premiums and copayments at 5% of family income for the relevant period. The phase-3 brief's interface for batch 7 lists identifiers for chp_annual_enrollment_fee and chp_eligibility_status but no identifier for a per-visit copayment schedule or for an aggregate-cap enforcement rule, so this batch creates only the parameter chp_cost_sharing_aggregate_cap_pct (CO-213, the 5% figure itself, cited to S247/S250/S252) and does not build either as a rule. Enforcing the cap correctly needs a running total of cost sharing charged in the eligibility period, which no supplied fact in this volume currently tracks. A future batch should add the 320 copayment table as parameters keyed by FPL band and service type, and a rule comparing a supplied or derived running cost-sharing total against chp_cost_sharing_aggregate_cap_pct times chp_household_income. Items: CO-213.

Phase 7 sweep: the current § 320 (D-66) still states the same copayment bands, all below the 142% CHP+ floor, and § 330.2 the 5% cap; building the schedule and a running cost-sharing total is a modeling gap no further text resolves.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the rule is clear (triage: NEEDS FACT) but this sweep did not model it; it needs a running total of the family's premiums and copayments for the period, and the § 320 schedule by service and FPL band; the exemptions of § 330.1 and § 330.4 are now modeled (OQ-64).

Phase-10 needs-fact sweep (2026-11-21): resolved. The phase 7 note above is wrong about the bands: § 320.1 (S1504) runs them from under 101% to 260% of poverty, so CHP+ families pay copayments. New facts chp_service_type (CO-2223) and chp_family_cost_sharing_paid_for_year (CO-2224, the running total); band limits CO-2225 to CO-2227; rules chp_copayment_income_band (CO-2228, at the eligibility determination), chp_cost_sharing_annual_limit (CO-2229: 5% of twelve months' income, § 330.2, S1505), chp_scheduled_copayment (CO-2230: the § 320.1 amounts, nothing for preventive services, § 330.3, for COVID-19 laboratory testing, or for emergency care with admission) and chp_copayment (CO-2231: nothing for an exempt enrollee, otherwise the scheduled amount up to what remains of the yearly limit). The whole-percent band edges leave gaps, recorded as OQ-470. No eligibility outcome reads the copayment, so no case states the facts.

### OQ-61. Resolved: CHP+ "not eligible for Medicaid" reads the Medicaid outcome

Body: Resolved in the phase 3 coordinator pass: chp_child_is_eligible, chp_prenatal_is_eligible and chp_eligibility_status read ma_magi_is_eligible (CO-553) directly, and the interim supplied fact is_eligible_for_medicaid (CO-091) was removed before any case used it. A person whose only Medicaid path is emergency-only counts as Medicaid-eligible for this test, which for children and pregnant persons cannot arise under 8.100.3.G's coverage regardless of status. Items: CO-705, CO-706, CO-707.

### OQ-62. Resolved: CHP+ reads the Medicaid MAGI household, size and income directly; separate CHP+ identifiers would only restate them

Body: The phase-3 brief's batch 7 interface lists chp_household_members, chp_household_size and chp_household_income alongside chp_income_within_limit, chp_child_is_eligible and the rest. 10 CCR 2505-3-50.13 and -150.1 both adopt the Medicaid MAGI household and income rules by cross-reference rather than stating a CHP+-specific test, so the natural derivation for each would be a bare reference to ma_magi_household_members, ma_magi_household_size and ma_magi_household_income respectively. This volume's engine governance rule `derived.alias` (src/engine/governance.ts) treats a derivation that is a bare reference to another item's identifier as an error ("an alias is not a rule, so use that fact directly"), so these three items were not created; every CHP+ rule in this batch that needs the MAGI household, size or income reads ma_magi_household_members, ma_magi_household_size, ma_magi_household_income and ma_magi_household_income_pct_fpl directly, which the phase-3 brief's own text says is expected ("cross-program reads of ma_ items are expected"). If a future amendment gives CHP+ its own household or income test that actually differs from Medicaid's, these three identifiers should be created then, with a real derivation rather than a pass-through. Items: (none created; see CO-704, CO-705, CO-706, CO-708 for the cross-program reads).

Phase 7 sweep: a design choice under the derived.alias governance rule; no published rule settles it.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): Checked 2026-11-21: 10 CCR 2505-3 §§ 50.13 and 150.1 (D-66), which adopt the MAGI household and income rules by cross-reference; the question is a ledger-design choice under derived.alias; no published rule settles it.

Resolution (open-question classification pass, 2026-11-22): answered by the rule text. 10 CCR 2505-3 § 50.13 (D-66: "“Household” shall be determined by relationships to the tax filer ... as required in 10 CCR 2505-10-8.100.4.E") and § 150.1 ("Calculation of income for the Children’s Basic Health Plan shall be determined as required in 10 CCR 2505-10-8.100.4.C") adopt the Medicaid MAGI household and income rules and state no CHP+ test of their own. CHP+'s household, size and income are therefore the Medicaid ones, and the CHP+ items read ma_magi_household_members, ma_magi_household_size, ma_magi_household_income and ma_magi_household_income_pct_fpl (CO-704 to CO-708) as the rule directs; three CHP+ identifiers that restated them would be aliases, which derived.alias forbids. No item changes and no test is added, since no value changes. If a later CHP+ rule states a household or income test of its own, an item with that derivation is added then.

### OQ-63. Resolved: the Children's Medicaid standard is 142%, matching CHP+'s 142% floor; there is no gap

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.4.G.2 states the Children's MAGI Medical Assistance income standard as "133% of the federal poverty level (MAGI-equivalent)" but also directs: "Refer to the MAGI-Medicaid income guidelines chart available on the Department's website" (S201). 10 CCR 2505-3-110.1.E sets CHP+'s children's-band floor at "greater than 142%... but not exceeding 260%" (S243). A child at, for example, 140% of FPL is over Medicaid's stated 133% (even with the 5% disregard, 135% > 133%) and not over CHP+'s 142% floor - eligible for nothing. CHIP is designed as the tier immediately above Medicaid (42 CFR 457.310(b)(2)(i), S248, excludes a child "found eligible or potentially eligible for Medicaid"), and HCPF's own 142% floor is a statement by the same agency that Medicaid for children reaches 142% - so the 133% figure in 8.100.4.G.2's own text is very likely the pre-conversion or stale figure, with the operative number on the chart the rule incorporates by reference, which this volume has not fetched (the HCPF "MAGI-Medicaid income guidelines chart" page returns HTTP 403 to this volume's fetch tooling as of 2026-09-18; hcpf.colorado.gov blocks automated retrieval). 10 CCR 2505-3-320.1.A-B's copayment bands ("less than 101%... between 101% and 150%") are also entirely below the 142% floor 110.1.E states, corroborating that at least one of these figures is stale text the chart has since outrun.

This volume does not resolve the gap by lowering CO-210 (the CHP+ 142% floor, S243, unambiguous and recently amended, 47 CR 23, effective 12/30/2024) or by raising CO-191 (the Medicaid 133% standard) without a fetched source stating a different number - both would be invented figures, which this volume's provenance rule forbids. The chart itself must be fetched (from HCPF's website, once fetchable, or the Colorado Secretary of State's CCR incorporation-by-reference filing) and CO-191/CO-540/CO-210/CO-545 versioned from it before this gap can be closed. Until then, a child with household income in the (133%, 142%] band of FPL is correctly modeled as ineligible for both programs by this volume's own stated figures, which is very likely wrong but not a figure this volume can correct without inventing a number. Items: CO-191, CO-540, CO-210, CO-545, CO-704.

Resolution (phase 7): the current Secretary of State text of 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.4.G.2 (D-65, S638) reads "Children applying for Medical Assistance whose total household income does not exceed 142% of the federal poverty level shall be determined financially eligible", and CMS's Medicaid/CHIP eligibility levels table (D-73, S641) lists Colorado's children's Medicaid standard as 142% at every age band with separate CHIP to 260%. ma_income_limit_child_pct (CO-191) is now 1.42; CO-540-T1 expects 1.42. The same current text also states 68% for Parents and Caretaker Relatives (§ 8.100.4.G.3, S639) and 195% for Pregnant Women (§ 8.100.4.G.5, S640), both confirmed by the CMS table, so CO-192 and CO-194 were corrected too (rule tests CO-539-T2/T4, CO-541-T1, CO-543-T1, CO-545-T5 and CO-547-T2 updated to the current figures). CO-05's p3 at 139% FPL is now a Medicaid child in January and April 2027. When the figures changed is not stated in any fetched document (OQ-211). Items: CO-191, CO-192, CO-194, CO-540, CO-545, CO-210, CO-704.

### OQ-64. Resolved: CHP+ cost-sharing exemptions (10 CCR 2505-3-330.1/330.4) and the § 320 copayment schedule are not modeled (fix round)

Body: 10 CCR 2505-3-320 (`documents/D-42.md:285-380`) states a full copayment schedule by FPL band and service type, and 330.1/330.4 (`documents/D-42.md:389,393,405`) state three categorical exemptions from cost sharing: "American Indians and Alaskan Natives shall be exempt from cost sharing requirements," "No copayments shall apply to preventive services," and "Prenatal Care Program clients shall be exempt from cost sharing requirements." This chapter carries only chp_cost_sharing_aggregate_cap_pct (CO-213, the 5% aggregate cap) and does not model either the copayment schedule (already flagged as not modeled, as an enforcement rule, by OQ-60) or the exemptions. The exemptions are narrower in scope than OQ-60's enforcement gap: they are pure eligibility-for-zero-cost-sharing determinations (is this person exempt from all cost sharing, yes or no), not a running-total enforcement mechanism, and this chapter already carries the supplied fact the American Indian/Alaskan Native exemption needs (snap_is_indian_or_urban_indian, stated on every person in CO-01) with nothing reading it for this purpose. A future batch should add a derived chp_is_exempt_from_cost_sharing (yes/no, person) reading snap_is_indian_or_urban_indian and chp_prenatal_is_eligible (CO-706) against 330.1/330.4, and a chp_copayment_schedule parameter table keyed by FPL band and service type against § 320, both cited to the already-fetched text - this does not depend on OQ-57's blocking gap, since §§ 320 and 330 (unlike § 300) rendered in full. Items: CO-213.

Phase 7 sweep: the current §§ 330.1, 330.3 and 330.4 (D-66) state the same exemptions; the rule is clear and the exemption item is a modeling gap still to author.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): § 330.1 exempts American Indians and Alaska Natives (a tribal member or a first or second degree descendant) and § 330.4 exempts Prenatal Care Program clients from cost sharing (S1236). New fact chp_is_american_indian_or_alaska_native (CO-1989, § 330.1's own definition, which is neither SNAP's nor 42 CFR 447.51's) and rule chp_is_exempt_from_cost_sharing (CO-1990). The § 320 schedule and the § 330.2 cap stay with OQ-60. No eligibility outcome changes.

### OQ-65. Resolved: 42 CFR 435.119(d) - the community engagement requirement on the Adults group - is not modeled

Body: 42 CFR 435.119(d) (added 91 FR 33469, June 3, 2026, the version of the CFR this volume fetched) states: "As of the implementation date in accordance with § 435.559, the 50 States and the District of Columbia must provide that eligibility under this section is subject to the community engagement requirement described at §§ 435.550 through 435.563." The Adults group (ma_is_adult_category, CO-548) is the largest MAGI group this chapter models, and this chapter has fetched no text of 435.550-435.563 or 435.559 (the implementation-date section), added no excerpt for 435.119(d) itself, and added no ma_meets_community_engagement rule or exemption items. This is very likely a large enough addition to be its own batch (435.550-435.563 defines the hours-per-month requirement, the list of exemption categories - disability, caregiving, pregnancy, and others - and the reporting and compliance-period mechanics, none of which this chapter has fetched). The volumes/mwr/medicaid chapter (WR-200, WR-214, WR-220, WR-226, WR-228) already models a community-engagement requirement for its own Medicaid adult group under its own state's rule; a future batch authoring this section for Colorado should read that chapter's treatment as a worked example of the item shape (medicaid_meets_community_engagement and its exemption tests), fetch 435.550-435.563 and 435.559 for Colorado's own citations, and add ma_meets_community_engagement as a conjunct of CO-548 with an `Effective` range starting at the § 435.559 implementation date - not simply copy the mwr volume's derivation, since Colorado's own exemption list and implementation date are not yet fetched. Items: CO-548.

Phase 7 sweep: 42 CFR 435.550-435.563 and the § 435.559 implementation date were not fetched in this phase, and the current 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100 (D-65) states no community engagement requirement, so no published Colorado rule yet settles Colorado's exemption list or date.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): modeled from the current eCFR text of 42 CFR 435.119(d) and 435.550-435.560 (D-121, S1200-S1213, S1216; 447.51, D-122, S1214; 42 U.S.C. 1396a(xx), S1215). The Adults group is conditioned on community engagement for medical assistance furnished on or after January 1, 2027 (ma_community_engagement_start_date, CO-1900): ma_magi_category (CO-551) places a person in the Adults group only when ma_meets_adult_group_community_engagement_condition (CO-1975) holds, and ma_magi_eligibility_status (CO-554) reports ineligible_community_engagement otherwise. The outcome ma_community_engagement_status (CO-1974, effective from 2027-01-01) is not_assessed, excluded (a specified excluded individual, 435.554), met or not_met, over the look-back of 435.556 (CO-1973: the months before the application month at application; the months since the most recent determination at renewal or a change-in-circumstances redetermination). Each look-back month is met (CO-1972) by 80 hours of work, in-kind or unpaid work, community service, work program and less than half-time education combined, by half-time enrollment, by MAGI-based income of at least the minimum wage times 80, or by a seasonal worker's six-month average (CO-1966-CO-1968); or is deemed met by a mandatory exception of 435.553 (under 19, Medicare, a mandatory group, a specified excluded individual, an inmate in the prior three months; CO-1969, CO-1970) or by the optional short-term hardship exception of 435.555 (CO-1971). The ten exclusions of 435.554(c) are CO-1960-CO-1965. Supplied facts CO-1915-CO-1945, parameters CO-1900-CO-1911. The mwr volume's WR-2xx items gave the shape; the federal text now differs from them in the 13-and-under dependent child, the family caregiver criteria, the 1115 branch, the three-month inmate look-back ending on the first day of the month and the renewal period. What Colorado has not published stays open: OQ-410 (look-back months), OQ-411 (hardship election, good faith exemption, early implementation), OQ-412 (other State options inside the exclusions and hours), OQ-413 (modeling choices).

### OQ-81. Resolved: the 2026 MSP resource limits are $9,950 (individual) and $14,910 (couple)

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.M.1 (S301) states the QMB/SLMB/QI resource limits were $8,180 (individual) and $13,020 (couple) effective January 1, 2011, and directs CMS to adjust them annually by the September CPI - but does not state the 2026 adjusted figures, and no CMS 2026 MSP resource limit publication could be fetched in this session (medicaid.gov and hcpf.colorado.gov both refused). ma_msp_resource_limit_individual and ma_msp_resource_limit_couple (CO-807, CO-808) are modeled as supplied facts rather than parameters, with `supplied_by` naming the CMS annual MSP resource limit publication and `meaning` stating the $8,180/$13,020 base and the CPI indexing rule the figure is an indexation of. A reviewer with access to the CMS 2026 publication should replace these supplied facts with versioned parameters once fetched. Items: CO-807, CO-808, CO-613, CO-614, CO-615, CO-616.

Resolution (phase 7): CMS's Medicare.gov publication of the Medicare Savings Programs' 2026 limits (D-74, S647) states a $9,950 individual and $14,910 married-couple resource limit for QMB, SLMB and QI, the figures § 8.100.5.M.1 (current text, S646) directs CMS to set by CPI adjustment of the 2011 base. ma_msp_resource_limit_individual (CO-807) and ma_msp_resource_limit_couple (CO-808) are now parameters versioned from 2026-01-01 (identifiers and ids unchanged; files moved from supplied/ to parameters/), and the case-stated illustrative 10000/15000 were removed from the household cases. No case expectation changed. Items: CO-807, CO-808, CO-613, CO-614, CO-615.

### OQ-82. Resolved: Buy-In premium schedules are dated parameters keyed by income band

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.6.P.1.f.iii (S317) and 8.100.6.Q.1.g.iv (S318) each state a premium amount by income-as-percentage-of-FPL band (five bands for WAwD, four for CBwD). volume.yaml's parameter `type` list has no type for a table keyed by a percentage band (only "table keyed by person" and "table keyed by household size" exist), so ma_wawd_premium (CO-619) and ma_cbwd_premium (CO-620) encode the band boundaries and dollar amounts as literal values in a `case` derivation, each cited directly to S317/S318 rather than to a separate parameter item. This differs from how this volume otherwise treats a published policy figure (as a parameter), and a future volume.yaml change adding a percentage-band table type would let a reviewer refactor these two items to parameters without changing behavior. Items: CO-619, CO-620.

Phase 7 sweep: the current § 8.100.6.P.1.f and Q.1.g (D-65) state the same bands and amounts; the question is the volume's parameter types, which no rule settles.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): Checked 2026-11-21: § 8.100.6.P.1.f.iii and Q.1.g.iv (D-65), which state the premium bands; the question is about the ledger's parameter types; no published rule settles it.

HCPF fix round (2026-11-21, docs/reviews/stakeholder-hcpf.md), S-8: the reviewer overruled the literal schedules because § 8.100.6.P.1.f.iv and Q.1.g.v re-base the premiums at the start of each State fiscal year (S1727, S1728). The engine now has a parameter type "table keyed by income band" and the pattern P48 (`band`, docs/conventions.md); ma_wawd_premium_table (CO-2457) and ma_cbwd_premium_table (CO-2458) carry the bands as versions from July 1, 2026 with a review_by date, and ma_wawd_premium (CO-619) and ma_cbwd_premium (CO-621) read them. Items: CO-619, CO-621, CO-2457, CO-2458.

### OQ-83. Resolved: a parent's income and resources are deemed to an applicant child under 18, after the allocations of 8.100.5.H

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.H (S308) describes a fuller income-allocation sequence for a household with ineligible children or an ineligible spouse/parent - the Ineligible Child Allocation and Ineligible Parent(s) Allocation are applied to gross income before the $20 and $65-plus-one-half disregards, and "only one $20 general income disregard is allowed per couple." ma_ssi_countable_earned_income (CO-605) and ma_ssi_countable_unearned_income (CO-606) model only the two-eligible-spouses (or single-applicant) case that combines both spouses' gross income before applying one $20 and one $65-plus-one-half disregard to the combined totals, per 8.100.5.H.1.a's "mutually available" rule; they do not model the ineligible-child or ineligible-parent allocation deductions, or income deeming from an ineligible spouse who is not applying, because this volume has no supplied facts distinguishing an ineligible spouse's income from an eligible applicant's. Items: CO-605, CO-606, CO-607.

Phase 7 sweep: the current § 8.100.5.H (D-65) states the same allocation sequence; modeling it needs ineligible-spouse and ineligible-child income facts - a modeling gap, not an unanswered reading.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the rule is clear (triage: NEEDS FACT) but this sweep did not model it; it needs facts for an ineligible spouse's and ineligible children's income and the allocations of § 8.100.5.H (S308).

Phase-10 needs-fact sweep (2026-11-21): partly resolved. The incomes were already in the case (each person's own income facts); what was missing was who is "not applying or eligible for SSI" (H.2.b-c) and the unemancipated-child test (H.2.a). New facts ma_is_applying_for_or_eligible_for_ssi (CO-2271) and ma_is_regularly_attending_school_or_training (CO-2272); parameters CO-2273, CO-2274, CO-2276 and CO-2280; rules ma_is_ssi_unemancipated_child (CO-2275), ma_ssi_ineligible_child_allocation (CO-2277: half the individual FBR per ineligible unemancipated child of the household, less the child's own income, child support in full), ma_ssi_child_allocation_from_spouse_unearned and _earned (CO-2278, CO-2279: taken from an ineligible spouse's income, unearned first, before the standard disregards, H.3.b.i-ii) and ma_ssi_household_unearned_income_counted (CO-2281, which also counts an unemancipated child's child support reduced by one third, H.4.c). ma_ssi_countable_unearned_income (CO-606) and ma_ssi_countable_earned_income (CO-605) now apply the $20 and $65-plus-one-half disregards to the income after the allocation (S1506). Which parent's income bears the allocation is OQ-473. Still open, as a gap in the published text: deeming a parent's income to an applicant child, which the Ineligible Parent(s) Allocation (H.3.a.ii, H.3.b.iii) presupposes but § 8.100.5.H never computes (whose income, and how it is divided among several applicant children); § 8.100.5.H.4.e incorporates 20 CFR 416.1112 only, not the SSI parent-to-child deeming rules. No household case has an ineligible spouse with children in the home, so no case states the new facts and no expectation changed.

Coverage round (2026-11-22, docs/reviews/coverage-medicaid-nonmagi.md NM-10): resolved; the class "state policy choice" was wrong. § 8.100.5.H.3.a itself says the allocations reduce "the gross income that is deemed available to the applicant/client" and that the Ineligible Parent(s) Allocation is "used to meet the needs of the ineligible parent(s) in the home with an applicant/client child" (S3405), deducted "after any ineligible child allocation disregards and after the standard income disregards" (H.3.b.iii); § 8.100.7.A.1 says parental income and resources "cease to be deemed available to the child" once the child is institutionalized (S3406); and § 8.100.6.A.1 sends the ABD categories to § 8.100.5 for income and resources under the SSI methodology. The SSI statute and rules the methodology carries say whose income and how: 42 U.S.C. 1382c(f)(2)(A) deems to a child under 18 "any income and resources of a parent of such individual (or the spouse of such a parent) who is living in the same household" (S3415); 20 CFR 416.1165 deducts the ineligible child allocations, then the $20 and $65-plus-one-half, then the parent allocation (the FBR for a couple when both parents live with the child, for an individual when one does), deems the rest to the child as unearned income and divides it equally among several eligible children (S3413); 416.1202(b) deems the parents' resources above the parents' limit, pension funds excluded (S3414). New items: ma_ssi_deeming_parents (CO-4624), ma_ssi_deeming_children (CO-4625), ma_ssi_deeming_ineligible_child_allocation (CO-4633), ma_ssi_deeming_parents_countable_income (CO-4634), ma_ssi_parental_income_deemed (CO-4626, added to unearned income in CO-2281) and ma_ssi_parental_resources_deemed (CO-4627, added in CO-608). 416.1165(e)(3) (a child whose own income already makes the child ineligible gets no more) and the 416.1165(i) / 416.1202(b)(2) exception for a child once paid SSI's reduced facility benefit are not modeled; a waiver child reaches Medicaid through the 300% group, which counts only the child's own income. No stored case had an applicant child living with an ineligible parent, so no expectation changed; new case CO-186 shows the deeming. Which parent's income bears an applicant's ineligible-spouse allocation stays OQ-473.

### OQ-84. Resolved: QDWI's disability and work history is derived from two supplied history facts

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.6.O.2.a-b (S315) requires a QDWI applicant to have been "a recipient of federal Social Security Disability Insurance (SSDI) benefits, who continues to be disabled but lost SSDI entitlement due to earned income in excess of the Social Security Administration's Substantial Gainful Activity (SGA) threshold" and to have "exhausted SSA's allowed extension of 'premium free' Medicare Part A coverage under SSDI." ma_qdwi_eligible (CO-616) reads this history through a single supplied fact, is_entitled_to_medicare_part_a_on_basis_of_disability, taken to mean the person currently holds (or would hold, but for having exhausted the premium-free extension) Part A entitlement on the disability basis described in 8.100.6.O.2.a-b, rather than deriving the SGA and extension-exhaustion facts themselves, which this volume does not carry as supplied facts. Items: CO-616, CO-804.

Resolution (phase 7): the current § 8.100.6.O.2 (D-65, S622) states the same two history conditions. lost_ssdi_due_to_substantial_gainful_activity (CO-1106) and has_exhausted_premium_free_part_a_extension (CO-1107) carry them separately, and ma_qdwi_meets_disability_work_history (CO-1157) joins them with continued disability (meets_ssa_disability_criteria). ma_qdwi_eligible (CO-616) reads CO-1157 in place of is_entitled_to_medicare_part_a_on_basis_of_disability, which had no other consumer and was removed (CO-804; the case facts stating it false were removed too). Items: CO-616, CO-1106, CO-1107, CO-1157.

### OQ-91. Resolved: the 2026 CSRA minimum and maximum, MMMNA cap and home equity limit are published; Colorado uses the indexed $500,000 home equity base

Body: 42 U.S.C. 1396r-5(f)(2) states the CSRA's $12,000/$60,000 base figures and (g)'s CPI indexing rule but not the 2026 published minimum/maximum; 1396p(f)(1) states the home equity disqualification's $500,000/$750,000 base and its CPI indexing beginning 2011, but not which figure Colorado elected or the 2026 indexed amount; 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.7.F.2.c states Colorado's transfer-penalty divisor is the state average monthly private-pay nursing facility rate by Bureau of Economic Analysis region, calculated annually by HCPF, but the rate table itself is not in any fetched document. medicaid.gov, cms.gov and hcpf.colorado.gov were not fetchable this session (see brief4.md). ma_csra_minimum (CO-827), ma_csra_maximum (CO-828), ma_mmmna_cap (CO-830), ma_home_equity_limit (CO-831) and transfer_penalty_divisor (CO-832) are modeled as supplied facts the worker or eligibility system states for the case, each citing the statute's base figure and indexing rule in its meaning, rather than parameters with an invented current-year value. Per the phase-4 review's G-1 finding, mmmna_standard is no longer among them: 8.100.7.Q.1.a states its formula (150% of the size-2 poverty guideline, itself already carried as federal_poverty_guideline_annual_table, CO-181) directly, so it is now a derived item (ma_mmmna_standard, CO-662) rather than a supplied fact - only the MMMNA cap and the other five figures remain genuinely unstated. Items: CO-646, CO-649, CO-650, CO-655, CO-827, CO-828, CO-830, CO-831, CO-832, CO-887.

Resolution (phase 7): CMS's CMCS Informational Bulletin of April 27, 2026 (D-72, S644) charts the 2026 standards: community spouse minimum resource standard $32,532.00, maximum $162,660.00, maximum monthly maintenance needs allowance $4,066.50 (effective 1-1-26), MMMNA $2,705.00 (effective 7-1-26), and home equity limits of $752,000.00 minimum and $1,130,000.00 maximum. The current § 8.100.5.M.2.a.ii (D-65, S645) states Colorado's limit as the $500,000 figure increased by CPI, which is the federal minimum, so Colorado's 2026 limit is $752,000. ma_csra_minimum (CO-827), ma_csra_maximum (CO-828), ma_mmmna_cap (CO-830) and ma_home_equity_limit (CO-831) are now parameters versioned from 2026-01-01 (ids and identifiers unchanged), and the illustrative case figures were removed; CO-06's CSRA is now 162,660. The transfer-penalty divisor and the institutional personal needs allowance are still not published in any fetchable document and move to OQ-212. Items: CO-827, CO-828, CO-830, CO-831, CO-646, CO-650, CO-655.

### OQ-92. Resolved: the spouse or dependent child living in the home that defeats the home equity disqualification is a supplied fact (phase-4 fix round C-20)

Body: 42 U.S.C. 1396p(f)(2) exempts an individual from the home equity disqualification if a spouse, or a child under 21 (or blind/disabled) of the individual, is lawfully residing in the individual's home; no fetched Colorado document (D-41's home-exemption text at 8.100.5.M.3.d speaks to the resource exclusion, not the LTC home-equity disqualification specifically) restates this list. ma_home_equity_disqualifies (CO-650) previously read only ma_ltc_couple (a community spouse living apart from the institution) as the exempting condition and did not model a dependent child living in the home. The phase-4 review's C-20 finding added a supplied fact, ma_spouse_or_qualifying_child_lives_in_home (CO-889), covering both the spouse and the child branch of 1396p(f)(2) directly, and CO-650 now reads it instead of ma_ltc_couple. Items: CO-650, CO-889.

### OQ-93. Resolved: PACE's minimum age is 55, by Colorado's incorporation of 42 CFR part 460

Body: brief4.md's interface describes PACE eligibility as "55+, level of care, financial," but D-57 (10 CCR 2505-10 §§ 8.500, 8.508, 8.509, 8.497) only contains PACE-organization regulatory requirements (application, agreements, sanctions), not a participant age floor, and D-41's only "age 55" text (8.100.5.D) concerns the estate recovery notice, not PACE eligibility. ma_pace_eligible (CO-654) does not test an age threshold; it tests level of care and financial eligibility only. Items: CO-654.

Resolution (phase 7): 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.497.1.B.1 (current text, D-76, S649) incorporates 42 CFR part 460 (2024) by reference, and 42 CFR 460.150(b)(1) (D-71, S650) states "Be 55 years of age or older." ma_pace_min_age (CO-1133) carries it and ma_pace_eligible (CO-654) tests it. Items: CO-654, CO-1133.

### OQ-94. Resolved: Transfer penalty months does not model the start-date/other-penalty-period timing of 8.100.7.F.2.c.b-c

Body: 8.100.7.F.2.c's penalty period calculation (uncompensated value ÷ the average private-pay rate) is the arithmetic ma_transfer_penalty_months (CO-649) implements, but 8.100.7.F.2.c.b-c also fix the penalty period's start date (the date the individual would otherwise be eligible, not overlapping a prior penalty period) and 8.100.7.F.8's apportionment between spouses, which are not modeled: ma_ltc_financially_eligible (CO-651) and ma_ltc_eligibility_status (CO-652) treat any positive computed penalty as a current-month disqualification rather than tracking the penalty period's calendar span. Items: CO-649, CO-651, CO-652.

Phase 7 sweep: the current § 8.100.7.F.2.c.ii (D-65) states the same start-date rule; tracking the penalty period's calendar span is a modeling gap.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the rule is clear (triage: NEEDS FACT) but this sweep did not model it; it needs the date the person would otherwise be eligible and any prior penalty period, and the spouses' apportionment (§ 8.100.7.F.2.c.b-c, F.8). The divisor itself is now a dated parameter (OQ-212).

Phase-10 needs-fact sweep (2026-11-21): resolved. The start date was already supplied (transfer_penalty_period_start_date, CO-886, read by ma_transfer_penalty_applies, CO-660, since the phase-4 fix round), and § 8.100.7.F.2.c.ii's third prong (no overlap with another penalty period) is part of that date's meaning. The apportionment of § 8.100.7.F.8 (S1507) is now modeled: new facts ma_transfer_penalty_is_apportioned_with_spouse (CO-2232) and ma_transfer_penalty_months_assigned_from_spouse (CO-2233), parameter ma_transfer_penalty_apportioned_share_rate (CO-2234, one half) and rule ma_transfer_penalty_share_rate (CO-2235); ma_transfer_penalty_months (CO-649) and ma_transfer_penalty_days (CO-659) apply the share and add the reassigned months (new tests CO-649-T4, T5, CO-659-T4). CO-01, CO-02 and CO-06 p1 have a community spouse, so the facts are false and 0 and CO-06's four months are unchanged.

### OQ-95. Resolved: the family allowance group is derived from the child, parent and tax-dependent edges, dependent siblings of either spouse included

Body: 8.100.7.T.1.c.ii defines "family member" for the family allowance as a dependent child, parent, or sibling of either spouse residing with the community spouse and claimable as a tax dependent by either spouse; this batch's relationships (parent/child, spouse, tax_filer/tax_dependent) could compose that definition, but doing so correctly (residing with the community spouse specifically, claimable by either spouse) needs a cross-batch read of the MAGI chapter's tax-dependency edges plus a "lives with" fact this volume does not yet have. family_members_with_community_spouse (CO-838) is a supplied group fact instead, stating the worker's own determination of who qualifies. Items: CO-657, CO-838.

Phase 7 sweep: a modeling choice about relationship facts; no published rule settles it.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): Checked 2026-11-21: § 8.100.7.T.1.c.ii (D-65), which defines the family member; the question is whether to derive the group from relationship edges; no published rule settles it.

HCPF fix round (2026-11-21, docs/reviews/stakeholder-hcpf.md): ma_family_allowance_members (CO-2469) derives the group from the relationship edges - a child or parent of either spouse (has_relative_in over the spouse closure), claimed as a tax dependent by either spouse (the tax_dependent edge), residing with the community spouse (ma_resides_with_community_spouse, CO-2468, the one fact the edges do not state) - and ma_family_allowance (CO-657) reads it in place of the supplied family_members_with_community_spouse (CO-838), which no item reads now. Still open: a dependent sibling of either spouse, whom § 8.100.7.T.1.c.ii also names, cannot be reached, because the ledger has no sibling edge to a spouse (P43 finds the siblings of the person evaluated only). Items: CO-657, CO-2469.

Resolution (engineering item follow-up pass, 2026-11-21; same-word audit SW-2): siblings are derived from shared parents, not stated (CO-2609). § 8.100.7.T.1.c.ii's "sibling of either spouse" is reached as a child of a parent of either spouse who is not a spouse; ma_family_allowance_members (CO-2469) adds that arm, with the same tax-dependent and residence tests (new test CO-2469-T5). A half sibling (one shared parent) counts; a step-sibling does not, since T.1.c.ii says sibling. Items: CO-2469.

### OQ-96. Resolved: Income trust mechanics (8.100.7.G) are read only as a special-income-group override, not modeled as their own item

Body: has_income_trust (CO-823) only feeds ma_meets_special_income_group (CO-642) as the 8.100.7.A.2.e alternative to the 300% test; the trust's own rules (the geographic private-pay-rate ceiling on trust use, the allowable deductions from trust distributions, the $20 trust-expense retention, the HCBS 300%-limited distribution) are quoted in D-41 (around 8.100.7.G) but not modeled as separate rule items in this batch, since brief4.md's interface does not name identifiers for them. Items: CO-642, CO-823.

Phase 7 sweep: the current § 8.100.7.G (D-65) states the same trust rules; not modeled, and the private-pay ceiling has no published figure (OQ-212).

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the rule is clear (triage: NEEDS FACT) but this sweep did not model it; it needs trust distribution facts (the monthly distribution, the $20 retention, HCBS distributions). The trust's gross income ceiling is now enforced (OQ-99).

Phase-10 needs-fact sweep (2026-11-21): resolved. In the current text the income trust rules are § 8.100.7's trust paragraph 6.a.i (S1508), not 8.100.7.G. New fact ma_income_trust_amount_retained_for_expenses (CO-2236), parameter ma_income_trust_expense_retention_max (CO-2237, $20) and rule ma_income_trust_distribution (CO-2238): the income transferred in less at most $20 retained, limited to 300% of the SSI rate for an HCBS member. ma_patient_payment (CO-658) applies the distribution for a trust user (new test CO-658-T6). The trust's deduction list omits the family allowance that the general rule allows; the draft keeps it (OQ-471). The HCBS member payment (PETI) computed on 300% of the SSI limit has no item, as before. No case has an income trust.

### OQ-97. Resolved: the community spouse's income is read from the spouse's own facts across the spouse edge

Body: brief4.md's interface offers community_spouse_income "as pm: the community spouse's own gross income — or read the spouse's income facts via the spouse relationship." This batch takes the supplied-fact reading: community_spouse_income (CO-833), shelter_costs_community_spouse (CO-834) and health_insurance_premiums_paid (CO-835) are stated directly for the institutionalized spouse's month rather than derived by reaching across the spouse relationship, because the engine's operator catalog (docs/conventions.md) has no example of a derived item of type "person" (needed to name "the community spouse" as a value) and reading a fact of a related person for a scalar (not a group aggregate) is not demonstrated elsewhere in this volume. ma_community_spouse was accordingly not created as its own item; ma_ltc_couple (CO-643) still tests for a community spouse's existence via exists_related. Items: CO-643, CO-655, CO-656, CO-657, CO-833, CO-834, CO-835.

Phase 7 sweep: a modeling choice about cross-person reads; no published rule settles it.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): Checked 2026-11-21: § 8.100.7.R.2 (D-65); the question is whether the community spouse's facts are supplied directly or read across the spouse edge, a ledger-design choice; no published rule settles it.

HCPF fix round (2026-11-21, docs/reviews/stakeholder-hcpf.md): ma_community_spouse_income (CO-2467) sums the income facts of the person's spouse who does not reside in a medical institution, less mandatory payroll taxes (ma_payroll_taxes_withheld, CO-2466, read as 0 when not stated), as § 8.100.7.R.2 (S358) defines it; ma_community_spouse_income_allowance (CO-656) reads it, and community_spouse_income (CO-833) is no longer read. In CO-01 and CO-02 the derived figure equals the 1,180 the cases supplied (p2's Social Security). The community spouse's shelter costs (CO-834) stay a statement about the couple's home, and health insurance premiums (CO-835) are the institutionalized spouse's own. Items: CO-656, CO-2467.

Dual-entry audit fix round (2026-11-21, docs/reviews/audit-dual-entry.md 1.1-1.4, 5.4): the remaining readers now read the community spouse's own facts. ma_csra_increased (CO-1977) reads ma_community_spouse_income (CO-2467), and community_spouse_income (CO-833) is retired. ma_mmmna (CO-655) reads ma_community_spouse_shelter_costs (CO-2802): the community spouse's own shelter_cost_monthly, a condominium or cooperative fee (housing_maintenance_fee, CO-2800) and the larger of the SNAP standard utility allowance her household reaches and her actual utility expenses (utility_expenses_actual, CO-2801), as § 8.100.7.Q.1.b (S1756) defines them; shelter_costs_community_spouse (CO-834) is retired. family_members_with_community_spouse (CO-838) and ma_resides_with_community_spouse (CO-2468) are retired; ma_family_allowance_members (CO-2469) reads the family member's lives_with. CO-01 and CO-02 were recomputed by hand (MMMNA 3,909.50, income allowance 2,729.50, patient payment 0, CSRA 259,660). Items: CO-655, CO-1977, CO-2469, CO-2800, CO-2801, CO-2802.

### OQ-98. Resolved: Veterans-related personal needs allowance ($90) wired into ma_patient_payment's PNA deduction (CO-1986, CO-255)

Body: 8.100.7.V.3.d.ii states a $90 veterans-related PNA for a veteran or widow(er) with no spouse or dependent child receiving a non-service-connected VA pension (parameter ma_veteran_personal_needs_allowance, CO-255), distinct from the base PNA (ma_personal_needs_allowance_institutional, CO-887 - a supplied fact, not a parameter, as of the phase-4 fix round's C-11) ma_patient_payment (CO-658) actually subtracts. Selecting between them needs a supplied fact for VA non-service-connected pension receipt this batch does not add (has_military_connection exists but does not distinguish pension type); ma_patient_payment always uses the non-veteran base PNA. Items: CO-887, CO-255, CO-658.

Phase 7 sweep: the current § 8.100.7.V.3.d.ii (D-65) states the same $90 veterans PNA; wiring it needs a VA non-service-connected pension fact - a modeling gap.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): C.R.S. 25.5-6-206(2)(b) (S1232) and § 8.100.7.V.3.d.ii (S1231) give $90 to a nursing facility resident who receives a VA non-service-connected disability pension and has no spouse or dependent child, and to a pensioner's surviving spouse with no dependent child. New fact ma_qualifies_for_veterans_personal_needs_allowance (CO-1986) and rule ma_personal_needs_allowance (CO-1987), which ma_patient_payment (CO-658) and ma_csra_increased (CO-1977) now deduct. The household cases state the new fact false; no expectation changed on that account.

### OQ-99. Partly resolved: Colorado's income trust private-pay-rate ceiling (8.100.7.G.1.a) is modeled; open: its OM 25-073 figures are unverified

Body: 8.100.7.G.1.a requires an income trust user's gross income to be "above the 300%-SSI limit but below the average cost of private Long Term Care institution care in the geographic region," a ceiling this batch has no supplied or parameter figure for (see OQ-91's transfer_penalty_divisor, which is the same average private-pay rate figure); ma_meets_special_income_group (CO-642) treats has_income_trust as an unconditional override once gross income exceeds 300%, without testing the upper ceiling. Items: CO-642.

Phase 7 sweep: the regional average private-pay rate is still not in any fetchable published document (OQ-212), so the ceiling cannot be stated.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): resolved pending verification. The product owner supplied the 2025 and 2026 regional average private-pay rates from HCPF Operational Memo OM 25-073 (D-125, which could not be retrieved: hcpf.colorado.gov refuses automated requests); they are recorded as the unverified excerpt S1230 and must be checked against the memo before the items move past Draft. The county-to-region mapping is in the current rule text (§ 8.100.7.G.6.a.i.a), S1229). New fact county_of_residence (CO-1979, shared; CCCAP's county residence can read it), rule ma_ltc_private_pay_region (CO-1980), parameters ma_income_trust_limit_region_i-iv (CO-1981-CO-1984, dated 2025 and 2026) and rule ma_income_trust_gross_income_limit (CO-1985). ma_meets_special_income_group (CO-642) now requires, with an income trust, gross income below the regional rate.

Hard-coded numbers fix round (audit H-7): the county-to-region assignment is ma_ltc_private_pay_region_by_county_table (CO-3021) and the four regional rates are one table keyed by region, ma_income_trust_limit_table (CO-3022), replacing CO-1981 to CO-1984. The figures and their verification status are unchanged. The transfer-penalty divisor (CO-832) stays a figure of its own, because the memo states it separately (S1228; audit D-2).

Class: blocked on a named document
Owner: LTSS policy owner
Assumption: The ceiling on income for a person with an income trust is the regional average private-pay rate for nursing facility care, using the 2025 and 2026 figures the product owner supplied from the operational memo.
Blocked on: HCPF Operational Memo OM 25-073, which states the regional private-pay rates (hcpf.colorado.gov refused)

### OQ-100. Resolved (overruled): long-term care eligibility is reported for the setting the person requests

Body: brief4.md's options (eligible_institutional, eligible_hcbs, eligible_pace, ...) are mutually exclusive, but a person meeting the nursing facility level of care, the HCBS target group, and PACE criteria simultaneously (as the target household's p1 might, once enrolled) is not disambiguated by any cited text; ma_ltc_eligibility_status (CO-652) tests PACE, then HCBS, then institutional, in that order, which is an authoring choice, not a reading of 8.100.7 - the phase-4 review's C-18 finding corrected this open question's own text, which previously said "institutional first" while the item's case list and precision both said PACE first. Items: CO-652.

Phase 7 sweep: no fetched text orders the settings; the choice remains an authoring one.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): Checked 2026-11-21: § 8.100.7 and the PACE rules incorporated by § 8.497 (D-55, D-65); none orders the settings for a person eligible in more than one; no published rule settles it.

HCPF fix round (2026-11-21, docs/reviews/stakeholder-hcpf.md), B-3: the reviewer (HCPF program manager) overruled the PACE-first ordering: PACE and HCBS are services a person chooses and enrolls in, so a nursing facility resident asking for nursing facility care is not in PACE because they could be. ma_ltc_eligibility_status (CO-652) now tests the setting in ma_ltc_setting_requested (CO-2411) - institutional, hcbs or pace - and reports not_eligible_for_requested_setting when the person meets every financial and functional test but not that setting's own conditions; ma_pace_eligible (CO-654) now tests the PACE service area and the safe-in-community condition (42 CFR 460.150(b)(3), (c); S1726). CO-01 and CO-02 p1 are eligible_institutional. Items: CO-652, CO-654, CO-2411.

### OQ-101. Partly resolved: Colorado Works unit composition is modeled; open: the need and grant standard of a unit with more than two caretakers, which the chart has no row for

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.1.F's Standards of Assistance Chart (S414) states three separate rows of need and grant standards - No Caretaker, One Caretaker, Two Caretakers - each keyed by number of children (0-10, plus an Each Additional Child amount). This previously modeled only the One Caretaker row, keyed by total unit size, understating the standard for a no-caretaker unit and overstating it for a two-caretaker unit. The fix round (C-5) re-keys cw_need_standard_table (CO-274) and cw_grant_standard_table (CO-276) by number of children (0-10) instead of unit size, adds cw_need_standard_table_no_caretaker (CO-351), cw_grant_standard_table_no_caretaker (CO-352), cw_need_standard_table_two_caretakers (CO-353), and cw_grant_standard_table_two_caretakers (CO-354) for the other two rows, and adds cw_unit_caretaker_count (CO-926, capped at 2, since the chart has no row above two caretakers) and cw_unit_child_count (CO-927) so cw_need_standard (CO-911) and cw_grant_standard (CO-912) select the correct row and column instead of defaulting to the One Caretaker row for every unit. What remains open: a unit with more than two non-dependent-child members is not otherwise modeled in this volume (cw_assistance_unit_members, CO-900, then built only a single caretaker/dependent-child parent pair; superseded by the phase-10 note below, coverage pass 3 P3-3), so cw_unit_caretaker_count's cap at 2 is never exercised by a real three-caretaker household under this batch's household-composition model; and the "table keyed by household size" catalog type still has no dedicated two-key form (K-10, a catalog change for the tech member), so this fix is three single-key tables plus a selector rather than one two-key table. Items: CO-274, CO-276, CO-351, CO-352, CO-353, CO-354, CO-911, CO-912, CO-926, CO-927.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the chart (S414) is fully carried; what remains is unit composition (a unit with more than two caretakers, and the C.1.c-e and C.2 members cw_assistance_unit_members, CO-900, does not build). That is NEEDS FACT and structure work with OQ-152; not done in this sweep.

Phase-10 open-question sweep (supplied-facts round): the unit composition part is resolved with OQ-152 and OQ-140. cw_assistance_unit_members (CO-900) now reaches the spouse of a pregnant parent (3.604.2.C.1.e) and, on request, a stepparent, a non-parent caretaker and that caretaker's spouse and the other optional members of C.2 (S1625; cw_requests_inclusion_in_assistance_unit, CO-2346; cw_is_optional_member_by_custody_or_support, CO-2347; cw_is_optional_assistance_unit_member, CO-2349; cw_is_excluded_from_assistance_unit, CO-2350), so a unit can now hold three adults (two parents and a requesting non-parent caretaker, for example), and cw_unit_caretaker_count (CO-926) reads the caretakers it counts. Still open: such a unit's need and grant standard. Checked 2026-11-21: 3.606.1.F's Standards of Assistance Chart (D-81, S414) has rows for no caretaker, one caretaker and two caretakers only, and no other text of 9 CCR 2503-6 prices a third; no published rule settles it, so CO-926 keeps its cap of two.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: A Colorado Works unit with three or more caretakers gets the two-caretaker need and grant standard.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.1.F, the Standards of Assistance Chart (D-81, S414), has rows for no caretaker, one caretaker and two caretakers only, and no other paragraph of 9 CCR 2503-6 prices a third. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-102. Partly resolved: the minor-parent income-deeming "needs" deduction counts the parents and their other children in the home; open: which dollar measure "needs" means

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.1.A.4.a (S413) says the needs of the non-participant parent of a minor parent, and of others in that parent's home for whom the parent is responsible, "shall be deducted from the result to determine the amount attributed to the assistance unit," but does not state which figure "needs" refers to. cw_deemed_parent_income_of_minor_parent (CO-909) reads the One Caretaker need standard for a unit of one (cw_need_standard_table's size-1 value, $253) as the non-participant parent's own needs deduction, on the reading that "needs" in this deeming formula means the same need standard 3.606.1.F otherwise uses for a unit of that composition. It does not add a further deduction for other people in the non-participant parent's home for whom the parent is responsible. Fix round (C-11(c)): this open question previously said no such person exists in this batch's target household, which was incorrect - in CO-01, p3 (the non-participant parent) is responsible for p4 (her own seventeen-year-old daughter, also living in the home), so the second deduction is skipped in the very household this question described as having no one to skip. It does not change CO-01's outcome (deducting the size-2 need standard $331 instead of $253 still leaves deemed income far above p4's unit's need standard), so the household does not test the rule either way; the deduction itself remains unmodeled (no item computes "one plus the count of the non-participant parent's other dependents in the home"). Items: CO-909.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): whose needs are deducted is answered by the text. 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.1.A.4.a (D-81, S1307): "The needs of the stepparent or parent of the minor parent, and the needs of individuals living in the home for whom the stepparent or parent are responsible shall be deducted from the result to determine the amount attributed to the assistance unit." The new cw_minor_parent_deeming_needs_deduction (CO-2000) deducts the need standard for the parent or parents in the home and their other children in the home (the minor parent's siblings and half siblings; not the minor parent herself, whose needs her own assistance unit meets): the One Caretaker row for one parent, the Two Caretakers row for two, keyed by the number of those children. cw_deemed_parent_income_of_minor_parent (CO-909) now deducts it once from the parents' combined countable income instead of the zero-children One Caretaker figure once per parent (new test CO-909-T6: a parent with another child in the home deducts $331, deeming $1,959 instead of $2,037). In CO-01 and CO-02 p3 has no child in the home other than the minor parent p4, so the deduction stays $253 and no expectation changed. Still open: which dollar measure "needs" means. Checked 2026-11-21: 3.605.1.A.4.a and 3.606.1.F (D-81) - the deeming paragraph names no figure and the need standard chart does not say it serves deeming; no published rule settles it. The draft reads the need standard. Items: CO-909, CO-2000.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: The needs deducted when a minor parent's own parent's income is deemed are measured by the Colorado Works need standard.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.1.A.4.a (D-81, S1307) deducts "the needs of" the parent and those the parent is responsible for without naming a figure, and the need standard chart of 3.606.1.F (D-81) does not say it serves deeming. No published rule settles it.

Coverage pass 2 (CW2-2): the persons whose needs are deducted are now the parents whose income is deemed, cw_minor_parent_deemed_parents (CO-4660: not in her unit and living in the same home), and the minor parent's siblings in that home who are their children and not in her unit (CO-2000). A parent living elsewhere, or one in her unit, no longer selects the Two Caretakers row. The dollar measure is unchanged.

### OQ-103. Resolved: Colorado Works sponsor deeming and disqualified-member income are modeled

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.1.A.5 (sponsor deeming to a sponsored non-citizen) and 3.605.1.A.6 (deeming the net income of a required assistance-unit member who is disqualified for citizenship, non-citizen status, lawful presence, or SSN reasons) are separate income-attribution rules this batch's fixed interface (cw_countable_income) does not name a dedicated item for. Only the 3.605.1.A.4 non-participant-parent-of-a-minor-parent deeming (cw_deemed_parent_income_of_minor_parent, CO-909) is modeled. Items: CO-910.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): current citation. 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.1.A.5-6 (`documents/D-81.md:2351-2400`) state sponsor deeming (20% or $175 of earnings, the 125% FPL exemption and the other exemptions) and disqualified-member deeming unchanged from D-61; open only because unmodeled.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): 3.605.1.A.5-6 (D-81:2351-2400) are clear; sponsor deeming needs the sponsor's income, household size and exemption facts, and disqualified-member deeming needs OQ-143's "in the unit for income but not for size" state. Not modeled in this sweep.

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep, supplied-facts round): 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.1.A.5 (S1627) deems a sponsor's and sponsor's spouse's net income - gross earnings less twenty percent or $175, whichever is less, less support paid to dependents outside the sponsor's home, plus unearned income - to a sponsored non-citizen, except for the seven exemptions of A.5.c and a rebuttal under A.5.b. New neutral facts stated on the sponsored person: sponsor_gross_earned_income, sponsor_spouse_gross_earned_income, sponsor_unearned_income, sponsor_spouse_unearned_income, sponsor_support_paid_to_dependents_outside_home, sponsor_spouse_is_cosponsor and is_victim_of_battery_or_extreme_cruelty (CO-2355 to CO-2359, CO-2362, CO-2363); Colorado Works facts cw_sponsor_deeming_household_size (CO-2360) and cw_sponsor_income_availability_rebutted (CO-2361); parameters for 125%, twenty percent, $175 and forty quarters (CO-2366 to CO-2369); cw_sponsor_deeming_applies (CO-2364) and cw_sponsor_deemed_income (CO-2365). is_sponsored_non_citizen (CO-1573) now means a binding Form I-864 affidavit, which carries A.5.c.3 and c.7. A.6's disqualified members are counted through OQ-143 (gross earnings, removed from the unit size); the difference between A.6's "minus employment disregards" and 3.604.2.M's "without applying income disregards" is recorded as OQ-490. cw_countable_income (CO-910) and cw_payment_countable_income (CO-923) add both (new tests CO-910-T3 and T4, CO-2364-T1 to T7, CO-2365-T1 to T3). No household case has a sponsored or disqualified member in a Colorado Works unit; no expectation changed.

Hard-coded numbers fix round (audit D-3): the forty quarters are federal_qualifying_quarters_threshold (CO-3033), the one federal figure SNAP, Medical Assistance and Colorado Works each restate; cw_sponsor_deeming_exempt_quarters (CO-2369) is retired.

### OQ-104. Work participation monthly-hour equivalent is an assumption

Body: 45 CFR 261.31 and 261.35 (S419, S420) state weekly hour minimums (30, or 20 for a single parent with a child under six); no fetched document states a monthly equivalent, and 26-2-706(2)(c) (S403) only requires an income conversion ratio, not an hours conversion ratio. cw_meets_work_participation (CO-920; coverage review P3-5, first written CO-919) assumes a monthly equivalent of the weekly figure times four, which is not stated in any fetched document. Items: CO-920.

Checked 2026-11-21: 45 CFR 261.31(a)(1) and 261.35 (S419, S420) state a weekly average over the month ("a minimum average of 30 hours per week") with no weeks-per-month factor, and they measure the State's participation rate; the current 9 CCR 2503-6 (D-81) states no individual monthly-hour equivalent. No published rule settles it.

CDHS stakeholder review fix round (2026-11-21): the CDHS reviewer notes that federal TANF reporting converts weekly hours with 4.33 weeks a month, not 4; the impact is low because participation is not an eligibility condition here. Recorded; no change.

Hard-coded numbers fix round (audit H-19): the four weeks are a parameter, cw_weeks_per_month (CO-3044), which carries this question, instead of a literal in cw_meets_work_participation (CO-920). SNAP converts by 4.3 (7 CFR 273.10(c)(2), snap_weeks_per_month, CO-3041) and Medical Assistance by 4.33 (ma_community_engagement_weeks_per_month, CO-1907); neither text governs Colorado Works, so the draft keeps four until the cash programs owner decides.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: A weekly hours requirement is turned into a monthly one by multiplying by four.

Checked 2026-11-22: 45 CFR 261.31(a) and 261.35 (S419, S420) state weekly averages with no weeks-per-month factor and measure the State's participation rate, and the current 9 CCR 2503-6 (D-81) states no monthly-hour figure for a person. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-105. Resolved: the Colorado Works earned income disregards follow the current 3.606.2.A - $90 at application, 100% in the first certification period and for a new source, 67% for a current source after it

Body: This question was written from the Cornell copy of 9 CCR 2503-6 (D-61, S416), which carried the pre-June 2023 3.606.2.A: a $90 disregard for the income test and a 67% disregard for the payment, at application and ongoing. The current Secretary of State text (D-81, version effective 2025-07-01; S1000), amended effective 2023-06-01 (S1019), reads differently, and the phase-8 review (B-1) found the items still paying on the old rule. Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): the volume now follows S1000. (1) At application the gross earned income minus $90, plus countable unearned income, is tested against the need standard (A.1, first sentence) - cw_countable_earned_income (CO-907) for a unit not yet receiving. (2) If it passes, the payment for the first certification period disregards 100% of earned income (A.1), and a unit currently receiving disregards 100% of a new source of earned income for the first certification period that the income impacts (A.2.a) - cw_new_earnings_disregard_rate (CO-1503), read by cw_payment_countable_earned_income (CO-922) for an applicant, for a recipient in its first certification period (cw_in_first_certification_period, CO-1501) and for a new source (cw_is_new_earned_income_source, CO-1502). (3) After the first certification period a current source receives the 67% disregard (A.1 last sentence, A.2.b; CO-350). (4) A unit currently receiving is tested against the need standard with those same disregards, since 3.606.1.J (S1001) applies "the appropriate earned income disregards ... as described in Section 3.606.2" when the unit reports earned income - CO-907 reads CO-922 for a recipient (receives_tanf_cash). A month mixing a new and a current source is OQ-350. Items: CO-350, CO-907, CO-922, CO-923, CO-1260, CO-1501, CO-1502, CO-1503.

### OQ-111. Resolved: OAP has no A/B split; it is one category (with OAP-C for court-committed clients)

Body: The version of 9 CCR 2503-5 effective 2026-03-02 (D-80) defines only "OAP" ("a program for a client sixty (60) years of age or older") and "OAP-C" (an OAP-age client committed to the Colorado Mental Health Institute or a Regional Center by court order) in 3.530.1 (S701); no OAP-A/OAP-B split exists in the rule. af_category (CO-953) now returns a single `oap` category for an OAP-age client, af_grant_standard (CO-949) and af_oap_grant (CO-951) test it, and the assumption items af_oap_group (CO-941) and af_oap_group_age_split (CO-286) are removed; cases CO-01, CO-02 and CO-07 no longer carry af_oap_group expectations and CO-07's p5 is `oap`. OAP-C is not modeled (no court-commitment fact; the client would be in a facility and receive a personal needs allowance, 3.532.C-D). The A/B split survives only as a Medicaid category label: phase 7 records that 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.6.K.1 (its D-65) defines OAP-A as 65 and older and OAP-B as 60 through 64 for the Old Age Pension health care program; that label belongs to the Medicaid chapter and does not split the cash category. Items: CO-949, CO-951, CO-953.

### OQ-112. Resolved: the current rule has no Aid to the Blind program; blind clients 0-59 are AND-SO

Body: D-62 states OAP (3.530-3.534), AND-SO (3.540-3.543), and AND-CS (3.546-3.549) as the three Adult Financial programs; there is no "3.545" or other AB-specific section, and 3.540's AND-SO description explicitly folds blindness into the AND-SO age range ("unless diagnosed with blindness, then age zero (0) through 59 years of age"). af_ab_eligible (CO-945) is modeled from the statute alone (26-2-111(5)(a), 26-2-103(3), S443, S444), and af_category's "ab" branch uses the AND-SO grant standard (af_and_so_grant_standard) since D-62 states no separate AB grant standard. Fix round (C-9): the phase-5 review proposed dropping af_category's `[<=, age, af_and_max_age]` cap from the "ab" branch, reading 3.520.61.A as silent on blindness. The fetched text (S530, 3.520.61.A) is not silent: "Be eighteen (18) through fifty-nine (59) years of age for AND-SO (unless diagnosed with blindness, then age zero (0) through 59 years of age)" folds blindness into the same 0-59 band as the rest of AND-SO, removing only the 18-year floor, not the 59-year ceiling. The cap is kept for that reason, contrary to the review's proposed fix, because the fetched regulatory text (as opposed to the silent statute) does impose one. Items: CO-945, CO-953.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): answered by the current text. 9 CCR 2503-5 3.500 (S1017, D-80 effective 2026-03-02) lists the Adult Financial programs as OAP, AND "consisting of AND-State Only (AND-SO) and AND-Colorado Supplement (AND-CS)", Home Care Allowance and Burial Assistance - no Aid to the Blind - and 3.520.61.A (S711) puts a blind client aged 0 through 59 in AND-SO, which af_and_so_eligible (CO-1203) models. The ab branch of af_category (CO-953) is kept only as the statute-only route of C.R.S. 26-2-111(5) (S443) and is reached only by a blind person AND-SO does not take (for example a blind SSDI recipient, whom 3.540 excludes from AND-SO but 26-2-111(5)(a)(I) names); af_and_grant (CO-952) says so. Items: CO-945, CO-952, CO-953.

CDHS stakeholder review fix round (2026-11-21) (N-1): the Aid to the Blind branch of af_category (CO-953), kept as a statute-only route, is removed, with its option "ab" and af_ab_eligible (CO-945): no current rule pays Aid to the Blind, so a county could not act on that result. af_grant_standard (CO-949) and af_and_grant (CO-952) lose their ab arms.

### OQ-113. Resolved: AND-SO's own medical certification process is not modeled

Body: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.541.1 (not excerpted) requires a State-prescribed medical certification form signed by a medical provider, reviewed and weighed by the county department, as the basis for the total-disability finding. af_and_so_disability_met (CO-944) instead reads the shared supplied facts meets_ssa_disability_criteria and snap_receives_disability_or_blindness_payment directly, treating an SSA/SSDI disability determination as evidence of Colorado's own total-disability standard (which 26-2-103(14)(a), S445, defines independently of the federal standard for persons not receiving Title XVI benefits). This is an assumption; the county's own medical certification process is out of scope for this batch. Items: CO-944.

Resolved (vocabulary fix round, docs/reviews/vocabulary-reuse.md R-5): af_and_so_disability_met (CO-944) reads af_has_total_disability_six_months (CO-1728), the county's finding on the state-prescribed medical certification of a total disability lasting or expected to last six months (C.R.S. 26-2-103(14)(a), 26-2-111(4)(a), 9 CCR 2503-5 3.541.A; S442, S445, S459), and receives_ssdi (CO-1727) for the SSDI-determined route. It no longer reads the SSA disability fact.

### OQ-114. Resolved: Adult Financial deems a spouse's, an AND-CS child's parents' and a sponsor's income

Body: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.534 (OAP), 3.544.D-G (AND-SO), and 3.549.C-G (AND-CS) each state detailed rules for deeming a non-recipient spouse's or a non-citizen client's sponsor's income to the client, with separate SSI-benefit-standard-based deductions for the spouse's or sponsor's own needs and dependents. af_countable_income (CO-946) and af_countable_resources (CO-947) read only the client's own income and the household's ssi_countable_resources_individual/_couple figures; they do not add a computed deemed amount from a non-recipient spouse or sponsor. Items: CO-946, CO-947.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): current citation. 9 CCR 2503-5 3.534, 3.544.D-E and 3.549.C-D (D-80, `documents/D-80.md:4093-4193, 4632-4717, 4996-5084`) state spousal and sponsor deeming; open only because unmodeled. The married $20 split (3.533.A.2.c.1, 3.549.A.2.c.1; S1002, S1003) is recorded here as well since OQ-115 was resolved.
