Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): 3.534, 3.544.D-E and 3.549.C-D (D-80) are clear; spousal and sponsor deeming need the non-recipient spouse's and sponsor's income, resources and dependents as facts. Not modeled in this sweep.

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep, supplied-facts round): 9 CCR 2503-5 3.534 (S1632), 3.544.D-G (S1633) and 3.549.C-G (S1634). The new af_deemed_relative_income (CO-2384) deems, for each spouse in the home and, for an AND-CS client under eighteen, each parent in the home, half the gross earnings over $65 and the unearned income less the program's deductions (for OAP the OAP grant standard, the children's needs of af_spouse_dependent_children_needs, CO-2392, medical payments for dependents, af_medical_payments_for_dependents, CO-2385, and court-ordered support; for AND-CS the client's unused part of the $20; for AND-SO $20 unless the client has unearned income), and nothing for a relative who receives Adult Financial, SSI or Medicaid (the new neutral receives_medicaid, CO-2393) with income within the OAP or SSI standard. The new af_deemed_sponsor_income (CO-2386) deems the sponsor's gross income less the SSI benefit standard for the sponsor, the standard or half of it for the spouse, and half of it for each dependent (sponsor_dependent_count, CO-2387; sponsor_has_spouse_in_household, CO-2388; sponsor_spouse_is_cosponsor, CO-2362), undivided among several sponsored clients. af_countable_income (CO-946) adds both; the program branch is read as CO-946 reads it (af_is_oap_age, af_and_cs_eligible) to avoid a cycle through af_category. Not modeled: the MMMNA deduction for an institutionalized spouse (the Medicaid post-eligibility items carry it). Household cases: CO-07's parents now state receives_medicaid true (Colorado Works parents on Medicaid), and with $400 and $0 of income each is within the $994 SSI standard, so nothing is deemed to Alma and her AND-CS grant stays $294 (994 - 700); CO-71's grandfather now states is_sponsored_non_citizen false (illustrative), so his OAP grant is unchanged. Verified by hand: CO-07 p3 countable 700, grant 294. New tests CO-2384-T1 to T5, CO-2386-T1 to T3, CO-2392-T1 and T2; CO-946's tests state the two deemed amounts as 0.

Final cash review (2026-11-22, docs/reviews/final-cash.md FC-4 and FC-22): the married $20 split this question also carried was still unmodeled after the resolution; the reviewer confirmed with the engine that two OAP spouses with $500 of Social Security each had 480 countable and a $552 grant each, where 3.533.A.2.c.1 (S2610) gives 490 and $542. Now modeled: af_client_general_income_disregard (CO-3829) gives a married client half of the $20 (af_married_couple_disregard_share, CO-3828), and af_countable_income (CO-946) and the spouse's deeming (af_deemed_relative_income, CO-2384) read the part applied and rolled (CO-3832, CO-3833). The reading of "split" is OQ-661. New tests CO-946-T13, T14; household case CO-120. Items: CO-946, CO-2384, CO-3829.

### OQ-115. Resolved: Adult Financial countable income subtracts SSI, applies the $20 to the rest, and adds the SSI back

Body: 3.533.A.2, 3.544.B, and 3.549.A.2 (S457, S461, S465) each describe subtracting any SSI amount from gross unearned income before applying the $20 disregard, then adding the full SSI amount back; 3.533.A.1.f and 3.549.A.2.f also roll an unused portion of the $20 disregard (when unearned income is under $20) into the earned-income calculation. No supplied fact in this volume states a person's dollar amount of SSI income (only the yes/no receives_ssi), so af_countable_income (CO-946) applies the $20 general disregard to the sum of all unearned income sources without separating out or adding back an SSI amount, and does not model the disregard rollover. This mainly affects AND-CS clients (who by definition receive SSI); af_and_cs_eligible (CO-950) does not depend on af_countable_income for that reason. K-6 fix round: ssi_income (CO-849) now states the dollar amount, and af_and_cs_eligible (CO-950) uses it directly for the separate 3.546 "not receiving the full SSI benefit standard" test (compared to ssi_federal_benefit_rate_individual, CO-235) - see the new derivation on CO-950. af_countable_income (CO-946) still does not subtract-and-add-back the SSI amount, still applies the $20 disregard to the unearned total undifferentiated, and still does not roll an unused portion of the $20 disregard onto earned income. K-8: 3.533.A.2.b.1), 3.544.B.2, and 3.549.A.2.b.1) (S531-region text, `documents/D-62.md:1629,1995,2145`, each stating "If the client is married, the $20.00 disregard shall be split between the client and the spouse so that no more than a $20.00 disregard is applied") also go unmodeled - af_countable_income (CO-946) applies the full $20 to each spouse of a married AND-SO or AND-CS couple applying separately, rather than splitting it. Items: CO-946, CO-950.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): answered by the current text. 9 CCR 2503-5 3.533.A.2.a-f (S1002) and 3.549.A.2.a-f (S1003) state the steps: gross unearned income from all sources, subtract any SSI, deduct $20 from the remainder (none for a client with SSI only), add the full SSI back; if gross unearned income is under $20, the difference goes to the earned income calculation. ssi_income (CO-849) states the amount, so af_countable_income (CO-946) now applies the steps (B-2): an AND-CS child with $700 of SSI has $700 countable and a $294 grant, not $994. The reading of "gross unearned income" in A.2.f is OQ-351; the married $20 split (A.2.c.1) remains with OQ-114. Items: CO-946, CO-952.

### OQ-116. Resolved: AND-CS's "not receiving the full SSI benefit standard" test is modeled with ssi_income

Body: 3.546 (S462) defines AND-CS as for SSI recipients "not receiving the full SSI benefit standard," but no supplied fact states the dollar amount of SSI a person receives (only receives_ssi, yes/no) against which the SSI benefit standard could be compared. af_and_cs_eligible (CO-950) tests SSI recipiency, disability or blindness, age, and residency only. Items: CO-950.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): implemented by the phase-5 K-6 fix: af_and_cs_eligible (CO-950) tests ssi_income (CO-849) below ssi_federal_benefit_rate_individual (CO-235). The current 3.546 (S706, D-80) restates the condition unchanged. Items: CO-950.

### OQ-117. Resolved: the general Adult Financial bar on Colorado Works recipients and sanction closures supersedes the Aid to the Blind carve-out

Body: 26-2-111(5)(a)(I) (S443) excludes from AB "any person who is a member of a household that is receiving public assistance under the aid to families with dependent children program." af_ab_eligible (CO-945) does not read cw_eligibility_status or any Colorado Works fact to apply this carve-out. Items: CO-945.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): answered by the current text. 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.61.F (S711) makes "Not be currently receiving or eligible for financial assistance from Colorado Works" a requirement of every Adult Financial program, and 3.520.71.F.2 (S1011) makes a client ineligible while a Colorado Works case is closed for a sanction, demonstrable evidence or disqualification. af_barred_by_colorado_works (CO-1511) states the bar from receives_tanf_cash and cw_eligibility_status (eligible or sanction_closure), and every branch of af_category (CO-953) and af_and_so_eligible (CO-1203) reads it; the statute's narrower AB household carve-out is covered by it. What the bar does not model is OQ-357. Items: CO-945, CO-953, CO-1203, CO-1511.

### OQ-118. Resolved: the OAP and AND grant standards are dated parameters whose versions follow the rule's stated effective dates

Body: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.531.C, 3.540.B and 3.546.B (D-80, S702, S705, S706) let the State Board adjust each grant standard to stay within appropriations with no appeal, and each adjustment appears in the rule as a new figure with its own effective date: OAP $1,032 effective January 1, 2026 (3.530.A, S700), AND-CS $994 effective January 1, 2026 (3.546.A, S706), AND-SO still $248.00 effective April 1, 2022 (3.540.A, S705). af_oap_grant_standard (CO-289) and af_and_cs_grant_standard (CO-291) carry the 2026 figures as new versions beside the 2025 ones, af_and_so_grant_standard (CO-290) cites the current text for its unchanged figure, and a future adjustment is one more version. The same rule adds payment floor standards (OAP $189, AND-CS $13, HCA $977 effective January 1, 2026; S700, S707, S713) with a State Supplementary Payment that raises a lower grant to the floor (3.531.D.2, S702); these are new parameters (CO-1200, CO-1201, CO-1202) read by af_oap_grant (CO-951), af_and_grant (CO-952) and af_hca_grant (CO-1219). MOE lump-sum pass-along payments (3.531.D.1) are irregular, not an entitlement, and are not modeled. Items: CO-289, CO-290, CO-291, CO-951, CO-952, CO-1200, CO-1201, CO-1202.

### OQ-119. Resolved: OAP follows the statute's citizen-or-qualified-alien test; "eligible to apply" speaks only of applying

Body: 26-2-111(2)(a)(I) (S441) grants OAP only to "a United States citizen or a qualified alien"; 3.520.67.E (S451) instead says "all non-citizens are eligible to apply for OAP." af_meets_citizenship_requirement (CO-943) is modeled on the statute's citizen-or-qualified-non-citizen test (the controlling law, per the phase-2 brief's rule that the statute controls where regulation and statute disagree), reading 3.520.67.E's language as describing a right to apply rather than a right to be found eligible; this is an assumption. Items: CO-943.

Resolution (open-question sweep, 2026-11-21): C.R.S. 26-2-111(2)(a)(I) (D-59, S441) grants the old age pension to a person who "is a United States citizen or a qualified alien"; 3.520.67.E (S451) says only that "all non-citizens are eligible to apply for OAP". The statute states the eligibility test, the rule the right to apply; af_meets_citizenship_requirement_oap (CO-929) follows the statute (citizen, national or qualified non-citizen), and the Aid to the Needy Disabled test is the shared meets_public_assistance_citizenship_requirement (CO-905; CO-943 was retired into it). No change. Checking the statute surfaced a further point, recorded as OQ-430: 26-2-111(2)(c) conditions OAP for a qualified alien on the entry-date or five-year-bar test of 26-2-111.8(2), which the rule (3.520.68.A) states "for AND only". Items: CO-929.

### OQ-120. Resolved: Adult Financial first-month proration and the OAP transition in the sixtieth birthday month

Body: 3.532.B.2-3 (not separately excerpted) prorates a client's first grant payment by days remaining in the month for a mid-month eligibility determination and transitions a client from another Adult Financial program to OAP effective the first day of the birthday month. af_oap_grant (CO-951) and af_and_grant (CO-952) compute only the full-month grant standard minus countable income; proration and same-month program transition are out of scope for this batch. Items: CO-951, CO-952.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): current citation. 9 CCR 2503-5 3.532.B.1-3 (`documents/D-80.md:3988-4003`), 3.543.B and 3.548.B (S1010 region) state the proration and the birthday-month transition; open only because unmodeled.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): 3.532.B.1-3, 3.543.B and 3.548.B (D-80) are clear; proration needs the eligibility date within the month and the birthday transition a program-change month. Not modeled in this sweep.

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep, supplied-facts round): 9 CCR 2503-5 3.532.B.1-3, 3.543.B.1-2 and 3.548.B.1-2 (S1635). The new af_eligibility_date (CO-2391) states the day eligibility begins; af_oap_grant (CO-951) and af_and_grant (CO-952) prorate the grant in that month by the days from that date through the month's end over the days in the month (new tests CO-951-T8 and T9). Whether the day of eligibility counts as a remaining day, and the absent rounding, are recorded as OQ-491. af_is_oap_age (CO-940) is true in the month a client receiving another Adult Financial grant turns sixty (3.532.B.3; new tests CO-940-T3 and T4), so af_category moves the client to OAP from the first of the birth month. No case states an eligibility date or a client turning sixty; no expectation changed.

### OQ-121. CCCAP county entry income limit is a county-set figure, not a single text-stated number

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1-3.111.H.1.a (S485) says entry eligibility "shall be set by the Department at a level based on the self-sufficiency standard, not to be set below one hundred eighty-five percent (185%) of the federal poverty level" - a floor, not a fixed percentage, and the Department sets the actual figure per county rather than the rule stating one number. cccap_entry_income_limit_pct_fpl (CO-870) is modeled as a supplied fact (the percentage the county's current CCCAP plan uses, at or above 185%), not a parameter, pending a fetch of the current county entry-limit schedule. The 100% FPG table the text does state (S485) is a parameter (cccap_poverty_guideline_monthly_table, CO-323) used as the base the supplied percentage multiplies. Items: CO-870, CO-986.

Phase 8: the version of 8 CCR 1403-1 effective 2026-08-14 (D-87, S780) keeps 3.111.H.1.a unchanged (entry eligibility "set by the Department at a level based on the self-sufficiency standard, not to be set below one hundred eighty-five percent (185%) of the federal poverty level"), so the entry percentage stays supplied. The FPG and 85% SMI tables it is applied to now carry the versions "Through September 30, 2026" and "Effective October 1, 2026" (S780, S781; CO-323, CO-324, CO-340, CO-341).

Phase-10 open-question sweep: stays blocked. 3.111.H.1.a (S1413) still has the Department set the entry level per county from the self-sufficiency standard; the county figures are published only by the Colorado Department of Early Childhood (cdec.colorado.gov, refused to this session), in its county self-sufficiency entry-eligibility schedule, which would settle it.

CDHS stakeholder review fix round (2026-11-21) (C-9): the CDHS/CDEC reviewer confirms the CDEC publishes each county's entry level; cdec.colorado.gov is refused, so the figure stays supplied. Still open: blocked on a refused host.

Hard-coded numbers fix round (audit H-18): the figure is no longer entered on each person. cccap_entry_income_limit_pct_fpl (CO-870) is derived from cccap_entry_income_limit_pct_fpl_table (CO-3004), a table keyed by county with no version loaded (the engine now holds a figure awaiting publication as an empty list of versions), never below the 185% floor (cccap_entry_income_limit_min_pct_fpl, CO-3005). Household cases CO-01, CO-02 and CO-08 state the county row they assume (1.85) under parameters:. Still open: the county figures themselves.

Class: blocked on a named document
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: Each county's entry income limit is loaded once it is published; until then the stored households state the 185 percent floor for their county.
Blocked on: the Colorado Department of Early Childhood's county entry-eligibility levels set from the self-sufficiency standard under 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.H.1.a (cdec.colorado.gov refused)

Checked 2026-11-22: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.H.1.a (D-87, S780) has the Department set entry eligibility "at a level based on the self-sufficiency standard, not to be set below" 185 percent; the rule states no county figure.

### OQ-122. Resolved: LEAP income limit (60% state median income) is the Department's published season table

Body: 9 CCR 2503-7-3.752.22.D (S491) sets the income test at "sixty percent (60%) of the state median income level released by the U.S. Department of Health and Human Services," referencing 45 C.F.R. 96.85 and a LIHEAP Information Memorandum that state the actual SMI dollar figures by household size; neither document is in this volume's library. leap_income_limit_monthly (CO-875 - corrected citation; a prior draft of this open question and CO-1003's precision named CO-877, which is has_primary_heating_source) is modeled as a parameter table keyed by household size (docs/reviews/cccap-leap-phase-6.md, C-13), with leap_income_limit_monthly_each_additional (CO-1808) for sizes above 8, per the review's fix: the values are backed out of the one state median income figure this library's fetched text does state - CCCAP's 85% SMI column, 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.H.2 (S485) - as value = 85%-SMI-figure / 0.85 x 0.60. This is a different program's SMI table (CCCAP's own Colorado figures, not LEAP's federal FY2023 HHS table 3.752.22.D actually cites), so it remains illustrative and this open question stays open pending a fetch of 45 C.F.R. 96.85 or the LIHEAP Information Memorandum. Items: CO-875, CO-344, CO-1003.

Phase 8: the version of 9 CCR 2503-7 effective 2025-11-01 (D-86, S773) still sets the limit at 60% of the HHS state median income "for federal fiscal year 2023" and now incorporates 45 CFR 96.85 (2025) and LIHEAP Information Memorandum 2025-02 by reference, giving the memorandum's acf.gov address. The memorandum could not be fetched this session (acf.gov answered with an empty 202 challenge response; the Firecrawl fetch service was out of credits), and 45 CFR 96.85 states only the method (a four-person estimate adjusted by household-size percentages), not Colorado's figure, so the illustrative table stays.

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep): Resolved. The Department's LEAP page (D-144, retrieved 2026-11-21 from cdhs.nxt-dev.colorado.gov, CDHS's next-generation site) states "LEAP Income Limits for 2026-2027 Season", the maximum gross monthly income for household sizes 1 through 15 ($3,774 for one to $11,541 for fifteen), for the season November 1, 2026 through April 30, 2027, at 60% of the state median income HHS released in April 2026 (S1406). leap_income_limit_monthly (CO-875) now carries that table as its only version (from 2026-11-01 to 2027-05-01) in place of the illustrative figures backed out of CCCAP's SMI column; leap_income_within_limit (CO-1003) looks it up by household size without an each-additional figure, because 3.751.2.C (S1407) allows at most fifteen members, and leap_income_limit_monthly_each_additional (CO-1808) is retired. The rule text still names the FFY 2023 table and IM 2025-02 (S773) while the Department applies the April 2026 release; the ledger follows the published season figures. Hand check of the target household: in November (CO-02) the family household of three has $3,560 against $6,097 and the grandmother alone $2,030 against $3,774, so the LEAP results are unchanged; in October (CO-01) no season table applies, and LEAP is ineligible for the month regardless (outside the November-April period).

CDHS stakeholder review fix round (2026-11-21) (S-7): https://cdhs.colorado.gov/leap was tried once and refused (HTTP 403), so the table stays sourced to D-144 on cdhs.nxt-dev.colorado.gov. The LEAP rule amendment effective November 1, 2026 (D-173, S1818-S1819), adopted September 4, 2026 and fetched from the Secretary of State's eDocket, now names the same standard: 60% of the state median income HHS released for federal fiscal year 2026 in the LIHEAP memorandum of April 24, 2026; that memorandum (acf.gov) did not answer this session. The figures agree with CCCAP's 85% state median income column (D-87) scaled by 60/85. See OQ-532.

### OQ-123. Resolved: the LEAP benefit formula (3.758.47) is modeled with supplied program-year figures

Body: The version of 9 CCR 2503-7 effective 2025-11-01 (D-86, S770-S772) states the Heating Fuel Assistance benefit method: estimated home heating costs for November-April (Step A), a statewide adjustment percentage set each program year (B), the electric-heat portion by dwelling type (C), a household income contribution of 0/10/20/30% by poverty band (D), division among households sharing the living arrangement (E), a $180 seasonal subsidized-housing allowance (F), and the Department's program-year minimum and maximum (G). leap_benefit_amount (CO-1281) computes it from leap_adjusted_heating_costs (CO-1280), leap_income_contribution_rate (CO-1279) and leap_household_income_pct_fpl (CO-1278), with the program-year figures the rule does not state (statewide adjustment, minimum, maximum) and the household's heating facts supplied (CO-1271-CO-1277) and the Step F allowance a parameter (CO-1282). The reading of how Steps B, D and F combine is OQ-253. Items: CO-1271, CO-1272, CO-1273, CO-1274, CO-1275, CO-1276, CO-1277, CO-1278, CO-1279, CO-1280, CO-1281, CO-1282.

Phase-10 open-question sweep: the income contribution is now taken in the application month (OQ-137), and the LEAP income limit is the Department's season table (OQ-122).

CDHS stakeholder review fix round (2026-11-21) (C-6): the program-year figures (statewide adjustment rate, minimum and maximum benefit) are the same for every household and belong in dated parameters, but they are not published on an allowed host, and a parameter must state a value; they stay supplied facts, and CO-01/CO-02 no longer state the illustrative 0.2, $100 and $1,000, so the November LEAP benefit is unknown (OQ-532). The CDHS reviewer accepts the additive Step B/D reading provisionally (OQ-253).

### OQ-124. Resolved: CCCAP eligible activity: no separate flag for training/education/post-secondary enrollment

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1-3.103.OO and 3.111.G (S482, not separately excerpted for G) list job search, employment, self-employment, training, basic education, and post-secondary education as eligible activities, and a teen parent's own secondary-school attendance is itself an eligible activity (3.103.ZZZZ, S481). This volume has only snap_is_enrolled_half_time_in_school_or_training (secondary school) and hours_worked among the shared supplied facts; cccap_caretaker_in_eligible_activity (CO-984) treats hours_worked > 0 as satisfying employment/self-employment, the new supplied fact cccap_is_in_job_search_activity (CO-871) as satisfying job search, and snap_is_enrolled_half_time_in_school_or_training as satisfying both a teen parent's school attendance and, as an assumption, an adult caretaker's training/education/post-secondary enrollment (no separate higher-education flag is read here, unlike snap_is_enrolled_half_time_in_school_or_training vs is_enrolled_higher_education_half_time elsewhere in this volume). It does not verify the federal-minimum-wage-per-hour test in 3.111.G.1.b/2.d. Items: CO-984.

Resolved (vocabulary fix round, R-2): cccap_caretaker_in_eligible_activity (CO-984) reads cccap_is_in_education_or_training_activity (CO-1722), stated for training, basic education and post-secondary education (8 CCR 1403-1 3.103.OO, S482) and a teen parent's school attendance; a caretaker in college is in an eligible activity. The minimum-wage-per-hour test remains unmodeled (OQ-162).

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): (K-7) the version of 8 CCR 1403-1 effective 2026-08-14 (D-87) adds, from July 1, 2026, an adult caretaker enrolled in a licensed, BHA-endorsed substance use disorder treatment program at an intensive outpatient level or higher (3.111.G.4.a.3, S1015), now a route in cccap_caretaker_in_eligible_activity (CO-984) through is_in_sud_treatment_iop_or_higher (CO-1516) and cccap_sud_treatment_activity_start_date (CO-1517); its one-year-per-instance limit is not modeled (OQ-162). The current teen-parent definition (3.103.EEEEE, S1016) makes a person under 21 in workforce training or post-secondary education an adult caretaker; that is the one place the volume now separates post-secondary and workforce training from secondary school (cccap_is_in_workforce_training_or_post_secondary_education, CO-1518, read by CO-981 and CO-992). snap_is_enrolled_half_time_in_school_or_training still stands in for training and education as an activity in CO-984. Items: CO-981, CO-984, CO-992, CO-1516, CO-1517, CO-1518.

Phase-10 open-question sweep: the minimum-wage-per-hour test is now modeled (OQ-162 K-6), so nothing of this question remains.

### OQ-125. Resolved: CCCAP household composition: absence, incapacity and the student prong

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1-3.111.D describes several distinct eligible-household shapes (one caretaker; two caretakers where one is involuntarily absent, voluntarily absent, or incapacitated; unrelated caretakers needing court/parent verification) and excludes an incapacitated single caretaker not in an eligible activity (3.111.E). cccap_household_members (CO-982) instead closes over parent/child, caretaker/dependent, and spouse edges unconditionally, the same convention snap_household_members and ma_magi_household_members use, without separately modeling absence or incapacity. Items: CO-982.

Related (C-15, minor): cccap_is_household_child (CO-990) also drops part of 3.103.GGG's student prong. The text is "all children under nineteen (19) years of age who are still in high school **and the responsibility of the adult caretaker(s)**" (S496); CO-990 tests age and snap_is_enrolled_half_time_in_school_or_training but never tests whose responsibility the child is, and snap_is_enrolled_half_time_in_school_or_training does not itself distinguish high school from any other schooling (CO-990's own precision already flags this as an assumption rather than a precision note). An 18-year-old at community college, or an 18-year-old in high school who is not the caretaker's responsibility, is a household member today and would not be one under a literal reading of GGG. If a `is_claimed_as_responsibility_of_caretaker` fact is out of reach, the existing `snap_is_under_parental_control_of_household_member` fact (used by the SNAP chapter for an analogous purpose) may serve in a future batch. Items: CO-990.

Resolution (Phase-10 open-question sweep): Resolved. 3.111.D.1 (S1409) has three shapes the volume now distinguishes: a caretaker voluntarily out of the home is still a caretaker and must be in an activity (D.1.a, c.2; no fact needed); an involuntarily absent caretaker leaves a one-caretaker household (D.1.b; cccap_caretaker_is_involuntarily_absent, CO-898, renamed from is_absent_from_home, OQ-136); and an incapacitated caretaker is excused when the other is active (D.1.c.3; cccap_caretaker_is_incapacitated, CO-1744), while 3.111.E denies an incapacitated single caretaker (the count condition in CO-991). The C-15 student prong reads cccap_is_attending_high_school (vocabulary round), and "the responsibility of the adult caretaker(s)" is the stated parent or caretaker relationship cccap_household_members (CO-982) closes over. An unrelated caretaker's verification (D.5) is a document, OQ-378.

### OQ-126. Resolved: § 8.100.7.M.1.a's CSRA formula is more generous than 42 U.S.C. 1396r-5(f)(2)(A), and it is unclear whether Colorado meant to adopt the federal shape

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.7.M.1.a (S355) states the CSRA as "the total resources of the couple but no more than the current maximum allowance" - no spousal-share (half-of-total) term and no CPI-indexed minimum-standard floor. 42 U.S.C. 1396r-5(f)(2)(A) (S371), which the federal Medicaid statute requires every state to implement, is instead the greatest of an indexed minimum ($12,000 base) or the lesser of half the couple's total resources and an indexed maximum ($60,000 base). As fetched, § 8.100.7.M.1.a is strictly more generous to the couple whenever total resources sit between the federal minimum and maximum (the couple keeps 100% of resources up to the state maximum, rather than only half). ma_csra (CO-646) follows the fetched Colorado text per the ledger's rule that the fetched state text controls, but this may be a drafting gap (Colorado is required to implement 1396r-5) rather than an intended state policy choice above the federal floor. A future revision should ask HCPF whether § 8.100.7.M.1.a is read together with the federal statute in practice (i.e., CBMS actually applies the federal spousal-share/minimum-standard shape) or whether Colorado's maximum-allowance-only formula genuinely governs. Items: CO-646.

Phase 7 sweep: the current Secretary of State text of § 8.100.7.M.1.a (D-65, S652) is unchanged - 'The total resources of the couple but no more than the current maximum allowance' - so the published rule still does not say whether Colorado applies the federal spousal-share/minimum shape; ma_csra_minimum (CO-827) now carries the 2026 CMS minimum for a future item.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): 42 U.S.C. 1396r-5(f)(2)(A)(i) (S1222) lets the State plan set the community spouse's standard anywhere up to the indexed maximum; a State that sets it at the maximum gives the community spouse the lesser of the couple's resources and the maximum, which is § 8.100.7.M.1.a. Colorado's text is a permitted federal shape, not a drafting gap. The sweep also found that § 8.100.7.M.1 (S1220) makes the CSRA the largest of (a), (b) the increased CSRA of § 8.100.7.S (S1221) and (c) a court-ordered amount, and ma_csra (CO-646) computed (a) only. New facts ma_csra_annuity_lowest_estimate (CO-1948), ma_institutionalized_spouse_refuses_income_allowance (CO-1949), ma_court_ordered_transfer_to_community_spouse (CO-1950) and rule ma_csra_increased (CO-1977: when the community spouse's income plus the income allowance the institutionalized spouse's income can fund is below the MMMNA, the maximum plus the lowest annuity estimate); CO-646 takes the largest of the three. No household case has a shortfall or a court order, so no expectation changed. ma_csra_minimum (CO-827) still has no Colorado consumer.

Coverage round (2026-11-22, docs/reviews/coverage-medicaid-nonmagi.md NM-18): § 8.100.7.L and M.1 (S3419, S1220) assess the couple's resources "at the time of initial Medical Assistance application" and base the CSRA on them "as of the time of Medical Assistance application"; 42 U.S.C. 1396r-5(c)(1)(A) computes the spousal share as of the beginning of the first continuous period of institutionalization. Under the formula this question settled (the couple's resources up to the maximum), the spousal share plays no part, and 1396r-5(c)(2) attributes resources at the time of application, so the application date governs. ma_spousal_resources_at_snapshot (CO-888) now says so.


### OQ-127. Resolved: § 8.7100.C.3's "spousal impoverishment rules do not apply to people in the Medicaid Buy-In program" is not modeled as a bypass of ma_ltc_couple

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.7100.C.3 (S376) states that spousal impoverishment rules "do not apply to people in the Medicaid Buy-In program," and a person reaching HCBS through the Working Adults with Disabilities Buy-In route ma_hcbs_waiver_eligible (CO-653, fix-round C-4) now models does not have a CSRA, MIA, or spouse-based home-equity exception computed under this text. ma_ltc_couple (CO-643) is not conditioned on the WAwD route, so a Buy-In HCBS participant with a community spouse still receives a CSRA/MIA/family allowance the text says should not apply to them. Modeling the bypass would require ma_ltc_couple to read ma_hcbs_waiver_eligible or ma_wawd_eligible, which in turn depend (through ma_ltc_financially_eligible) on ma_home_equity_disqualifies, itself downstream of ma_ltc_couple; the phase-4 fix round did not attempt this reordering. A future revision should add the bypass, most likely by having ma_ltc_couple test something like "eligible for HCBS other than solely through the WAwD Buy-In route" rather than the raw ma_wawd_eligible/target-group facts, to avoid the dependency cycle. Items: CO-643, CO-653.

Phase 7 sweep: the current § 8.7100.C.3 (D-75) still states 'Spousal impoverishment rules do not apply to people in the Medicaid Buy-In program'; the rule is clear and the gap is the dependency-order problem described above.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the rule is clear (triage: NEEDS FACT) but this sweep did not model it; wiring § 8.7100.C.3 makes ma_ltc_couple (CO-643) depend on the Buy-In route into HCBS while waiver eligibility depends on the couple test; it needs the route decided before the couple test (a fact or a restructured dependency).

Phase-10 needs-fact sweep (2026-11-21): resolved. New fact ma_is_in_buy_in_program (CO-2210): the person applies for or receives Medical Assistance through a Medicaid Buy-In program (§§ 8.100.6.P-Q). ma_ltc_couple (CO-643) is false for such a person, as § 8.7100.C.3 says (S1509), so no CSRA, MIA or family allowance is computed; the route is supplied, which avoids the dependency cycle. New test CO-643-T4. CO-01, CO-02 and CO-06 p1 state it false; no expectation changed.

### OQ-132. Resolved: LEAP economic unit joins unrelated persons through a shares_heat_with relationship

Body: 3.751.1's "Household" definition (S489) joins "any individual or group of individuals" living together as one economic unit sharing heat in common, not only relatives, and the review's own proposed fix (docs/reviews/cccap-leap-phase-6.md, C-1) would add `shares_heating_fuel_with` as its own relationship role so `reachable` (src/engine/evaluate.ts) could cross it directly, joining a roommate or unrelated adult who shares heat with no family-line edge at all. This volume's engine only traverses roles registered with an inverse in `INVERSE_ROLE` (src/engine/evaluate.ts), which a codex-only change cannot add, and registering a new symmetric role is an engine change outside this fix round's scope (`docs/conventions.md` and `docs/reviews`, no `src/engine`). `shares_heating_fuel_with` (CO-895) is instead modeled as a per-person yes/no fact read as an additional join condition on the existing spouse/parent/child edges `leap_household_economic_unit`-adjacent items (CO-1000, CO-1009) already traverse, which lets a household affirmatively state that an over-age child (or a parent who has not been separately excluded) shares heat with the family line they are already related to, but still cannot join two people with no stated relationship edge between them at all (for example, an unrelated adult roommate with no parent/child/spouse edge, or two family lines under one roof with no relationship between them). A future engine change registering a symmetric relationship role for LEAP's economic unit would close this gap. Items: CO-895, CO-1000, CO-1009.

Phase 8: the current 9 CCR 2503-7 (D-86) does not change the household definition; the question is about the engine's relationship roles, not the text, and stays open. The Step E shared-living division (3.758.47, S772) is now modeled through a supplied count of households sharing the dwelling (CO-1274), which closes the benefit-side half of OQ-163's K-7.

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep): Resolved. The engine now registers a symmetric relationship role, shares_heat_with (src/engine/evaluate.ts INVERSE_ROLE, src/engine/types.ts RELS), and leap_household_members (CO-1000) and leap_household_members_for_income (CO-1009) cross it unconditionally (subject to the institutional and verification rules of OQ-358 and OQ-370), so any two persons who customarily buy primary heating fuel in common or pay heat jointly in rent are one economic unit (3.751.1 "Household", S1400), related or not. shares_heating_fuel_with (CO-895) remains the per-person override on family edges. New tests CO-1000-T13, T14, CO-1009-T6. No case states the new edge, so no expectation changed; CO-08's undocumented relative stays his own unit because the case does not say he buys heat in common.

### OQ-133. Resolved: CCCAP entry/exit income limit distinction (3.111.H.1.a vs H.1.b) wired into cccap_eligibility_status

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.H.1.a sets the entry income limit the county's CCCAP plan applies at application, and H.1.b sets a separate, higher 85% SMI exit limit that keeps an already-eligible household eligible until its income rises past that ceiling (S485). This fix round adds `cccap_smi_income_limit_table` (CO-1804), `cccap_smi_income_limit_each_additional` (CO-1805) and `cccap_income_within_exit_limit` (CO-993) so H.1.b's figures and test are represented, and folds the 85% SMI table in as a hard cap on the entry limit itself (CO-986, `[min, entry limit, 85% SMI lookup]`, per the review's C-5 fix). It does not wire `cccap_income_within_exit_limit` into `cccap_eligibility_status` (CO-989) as an alternate test for a continuing (redetermination) household, because doing so needs an `is_cccap_redetermination` (or equivalent application/redetermination) fact this batch's interface does not have, and the review's own C-5 fix explicitly permits deferring that distinction to an OQ rather than leaving H.1.b unrepresented. A future batch should add that supplied fact and make CO-989 read the exit limit instead of the entry limit for a household already receiving assistance. Items: CO-989, CO-993.

Resolution (Phase-10 open-question sweep): Resolved. The new supplied cccap_is_receiving_assistance (CO-2103) says whether the household already receives Low-Income Child Care, and cccap_meets_income_guidelines (CO-2104) reads the exit limit (CO-993, 85% SMI, H.1.b) for such a household and the entry limit (CO-986, H.1.a) otherwise (S1413). cccap_low_income_child_care_eligible (CO-987) and cccap_eligibility_status (CO-989) read it. Every CCCAP case states the fact (false: all are applications); no expectation changed.

### OQ-134. Resolved: Colorado Works Child Care referral fact is stated on every case; no default

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.116.A (S487) conditions Colorado Works Child Care on a county Colorado Works worker's referral for child care, in addition to work-eligibility. The new supplied fact `is_referred_for_colorado_works_child_care` (CO-896) carries this condition in `cccap_colorado_works_child_care_eligible` (CO-988), but is read with `[otherwise, ..., true]` (defaults to referred/true when the case does not state it), rather than leaving it unknown, because most existing CCCAP cases in `volumes/co/tests/cases.yaml` (outside CO-01/CO-02, which this fix round's edit scope does not reach) do not state this fact, and an unknown value would silently turn every one of their `cccap_eligibility_status` expectations unknown wherever a Colorado Works grant or eligibility already exists. The default assumes a county worker's referral accompanies an existing Colorado Works determination unless a case affirmatively says otherwise. A future batch that can edit every case's facts should replace the default with an explicit fact on every person and remove the `otherwise`. Items: CO-896, CO-988.

Resolution (Phase-10 open-question sweep): Resolved. cccap_colorado_works_child_care_eligible (CO-988) reads is_referred_for_colorado_works_child_care (CO-896) without a default; the cases that reach the Colorado Works Child Care branch (CO-01, CO-02, CO-91, CO-92) already stated it, and no expectation changed.

Coverage pass 5, X5-3 (docs/reviews/coverage5-care-energy.md): seven verification readers (CO-1440 to CO-1445, CO-2873) still read the referral of any household member as not referred when not stated, which contradicted the resolution. They now read the Colorado Works Child Care household as CO-988 does (cccap_colorado_works_child_care_eligible), with no default: 3.116.C-F (S3637) make the county Colorado Works program the source of the residency, citizenship, age and identity verification and the earned income for a Colorado Works Child Care case. No reader of the referral keeps a default. Items: CO-896, CO-988, CO-1440, CO-1441, CO-1442, CO-1443, CO-1444, CO-1445, CO-2873.

### OQ-135. Resolved: adult caretaker's "financially contributing to the welfare of the child" (3.103.C) is stated; no default

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.103.C (S481) requires an adult caretaker to be "financially contributing to the welfare of the child," in addition to the parent/guardian/loco-parentis relationship and physical custody `cccap_is_adult_caretaker` (CO-992) otherwise tests. The new supplied fact `cccap_is_financially_contributing_to_child` (CO-897) carries this condition, read with `[otherwise, ..., true]` for the same reason as OQ-134: most existing CCCAP cases do not state it, and defaulting to unknown would turn `cccap_household_caretakers_in_eligible_activity` (CO-991) and every downstream CCCAP outcome unknown for cases outside this fix round's edit scope. The default assumes a household member who is already a stated parent/caretaker of an eligible child is financially contributing unless a case affirmatively says otherwise. A future batch should add the fact explicitly across the test suite and remove the default. Items: CO-897, CO-992.

Resolution (Phase-10 open-question sweep): Resolved. cccap_is_adult_caretaker (CO-992) and cccap_is_teen_parent (CO-981) read cccap_is_financially_contributing_to_child (CO-897) without a default. CO-01/CO-02 (p3, p4) and CO-08 (p1, p2) now state it true; no expectation changed.

### OQ-136. Resolved: CCCAP involuntary and voluntary absence distinguished

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.D.1.b (involuntary absence) and c.2 (voluntary absence, with both caretakers still required to be in an eligible activity) are two different shapes with different consequences (b reduces the household to one caretaker; c.2 keeps both caretakers' activity requirement in force), but the new supplied fact `is_absent_from_home` (CO-898) does not distinguish them, and `cccap_household_caretakers_in_eligible_activity` (CO-991) applies it the same way 3.111.D.1.c.3's incapacity exemption is applied (exempting the absent caretaker from the activity test when there is a second caretaker present), which is c.2's shape, not b's. It is also read with `[otherwise, ..., false]` for the same case-coverage reason as OQ-134/OQ-135. No verification standard for either shape is modeled (3.111.D.1.b does not state one; c.2 and c.3 both require documentation this batch has no fact for). A future batch should split the fact into involuntary/voluntary variants and model each shape's distinct consequence, and add a verification fact. Items: CO-898, CO-991.

Resolution (Phase-10 open-question sweep): Resolved. 3.111.D.1 (S1409): only involuntary absence (b) reduces the household to one caretaker; a voluntarily absent caretaker stays a caretaker who must be in an activity (a, c.2). The fact is renamed cccap_caretaker_is_involuntarily_absent (CO-898, same ID) with that meaning, and cccap_household_caretakers_in_eligible_activity (CO-991) exempts only that caretaker. If not stated it reads false, which keeps the activity requirement (not an eligibility-granting default). New test CO-991-T8.

### OQ-137. Resolved: LEAP eligibility determined at the application date (point in time)

Body: 3.751.1's "Point in Time" definition ("eligibility is determined by accounting for the circumstances of the household on the date of the application, regardless of any changes thereafter") means LEAP is decided once per program year from the application-date facts, not re-derived for every month of the eligibility period. `leap_is_eligible` (CO-1007) remains `scope: person-month` and is re-evaluated for each month, which this fix round's C-3 addition (`leap_month_in_eligibility_period`, CO-1008) only gates to the November-April window rather than restructuring; a household whose circumstances change between November and April would see `leap_is_eligible` change month to month under the current scope, contrary to the "regardless of any changes thereafter" text. The review (docs/reviews/cccap-leap-phase-6.md, C-3) recorded this as an item to raise rather than fix in this round; this open question carries that forward. Items: CO-1007.

Phase 8: the current 9 CCR 2503-7 (D-86, S776) keeps the "Point in Time" definition ("eligibility is determined by accounting for the circumstances of the household on the date of the application, regardless of any changes thereafter"); the new benefit outcome leap_benefit_amount (CO-1281) is likewise a heating-season figure evaluated per month and inherits this question.

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep): Resolved. The new supplied leap_application_date (CO-2122) carries the date "Point in Time" (3.751.1, S776) refers to. leap_is_eligible (CO-1007) evaluates the residency, countable-member, income, vulnerability and heating-source tests in the month of that date (the engine's `at`), and holds for a month that is in the eligibility period, in the same program year as the application and not before it; leap_benefit_amount (CO-1281) takes the household income contribution in the application month. CO-02 and CO-08 state the application date as their determination month, so no expectation changed.

### OQ-138. Statute permits an SSDI-determined route into AND-SO; the implementing regulation excludes an SSDI recipient

Body: 26-2-111(4)(a) (S442) states two independent routes to AND-SO's total-disability test: "a total disability ... that has lasted or can be expected to last for a period of six months or more **or** he or she is determined to be disabled and eligible for social security disability insurance benefits" - the second disjunct offers SSDI-determined status as its own qualifying route. 3.540 (S458), the implementing regulation, instead describes AND-SO as interim assistance for clients "who are disabled or blind **but have not been approved** for Supplemental Security Income (SSI) or Social Security Disability Insurance (SSDI)," and 3.520.75.B directs an AND-SO client to apply for SSDI where a work history exists - consistent with AND-SO being the bridge program for someone *awaiting* a determination, not one who has already received it. af_category (CO-953)'s and_so branch follows the regulation's direction of travel (3.540, 3.520.75.B) and excludes a current SSDI recipient (`[not, snap_receives_disability_or_blindness_payment]`, `[not, receives_ssi]`), reading the statute's SSDI-determined disjunct as describing eligibility for AND-SO's own six-month total-disability finding (satisfied through an SSDI determination) rather than as a route that survives once SSDI is actually being paid. This is the reading the phase-5 review's C-7 finding adopts; the statute read alone would go the other way. Items: CO-953.

Checked 2026-11-21: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.540 (D-80:4197-4200, version effective 2026-03-02) still describes AND-SO for clients who "have not been approved for Supplemental Security Income (SSI) or Social Security Disability Insurance (SSDI)", and C.R.S. 26-2-111(4)(a) (D-59) names SSDI eligibility as a route to the disability test. The texts conflict and no published rule settles it.

Class: needs Legal
Owner: Legal, Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: A person already approved for Social Security disability benefits is not eligible for interim Aid to the Needy Disabled.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.540 (D-80, version effective 2026-03-02) describes AND-SO for clients who "have not been approved for Supplemental Security Income (SSI) or Social Security Disability Insurance (SSDI)", and C.R.S. 26-2-111(4)(a) (D-59) names being "determined to be disabled and eligible for social security disability insurance benefits" as a route to the disability test. The readings: (a) the statute governs and an SSDI recipient may qualify; (b) the rule's bridge-program reading excludes one, the reading in force. No published rule reconciles them.

### OQ-139. Resolved: the current rule has no Aid to the Blind program, so the AB good-cause exception has no rule to implement it

Body: 26-2-111(5)(a)(II) (S443) requires an AB applicant to have "applied for supplemental security income benefits and complied with any recommendations for referrals made by the county department except for good cause shown." has_applied_for_ssi (CO-848) and af_ab_eligible (CO-945) model the application requirement itself but not the "except for good cause shown" exception - no supplied fact states whether a county has excused noncompliance for good cause, so a client who has not applied for SSI is always ineligible under this item even where a county would excuse it. Items: CO-848, CO-945.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): answered with OQ-112: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.500 (S1017) has no Aid to the Blind program, so there is no Colorado rule stating how a county finds good cause under 26-2-111(5)(a)(II); a blind client 0-59 is AND-SO, whose SSI application duty (3.520.71.B, S710) is OQ-151's. The statute-only ab branch keeps the application requirement without the exception, as before. Items: CO-848, CO-945.

### OQ-140. Resolved: the Colorado Works budgetary unit and its income are modeled

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.2.D.1 (S527) requires the budgetary unit to include, beyond the assistance unit itself, the spouse of a parent or non-parent caretaker who requested assistance (b), the unborn child of a pregnant parent (c), the non-recipient parent(s) of a minor parent (d), and the sponsor of a non-citizen (e); 3.606.1.J.1-2 (S528) then computes the income test over the budgetary unit, not the assistance unit alone. cw_countable_income (CO-910) sums cw_assistance_unit_members plus, through cw_deemed_parent_income_of_minor_parent (CO-909), item (d) - the one budgetary-unit member category this volume already reaches by a different route - but does not reach (b), (c), or (e); no cw_budgetary_unit_members item exists. Fix round: CO-907's per-person-not-per-unit reading of the $90 disregard is confirmed by S528's "each employed member of the budgetary unit," cited on CO-907, but the wider budgetary-unit membership itself is not modeled this round. Items: CO-910.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): current citation. 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.2.D.1 (`documents/D-81.md:1299-1315`) is unchanged in the current text; open only because unmodeled.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): 3.604.2.D.1 (D-81:1299-1315) is clear; a cw_budgetary_unit_members item needs the requesting caretaker's spouse, the unborn child and the sponsor as members, and the sponsor's income as a fact (OQ-103). Not modeled in this sweep.

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep, supplied-facts round): 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.2.D (S1626) and 3.606.1.J (S528). cw_budgetary_unit_members (CO-2352) is the assistance unit plus the spouse of a parent or non-parent caretaker in it, whether or not the spouse asked for assistance (D.1.b), less SSI and Title IV recipients (D.2.b). cw_budgetary_member_deemed_income (CO-2353) counts a stepparent's income under 3.605.1.A.4.a (S1307: gross earnings less $90, plus unearned income, less court-ordered support and payments to tax dependents outside the home, cw_support_paid_to_tax_dependents_outside_home, CO-2354, less the stepparent's needs) and a non-parent caretaker's spouse's under J; cw_countable_income (CO-910) and cw_payment_countable_income (CO-923) add it. The same A.4.a deductions now apply in cw_deemed_parent_income_of_minor_parent (CO-909, new test CO-909-T7). The non-recipient parents of a minor parent (D.1.d) stay with CO-909 and the sponsor (D.1.e) with cw_sponsor_deemed_income (CO-2365, OQ-103); the unborn child (D.1.c) has no income, and the standards count the assistance unit (3.606.1.F, "based upon those included in the assistance unit"). In CO-01 and CO-02 the minor parent's mother states no support paid, so the deemed $2,037 is unchanged; no household case has a stepparent; no expectation changed.

### OQ-141. Resolved: Colorado Works sanctions reduce the unit's grant by $1, 25%, 25%, then close the case

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.608.4.C in the version effective 2025-07-01 (D-81, S760): "The reduction for the first level sanction shall be one dollar ($1.00), second and third instance of sanction shall be twenty five percent (25%) of an assistance unit's grant payment. The sanction shall be in effect for one month for each level sanction ... The reduction for a fourth instance of sanction is 100% and shall result in case closure"; 3.608.4.F has a case serve one level a month, the higher where several members are sanctioned. Modeled: work_sanction_level_served (CO-1261, supplied, the level the county imposed), cw_unit_sanction_level (CO-1264, the highest level among unit members), cw_sanction_reduction (CO-1265, with CO-1262 and CO-1263), cw_grant_before_sanction (CO-1260, the former CO-914 computation), cw_grant_amount (CO-914, now less the reduction with the cents dropped), and a sanction_closure status in cw_eligibility_status (CO-921) for a fourth-level sanction. Whether a sanction is imposed (non-participation without good cause, the re-engagement process, progression to the next level) is county process and enters through the supplied level. Items: CO-914, CO-921, CO-1260, CO-1261, CO-1262, CO-1263, CO-1264, CO-1265.

### OQ-142. Resolved: the 20% statewide cap on time-limit extensions is a condition on the county's grant, which the extension facts record

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.6.G (S526): "Up to twenty percent (20%) of the Statewide caseload receiving Colorado Works may be granted an extension beyond the sixty (60) month time limit due to hardship or domestic violence." cw_unit_time_limit_extended (CO-925) and cw_time_limit_reached (CO-918) read a per-person/per-unit extension fact (has_domestic_violence_extension_approved, has_hardship_extension_approved) as county-granted, which already presupposes the county's own accounting for the statewide 20% ceiling; this volume has no statewide-caseload aggregate to test against and no per-person rule could express one. An approver reading cw_unit_time_limit_extended as "this unit's extension" should not read it as "an extension is unconditionally available" - the statewide cap is a real constraint on whether the county grants one in the first place, upstream of this item. Items: CO-925.

Resolution (open-question sweep, 2026-11-21): the current 3.606.6.G (D-81, S1313) settles where the cap operates: "The State Department has the sole responsibility of monitoring this federal limit. County departments are not restricted to a certain number of extensions unless the Statewide limit is reached." The cap is a State-level limit on whether counties may grant extensions, not an individual eligibility test; the existing supplied facts has_domestic_violence_extension_approved (CO-846) and has_hardship_extension_approved (CO-847) record the county's grant, and their precision now says a recorded grant is one made within the statewide limit. No new fact; no expectation changed. Items: CO-846, CO-847, CO-925.

### OQ-143. Resolved: a disqualified or ineligible required member is left out of the unit size, counted at gross income and has the month counted

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.2.M (S523) states three consequences for a disqualified or ineligible required member: removal from the assistance unit "for the purposes of determining the assistance unit size," counting that person's income "without applying income disregards," and counting their month toward their own sixty-month maximum "when a grant payment is received for others in the assistance unit." Fix round (C-3(c)): cw_is_disqualified_member (CO-928) is added and wired into cw_unit_time_limit_extended (CO-925)'s bar on granting an extension to a unit containing a disqualified member (3.604.2.M's own governing paragraph for 3.606.6.C). The other three consequences - removing the disqualified member from cw_assistance_unit_members (CO-900) for unit-size purposes while still counting their undisregarded income in cw_countable_income (CO-910) and cw_payment_countable_income (CO-923), and counting their month under cw_months_of_assistance_used (CO-915) regardless of head-of-household/spouse status - are not modeled this round because they require reworking CO-900's unit-membership closure (which today either includes or excludes a person wholesale, with no "in the unit for income purposes but not for size" state) and CO-907/CO-909's disregard application. Items: CO-900, CO-907, CO-910, CO-915, CO-923, CO-928.

Phase 8: the current 9 CCR 2503-6 (D-81, effective 2025-07-01) does not change 3.604.2.M; the sanction rules of 3.608.4 (S760) concern Workforce Development non-participation, not the disqualified-member consequences this question records, so it stays open.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): the current 3.604.2.L-M (S1004, D-81; amended effective 2023-06-01, S1019) no longer lists a drug-related felony conviction at L or M - its categories are the two-state IPV, IPV/fraud, fleeing felons, failure to apply for an SSN unless a federally allowable reason under 20 CFR 422.107 exists (M.4), and non-qualified non-citizens - so cw_is_disqualified_member (CO-928) no longer reads snap_is_disqualified_drug_felony (S-2). M.4 has no supplied fact; the three penalties above remain unmodeled. Items: CO-928.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): 3.604.2.M (S1004) is clear; the three consequences need a unit-membership state "counted for income without disregards but not for size" and an SSN-application fact for M.4. Not modeled in this sweep.

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep, supplied-facts round): 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.2.M (S1004). cw_is_disqualified_member (CO-928) now also reads M.4 (the new supplied cw_has_failed_to_apply_for_ssn, CO-2348) and M.6 and L.3 (a member who does not meet meets_public_assistance_citizenship_requirement). The new cw_assistance_unit_members_for_size (CO-2351) leaves such a member out, and cw_unit_size (CO-903), cw_unit_caretaker_count (CO-926) and cw_unit_child_count (CO-927) count it; cw_countable_income (CO-910) and cw_payment_countable_income (CO-923) count the member's gross earnings without disregards (M; 3.606.2, S1628; the A.6 wording is OQ-490); cw_months_of_assistance_used (CO-915) counts the member's own months even when the member is not the head or the head's spouse. New tests CO-928-T6 and T7, CO-2351-T1 and T2, CO-910-T3, CO-915-T6. No household case has a disqualified member in a unit with a child (CO-01's undocumented mother is outside her minor daughter's unit by 3.604.2.C.1.b); no expectation changed.

### OQ-144. CBwD premium schedule's household-size keying and "individuals" versus "households" wording

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.6.Q.1.g (S318) states the Children with Disabilities Buy-In premium schedule is "on a sliding scale based on household size and income," but ma_cbwd_premium (CO-621) keys only on ma_magi_household_income_pct_fpl, which is itself already a household-size-adjusted percentage of FPL (CO-504 divides by the size-adjusted poverty guideline) rather than a separate household-size axis on the band table. S318's own text for the third and fourth bands also says "individuals" where the first two say "households," a discrepancy quoted as fetched rather than silently harmonized - the phase-4 review's C-16 finding. Items: CO-621.

Phase 7 sweep: the current § 8.100.6.Q.1.g.iv (D-65) keeps the same wording - 'households' in the first two bands and 'individuals' in the third and fourth - so no published text settles it.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): Checked 2026-11-21: § 8.100.6.Q.1.g (S318, current D-65), whose band table says "individuals" and "households" in different rows; no published rule settles it.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Premium and finance owner, Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The children's buy-in premium bands are read by household income as a percentage of poverty for the household's size, and the rows that say individuals are read as households.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.6.Q.1.g (D-65, S318) puts the premiums "on a sliding scale based on household size and income" and says "households" in two bands and "individuals" in two. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-145. Resolved: MMMNA standard's July-versus-January adjustment timing is not modeled

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.7.Q.1.a (S358) states the MMMNA "is adjusted in July of each year," but federal_poverty_guideline_annual_table (CO-181) versions its HHS poverty guideline figures from January 1 of the calendar year the guideline is published for, not from the following July when Colorado's MMMNA standard actually changes. ma_mmmna_standard (CO-662) accordingly uses the calendar-year guideline for any month, so a determination made January through June of a year picks up that year's guideline one adjustment cycle early, rather than continuing to use the prior July's figure until the new one takes effect. Items: CO-662, CO-181.

Phase 7 sweep: CMS's 2026 chart (D-72, S644) confirms the July timing and gives the MMMNA effective July 1, 2026 as $2,705.00, which equals ma_mmmna_standard's (CO-662) computation from the 2026 poverty guideline for determinations from that date; determinations from January through June 2026 still use the 2026 guideline one cycle early, because CO-181 carries no 2025 version and the engine cannot look a parameter up as of a different date. Left open for that period.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the 2025 HHS poverty guidelines (D-124, S1219: $21,150 for two) give the MMMNA that applies January through June 2026: 150% x 21,150 / 12 = $2,643.75; from July 1, 2026 it is $2,705.00 (D-72). New parameter ma_mmmna_poverty_guideline_family_of_two_annual (CO-1954), dated from each July 1 (2025-07-01: 21,150; 2026-07-01: 21,640), which ma_mmmna_standard (CO-662) now reads instead of the January-dated federal_poverty_guideline_annual_table. No household case falls in January-June 2026 with a spouse, so no expectation changed.

Hard-coded numbers fix round (audit D-1): the MMMNA reads the one federal table, federal_poverty_guideline_annual_table (CO-181), as of the most recent July 1 (ma_mmmna_adjustment_month, CO-3031; P25 and P50) for a family of ma_mmmna_family_size (CO-3032); the July-dated copy CO-1954 is retired.

### OQ-151. Resolved: AND-SO's SSI/SSDI application and appeal duty is a condition of af_and_so_eligible

Body: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.75.B (S529): "The AND-SO client shall apply for Supplemental Security Income (SSI) benefits. If the client has work hours during his or her lifetime, the client shall also apply for Social Security Disability Insurance (SSDI)... The client shall appeal all negative decisions regarding their SSI eligibility." Neither af_and_so_disability_met (CO-944) nor af_category (CO-953)'s and_so branch reads an application-duty fact; only Aid to the Blind's parallel statutory prong (26-2-111(5)(a)(II), has_applied_for_ssi, CO-848) is modeled, for AB, not AND-SO. Items: CO-944, CO-953.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): current citation. 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.71.B (S710, D-80) restates the AND-SO SSI and SSDI application duty; open only because unmodeled.

Resolution (open-question sweep, 2026-11-21): 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.71.B (D-80, S1315) requires the AND-SO client to apply for SSI, to apply for SSDI with a lifetime work history, and to appeal negative SSI decisions within thirty days, and says failure "shall result in denial or discontinuation of and grant payments". The new supplied af_has_met_and_so_benefit_application_duty (CO-2004) states the county's record of that duty, and af_and_so_eligible (CO-1203) requires it (new test CO-1203-T4; CO-1203-T1 to T3 and CO-953-T4 now state it true). The Aid to the Blind fact has_applied_for_ssi (CO-848) is not reused: it carries the statute's referral-compliance condition and nothing of SSDI or appeals. No household case has an AND-SO client; no case expectation changed. Items: CO-1203, CO-2004.

### OQ-152. Resolved: the optional assistance-unit members of 3.604.2.C.2 join on request

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.2.C.2 lists seven categories of persons who may optionally be included in the assistance unit at the caretaker's request (siblings, half-siblings, the spouse of a pregnant parent, and, at C.2.g, the parent(s) of a minor parent who is not otherwise a mandatory member). cw_assistance_unit_members (CO-900) models only the mandatory parent/dependent-child pair of 3.604.2.C.1.a-b; CO-900's own precision field already lists this gap. C.2.g in particular interacts with C-12's fix and with cw_deemed_parent_income_of_minor_parent (CO-909), because the same non-participant parent of a minor parent is either an optional assistance-unit member (if the parent requests inclusion) or a deeming source (if not) depending on a request this volume has no supplied fact for. A future batch adding a requests_assistance_for_self supplied fact could model C.2 as a family of optional-member branches. Items: CO-900.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): 3.604.2.C.2 is clear; each optional member needs a request-for-inclusion fact, and C.2.g interacts with minor-parent deeming (CO-909, CO-2000). Not modeled in this sweep.

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep, supplied-facts round): 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.2.C.2 (S1625) includes the optional members "when living in the home and requesting assistance". New: cw_requests_inclusion_in_assistance_unit (CO-2346, false when unstated), cw_is_optional_member_by_custody_or_support (CO-2347, for C.2.d-f, which turn on child support and legal custody), cw_is_optional_assistance_unit_member (CO-2349: a non-parent caretaker, a stepparent or a caretaker's spouse whose spouse is not pregnant, or C.2.d-f) and cw_is_excluded_from_assistance_unit (CO-2350). cw_assistance_unit_members (CO-900) crosses spouse edges from a parent, caretaker or pregnant member, crosses the minor parent's edge to her parent when that parent requests inclusion (C.2.g), and filters out excluded persons. A non-parent caretaker who does not request assistance now leaves a child-only unit (new tests CO-900-T6 to T10; CO-2349-T1 to T5; CO-2350-T1 to T4). No household case with a Colorado Works outcome has a non-parent caretaker, a stepparent or a requesting grandparent; CO-01's p3, the minor parent's mother, does not request inclusion, so p4's unit stays [p4, p5]; no expectation changed.

### OQ-153. Tension between "shall not exceed" (3.606.1.H) and "equals or exceeds" (3.606.1.J.4) at the income-equals-need-standard boundary

Body: 3.606.1.H (`documents/D-61.md:959`) says countable income "shall not exceed the need standard" - income equal to the standard passes. 3.606.1.J.4 (`documents/D-61.md:977`) says "If the net countable income equals or exceeds the need standard, the assistance unit is not eligible" - income equal to the standard fails. cw_meets_income_test (CO-913) takes J.4's reading (strict `<`), the better-supported reading because J.4 states the operative eligibility consequence directly ("the assistance unit is not eligible") while H states a general income-test description; CO-913-T2 tests exactly the disputed boundary (income 331, standard 331 -> false). The conflict itself was in the fetched text with no open question naming it until this fix round (M-2). Items: CO-913.

Phase 8: the version effective 2025-07-01 (D-81) keeps both sentences unchanged (3.606.1.H "shall not exceed", 3.606.1.J.4 "equals or exceeds ... not eligible"), so the tension remains and CO-913 keeps J.4's reading.

Checked 2026-11-21: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.1.H ("shall not exceed the need standard", D-81:3029) and 3.606.1.J.4 ("equals or exceeds the need standard, the assistance unit is not eligible", D-81:3055), both in the version effective 2025-07-01. No published rule settles it.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: A Colorado Works unit whose countable income equals the need standard is not eligible.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.1.H ("shall not exceed the need standard") and 3.606.1.J.4 ("equals or exceeds the need standard, the assistance unit is not eligible") in the version effective 2025-07-01 (D-81) point different ways at equality. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-157. Resolved: CCCAP teen-parent eligible-activity conjunct read without a default

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.103.ZZZZ (S481) requires a teen parent to be "in an eligible activity," in addition to being under 21 and having physical custody, which cccap_is_teen_parent (CO-981) previously did not test at all (its own precision deferred the prong to cccap_caretaker_in_eligible_activity, CO-984, but nothing read CO-984 from CO-981 itself). The review (docs/reviews/cccap-leap-phase-6.md, C-8, should-fix) found this truncation let CO-985 and CO-991 use the truncated term as a live classifier with the wrong consequence in both directions. The new activity conjunct is read with `[otherwise, cccap_caretaker_in_eligible_activity, false]` (defaults to not in an eligible activity when the case does not state hours_worked/cccap_is_in_job_search_activity/snap_is_enrolled_half_time_in_school_or_training for this person), rather than leaving it unknown, for the same case-coverage reason as OQ-134/OQ-135/OQ-136: most existing tests and cases in this chapter that mark a custodial minor a teen parent do not state her activity facts, since the prior item never read them, and an unknown value would turn every one of those cases' downstream CCCAP determinations unknown. The default is the more conservative reading (a teen parent without a stated activity is treated as not a teen parent, so her earnings count under CO-985 and she does not count as a caretaker whose inactivity could deny the household under CO-991) - the opposite direction of risk from OQ-134/135/136's "defaults to more favorable" pattern. A future batch that can edit every case's facts should replace the default with an explicit fact and remove the `otherwise`. Items: CO-981.

Resolution (Phase-10 open-question sweep): Resolved. cccap_is_teen_parent (CO-981) reads cccap_caretaker_in_eligible_activity (CO-984) without a default; the target household's teen parent (p4) states her activity facts and cccap_education_activity_time_limit_reached false (a teen parent in basic education has no time limit, 3.111.G.4.b.2, S1412).

### OQ-158. Resolved: the six remaining non-vulnerable living arrangements are read from a living-arrangement fact

Body: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.752.25.B (D-86, S774) is unchanged in the current version. leap_is_vulnerable_to_heating_cost (CO-1004) now also excludes room and board (B.2), correctional facilities (B.3), dormitories (B.4), subsidized housing without an individual heat check meter or other evidence of responsibility for heating surcharges (B.5), commercial properties that are also the dwelling (B.7) and hotels without thirty days' proof (B.8) through the new supplied fact leap_living_arrangement (CO-1283). Where a case does not state it, the arrangement is read as an ordinary dwelling (`otherwise traditional_dwelling`): 3.752.25.B lists the arrangements in which households "shall not be considered to be vulnerable", exceptions a case shows, so a household not shown to be in one of them is vulnerable as the paragraph's general rule reads (three-pattern sweep D-EXC; restated by coverage pass 7, R7-6: before, this resolution called it a case-coverage default, the reading decision 4 removed elsewhere). The reading is declared on residence_setting_type (CO-3330) and leap_living_arrangement (CO-1283) as convention rule 12 asks; a case that should test an excluded arrangement states it. Items: CO-1004, CO-1283, CO-3330.

### OQ-159. Resolved: CCCAP residency (3.111.A) is a county test

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.A (S484) requires the adult caretaker(s)/teen parent(s) to be "verified residents of the county from which assistance is sought and received at the time of application and re-determination" - a county-level test. cccap_low_income_child_care_eligible (CO-987) reads is_colorado_resident (CO-505, Medicaid's chapter) for the residency conjunct, the same cross-program reuse leap_is_eligible (CO-1007) already makes for LEAP's parallel 3.753.21 test, because 3.111.A's state-residency prong and 8.100.3.B's test derive identically and governance's dup.compact check rejects a same-shape item under a new id. This implements only the state-residency prong; this volume has no CCCAP county-of-application or county-of-residence supplied fact. Per the review (docs/reviews/cccap-leap-phase-6.md, C-11): "CCCAP's test is a county residency test, which this volume has no fact for - that is the honest difficulty and the reason to raise it rather than skip it." A future batch should add a county_of_residence/cccap_county_of_application pair of supplied facts and extend the residency test to check county match, not merely state residency - which would also require its own item rather than reusing is_colorado_resident, since a county test is no longer identical to Medicaid's. Items: CO-987.

Resolution (Phase-10 open-question sweep): Resolved. New supplied county_of_residence (CO-1979, neutral; the sweep's CO-2105 duplicate was retired at merge) and cccap_application_county (CO-2106), and cccap_meets_county_residency (CO-2107): a Colorado resident whose county of residence is the county applied to (3.111.A, S1408). cccap_low_income_child_care_eligible (CO-987) reads it instead of is_colorado_resident. 3.111.A's carve-out for a household that moves during the eligibility period is not modeled (CO-2107's precision). The cases state an illustrative county for each person; no expectation changed.

### OQ-160. Resolved: CCCAP child citizenship requirement read without a default

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.F.1 (S514) requires every child who has had a CCCAP application made on their behalf, or who is receiving child care assistance, to verify citizen/legal-resident status. The new cccap_child_meets_citizenship_requirement (CO-998) carries this test in cccap_is_eligible_child (CO-980), but is read with `[otherwise, ..., true]` (defaults to met when the case does not state citizenship_status/lawful_presence_status for the child), rather than leaving it unknown, for the same case-coverage reason as OQ-134/OQ-135/OQ-136: most existing CCCAP cases in `volumes/co/tests/cases.yaml` and this chapter's own item tests do not state a child's citizenship facts, and an unknown value would silently turn every one of those cases' `cccap_eligibility_status` expectations unknown. The default assumes a child already treated as an eligible child in a case is a verified citizen/legal resident unless the case affirmatively says otherwise. A future batch that can edit every case's facts should replace the default with an explicit fact on every child and remove the `otherwise`. Items: CO-980, CO-998.

Resolution (Phase-10 open-question sweep): Resolved by the vocabulary fix round, which removed the default from cccap_is_eligible_child (CO-980) (OQ-395); the reach of "legal resident" is OQ-394.

### OQ-161. Resolved: CCCAP asset self-declaration reads a CCCAP-specific fact defined by 3.103.K

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.H.4.c (S515) is CCCAP's own asset self-declaration; cccap_meets_asset_limit (CO-996) reads snap_countable_liquid_resources and snap_countable_nonliquid_resources, the same supplied facts the SNAP chapter's snap_household_resources (CO-343) uses, since no CCCAP-specific resource fact exists and 3.111.H.4.c's "liquid and non-liquid assets" language matches those facts' own scope closely enough to reuse rather than duplicate per docs/conventions.md's reuse preference. If a future batch finds CCCAP's asset definition diverges from SNAP's (for example, different vehicle or retirement-account treatment), a CCCAP-specific pair of resource facts should be added instead. Items: CO-996.

Resolution (Phase-10 open-question sweep): Resolved. The vocabulary fix round gave CCCAP its own cccap_declared_assets (CO-1741), which cccap_meets_asset_limit (CO-996) reads; its meaning now states 3.103.K's definition (S1414): every vehicle counts except the primary caretakers' automobile, the primary home is excluded, and no SNAP or retirement-account exclusion applies.

### OQ-162. Partly resolved: CCCAP K-4/K-5/K-6 (need for care, time-limited activities, minimum-wage-per-hour test) and the education limits by kind of activity are modeled; open: a county plan's longer job search or education period

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.110/3.111.D.1.a/3.111.F (need for child care assistance and the eligible child's "less than twenty-four hours" test, S483/S497/S482), 3.111.G.3.a/G.3.g/G.4 (the 13-week job search cap and the 104-208-week training/post-secondary cap, `documents/D-64.md:462,476`), and 3.111.G.1.b/2.d (the federal-minimum-wage-per-hour verification, `documents/D-64.md:440`) are each stated, eligibility-determining conditions this batch does not implement, per the review's K-4, K-5, K-6 findings (docs/reviews/cccap-leap-phase-6.md). K-4 would need a supplied needs_child_care_assistance fact and an hours-of-care condition on cccap_is_eligible_child (CO-980); K-5 would need job_search_weeks_used/training_weeks_used_lifetime supplied facts and 13/208-week parameters, extending cccap_caretaker_in_eligible_activity (CO-984), which OQ-124 already covers for activity mapping but not for these limits; K-6 would need a federal minimum wage parameter (which the SNAP chapter's own work rules, 7 CFR 273.7(b)(1)(vii), would also use) and a gross-income-divided-by-hours test in CO-984. None were in this batch's assigned item range. Items: CO-980, CO-984.

Separate gap in this same batch (C-12): 3.111.J (S486 quotes only J.1) also excludes SSI (J.2), SNAP/WIC/school-meal benefits (J.4), county foster/kinship/adoption-subsidy payments (J.10), LEAP benefits (J.12), the Earned Income Credit (J.14), undergraduate grants and loans (J.16, J.19), public cash assistance (J.26, correct by construction since cccap_household_income, CO-985, does not read tanf_cash_income), and irregular income under $90/quarter (J.28); cccap_household_income (CO-985) sums other_unearned_income_nontaxable, social_security_income, pension_income, unemployment_income, and a non-household-child's wages_gross/self_employment_net_earnings_for_tax wholesale, getting J.10, J.12, J.14, J.16 and J.28 wrong for any case that puts those amounts in one of those buckets - and cw_receives_title_iv_foster_adoption_kinship_payment is already a supplied flag on every person in CO-01/CO-02. Per the review, CO-985's precision now states the SNAP chapter's convention (docs/colorado-notes.md, phase 2, "Income exclusions"): the supplied income facts are the countable, post-exclusion amounts, pushing the J exclusions onto the fact-assembly layer rather than this item. Separately, 3.111.K (`documents/D-64.md:749`) deducts verified court-ordered child support paid for children not in the household before comparing income to the guidelines; child_support_paid_legally_obligated is already a supplied fact the SNAP chapter reads for its own analogous deduction, but CO-985 does not implement 3.111.K this batch. Items: CO-985.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): current citation. 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.G.3-4 (D-87, `documents/D-87.md:1590-1650`) now states the 13-week job search (more by county plan), the at-least-104-week (208 by approved county plan) training and post-secondary limit, the 52-week basic education limit and the new one-year-per-instance substance use disorder treatment activity (S1015); the figures are available but the time limits stay unmodeled.

Resolution (Phase-10 open-question sweep): K-4 resolved: cccap_is_eligible_child (CO-980) reads the new cccap_child_needs_care_for_part_of_day (CO-2108), care for a portion of the day but less than twenty-four hours (3.110, 3.111.F, S1408). K-5 resolved in part: cccap_caretaker_in_eligible_activity (CO-984) counts job search while cccap_job_search_weeks_used (CO-2109) is below cccap_job_search_weeks (CO-2110, 13; G.3.a, g, S1411), education or training until cccap_education_activity_time_limit_reached (CO-2111; G.4, S1412), and SUD treatment for cccap_sud_treatment_activity_max_months (CO-2112, 12) from its start (G.4.a.3).
