K-6 resolved: employment and self-employment count only when gross wages plus CCCAP self-employment income per hour worked is at least federal_minimum_wage (CO-160), or the self-employment is in its launch period (cccap_self_employment_in_launch_period, CO-2113; G.1.b, G.2.d-e, S1410). Still open: a county plan's longer job search or education period (a county figure, like OQ-121), and the education limits enter as a yes/no because the activity fact does not separate post-secondary, workforce training and basic education. The C-12 income exclusions (3.111.J) and the 3.111.K child support deduction are OQ-390's. No case expectation changed.

Open-question classification pass (2026-11-22): the education limits by kind of activity are carried after all. cccap_education_activity_time_limit_reached (CO-2111) states in its meaning the limit for each kind (104 weeks for training or post-secondary education and for workforce training, 208 under an approved county plan, and 52 weeks for basic education, none for a teen parent in basic education; 3.111.G.4, S1412), so the person who supplies it applies the limit for the caretaker's activity and nothing is missing from the ledger. What stays open is a county's approved plan: a longer job search period than 13 weeks, or more weeks of education.

Second-pass review (2026-11-22): the J.28 point is not a county-plan question. J.28 is the state rule's own exclusion and is now modeled (cccap_excluded_irregular_income, CO-4076, with cccap_irregular_income_quarterly_limit, CO-4075, read by cccap_person_unearned_income, CO-3916); its remaining reading is OQ-713. This question keeps only the county-plan periods.

Coverage round (docs/reviews/coverage-cccap.md, F-19): the same county plans define the closure for not using the Attendance Tracking System: 3.112.J (S3262) says "Non-cooperation with the use of the ATS may result in case closure and/or non-payment of the child care benefits as defined by Department-approved county policy" (also 3.133.A.4). The volume closes no case for it; a case the county closes under its policy is not modeled until the county policies are available.

Class: blocked on a named document
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: Job search counts for 13 weeks and education for the statewide limits, with no county extension; no case is closed for Attendance Tracking System non-cooperation.
Blocked on: each county's CCCAP county plan approved by the Department under 8 CCR 1403-1 3.104, which states any longer job search or education period and any closure for Attendance Tracking System non-cooperation (cdec.colorado.gov refused; the plans are not published on an allowed host)

### OQ-163. Partly resolved: CCCAP/LEAP K-7 (shared-fuel arithmetic) and K-8 (duplicate-household denial) are modeled; open: K-9, the teen-parent household merge

Body: Three further completeness gaps from the review (docs/reviews/cccap-leap-phase-6.md) remain open. K-7: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.752.25.A.4, A.6 (`documents/D-63.md:463-467`) makes a multi-household residence vulnerable when it contributes toward shared expenses and divides estimated home heating costs by the number of parties on a shared meter - the machinery for exactly the multi-household-dwelling situation C-1's shares_heating_fuel_with fix creates, and unmodeled; it belongs with OQ-123's benefit-amount gap if a future batch builds leap_benefit_amount. K-8: 3.751.2.A (`documents/D-63.md`, not separately excerpted this batch) makes "any individual considered as part of an approved household cannot subsequently be considered as part of another household during the same eligibility period" a hard rule, and 3.754.1's weatherization-refusal and other denial factors beyond the application-period timing (leap_month_in_eligibility_period, CO-1008) are not modeled; leap_is_eligible (CO-1007)'s precision already acknowledges the 3.754.1 list generally. K-9: cccap_household_members (CO-982)'s closure joins a teen parent to both her parent's household and her own child, so a teen parent's household income is tested together with her parent's, a modeling choice 3.103.GGG's "adult caretaker(s) or teen parent(s)" phrasing does not itself resolve and 3.111.D.3 (separate applications for a child across two households) may cut against; this is, per the review, "the single modeling choice in the CCCAP chapter with the largest effect on the target household." None were in this batch's assigned item range. Items: CO-982, CO-1007, CO-1008.

Phase-10 open-question sweep. K-7: resolved. 3.752.25.A.4 makes a household in a residence shared with other households vulnerable when it contributes toward the residence's total expenses, and A.6 divides the estimated home heating cost among the parties on a shared primary fuel (D-86, S1402); is_responsible_for_heating_costs (CO-876) now states the A.4 ground and leap_households_sharing_living_arrangement (CO-1274), which Step E already divides by, counts the A.6 parties. K-8: resolved. 3.751.2.A (S1400) is the new supplied leap_is_in_another_approved_household (CO-2100), which removes the person from leap_household_members (CO-1000) and leap_household_members_for_income (CO-1009); the 3.754.1 denial reasons beyond timing remain outside leap_is_eligible's precision. K-9 stays open: checked 2026-11-21: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.103 ("Household", "Teen parent") and 3.111.D (D-87); no published rule settles whether a teen parent living with her own parent forms one CCCAP household with that parent or a separate one.

CDHS stakeholder review fix round (2026-11-21): the CDHS/CDEC reviewer states that K-9 cannot be answered from the rule text and returns it to the CDEC as a blocker for CO-01's CCCAP expectation. Still open.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: A teen parent who lives with her own parent is in one CCCAP household with that parent.

Checked 2026-11-22: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.103 ("Household", "Teen parent") and 3.111.D (D-87): the definitions join adult caretakers or teen parents and their children but do not say whether a teen parent living with her own parent forms a separate household. No published rule settles it. Final pass (2026-11-22, section 6 of docs/reviews/final-cccap-leap-sebt.md): 3.103.C also requires an adult caretaker to have "custody of the child during the period of time child care is being requested", and 3.103.LLL's household is the children plus "the adult caretaker(s) or teen parent(s)"; read together these lean toward the teen parent's own household without her parent, who is not a caretaker of the child in care. The text still does not decide it, so the class stands and the owner's answer is required.

### OQ-200. Emergency Medicaid's "otherwise eligible" is read as a MAGI category, the SSI-related standards, or institutional long-term care

Body: 42 U.S.C. 1396b(v)(2)(B) and 42 CFR 440.255(c)(2) require an alien to meet "the eligibility requirements for medical assistance under the State plan" other than receipt of AFDC, SSI or a State supplementary payment, and 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.3.F.1.k says "the criteria for one of the categorical Medical Assistance programs". ma_meets_requirements_other_than_status (CO-1150) reads that as residency plus a MAGI category (whose income test is part of the category), or - for an aged, blind or disabled person - the SSI-related income and resource standards or institutional long-term care financial eligibility at the nursing facility level of care. The Buy-In programs, HCBS and the protected groups are not routes, because their own items read citizenship, and the Cover All Coloradans population (children, pregnant and postpartum people) never reaches this test because it meets status. The emergency medical condition is a month-level supplied fact (has_emergency_medical_condition, CO-1100), not a claim-by-claim determination, and the organ-transplant exclusion of 1396b(v)(2)(C) is not represented. Items: CO-1150, CO-1151, CO-510, CO-1100. Phase 7 fix round: the SSI-related route is joined by OAP SISC B (an aged, blind or disabled OAP recipient within the SSI resource standard, § 8.100.6.K.4, S621, S665), and every route now also requires § 8.100.3.G.1.c's institution for mental diseases requirement (ma_meets_imd_requirement, CO-1179, S659). HCBS and PACE now read status themselves (B-3), so an HCBS or PACE applicant who fails status is emergency-eligible only through the SSI-related, OAP or institutional route; whether the 300% special income group should also be a route for a non-institutionalized HCBS applicant is part of this question. The Cover All Coloradans population no longer meets status for every category (OQ-214): a child, pregnant or postpartum person who fails status is emergency-only when the only category met is one ix.1.a-b does not list (CO-1175, CO-510). Items added: CO-1175, CO-1177, CO-1179.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): Checked 2026-11-21: 42 U.S.C. 1396b(v)(2)(B), 42 CFR 440.255(c)(2) and § 8.100.3.F.1.k (D-26, D-65); whether the HCBS special income group counts for a non-institutionalized applicant is not stated; no published rule settles it.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: For emergency Medicaid, a non-citizen is otherwise eligible through a MAGI group, the SSI-related standards, an Old Age Pension route or nursing facility care, but not through the higher income limit for home and community services alone.

Checked 2026-11-22: 42 U.S.C. 1396b(v)(2)(B), 42 CFR 440.255(c)(2) and 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.3.F.1.k (D-26, D-65) require "the criteria for one of the categorical Medical Assistance programs" without saying whether the HCBS special income group counts for a person who is not in an institution. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-201. BCCP's "woman" is read as recorded sex female

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.715.2.C.1 requires "a woman who has not yet attained the age of 65"; 42 U.S.C. 1396a(aa) itself states no sex requirement. ma_bccp_eligibility_status (CO-1152) tests sex (CO-1101) = female. Whether a person recorded otherwise who is screened and in need of treatment for breast or cervical cancer qualifies is not settled by the fetched text. Items: CO-1152, CO-1101.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): Checked 2026-11-21: § 8.715.2.C.1 ("a woman", D-65) and 42 U.S.C. 1396a(aa), which states no sex; no published rule settles it.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: Only a person recorded as female can qualify for the breast and cervical cancer treatment program.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.715.2.C.1 (D-67: "Be a woman who has not yet attained the age of 65") and 42 U.S.C. 1396a(aa), which states no sex requirement. No published rule says how "woman" is read for a person recorded otherwise.

### OQ-202. BCCP's out-of-state transfer requirement and stale cross-reference

Body: § 8.715.2.C lists as a seventh requirement that members "Be a member who has previously qualified and enrolled in a NBCCED program in another state and chooses to transfer her enrollment to CWCCI", which read literally would exclude every Colorado-screened woman; it is read as an alternative way to meet the screening requirement (C.4) and folded into is_screened_in_need_of_breast_or_cervical_cancer_treatment (CO-1102). § 8.715.2.C.3's exception cross-references "Section 8.100.3(G)(1)(g)(viii)", which in the current § 8.100 (D-65) is the Afghan humanitarian parolee paragraph; the Cover All Coloradans exception it evidently means is now ix. ma_bccp_eligibility_status reads ma_meets_citizenship_requirement (CO-509), which carries the Cover All Coloradans exception. Items: CO-1102, CO-1152. Phase 7 fix round: the literal current '(viii)' is the Afghan humanitarian parolee paragraph (§ 8.100.3.G.1.g.viii), itself a plausible exception; '(vi)-(vii)' now points vi at the incorporation-by-reference paragraph; and '(ii)-(iv)' skips v (Cuban/Haitian entrants and COFA migrants, S657). ma_bccp_eligibility_status now reads ma_meets_status_for_cover_all_coloradans_categories (CO-1174): the status test CO-509, which covers v (the better outcome), or the Cover All Coloradans population, the exception C.3 evidently means.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): Checked 2026-11-21: the current § 8.715.2.C (D-65), whose cross-reference to § 8.100.3(G)(1)(g) is stale and whose transfer requirement reads as a seventh condition; no published rule settles it.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: Moving an enrollment from another state's program is one way to meet the screening requirement, not a seventh condition, and the outdated cross-reference is read as the Cover All Coloradans exception.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.715.2.C (D-67), whose seventh requirement reads literally as excluding every woman screened in Colorado and whose exception cites a paragraph of § 8.100.3.G.1.g that now covers Afghan parolees. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-203. BCCP creditable coverage: the 2002 edition of C.R.S. 10-16-102 is not fetched (narrowed in the phase 7 fix round)

Body: § 8.715.3.A defines creditable coverage by C.R.S. 10-16-102 "(2002)". The current (2024) 10-16-102(16) was fetched (D-79, S674): Medicare, the Colorado Medical Assistance Act, the children's basic health plan, employer and group plans, individual plans, a state risk pool, TRICARE, FEHB, a public health plan and a Peace Corps plan. § 8.715.3.B removes limited-scope, single-disease (other than breast or cervical cancer) and Indian Health Service or tribal coverage, and § 8.715.3.D keeps coverage with yearly drug or visit limits or high deductibles (S672). ma_bccp_eligibility_status (CO-1152) now reads the BCCP-specific ma_bccp_has_creditable_coverage (CO-1191), which carries those rules, and ma_bccp_creditable_coverage_exception_applies (CO-1192), the two § 8.715.3.C exceptions, in place of the CHP+ fact CO-090. What remains open: the 2002 edition the rule incorporates was not fetched, and the 2024 definition is read as stating it. Items: CO-1152, CO-1191, CO-1192.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): still blocked: leg.colorado.gov publishes only the current C.R.S. editions (2024 is the latest title PDF), and no allowed host carries the 2002 edition of C.R.S. 10-16-102 that § 8.715.3.A incorporates; that edition would settle it. The product owner's research quotes the 2002 C.R.S. 10-16-102(21) definition of "health benefit plan" (any hospital or medical expense policy or certificate, hospital or medical service corporation contract, health maintenance organization subscriber contract, or other similar health contract subject to the commissioner's jurisdiction offered or sold in Colorado, excluding accident-only, credit, dental, vision, Medicare supplement, long-term care, disability income, supplements to liability insurance, workers' compensation, automobile medical payment, and specified-disease or hospital-indemnity coverage). That definition (unverified, not fetched) is consistent with ma_bccp_has_creditable_coverage (CO-1191), whose § 8.715.3.B exclusions already remove limited-scope and single-disease coverage, so no item changes; the 2002 creditable-coverage paragraph itself remains unseen.

Class: blocked on a named document
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The current statutory definition of creditable coverage stands in for the 2002 edition the rule incorporates.
Blocked on: C.R.S. 10-16-102, 2002 edition, which 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.715.3.A incorporates (not published by an allowed host: leg.colorado.gov serves only current editions)

### OQ-204. Limited Family Planning: "a household of two in addition to any other household members" and the 5% disregard

Body: § 8.100.4.E.3 counts the family planning applicant "as a household of two in addition to any other household members". ma_family_planning_household_income_pct_fpl (CO-1153) reads that as the MAGI household size plus one. § 8.100.4.G.8 states the band as greater than 133% and not exceeding 260%; ma_family_planning_income_in_band (CO-1155) applies the 5% MAGI disregard at the 260% ceiling only (the highest standard the person is tested against) and not at the floor. Neither reading is spelled out in the fetched text. Items: CO-1153, CO-1155, CO-1154. Both readings have published support (phase 7 review): § 8.100.4.F.2.c's limited family planning presumptive eligibility counts the applicant 'individually as a household member of two' (S678), which supports size plus one, and § 8.100.4.D.1.a applies the 5% disregard 'for each qualifying MAGI program as the last step' (S678), which supports applying it at the 260% ceiling. Neither sentence is the G.8 determination itself, so the question stays open with these as the evidence for the current reading.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): Checked 2026-11-21: § 8.100.4.E.3 and § 8.100.4.G.8 (D-65), which state the household of two and the band without saying where the 5% disregard applies; no published rule settles it.

Hard-coded numbers fix round (audit H-21): the count is a parameter, ma_family_planning_applicant_household_count (CO-3029, 2), and the household size is ma_magi_household_size plus that count less one; the reading is unchanged and stays open.

Coverage pass 3 (C3-01, 2026-11-22): the plus-one household also moves G.8's floor. 133% of the two-person guideline is 180.3% of the one-person guideline, so a single adult between 138% (the Adults limit with the disregard) and 180.3% (a family of three: 138% to 160.6%) was over the Adults limit and under the floor, and had no coverage. The approved state plan page for the group, S59 (D-315; S3681), covers under 42 CFR 435.214 (S3680) individuals "who are not pregnant, and have household income at or below a standard established by the state", with no floor; G.8's last sentence gives the group's purpose as covering "those not eligible for a Medicaid eligibility category that provides full coverage". The volume now follows the state plan: ma_family_planning_income_in_band (CO-1155) and the presumptive tests (CO-2439, CO-3155) test only the 260% ceiling, each with a statute_override note; the full-coverage exclusion is applied by ma_family_planning_status's order (CO-1154) and, for presumptive eligibility, by testing the full-coverage groups first (CO-2439) or on the declarations (CO-3155); ma_family_planning_income_lower_pct (CO-1131) is no longer read. A consequence beyond the gap: a person who is not eligible for full coverage and whose income is at or below 133% on the enlarged household is now limited_family_planning (CO-01 and CO-02 p2, 81 and on Medicare, at 65.4%; was ineligible_income). The size and disregard readings above are unchanged. Items added: CO-4976 (the enlarged size, factored out of CO-1153), CO-4977 (the data source on that size). The class moves to needs Legal: the rule's floor and the approved state plan conflict, and only HCPF can say whether the rule or the state plan is to be amended.

Class: state policy choice (reclassified from "needs Legal" in coverage pass 4, 2026-11-23, docs/reviews/coverage4-medicaid-magi-chp.md section 4: the two readings each rest on text on file (the rule's "greater than 133%" and its silence on the disregard, against the approved state plan page S59, which sets no floor); no document not on file would decide it, and which one governs is the State's to settle by amending the rule or the plan, with Legal advising)
Owner: Legal, Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The family planning applicant is counted as two in the household, which adds one to its size; the 5 percent disregard applies only at the 260 percent ceiling; and, as the approved state plan sets no floor, a person who is not eligible for full coverage qualifies at any income at or below 260 percent.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.4.E.3 ("a household of two in addition to any other household members") and § 8.100.4.G.8 (D-65) state the count and the band without saying where the 5 percent disregard applies. No published rule settles it.

Checked 2026-11-22 (coverage pass 3): Colorado State Plan page S59 (CO-13-0045-MM1, D-315:2574-2584, approved 12/18/2013, effective 01/01/2014); 42 CFR 435.214(b)-(c) (D-32; the eCFR text current on 2026-11-22 is the same); § 8.100.4.G.8, § 8.100.3.R.2.c and § 8.100.4.F.2.c (D-65, effective 11/14/2026). The readings: (a) the state plan governs and no floor applies, the reading in force; (b) the rule's "greater than 133%" applies to the enlarged household and leaves the gap. No published text reconciles them.

### OQ-205. Resolved: Reproductive health-care program: "would be eligible to enroll in the medical assistance program" and declining state medical assistance

Body: C.R.S. 25.5-2-103(1)(b) defines an eligible individual as one with reproductive capacity "who would be eligible to enroll in the medical assistance program ... but is not eligible due solely to the individual's immigration status, and who is not eligible for, or declines to enroll in, state medical assistance". ma_family_planning_status (CO-1154) reads "would be eligible" as meeting a categorical program other than status (CO-510) or the Limited Family Planning income band (CO-1155), since limited family planning is part of the medical assistance program. A child who declines state medical assistance (C.R.S. 25.5-2-104) is not modeled: children meet status under CO-509 and are reported full_coverage. No HCPF rule implementing § 25.5-2-103's enrollment was found in the fetched 10 CCR 2505-10 sections. Items: CO-1154. Phase 7 fix round: C.R.S. 25.5-2-104 (D-69:142, S654) confirms state medical assistance is for children under 19 only, so a pregnant adult's 'not eligible for ... state medical assistance' condition is met. Children no longer meet status everywhere (OQ-214), so ma_family_planning_status now reports a child under 19 who fails status but would be eligible for Medical Assistance (CO-510) or the Limited Family Planning band as full_coverage through state medical assistance (25.5-2-104(2)), not the reproductive health-care program; a child who declines state medical assistance is still not modeled.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the rule is clear (triage: NEEDS FACT) but this sweep did not model it; it needs a fact for a person who declines state medical assistance (C.R.S. 25.5-2-103(1)(b)). Note that OQ-214's resolution makes a child under 19 who would be eligible but for status eligible for state medical assistance in any category, which narrows who reaches the reproductive health-care program.

Phase-10 needs-fact sweep (2026-11-21): resolved. New fact ma_has_declined_state_medical_assistance (CO-2207). ma_family_planning_status (CO-1154) reports full_coverage through state medical assistance only for a child who has not declined it; a child who declines and has reproductive capacity reaches the reproductive health-care program, as C.R.S. 25.5-2-103(1)(b) says ("who is not eligible for, or declines to enroll in, state medical assistance", S615). New test CO-1154-T12. No household case has an undocumented child, so no case states it.

### OQ-206. Resolved: Old Age Pension Health Care: OAP-C, the "supplemental" program, and SISC B's "except for the level of their income"

Body: § 8.941.3.B excludes OAP-C (court-committed) recipients, but af_category (CO-953) has no OAP-C option, so ma_oap_health_care_status (CO-1156) does not test it. C.R.S. 25.5-2-101's heading still names a "supplemental old age pension health and medical care fund", but its subsection (3) is repealed (D-69, S619) and no current rule in 10 CCR 2505-10 §§ 8.940-8.942 (D-68) states a separate supplemental program, so none is modeled. § 8.100.6.K.4's "meet SSI eligibility criteria except for the level of their income" is read as aged, blind or disabled and within the SSI resource standard. Items: CO-1156. Phase 7 fix round: SISC B is now its own item (ma_oap_sisc_b_eligible, CO-1177), read by CO-612, CO-1150, CO-1156 and CO-1176. § 8.100.3.M.2.b defines code B for OAP "A" recipients only (S665) while § 8.100.6.K.4 names OAP-A and OAP-B (S621); af_category carries one OAP category, so both are read. § 8.100.6.K.7 (S667) keeps in the state-only program (SISC C) OAP-A, OAP-B 'or OAP Refugees who lost their OAP financial assistance because of a cost of living adjustment other than OASDI'; CO-1156 reads current OAP receipt only, so those former recipients are reported not_oap_recipient, and OAP Refugees (§ 8.100.3.F.1.j) are not distinguished. Items added: CO-1177.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the rule is clear (triage: NEEDS FACT) but this sweep did not model it; it needs an OAP-C (court-committed) fact or option and a fact for former OAP receipt lost to a cost-of-living increase.

Phase-10 needs-fact sweep (2026-11-21): resolved. New facts receives_oap_c (CO-2208; OAP-C is defined in 9 CCR 2503-5 3.530.1, S701, and § 8.941.2.F, S1510) and ma_lost_oap_to_non_oasdi_cost_of_living_adjustment (CO-2209; § 8.100.6.K.7, S667). ma_oap_health_care_status (CO-1156) treats a former OAP-A, OAP-B or OAP Refugee recipient who lost OAP to a non-OASDI cost of living adjustment as an OAP recipient for SISC C, and reports an OAP-C recipient who does not qualify for Medicaid as the new option ineligible_oap_c (§ 8.941.3.B, S618). New tests CO-1156-T7, T8. The cases state both facts false (CO-01, CO-02, CO-71); no expectation changed. The "supplemental" program and SISC B's reading were settled earlier and are unchanged.

### OQ-207. Resolved: Pickle countable income subtracts the disregard from total countable income

Body: § 8.100.6.E.1.d-e subtracts the Pickle disregard from the current OASDI income to get countable OASDI income, then compares "the countable OASDI income and all other countable income" with the SSI standard (E.2). ma_pickle_countable_income (CO-1158) subtracts the disregard from ma_ssi_countable_income (after the $20 and earned income exclusions) and floors at zero; when the disregard exceeds the OASDI income less $20, re-applying the unused part of the $20 exclusion to earned income would give a slightly different figure. Items: CO-1158. § 8.100.6.E.3 (S666) also applies the disregard to 'any OASDI cost of living increases paid to any financially responsible individual such as a parent or spouse whose income is considered'; CO-1158 does not subtract a parent's or spouse's COLAs, because no fact states them. Phase 7 fix round (S-6): ma_pickle_eligible (CO-1159) now compares with the greater of the SSI federal benefit rate and the OAP grant standard for a person of OAP age, reading E.2's 'current SSI or OAP standard'.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): § 8.100.6.E.1.e subtracts the Pickle disregard from the current OASDI income to give the countable OASDI income, and 42 CFR 435.135(a)(3) (S1233) deducts the increases from current OASDI benefits; the SSI exclusions then apply to the reduced income. ma_pickle_countable_income (CO-1158) now subtracts the disregard from the household's unearned income before the $20 general exclusion and carries any unused part of the $20 to earned income (CO-1158-T2 shows the difference: 207.5, where the old order gave 197.5). The increases of a financially responsible spouse or parent (435.135(b), § 8.100.6.E's S666) remain unmodeled. No household case is a Pickle case.

Coverage round (2026-11-22, docs/reviews/coverage-medicaid-nonmagi.md NM-17): the resolution had left § 8.100.6.E.3 (S666) unmodeled. The cost-of-living increases of a spouse living with the person are now disregarded too: new fact social_security_income_when_spouse_lost_ssi_or_oap (CO-4635, read as the spouse's current benefit when not stated) and CO-1158 subtracts the spouse's current OASDI less that base (CO-1158-T4). A parent's increases have nothing to come off, because the Pickle income (CO-1158) counts the person's and the spouse's income and no deemed parental income; a Pickle child under 18 living with ineligible parents is the one case where E.3's parent clause would matter (deferred, coverage round disposition).


### OQ-208. Resolved: Disabled Adult Child countable income subtracts the OASDI from total countable income

Body: § 8.100.6.J.1.c disregards "the current OASDI income of the applicant" and compares "other countable income" with the SSI FBR. ma_dac_countable_income (CO-1160) subtracts social_security_income from ma_ssi_countable_income and floors at zero, which can overstate the exclusion when the $20 general exclusion was absorbed by the OASDI and would otherwise apply to other income. Items: CO-1160.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): § 8.100.6.J.1.c disregards the current OASDI income and tests "other countable income", so the OASDI comes off before the $20 general exclusion. ma_dac_countable_income (CO-1160) now computes the SSI countable income of the household's income other than the person's OASDI, with the unused $20 carried to earned income. No household case is a Disabled Adult Child case.

### OQ-209. Disabled widow(er)s: the closed historical groups (the income and resource test is resolved)

Body: § 8.100.6.I.1.f requires disabled widow(er)s to "meet all other Medical Assistance requirements" and states no income or resource test of its own; ma_disabled_widow_eligible (CO-1162) reads it as residency and status. The 1972-disregard, 1973 and 1983-actuarial-change groups (§§ 8.100.6.F-H) are closed cohorts (H requires reapplying before July 1, 1987) and are not modeled; the federal early widow(er)s group (age 60-64) is not stated in Colorado's rule except inside § 8.100.6.I's 50-through-64 group. Items: CO-1162, CO-1163. Phase 7 fix round (S-7): § 8.100.6.I.1.b ('have received SSI in the past') and I.1.d are now read through lost_ssi_due_to_social_security_benefits (CO-1180), so a person who lost only OAP is not a disabled widow(er) although I.1's opening sentence says 'lost SSI and/or OAP'; the lettered conditions are read as controlling.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): Checked 2026-11-21: § 8.100.6.I.1.f and §§ 8.100.6.F-H (D-65), which state no income or resource test for disabled widow(er)s and describe closed cohorts; no published rule settles it.

Coverage round (2026-11-22, docs/reviews/coverage-medicaid-nonmagi.md NM-9): the first half is resolved by text; it was not a state policy choice. § 8.100.6.A.1 (S3411) sends every ABD category to § 8.100.5 "for income and resource criteria", and 42 U.S.C. 1383c(d)(2) (D-320, S3416) deems the group SSI recipients only "for so long as such person (i) would be eligible for such supplemental security income benefits ... in the absence of benefits described in paragraph (1)(A)"; 42 CFR 435.138(a) (S3417) deems them "title XVI payment beneficiaries under section 1634(d)". ma_disabled_widow_eligible (CO-1162) now requires the SSI resource standard (ma_meets_ssi_resource_standard) and countable income with the Social Security benefit disregarded (ma_dac_countable_income, CO-1160) below the Federal Benefit Rate. What stays open is the closed cohorts alone.

Class: scope decision of the product owner (reclassified from "state policy choice" in coverage pass 6, non-MAGI F6-12, 2026-11-24)
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The closed historical groups of §§ 8.100.6.F-H are not offered.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 §§ 8.100.6.F-H (D-65), which describe cohorts closed to new applicants (H requires reapplying before July 1, 1987), and 42 U.S.C. 1383c(b) (D-320), whose protection needed a written application by July 1, 1988. Whether Colorado still serves anyone in those cohorts is not in any published rule.

Coverage pass 6, non-MAGI F6-12 (2026-11-24; docs/reviews/coverage6-medicaid-nonmagi.md): the class was wrong. §§ 8.100.6.F.1 and G.1-3 (D-65) say "Medical Assistance must be provided" to the 1972 and 1973 cohorts, so their coverage is not the State's choice; whether the codex models them is a scope decision. Recorded as one, for the product owner, rather than modelled now: modelling F needs each person's August 1972 assistance and the amount of the October 1972 20% Social Security increase (F.2.c's "1972 disregard amount"), and G.1-3 the December 1973 minimum income level and eligibility criteria, none of which any document on file states, so each group would be a supplied finding of the whole determination; and the cohorts, now in their seventies or older (F: assistance in August 1972; G: eligibility in December 1973), nearly all fall within the aged group the codex determines (§ 8.100.6.C, K). H's group closed with the July 1, 1987 deadline. A person found in one of these cohorts is determined outside the codex. Owner: product owner, with the Medicaid policy owner. Items: CO-1162, CO-1163.

### OQ-210. Resolved: HCBS waiver target groups: only age (and CES marital status) is derived

Body: § 8.7101 states each waiver's target group as diagnoses, levels of care, settings and ages; ma_hcbs_waiver_age_criteria_met (CO-1164) derives only the age bounds (and CES's "unmarried") against the Case Management Agency's designation (CO-821), which carries the rest (§ 8.7100.D.1.a). EBD's subgroups are collapsed to an 18-or-older floor, and BI's "Brain Injury that occurred prior to the individual's 65th birthday" is not tested. Items: CO-1164, CO-821, CO-653. Phase 7 fix round (S-4): the waiver-specific level of care is no longer collapsed to nursing facility level of care: ma_meets_designated_waiver_level_of_care (CO-1178) carries the Case Management Agency's finding of the level of care the designated waiver requires (ICF/IID for CES, SLS, DD and CHRP's developmental disability route; nursing facility or hospital for CwCHN and CIH; S662-S664). Diagnoses and settings remain in the designation itself.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the rule is clear (triage: NEEDS FACT) but this sweep did not model it; it needs diagnosis, setting and brain-injury onset facts for each waiver's target group (§ 8.7101).

Phase-10 needs-fact sweep (2026-11-21): resolved. New facts for the criteria § 8.7101 states (S1511-S1513): ma_has_developmental_disability (CO-2258; CES, CHRP, SLS, DD), ma_meets_ces_setting_and_member_criteria (CO-2259), ma_has_serious_emotional_disturbance (CO-2260; CHRP), ma_has_brain_injury and ma_brain_injury_date (CO-2261, CO-2262; BI), ma_has_severe_and_persistent_mental_health_need (CO-2263; CMHS), ma_hcbs_ebd_subgroup (CO-2264; EBD), ma_has_cih_qualifying_condition_and_cannot_ambulate (CO-2265; CIH), ma_requires_continuous_twenty_four_hour_supervision and ma_resides_in_sls_setting (CO-2266, CO-2267; SLS) and ma_requires_twenty_four_hour_services_and_supports (CO-2268; DD); parameter ma_hcbs_bi_injury_age_limit (CO-2269). ma_meets_hcbs_waiver_condition_criteria (CO-2270) tests them for the designated waiver, and ma_hcbs_waiver_eligible (CO-653) reads it. ma_hcbs_waiver_age_criteria_met (CO-1164) now applies EBD's subgroup ages (aged 65 or older; physically disabled or blind and HIV/AIDS 18 or older, a physically disabled participant reaching 65 being reclassified as aged). No household case has a designated waiver, so no case states them.

### OQ-211. Resolved: the MAGI percentages changed from 133/60/185 to 142/68/195 effective 06/30/2022

Body: Resolved in the phase 7 fix round. The Secretary of State's version list for 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100 (ruleId 2918) carries archived versions; the version effective 04/14/2022 (D-78, S676) states 133% (children, § 8.100.4.G.2), 60% (parents and caretaker relatives, G.3) and 185% (pregnant women, G.5), and the next version, effective 06/30/2022 (D-77, S675), states 142%, 68% and 195% (and the 12-month postpartum period of OQ-52). That date matches the CHP+ amendment to 10 CCR 2505-3 § 110.1.E-F effective 06/30/2022 (D-66:933). ma_income_limit_child_pct (CO-191), ma_income_limit_parent_caretaker_pct (CO-192) and ma_income_limit_pregnant_pct (CO-194) now carry dated versions: the earlier figure from 2022-04-14 (the earliest version fetched) to 2022-06-30, and the current figure from 2022-06-30. The volume states no figure before 2022-04-14. The children's presumptive eligibility figure in the 06/30/2022 version (§ 8.100.4.F.2.b) was still 133%; ma_is_presumptively_eligible_child (CO-559) reads the category standard and so applies 142% from 06/30/2022, which matters only for a determination between that date and the later amendment of F.2.b. Items: CO-191, CO-192, CO-194.

### OQ-212. Partly resolved: the transfer-penalty divisor and the institutional personal needs allowance are dated parameters; open: their OM 25-073 figures are unverified

Body: The current § 8.100.7.F.2.c.i (D-65, S648) defines the transfer-penalty divisor as "the average of the regions ... monthly private pay cost for Long-Term Care institution care in the state of Colorado at the time of application", and § 8.100.7.V.3.d.i.2 sets the personal needs allowance base by annual adjustment from $75 (2015); neither figure for 2026 is stated in any document fetchable from the allowed sources (hcpf.colorado.gov, where HCPF publishes both, refuses automated retrieval). transfer_penalty_divisor (CO-832) and ma_personal_needs_allowance_institutional (CO-887) stay supplied facts. The same private-pay rate is the income trust ceiling of OQ-99. Items: CO-832, CO-887, CO-649, CO-659, CO-658.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): resolved pending verification. The product owner supplied the 2025 and 2026 figures from HCPF Operational Memo OM 25-073 (D-125, which could not be retrieved: hcpf.colorado.gov refuses automated requests): the transfer-penalty divisor $10,475.11 (2025) and $10,814.64 (2026), and the nursing facility personal needs allowance $108.73 (2025) and $110.36 (2026). They are recorded as the unverified excerpt S1228 and must be checked against the memo before the items move past Draft. transfer_penalty_divisor (CO-832) and ma_personal_needs_allowance_institutional (CO-887) are now dated parameters (moved to parameters/, IDs and identifiers unchanged). Changed expectations: CO-01 and CO-02 p1 ma_patient_payment 215 -> 179.64 (2030 - 110.36 - 1740); CO-06 p1 ma_transfer_penalty_months 6 -> 4 (54,000 / 10,814.64 = 4.993: four whole months and 30 days). The residential HCBS personal needs allowance (HCPF IM 25-030) is not read by any item and is not modeled.

Class: blocked on a named document
Owner: LTSS policy owner
Assumption: The transfer penalty divisor and the nursing facility personal needs allowance are the 2025 and 2026 figures the product owner supplied from the operational memo.
Blocked on: HCPF Operational Memo OM 25-073, which states the 2025 and 2026 figures (hcpf.colorado.gov refused)

### OQ-213. Partly resolved: five-year rule exemptions - the refugee, asylee and entrant reading of § 8.100.3.G.1.g.iv and the count from qualified status are settled; open: adult parolees, conditional entrants and battered non-citizens

Body: The current § 8.100.3.G.1.g.iv (S656) opens "Be a Legal Permanent Resident(LPR) applying for Medical Assistance, who arrived in the United States on any date is exempt from the five-year rule" and then lists persons "granted asylum", refugees and trafficking victims (iv.9-11), who are often not LPRs; the Cornell copy's iv said "a non-citizen who arrived ... on any date" (D-39). Read literally, a refugee or asylee who has not adjusted to LPR falls under no adult category, which conflicts with 8 U.S.C. 1613(b)(1), which iv cites. ma_is_five_year_bar_exempt (CO-1172) reads the refugee, asylee, Cuban/Haitian entrant and COFA migrant statuses of citizenship_status as exempt whether or not adjusted (v.1-2 say "on any date" in any case), reads a trafficking victim as exempt through ma_five_year_bar_exempt_category (CO-1170), and reads the 40-work-quarters exemption (iv.1) for LPRs only. Separately, the Cornell iii (subject to the five-year rule) also listed parolees of at least one year, conditional entrants and battered non-citizens; the current iii is LPRs only (S655) and those groups appear only in vii (pregnant women and children, S658). An adult in one of those statuses meets no adult status category in the current text; citizenship_status records them as other_qualified_non_citizen, which is_qualified_non_citizen (CO-506) still treats as qualified and therefore subject to the five-year rule, the Cornell reading. Also, iii counts five years from "the individual's date of entry into the United States"; has_held_qualified_status_five_years (CO-508) counts from qualified_status_date (Medicaid reads ma_has_held_qualified_status_five_years_in_month, CO-7550, from first_qualified_status_date since coverage pass 7). Items: CO-509, CO-1172, CO-1170, CO-506, CO-508, CO-7550, CO-7770.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the settled half: 8 U.S.C. 1613(a) runs the five years from entry with a qualified status (restated in coverage pass 7, C7-04 and C7-11, 2026-11-24: counting from the first qualified status, first_qualified_status_date, CO-7770, matches it; qualified_status_date, the date of the status held now, matches it only where that status is the first, so a parolee, battered non-citizen or conditional entrant who later became a lawful permanent resident does not restart the five years at the adjustment; 10 CCR 2506-1 4.305.B.3.a, S12, says the same: "The five (5) year waiting period begins on the date the non-citizen obtains status as a qualified non-citizen or enters the U.S. in a qualifying status"), and 1613(b)(1) (S1150) exempts refugees, asylees, withholding grantees, Cuban and Haitian entrants and Amerasians whether or not adjusted to LPR, which supports CO-1172's reading of iv. Checked 2026-11-21: the current § 8.100.3.G.1.g.iii-iv (D-65) and 8 U.S.C. 1612(b) (D-25), under which covering adult parolees, conditional entrants and battered non-citizens after five years is a State option; no published rule settles it.

HCPF fix round (2026-11-21, docs/reviews/stakeholder-hcpf.md), B-1: the open half is superseded for adults from October 1, 2026. 42 U.S.C. 1396b(v)(5), added by Pub. L. 119-21 sec. 71109 (S1700), allows federal payment for full Medical Assistance only for citizens and nationals, lawful permanent residents, Cuban and Haitian entrants and COFA migrants, except emergency services and the lawfully residing children and pregnant women option; CMS SHO #26-001 (S1702, S1705) lists parolees, battered non-citizens, refugees, asylees and trafficking victims among those who lose it. ma_meets_citizenship_requirement (CO-509) applies the limit from that date through ma_is_in_federal_payment_status_category (CO-2400), so an adult parolee, conditional entrant or battered non-citizen who is not a lawful permanent resident fails status after it whatever the reading of iii; the question remains only for determinations dated before October 1, 2026. Items: CO-509, CO-1172, CO-1170, CO-506, CO-508, CO-2400.

OQ-44 fix (audit fix round, 2026-11-21): ma_five_year_bar_exempt_category (CO-1170) is retired; CO-1172 reads the trafficking victim from immigration_status (trafficking_victim) and the other iv categories from status_before_lpr_adjustment (CO-2700), is_american_indian_born_in_canada_or_tribal_member (CO-2701) and is_hmong_or_highland_lao_veteran (CO-2702); the lumped other_qualified_non_citizen is now parolee_one_year_or_more, conditional_entrant, battered_non_citizen or deportation_withheld, each still qualified (CO-506). The open half is unchanged.

Class: state policy choice (reclassified from "needs Legal" in coverage pass 4, 2026-11-23, docs/reviews/coverage4-medicaid-magi-chp.md section 4: 8 U.S.C. 1612(b) (D-25, on file) makes covering these adults after five years a State option, and the current rule's list differs from the earlier text; no document not on file would decide it: it is Colorado's choice under the option, with Legal advising on the rule's wording)
Owner: Legal, Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: For a determination dated before October 2026, an adult parolee, conditional entrant or battered non-citizen waits five years and is then covered.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.3.G.1.g.iii-iv (D-65), whose subject-to-five-years list now names lawful permanent residents only, and 8 U.S.C. 1612(b) (D-25), under which covering these groups after five years is a State option. The readings: (a) the earlier Cornell text listed them as covered after five years, the reading in force; (b) the current text covers no adult in these statuses. No published rule says which Colorado chose.

### OQ-214. Partly resolved: Cover All Coloradans reaches a child or pregnant person in any category; open: a postpartum person not covered while pregnant reaches only the § 8.100.3.G.1.g.ix.1.a-b list

Body: § 8.100.3.G.1.g.ix.1 (S607) says persons under 19, pregnant or within 12 months postpartum "shall not be excluded from the following Medical Assistance Categories on the basis of immigration status" and lists MAGI Children, MAGI Pregnant, Psych <21, Transitional Medical Assistance, 4 Month Extended Medical Assistance, MAGI prenatal and child presumptive eligibility, the two Buy-In programs, and the Long-Term Care waivers except PACE. The phase 7 fix round (S-2) reads the list as exhaustive: ma_meets_citizenship_requirement (CO-509) no longer carries the population; ma_is_cover_all_coloradans_person (CO-1173) states it; ma_meets_status_for_cover_all_coloradans_categories (CO-1174) is read by the Buy-In programs, HCBS waivers, CHP+ and BCCP (whose § 8.715.2.C.3 cross-references the exception); ma_magi_is_fully_eligible (CO-562) applies it to the Children's and Pregnant categories only; and every other category (MAGI Adult and Parent/Caretaker, SSI-related, institutional care, PACE, the protected groups, OAP SISC B, limited family planning) reads CO-509 alone. So an undocumented postpartum adult whose only category is Adult is emergency-only, an undocumented disabled child is not SSI-related eligible (but is covered in the Children's category or a waiver when eligible there), and a pregnant undocumented woman fails limited family planning's status test and reaches the reproductive health-care program. If HCPF reads ix.1 as reaching every category, CO-509 should again include CO-1173. Items: CO-509, CO-1173, CO-1174, CO-1175, CO-562, CO-510, CO-1151, CO-1152, CO-1154, CO-652, CO-653.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): partly resolved. C.R.S. 25.5-2-104(2) (S1217) makes a child under 19 who would be eligible for medical assistance but for immigration status eligible for state medical assistance, with the same benefits (25.5-2-104(1)); C.R.S. 25.5-5-201(6)(a) (S1218) makes a pregnant non-citizen who meets every other requirement eligible for medical assistance, with twelve months postpartum under (6)(b). Neither is limited to the categories § 8.100.3.G.1.g.ix.1.a-b lists, so for a child or a pregnant person the exhaustive reading was wrong: ma_magi_is_fully_eligible (CO-562) now counts a child or pregnant person in any MAGI category, and ma_is_covered_under_cover_all_coloradans (CO-1175) covers a child or pregnant person who meets every requirement of any categorical program other than status (an undocumented disabled child meeting only the SSI-related standards is covered, not emergency-only). What stays open: a postpartum person who was not covered while pregnant, whom the statute does not reach, still reaches only the rule's ix.1.a-b list (CO-1173 carries the postpartum term). No household expectation changed.

Class: state policy choice (reclassified from "needs Legal" in coverage pass 4, 2026-11-23, docs/reviews/coverage4-medicaid-magi-chp.md section 4: the rule's list (S607) and the statutes (S1217, S1218) are on file and the statutes reach only children and people covered while pregnant; whether Colorado extends the exception beyond its list for a postpartum person is the State's choice, with Legal advising; no unfetched document would decide it)
Owner: Legal, Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: A postpartum person who was not covered while pregnant is exempt from the immigration test only in the categories the Colorado rule lists.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.3.G.1.g.ix.1 (D-65, S607) lists the categories, and C.R.S. 25.5-2-104(2) and 25.5-5-201(6) (S1217, S1218) reach children and pregnant people, with postpartum coverage only for a person covered while pregnant. The readings: (a) the list governs for this person, the reading in force; (b) the exception reaches every category. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-215. Resolved: limited family planning has no age or gender restriction; § 8.730's child-bearing age describes the services

Body: § 8.100.4.G.8 says "The limited family planning services eligibility category has no age or gender restriction" (S613), so ma_family_planning_status (CO-1154) reports limited_family_planning for anyone in the income band who meets status, including CO-01's 81-year-old man and CO-71's six-year-old. But § 8.730.1 defines Family Planning Services as "those services provided to individuals of child-bearing age" (S677), and § 8.730.2.A says "All Medicaid clients of childbearing age are eligible" (S616). Whether the limited category requires reproductive capacity or childbearing age is not settled by the fetched text; the item follows G.8's letter, and has_reproductive_capacity (CO-1103) is read only for the reproductive health-care program, which C.R.S. 25.5-2-103 conditions on it. Items: CO-1154.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): Checked 2026-11-21: § 8.100.4.G.8 ("no age or gender restriction") against § 8.730.1-2 (childbearing age) in D-65; no published rule settles it.

Audit fix round 4 (2026-11-21): re-read § 8.100.4.G.8 and § 8.730 in the current texts (D-65, D-67; rule versions effective 11/14/2026) and decided. G.8 is the eligibility rule for the category and says it "has no age or gender restriction and is available for those not eligible for a Medicaid eligibility category that provides full coverage" (S613), as § 8.100.4.F.1 and § 8.100.3.R.1.d say for its presumptive form ("There are no age or gender restrictions"). The federal group G.8 implements is defined by income and not being pregnant, with no age or sex condition (42 U.S.C. 1396a(ii)(1), D-204, S2114). § 8.730.1 defines Family Planning Services, the benefit, as "those services provided to individuals of child-bearing age, including sexually active minors" (S677), in the words of the federal service definition, 42 U.S.C. 1396d(a)(4)(C) ("family planning services and supplies furnished ... to individuals of child-bearing age (including minors who can be considered to be sexually active)", S2114), and § 8.730.2.A says which Medicaid clients may receive those services (S616). They govern the services, not eligibility for the category. ma_family_planning_status (CO-1154) keeps reporting limited_family_planning for anyone in the income band who meets status, CO-01's 81-year-old man and CO-71's six-year-old included, and its precision now says so plainly. Which family planning services such a member can use is a benefit question outside this volume. Items: CO-1154.

### OQ-216. Resolved: lottery and gambling winnings - the boundaries, the increment, several receipts per person and the hardship exemption for any household member

Body: § 8.100.4.C.1.b.xiv (S669) counts winnings "less than $79,999" in the month received, "over $79,999 but less than $89,999" over two months, and adds a month "For every additional $10,000 over $89,999". The text places exactly $79,999 and exactly $89,999 in no paragraph; CO-1183 and CO-1185 read $79,999 as the month received and $89,999 as two months. "Every additional $10,000" is read as each started $10,000 band ($90,000 to $99,999 gives three months), matching the federal rule's $10,000 increments; a full-$10,000 reading would give two months up to $99,999. No upper limit on months is stated. The supplied facts (CO-1181, CO-1182) carry one receipt per person, the most recent, so overlapping spreads from two wins are not added. xiv.4 (household members count the winnings only in the month received) is modeled by counting the whole amount in each member's income in the month received (CO-1186) and swapping in the spread amount only for the winner's own household (CO-503, CO-1187). The hardship exemption (xiv.5: severe medical or financial hardship, written request within 30 days of a denial or termination) is a county decision after the fact and is not modeled. Items: CO-1181, CO-1182, CO-1183, CO-1184, CO-1185, CO-1186, CO-1187, CO-503.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): partly resolved. 42 U.S.C. 1396a(e)(14)(K) (S1234) sets the thresholds at $80,000, $90,000 and $100,000 with one more month per $10,000 increment, which ma_lottery_winnings_spread_months (CO-1185) matched at every whole-dollar boundary ($79,999 one month, $89,999 two, $99,999 three, $100,000 four; time audit T-4 (2026-11-23): not to the cent, so CO-1183 is now the statute's $80,000 compared with "less than" and CO-1185 counts whole $10,000 increments above it, under a statute_override of Colorado's $79,999 and $89,999), and caps the period at 120 months, which Colorado does not restate: new parameter ma_lottery_winnings_spread_max_months (CO-1988) and the cap in CO-1185. Still open: one receipt per person and the hardship exemption (State procedures under (K)(iii)) need facts this sweep did not add.

Phase-10 needs-fact sweep (2026-11-21): the hardship exemption is resolved for the winner. New fact ma_lottery_winnings_hardship_exemption_granted (CO-2222; § 8.100.4.C.1.b.xiv.5, S1519): under 42 U.S.C. 1396a(e)(14)(K)(iii) (S1520) the person "shall continue to be eligible", so ma_lottery_winnings_countable_for_recipient (CO-1187) counts nothing for a person granted it (new test CO-1187-T7). Still open, as structure rather than a reading: one receipt per person (several receipts need a per-month receipt fact and a sum over the spread months, restating every case's winnings), and an exemption granted to another household member denied because of the winnings counted in the month received (CO-1186), which would need the exemption stated per member and winner. No case has winnings, so no case states the new fact.

Audit fix round 4 (2026-11-21, fixed in full at the product owner's direction): both remaining parts are modeled. (1) Several receipts per person: lottery_gambling_winnings_received (CO-1181, renamed from lottery_gambling_winnings_amount and made a per-month fact; lottery_gambling_winnings_month, CO-1182, retired) states each month's receipt. ma_lottery_winnings_spread_months (CO-1185) is read [at] each receipt month, and ma_lottery_winnings_countable_for_recipient (CO-1187) adds, for the ma_lottery_winnings_spread_max_months months ending with the month evaluated, each receipt's equal installment while its spread lasts (42 U.S.C. 1396a(e)(14)(K)(i)-(ii), S1234; § 8.100.4.C.1.b.xiv.1-3, S669), so overlapping spreads are summed (CO-1187-T8 to T10). The sum over months needed a new pattern, P54 ("the sum of [number] for each month in [months]", with "that month" bound to each month; docs/conventions.md, docs/operators.md). The other members' month-received count (xiv.4) is the receipt itself, read by ma_person_magi_income (CO-502); ma_lottery_winnings_counted_in_month_received (CO-1186), which restated it, is retired. Receipts in the same month are stated together and spread as one amount (OQ-592). (2) Another member's hardship exemption: xiv.5 lets "an applicant/member" denied or terminated "due to Lottery/Gambling winnings" request the exemption (S1519), and 1396a(e)(14)(K)(iii) keeps the person eligible (S1520); ma_lottery_winnings_hardship_exemption_granted (CO-2222) is stated per member, and ma_magi_household_income (CO-503) counts no member's winnings in the household of a person granted it, winner or not (CO-503-T5, T6). If the exemption is not stated, Medicaid reads it as not granted. Complexity added (reported to the product owner): one engine pattern (P54, two operators, three unit tests); facts: one supplied fact rescoped from per person to per month, one supplied fact and one derived item retired (net one fewer of each); items changed: CO-502, CO-503, CO-1185, CO-1187, CO-2222; rule tests: five new (CO-1187-T8 to T10, CO-503-T5, T6), 21 restated, three retired with CO-1186; household cases: 51 person facts moved to month defaults (lottery_gambling_winnings_received: 0), no expectation changed. Items: CO-1181, CO-1185, CO-1187, CO-2222, CO-502, CO-503.

### OQ-217. Resolved: discharged student loans follow the Internal Revenue Code; the named exclusions are applied consistently

Body: § 8.100.4.C.1.b.xv (S670) counts discharged, forgiven or cancelled student loan debt as the borrower's income "with certain exceptions": none when discharged for the death or permanent and total disability of the student "during tax years 2018 through 2025" (xv.1), and "At the Departments discretion" none under programs "which include, but not limited to" Public Service, Teacher and Healthcare loan forgiveness and Closed School discharge (xv.2). ma_countable_discharged_student_loan_income (CO-1190) applies the four named programs always and no other program, and reads the tax-year window by the month of discharge. Whether the discretion is exercised case by case, and which other programs it reaches, is not stated. Items: CO-1190, CO-1189.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): Checked 2026-11-21: § 8.100.4.C.1.b.xv (S670, D-65), which reserves the exclusions to the Department's discretion; no published rule settles it.

HCPF fix round (2026-11-21, docs/reviews/stakeholder-hcpf.md), S-10: the reviewer answered that HCPF applies the named programs consistently, not case by case, and that MAGI follows the Internal Revenue Code (42 CFR 435.603(e)). 26 U.S.C. 108(f)(5) as amended by the American Rescue Plan excluded every student loan discharge from 2021 through 2025 (S1709), and as amended by Pub. L. 119-21 sec. 70119 excludes discharges on account of death or total and permanent disability after 2025 (S1708). ma_countable_discharged_student_loan_income (CO-1190) now applies both, with the dates as parameters (CO-2463 to CO-2465). § 8.100.4.C.1.b.xv's "tax years 2018 through 2025" and its counting of other 2021-2025 discharges conflict with the Code; flagged for HCPF rule cleanup. Items: CO-1190.

### OQ-250. Partly resolved: Home Care Allowance - the HCBS transition and the "home" test are modeled; open: the tier dollar amounts

Body: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.570.13.C.3 (S714) assigns a tier from the functional capacity and need-for-paid-care scores but the rule (D-80) states no maximum authorized dollar amount for any tier; 3.570.13.D only says the Case Management Agency "shall not approve the maximum authorized HCA amount for the tier" in stated cases, and 3.570.11.B (S713) gives a payment floor of $977 for all tiers. af_hca_grant (CO-1219) therefore reads the CMA-authorized amount as a supplied fact (hca_authorized_amount, CO-1218) and raises it to the floor; the tier maximums should become a parameter table once a source states them. Also not modeled: the up-to-three-month stay on HCA for a client found functionally eligible for HCBS at reassessment whose transition is delayed outside the client's control (3.570.11.A.2.b, S712) - af_hca_eligibility_status (CO-1217) denies HCA on functional HCBS eligibility at any time; and the "home" definition's exclusion of a homeless shelter or other temporary setting (3.570.12, S718) - only a facility stay (snap_resides_in_institution_providing_meals) is tested. af_hca_functionally_eligible_for_hcbs (CO-1206) reads HCBS functional eligibility as nursing facility level of care plus a waiver target group, the Medicaid chapter's own pair of functional facts. Items: CO-1206, CO-1217, CO-1218, CO-1219.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): (S-5) the homeless-shelter half of the "home" question is closed: af_hca_eligibility_status (CO-1217) now reads snap_is_homeless (CO-037) beside snap_resides_in_institution_providing_meals; an "other temporary setting" still has no fact. (N-3) 3.570.11.E.2 (D-80) says the State Supplementary Payment brings "the client's monthly benefit amount equal to the payment floor standard defined for the program(s) in which the client is enrolled". The volume floors each program's grant separately (af_oap_grant, CO-951, to $189; af_hca_grant, CO-1219, to $977), so a client on OAP and HCA (CO-13 p1) receives 352 + 977; if the floor applies to the client's combined Adult Financial benefit, her 352 + 900 already exceeds $977 and no HCA supplement is due. The per-program reading is kept because the floor is stated for each program (3.530.C, 3.546.E, 3.570.11.B). Items: CO-1217, CO-1219, CO-951.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the tier dollar amounts remain unconfirmed. 9 CCR 2503-5 3.570.11.B and 3.570.13.C-D (D-80) and C.R.S. 26-2-122.3 (D-59) state no tier maximum. CDHS's Adult Financial Programs page (D-135, S1319, retrieved 2026-11-21) states "The HCA payment floor standard for all three tiers is $977, effective January 1, 2026" and gives no per-tier amounts; it corroborates af_hca_payment_floor (CO-1202), which now cites it. Outside research reported by the product owner conflicts on the tiers: one source calls the $330, $472 and $605 tier maximums of the Cornell copy of 9 CCR 2503-5 3.570 (effective 2018) out of date; another says a December 2025 Adult Financial Reference Guide still lists them. Neither the Reference Guide nor a current tier schedule is in the volume, and a $977 floor for every tier above all three of those maximums suggests they are superseded, so no per-tier figure is adopted and af_hca_grant (CO-1219) keeps reading the authorized amount raised to the floor. The other two parts are resolved: the three-month HCBS transition (3.570.11.A.2.b, S712) is the new supplied af_hca_is_in_hcbs_transition_period (CO-2009), and the "other temporary setting" of 3.570.12 (S718) the new af_hca_lives_in_shelter_or_temporary_setting (CO-2010); af_hca_eligibility_status (CO-1217) reads both, each as false when a case does not state it (the old behavior, so no expectation changed); new tests CO-1217-T9 and CO-1217-T10. Only the tier amounts stay open.

CDHS stakeholder review fix round (2026-11-21) (C-5): the CDHS reviewer confirms that the tier maximums are set administratively within appropriations (3.570.11.D) and published to the CMAs, not in rule; the schedule is with CDHS's Adult Financial program office, and cdhs.colorado.gov refused this session. Still open: blocked on a refused host.

Coverage review 2 of Adult Financial (G-3, 2026-11-22): the statement above that the "home" test is modeled had stopped being true after the vocabulary round (R-8), which made the facility test resides_in_medical_institution (CO-1742), a medical institution only; an assisted living residence, group or host home or other long-term care facility passed as a home. af_hca_eligibility_status (CO-1217) now treats every residence_setting_type other than a private dwelling, room and board or commercial property as not a home (3.570.12 "Home"; 3.510 "Facility", S3501; 3.570.17.C.4.a and C.7), keeping C.4.b's thirty days only for a medical stay away from a home the client keeps. The "home" test is modeled again; only the tier amounts stay open.

Class: blocked on a named document
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: The Home Care Allowance pays the amount the case management agency authorizes, raised to the $977 floor.
Blocked on: CDHS's Home Care Allowance tier maximum schedule, set administratively under 9 CCR 2503-5 3.570.11.D (cdhs.colorado.gov refused)

### OQ-251. Partly resolved: the FY2027 elderly/disabled resource limit is settled; open: the date from which FNS applies H.R.1's utility-allowance limit, which Colorado's SNAP rule has not adopted

Body: Two places where the version of 10 CCR 2506-1 effective 2026-10-01 (D-82) and the federal source differ. (1) 4.407.31.A.1.b (S743) still grants the Heating and Cooling Utility Allowance to any household that "Received a Low-Income Energy Assistance Program (LEAP) or an Energy Electronic Benefit Transfer (E-EBT) payment within the previous twelve (12) month period"; Pub. L. 119-21 sec. 10103 (S136) limits that route to households with an elderly or disabled member. snap_entitled_to_heating_cooling_allowance (CO-1243) follows the statute, as the volume does for secs. 10102 and 10108 (OQ-1, OQ-31, OQ-32). (2) 4.408.E (S748) states the resource limit for a household with a member aged 60 or older or disabled as $4,500 "Effective October 1, 2024"; FNS's FY2027 table (D-83, S732) states $4,750. snap_resource_limit_elderly_disabled (CO-119) takes the FNS figure from 2026-10-01, because the limit is the federally indexed figure of 7 U.S.C. 2014(g) and Colorado's other COLA tables in the same rule were updated to FY2027. The limit only matters for a Standard Eligibility household (categorically eligible households skip the resource test). Items: CO-119, CO-1243.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): (2) is resolved: the elderly/disabled resource limit is the figure 7 U.S.C. 2014(g)(1) indexes and FNS publishes ($4,750 for FY2027, D-83, S732); a State rule cannot set a lower federal resource limit, so snap_resource_limit_elderly_disabled (CO-119) keeps the FNS version (no change). (1) stays open on its date only: sec. 10103 governs and snap_entitled_to_heating_cooling_allowance (CO-1243) follows it, but FNS's "SNAP Implementation of the One Big Beautiful Bill Act of 2025 - Treatment of Energy Assistance Payments - Revised" memo, which would state the implementation date, is served only from www.usda.gov, which refused the request (Access Denied). Items: CO-1243.

CDHS stakeholder review fix round (2026-11-21): as for OQ-1. Sec. 10103 (D-5) has no effective-date clause; snap_entitled_to_heating_cooling_allowance (CO-1243) now carries an effective range from July 4, 2025 and a statute_override note naming 10 CCR 2506-1 4.407.31.A.1.b; the CDHS position on ongoing cases (recertification) is recorded as program knowledge in OQ-533, not adopted. Items: CO-1243.

Class: blocked on a named document
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: The narrower route to the heating and cooling utility allowance applies from the day the 2025 Act was enacted.
Blocked on: FNS memorandum "SNAP Implementation of the One Big Beautiful Bill Act of 2025 - Treatment of Energy Assistance Payments - Revised" (www.usda.gov refused, HTTP 403 on 2026-11-22; fns.usda.gov links to it)

Checked 2026-11-22: Pub. L. 119-21 sec. 10103 (D-5) has no effective-date clause, and 10 CCR 2506-1 4.407.31.A.1.b (D-82, S743) still grants the allowance for any LEAP payment; only the memorandum states the date FNS applies the change.

### OQ-252. Colorado Works sanctions and diversion: defaults and county options

Body: (1) work_sanction_level_served (CO-1261) is read as "none" when a case does not state it (cw_unit_sanction_level, CO-1264, with `otherwise`): a sanction is "a reduction in Colorado Works grant payments for an established period of time as a result of not participating in the Workforce Development program" (9 CCR 2503-6 3.601 "Sanction") that the county imposes and serves (3.608.4.A, C), so a unit has one only when the county has imposed it, and a case that states no sanction level has none; the level is the county's finding, as decision 4 of 2026-11-23 allows for a county finding. A case that should test a sanction states the level. (Coverage pass 6 CA6-13: the reason was given as case coverage, which decision 4 does not allow; the reading is unchanged.) (2) 9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.8.A.6 (S762) lets a county pay diversion above basic-cash-assistance eligibility "at county option, and based on the county's policy, a higher income limit not to exceed the maximum criteria established in the State plan for non-recurrent short-term benefits"; neither the county limits nor the State plan maximum are in the fetched text, so the county test is a supplied yes/no (cw_meets_county_diversion_income_limit, CO-1268). The current rule states one diversion program with that county option; it does not state separate "state diversion" and "county diversion" programs, so cw_diversion_eligible (CO-1270) is one outcome. (3) The diversion payment amount is needs-based and not modeled. Items: CO-1261, CO-1264, CO-1268, CO-1270.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): (S-1) (2) is narrowed: the county's higher income limit relaxes only the income test. 3.606.8.A.6 pays diversion to a client "eligible for basic cash assistance, or, at county option ... a higher income limit", so cw_diversion_eligible (CO-1270) now accepts the county limit only for a unit whose sole failing is ineligible_income; a unit with no dependent child, a residency, status or living-arrangement failure, a reached time limit or a sanction closure is not diversion-eligible whatever the county limit. Items: CO-1270.

Checked 2026-11-21: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.8.A.6 (S762) leaves the higher income limit to "the county's policy" up to "the maximum criteria established in the State plan for non-recurrent short-term benefits"; neither the county policies nor the State plan maximum is in the rule, and the sanction level is the county's finding (3.601 "Sanction", 3.608.4), not a text question. No published rule settles it.

CDHS stakeholder review fix round (2026-11-21) (C-3): part (3) is now modeled as far as the text allows. The diversion amount is the county-approved amount in the agreement (cw_county_approved_diversion_amount, CO-2559) and cw_diversion_payment (CO-2560) pays it when cw_diversion_eligible holds; 3.606.8 states no dollar cap (OQ-531).

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Coverage review CW-27 (2026-11-22): 3.606.8.A.4 (S3152) says the diversion period of ineligibility "shall not be longer than the equivalent maximum grant payment for the household size over the same period of time". The POI is the issuing county's (in_diversion_period_of_ineligibility, CO-1269, a supplied fact) and the volume does not check that bound; since coverage review CW-1 the POI bars basic cash assistance for the unit (cw_eligibility_status, CO-921).

Assumption: A case that states no sanction has none, and a county's higher diversion income limit is entered as a yes or no for the case. The county's POI is taken as stated, without checking the A.4 bound.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.8.A.6 (D-81, S762) leaves the higher income limit to "the county's policy" up to "the maximum criteria established in the State plan for non-recurrent short-term benefits"; neither the county policies nor the State plan figure is in the rule. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-253. How LEAP's Step B, Step D and Step F adjustments combine, and which poverty guideline Step D uses

Body: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.758.47 Step G (S772) says the benefit is determined "by subtracting the applicable adjustments listed above, in Steps B-F from the household's estimated home heating costs (EHHC)". Step B is a percentage "of the estimated home heating costs (EHHC) of the applicant household to be adjusted" and Step D reduces "the amount the of estimated home heating costs (EHHC)" by the income-band percentage (S770, S771), so leap_benefit_amount (CO-1281) reads both as shares of the same costs (after the Step C electric portion and the Step E division) and subtracts their sum; a sequential reading (apply B, then D to what remains) would give a slightly larger benefit. Step F deducts the $180 allowance "from the amount remaining after Step B", which the item applies after both percentages. Step D's bands are stated as "Federal Poverty Level" percentages without naming a table; leap_household_income_pct_fpl (CO-1278) uses the HHS guideline in force on the determination date and annualizes the month's income. The statewide adjustment rate and the program-year minimum and maximum are not stated in the rule (3.758.48 says the State notifies counties) and are supplied. Items: CO-1272, CO-1276, CO-1277, CO-1278, CO-1281.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): (C-2) Step E divides the estimated home heating cost "by the number of separate households sharing the living arrangements" (3.758.47). The target household (CO-01/CO-02) stated one sharing household while the engine formed two LEAP households in the same home, each taking the full $900; the cases now state that the family buys heat in common (shares_heating_fuel_with), so it is one LEAP household and the count of one is consistent. A case that forms more than one LEAP household in one dwelling must state the count to match; no rule derives it from the households formed. Items: CO-1280.

Phase-10 open-question sweep: checked 2026-11-21: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.758.47 Steps A-G and 3.758.48 (D-86, S770-S772) and the Department's LEAP page (D-144); no published rule settles how Steps B, D and F combine or names the poverty table for Step D, and the program-year adjustment, minimum and maximum are not published there.

CDHS stakeholder review fix round (2026-11-21): the CDHS reviewer accepts the additive reading provisionally; Step G's "subtracting the applicable adjustments ... in Steps B-F from ... EHHC" supports it. The LEAP office should confirm it against the LEAP system's calculation before approval. Not resolved by text.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Energy assistance (LEAP) policy owner
Assumption: The statewide and income-band reductions are each taken as a share of the same heating costs and added together, and the income bands use the federal poverty guideline in force on the determination date.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.758.47 Steps A-G and 3.758.48 (D-86, S770-S772) and the amendment effective November 1, 2026 (D-173) say the adjustments are subtracted but not in what order, and name no poverty table for Step D. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-254. Partly resolved: CCCAP parent fee - children in care, the post-July 2028 formula and the county waivers are modeled; open: the dollars at the post-2028 band edges

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.124.P (D-87, S785), in force July 1, 2021 through July 31, 2028, assesses 1% of gross countable household income at or below 100% of the FPG, and above it 1% of income plus 14% of every dollar above the FPG plus $15 for each additional child "when households are requesting care for more than one (1) child". (1) No fact states for which children care is requested; cccap_parent_fee_formula_amount (CO-1289) counts the household's eligible children (cccap_is_eligible_child), which overstates the fee for a household with an eligible child not in care. (2) 3.124.Q (S786) replaces the formula from August 1, 2028 with marginal rates by FPG band (1% to 100%, 3% 101-130%, 5% 131-160%, 7% above 160%); CO-1289 carries an effective range ending 2028-08-01 and the Q formula is not modeled, so cccap_parent_fee is unknown for later dates. Q's table also leaves the band edges between 100% and 101% (and 130%/131%, 160%/161%) unstated. (3) cccap_parent_fee (CO-1290) models the no-income, protective-services, Colorado-Works-non-paid-activity exceptions and the 20% quality and 55% part-time reductions, but not the county-discretion waivers (Head Start dual enrollment, teen-parent hardship, declared disaster, the $5 hardship reduction), the homelessness stabilization period, or a protective services household whose child has countable income (3.124.G, I.1, I.5, I.6, I.8, S, X); it treats a Colorado Works household with no earned or self-employment income as one in a non-paid activity. Items: CO-1289, CO-1290.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): (N-2) 3.124.A says parent fees "must be calculated in whole dollars by dropping the cents", and H and U reduce "the regularly calculated" / "above-calculated" fee. cccap_parent_fee (CO-1290) applies the 20% and 55% reductions to the formula amount with its cents and drops the cents once at the end; dropping the cents first and again after a reduction can differ by $1 (for example 192.50 x 0.44 = 84.70 against 192 x 0.44 = 84.48, both $84 here). The single final drop is kept as the reading closest to "regularly calculated". Items: CO-1290.

Phase-10 open-question sweep. (1) Resolved: cccap_is_eligible_child (CO-980) now requires cccap_child_needs_care_for_part_of_day (CO-2108), care requested because the child needs care for part of the day, so cccap_parent_fee_formula_amount (CO-1289) counts only the children for whom care is requested. (2) Resolved in part: CO-1289 now computes 3.124.Q from August 2028 with the marginal rates 1%, 3% (CO-2115), 5% (CO-2116) and 7% (CO-2117) of the income in each band of the monthly FPG, reading the bands as continuous at 100%, 130% and 160%; checked 2026-11-21: 3.124.Q (S786) and 3.124.P (S785); no published rule settles the dollars between 100% and 101% (and 130/131%, 160/161%), so that reading stays open. (3) Stays open: the county-discretion waivers and the other exceptions of 3.124 need facts about Head Start enrollment, hardship, declared disasters and the homelessness stabilization period that no case states; checked 2026-11-21 against 3.124.G, I, S and X (D-87).

CDHS stakeholder review fix round (2026-11-21): (C-8) the $15 per additional child now counts the eligible children for whom care is requested (cccap_care_is_requested_for_child, CO-2579, read as requested when not stated). (C-7) the teen-parent hardship waiver (I.1), Child Welfare Child Care (I.4) and the homelessness stabilization period (I.5) are modeled with facts CO-2575-CO-2577, and a protective services household whose children have countable income pays the formula fee (I.6) on that income (cccap_parent_fee_income, CO-2578; OQ-536). Still open: the dollars at the post-2028 band edges (the CDHS reviewer reads them as continuous, as the draft does) and the county-discretion waivers of 3.124.G, I.8, S and X.

Open-question classification pass (2026-11-22): part (3) is resolved. 8 CCR 1403-1 3.124.G, I.8, S and X (D-87, S2302) let a county waive the fee for a child dually enrolled in Head Start or Early Head Start, for a declared disaster or emergency (up to twelve months) or for a one- or two-teen-parent household for whom it is a hardship, and reduce it to $5 for hardship under an approved county plan. Each is a county decision documented in the case: new supplied facts cccap_parent_fee_waived_by_county (CO-3505) and cccap_parent_fee_hardship_reduction_approved (CO-3506), each read as false when not stated, and the parameter cccap_parent_fee_hardship_reduced_amount (CO-3507, $5). The fee the formula assesses after the quality and part-time reductions is now its own item, cccap_parent_fee_assessed (CO-3508), and cccap_parent_fee (CO-1290) pays nothing under a waiver and no more than $5 under a hardship reduction (new tests CO-1290-T11 to T13, CO-3508-T1 and T2). No household case states a waiver; no expectation changed. Still open: the dollars between 100% and 101%, 130% and 131%, and 160% and 161% of the guideline after July 2028. Items: CO-1289, CO-1290, CO-3505, CO-3506, CO-3507, CO-3508.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: After July 2028 the parent fee bands are read as continuous at 100, 130 and 160 percent of the poverty guideline.

Checked 2026-11-22: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.124.Q (D-87, S786) states the bands as "101-130%", "131-160%" and above, in whole percentages, and 3.124.P (S785) states the earlier formula; neither says which band an income between two whole percentages falls in. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-255. Partly resolved: Summer EBT (SUN Bucks) - no Colorado rule exists, and school age, special provision schools, the guideline year and the household reading of categorical eligibility are settled; open: Colorado's optional categorical sources
