Body: The SUN Bucks chapter is built from 7 CFR part 292 (D-88), FNS's 2026 benefit levels notice (D-89) and the 2026-2027 school meal Income Eligibility Guidelines notice (D-90). (1) No Colorado rule for Summer EBT was found: the Secretary of State's lists for the Department of Human Services' SNAP (10 CCR 2506-1), Income Maintenance (9 CCR 2503) and Food Distribution (12 CCR 2514-1) agencies carry none, and 10 CCR 2506-1 (D-82) does not mention Summer EBT; Colorado's Plan for Operations and Management (292.8), which states its streamlined-certification sources and summer operational period, was not fetched. (2) "School aged" is Colorado's compulsory-attendance age (292.2, S793); the Colorado statute was not fetched, so sebt_is_school_aged (CO-1292) is supplied rather than derived from date_of_birth. (3) Whether Colorado uses Medicaid or another means-tested program as a streamlined-certification source (292.12(d)(2)) is not stated in any fetched text; such a ground enters through sebt_other_categorical_basis (CO-1296), with SNAP and TANF read from the volume's own facts (snap_is_eligible, receives_tanf_cash). (4) The guideline chart in D-90 is an image; sebt_income_eligibility_limit (CO-1297) derives the monthly figure from the method the notice states (2026 poverty guideline x 1.85, rounded up, then divided by twelve and rounded up), using the HHS guideline in force on the determination date, so January-June determinations use the new year's poverty figures before the July 1 guideline change (292.5(b)). (5) The special-provision-school distinctions of 292.6(c) (no school-meal application route other than the NSLP/SBP or Summer EBT application) are not separately modeled. Items: CO-1292, CO-1296, CO-1297, CO-1298.

Phase-8 fix round (docs/reviews/cash-snap-other-phase-8.md): (S-4) part (4) is fixed rather than recorded: sebt_income_eligibility_limit (CO-1297) now reads the poverty guideline of the school meal guideline year (sebt_poverty_guideline_table, CO-1519; sebt_poverty_guideline_each_additional, CO-1520), versioned from July 1, 2026 to July 1, 2027, so a determination dated January-June 2027 uses the guidelines in effect on that date (292.5(b)); a date outside the fetched guideline year is unknown rather than computed from the wrong year. (N-4) categorical eligibility in 292.2 (S792) reaches a child when "one or more children in that household are receiving assistance under SNAP, TANF ..."; sebt_eligibility_status (CO-1298) reads the child's own snap_is_eligible and receives_tanf_cash, so an ineligible-member child (for example undocumented) in a SNAP household with a receiving sibling is categorical under the text and not under the item. No fact states the school meal household's membership (school_meal_household_size is a count), so the household reading cannot be modeled yet. Items: CO-1292, CO-1296, CO-1297, CO-1298, CO-1519, CO-1520.

Phase-10 open-question sweep. (1) Confirmed: there is no Colorado Summer EBT rule. The Secretary of State's lists of the Department of Human Services' rule divisions and of the Income Maintenance, SNAP, Human Services Administration, Special Projects and Food Distribution series, and of the State Board of Education's rules (whose 1 CCR 301-3 Food and Nutrition Services, effective 06/30/2015, does not mention summer benefits), carry none (D-143, S1405), although C.R.S. 26-2-309(4)(a)(I) and (VI) direct the state department to "Establish eligibility criteria and distribute benefits consistent with federal law" and to "Promulgate rules" (D-142, S1404). The chapter therefore rests on 7 CFR part 292 alone. (2) Resolved: C.R.S. 22-33-104(1)(a) (D-141, S1403) requires attendance of "every child who has attained the age of six years on or before August 1 of each year and is under the age of seventeen years"; sebt_is_school_aged (CO-1292) is now derived from date_of_birth against the most recent August 1 with the new parameters compulsory_school_attendance_min_age (CO-2101, 6) and compulsory_school_attendance_age_limit (CO-2102, 17), and the cases no longer state it. In CO-01/CO-02 p4 (17 on August 1, 2026) is no longer school aged; her SUN Bucks results do not change because she is enrolled in an NSLP school (292.6(b)). (3) Stays open: checked 2026-11-21: C.R.S. 26-2-309 (D-142) and the Secretary of State's rule lists (D-143); no published rule settles which optional streamlined-certification sources Colorado uses. (4) Was fixed in the phase-8 fix round. (5) Resolved: the only difference 292.6(c) makes for a special provision school is that 292.6(b)(3)'s "otherwise determined eligible" route is closed; sebt_other_categorical_basis (CO-1296) now says that an individual certification counts for such a child only when made through an NSLP/SBP application. The household-membership reading of categorical eligibility (N-4) remains as stated above.

CDHS stakeholder review fix round (2026-11-21) (C-11): the reviewer's understanding of Colorado's streamlined-certification sources is program knowledge to be confirmed by CDHS and CDE from the Plan for Operations and Management, which is not published on an allowed host; sebt_other_categorical_basis stays a supplied fact. CDE is now a co-approver of SUN Bucks items (volume.yaml).

Hard-coded numbers fix round (audit D-1): sebt_income_eligibility_limit (CO-1297) reads the federal table as of the most recent July 1 (sebt_income_guideline_start_month, CO-3015; P25 and P50); the guideline-year copies CO-1519 and CO-1520 are retired.

Open-question classification pass (2026-11-22): the household reading of categorical eligibility (N-4) is resolved with OQ-552: the household is the persons living with the child as one economic unit (7 CFR 292.2, S2301), read through the new lives_as_one_economic_unit_with relationship (sebt_household_child_receives_snap_or_tanf, CO-2708). Only Colorado's optional streamlined-certification sources remain.

Class: blocked on a named document
Owner: SNAP policy owner, School meals (CDE) co-approver
Assumption: Only SNAP and TANF receipt, and the other grounds a case states for a child, make a child categorically eligible; Colorado is not assumed to use any further program as a source.
Blocked on: Colorado's Summer EBT Plan for Operations and Management under 7 CFR 292.8, which names its streamlined-certification sources (not published on an allowed host; cdhs.colorado.gov refused)

### OQ-300. Resolved: each program has its own verification request date

Body: Each program runs its period to provide verification from its own written request: SNAP from the request for the particular verification (7 CFR 273.2(h)(1)(i)(C), S813; 4.205.2.B, S834), Colorado Works and Adult Financial from the written request (3.604.3.A, S871; 3.520.4.D.1, S879), CCCAP from the noticing period (3.111.H.5.e, S891), LEAP from the postmarked notice (3.756.12.D, S899), and Medical Assistance from the notice that opens the reasonable opportunity period (435.956(b)(2), S841). A PEAK application usually produces one CBMS checklist, so the volume carries one supplied verification_request_date per person and every program's due date reads it. A case in which programs sent separate requests on different dates cannot state both; the supplier states the earliest outstanding one. If per-program request dates are needed, the fact should be split by program prefix (snap_verification_request_date and so on) and each due-date item repointed. Items: CO-1343, CO-1392, CO-1413, CO-1414, CO-1426, CO-1437, CO-1450, CO-1461.

Phase-10 open-question sweep: resolved for CCCAP and LEAP. cccap_verification_request_date (CO-2120) and leap_verification_request_date (CO-2121) state each program's own request date, and cccap_verification_due_date (CO-1450) and leap_verification_due_date (CO-1461) read it, falling back to the shared verification_request_date when it is not stated (documented on each fact). The SNAP, Medical Assistance, CHP+, Colorado Works and Adult Financial due dates belong to the other sweeps and still read the shared date.

Phase-10 needs-fact sweep (2026-11-21): resolved for Medical Assistance and CHP+. ma_verification_request_date (CO-2217) and chp_verification_request_date (CO-2218) state each program's own request date. ma_verification_due_date (CO-1414) reads Medical Assistance's, and the new chp_verification_due_date (CO-2219: the CHP+ request plus the 30 days of § 8.100.4.C.2, which 10 CCR 2505-3 § 130.1 applies) replaces ma_verification_due_date in chp_verification_status (CO-1418); each falls back to the shared verification_request_date when its own is not stated. The reasonable opportunity period keeps running from its own notice (ma_reasonable_opportunity_notice_date). The SNAP, Colorado Works and Adult Financial parts are outside this sweep.

Phase-10 open-question sweep (supplied-facts round): resolved for SNAP, Colorado Works and Adult Financial as well. snap_verification_request_date (CO-2380), cw_verification_request_date (CO-2381) and af_verification_request_date (CO-2382) state each program's own written request, read by snap_verification_due_date (CO-1392), cw_verification_due_date (CO-1426), cw_status_verification_due_date (CO-1656), af_verification_due_date (CO-1437) and the AND-SO certification period of af_verification_status (CO-1438), each falling back to the shared verification_request_date when it is not stated (new tests CO-1392-T2, CO-1426-T5, CO-1437-T2). Only Medical Assistance and CHP+ still read the shared date, for the Medicaid sweep.

Status (merge reconciliation, 2026-11-21): the three sweeps above ran in parallel, so each says the other programs still read the shared date. Taken together, every program now has its own request date: CCCAP and LEAP (CO-2120, CO-2121), Medical Assistance and CHP+ (CO-2217, CO-2218), and SNAP, Colorado Works and Adult Financial (CO-2380 to CO-2382), each falling back to the shared verification_request_date when it is not stated. Nothing remains open. Items: CO-1392, CO-1414, CO-1426, CO-1437, CO-1450, CO-1461, CO-2219.

### OQ-301. Resolved: When the Medical Assistance reasonable opportunity period begins

Body: 42 CFR 435.956(b)(2)(i) (S841) begins the reasonable opportunity period on the date the notice is received, "considered to be 5 days after the date on the notice." 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.3.G.3.a.i and H.9.a (S853, S859) say the period "shall begin on the date of the Notice of Action." Read literally, Colorado's period ends five days before the federal one. The volume follows the federal rule, which a state rule cannot shorten, by adding ma_notice_receipt_days (5) before the 90 days; verification_request_date is the notice date. A reviewer could instead read Colorado's "date of the Notice of Action" as the date the notice takes effect on receipt. Items: CO-1348, CO-1413.

Resolution (phase-9 fix round, docs/reviews/verification-phase-9.md): Resolved. 42 CFR 435.956(b)(2)(i) (S841) is a federal floor: the period begins when the notice is received, "considered to be 5 days after the date on the notice", and 8.100.3.G.3.a.i and H.9.a (S853, S859) cannot shorten it. The volume's reading stands; ma_reasonable_opportunity_period_end_date (CO-1413) is unchanged and no longer carries this question.

### OQ-302. Partly resolved: reasonable compatibility for aged, blind and disabled applicants uses the program's standard, and attested income above the standard is taken as attested (verification plan); open: the 20% threshold (20% of what)

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.4.C.2.a.ii (S864) treats income as reasonably compatible when the attested amount is below the standard, the data amount above it, "and the difference is within the reasonable compatibility threshold percentage of 20%." The text does not say 20% of what. ma_income_is_reasonably_compatible (CO-1402) reads it as the data figure being no more than 20% above the attested figure (data at most attested times 1.2), compared as percentages of the poverty guideline for the MAGI household; a reading as 20 percentage points of FPL, or 20% of the standard, would give different answers near the line. The case the text leaves out (attested above the standard, data below) is treated as not reasonably compatible. 8.100.5.B.1.c (S867) applies the same test to aged, blind and disabled applicants against "the applicable Medical Assistance program income standard"; the item compares against ma_magi_highest_income_standard_pct for everyone, which is the wrong standard for an SSI-related determination. Items: CO-1350, CO-1402, CO-1403.

Phase-9 fix round: the second half is resolved. 8.100.5.B.1.c (S867) applies the test against "the applicable Medical Assistance program income standard", so ma_abd_income_is_reasonably_compatible (CO-1640) compares an aged, blind or disabled applicant's gross income with the SSI-related standard (OQ-374), and chp_income_is_reasonably_compatible (CO-1641) uses the CHP+ standard. ma_requires_verification_of_earned_income (CO-1403) chooses between them and now also asks an adult who attested no earnings when the data is not reasonably compatible. The first half (20% of what) remains open.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): Checked 2026-11-21: § 8.100.4.C.2.a.ii (S864, D-65), which does not say 20% of what; Colorado's Medicaid verification plan, which would, is not published on an allowed host; no published rule settles it.

Coverage round (2026-11-22; coverage review F-12): Colorado's MAGI-based eligibility verification plan is on medicaid.gov (D-317, created October 2015) and was read. It states the test as "If the individual attests to income below the applicable income standard, and the data source indicates income above the applicable standard, and the difference between the two is greater than 10% then a reasonable explanation or paper documentation will be requested" (S3364): a 10% threshold, which the current rule's 20% replaces, and, like the rule, no base for the percentage. It also settles the case the rule leaves out: "When an individual attests to income above the applicable standard and data source indicates income below the standard, the state will take the attestation and make the person ineligible ... Additional information will not be requested." ma_income_is_reasonably_compatible (CO-1402) now treats attested-above with data-below as compatible, so ma_requires_verification_of_earned_income (CO-1403) asks for no documentation and the person is determined on the attested income (CO-1402-T5 changed from false to true). The question is reclassified from blocked to a state policy choice: the named document was read and does not state the base. Items: CO-1402, CO-1403.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner, Eligibility operations owner
Assumption: Income is reasonably compatible when the data figure is no more than 20 percent above the attested figure.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.4.C.2.a.ii (D-65, S864) gives "the reasonable compatibility threshold percentage of 20%" without saying 20 percent of what; Colorado's MAGI-based eligibility verification plan (D-317, S3364) gives "the difference between the two is greater than 10%" without saying 10 percent of what. No published text states the base.

### OQ-303. The period for Medical Assistance documentation other than citizenship, identity and immigration status

Body: The fetched Medical Assistance texts give a 90-day reasonable opportunity period for citizenship, identity and immigration status (S841, S853, S859) and a 30-day reasonable opportunity period for income that is not reasonably compatible (8.100.4.C.2, 8.100.5.B.1.c; S864, S867). For any other documentation (questionable residency, age, relationships or pregnancy, ABD unearned income and resources, a missing SSN) 42 CFR 435.952(c)(2)(iii) (S838) requires only "a reasonable period," and 8.100.3.D.2 allows extensions on request. The volume uses the 30-day income period for all of these (ma_verification_due_date, CO-1414). A reviewer may prefer the 45- or 90-day processing standard of 8.100.3.D.1 as the outer bound. Items: CO-1349, CO-1414, CO-1415.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): Checked 2026-11-21: 42 CFR 435.952(c)(2)(iii) ("a reasonable period") and § 8.100.3 and 8.100.5.B (D-65), which state no period for other documentation; no published rule settles it.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner, Eligibility operations owner
Assumption: Documents other than citizenship, identity and immigration status are due 30 days after the request, the period Colorado gives for income that is not reasonably compatible.

Checked 2026-11-22: 42 CFR 435.952(c)(2)(iii) (S838) requires only "a reasonable period", and 10 CCR 2505-10 §§ 8.100.3 and 8.100.5.B (D-65) state 90 days for citizenship and immigration status and 30 days for income but no period for other documentation. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-304. Resolved: Identity documents: two or three corroborating documents; children's records to 16 or 19

Body: 42 CFR 435.407(c)(1)(x) (S848) accepts "two other documents containing consistent information," and (c)(1)(viii) accepts a clinic, doctor, hospital or school record "for children under age 19." 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.3.H.3.h (S857) requires "three or more documents," and H.3.i (S858) limits children's records to "children under 16." The federal rule says the agency "must accept" these documents, so the narrower state text cannot be enforced against an applicant; ma_identity_document_is_acceptable (CO-1399) follows the federal rule (corroborating_documents without a count, children's records and a parent's affidavit under ma_magi_child_age_limit, 19). Items: CO-1302, CO-1399.

Resolution (phase-9 fix round, docs/reviews/verification-phase-9.md): Resolved. 42 CFR 435.407(c)(1) (S848) says the agency "must accept" the listed identity documents, so the narrower state counts (three corroborating documents, children's records to 16) cannot be enforced against an applicant. ma_identity_document_is_acceptable (CO-1399) follows the federal list as drafted; its precision records the conditions left to the worker (review C-22).

### OQ-305. Resolved: The Medical Assistance stand-alone tier: driver's licenses and vital-records matches

Body: 42 CFR 435.407(a) (S845) makes stand-alone evidence of citizenship (and so of identity) a passport, certificates of naturalization and citizenship, a tribal document, a driver's license from a state that requires proof of citizenship or verifies an SSN, an SSA match, a state vital statistics record, and a SAVE match. 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.3.H.2.a (S855) lists only the first four, and treats SSA as an interface (H.1.a.i). ma_citizenship_document_is_stand_alone (CO-1396) follows the federal list for documents (adding drivers_license_requiring_proof_of_citizenship and vital_statistics_record_match), since a state may not refuse what 435.407(a) says must be accepted; SSA and SAVE matches are carried by citizenship_verified_by_electronic_data_source. Colorado licenses non-citizens, so a Colorado license will not usually qualify. Items: CO-1306, CO-1396, CO-1397.

Resolution (phase-9 fix round, docs/reviews/verification-phase-9.md): Resolved. 42 CFR 435.407(a) (S845) says stand-alone evidence "must be accepted", so ma_citizenship_document_is_stand_alone (CO-1396) keeps the federal list.

### OQ-306. Resolved: Medical Assistance special-circumstances exception applied only to residency, age and household composition

Body: 42 CFR 435.952(c)(3) (S839) requires an exception permitting self-attestation of "all eligibility criteria when documentation does not exist ... or is not reasonably available, such as in the case of individuals who are homeless or have experienced domestic violence or a natural disaster," except citizenship and immigration status. The exception is case-by-case; the volume has no fact for domestic violence or a natural disaster and no general "documentation not reasonably available" fact, so the Medical Assistance requirement items apply it, through snap_is_homeless, only to the documentary elements a homeless applicant is least able to produce: residency, age and household composition (CO-1407, CO-1408, CO-1409). Income and pregnancy are untouched. A supplied documentation_not_reasonably_available fact would let every element apply it. Items: CO-1407, CO-1408, CO-1409.

Resolution (phase-9 fix round, docs/reviews/verification-phase-9.md): Resolved. 42 CFR 435.952(c)(3) (S839) makes the special-circumstances exception case-by-case and applies it to every criterion except citizenship and immigration status. The new supplied documentation_not_reasonably_available (CO-1561) replaces snap_is_homeless in ma_requires_verification_of_residency, _age and _household_composition (CO-1407-CO-1409) and is now read by the earned income, unearned income, resources and pregnancy items too (CO-1403-CO-1406) and by chp_requires_verification_of_earned_income (CO-1642).

### OQ-307. Resolved: Medicare Savings Program attestation rules not modeled

Body: 42 CFR 435.952(e) (D-96) requires the agency to accept attestation of interest and dividend income, non-liquid resources, burial funds and life-insurance values for Medicare Savings Program applicants, verifying them (if at all) only after eligibility with at least 90 days to respond. ma_requires_verification_of_resources (CO-1405) requires resource verification of every aged, blind or disabled applicant with resources, including one applying only for an MSP. The volume has no fact distinguishing an MSP-only application; until it does, an MSP-only applicant's resource verification is overstated. Items: CO-1405.

Resolution (phase-9 fix round, docs/reviews/verification-phase-9.md): Resolved. 435.952(e) (S1089) requires the attested interest and dividend income and non-liquid resources of a Medicare Savings Program applicant to be accepted. The new supplied ma_application_is_msp_only (CO-1588) is read by ma_requires_verification_of_resources (CO-1405): an MSP-only applicant verifies liquid resources only. The interest-income half is recorded in CO-1405's precision, since unearned income is not split by kind.

### OQ-308. Resolved: Medical Assistance immigration verification for children and pregnant persons

Body: 10 CCR 2505-3 110.1.C.3.a (S870) exempts CHP+ coverage under Cover All Coloradans from immigration-status verification, and ma_meets_citizenship_requirement (CO-509) already treats children and pregnant persons as meeting the citizenship requirement whatever their status. 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.3.G.2 (S852) applies VLP verification to non-citizens in listed categories who declare a legal status, without an express Cover All Coloradans exemption in the fetched text. ma_requires_verification_of_immigration_status (CO-1400) exempts children and pregnant persons by analogy, because their eligibility does not turn on the result. If HCPF verifies a child's declared status anyway (for federal matching), the exemption should be removed. Items: CO-1400.

Resolution (phase-9 fix round, docs/reviews/verification-phase-9.md): Resolved against the exemption. 10 CCR 2505-3 110.1.C (S870) requires CHP+ to verify every declared legal status and exempts only applicants covered by Cover All Coloradans (who declare none), and 8.100.4.B.1.e (S1085) verifies every declared status for Medical Assistance with no age or pregnancy exception. ma_requires_verification_of_immigration_status (CO-1400) was retired; the pending item ma_immigration_status_verification_is_pending (CO-1622) and the required counts (CO-1412, CO-1416) read claims_lawful_immigration_status. A child's or pregnant person's coverage when the period expires is ma_meets_citizenship_requirement's (CO-509) business, not a reason to skip the request. Case CO-92 exercises an LPR child.

### OQ-309. Resolved: SNAP "past due" marks the end of the time allowed, not the denial date

Body: SNAP must give at least 10 days to provide verification (S800, S813, S834) and may deny on the thirtieth day, or send a notice of pending status and hold the application up to 60 days (7 CFR 273.2(g)(3), (h)(2)). Those are application-processing choices outside the volume. snap_verification_status (CO-1394) reports past_due once the request date plus 10 days has passed with an eligibility factor still unverified: the delay is then the household's (273.2(h)(1)(i)(C)), not that the application has been denied. Items: CO-1394.

Resolution (open-question sweep, 2026-11-21): 7 CFR 273.2(h)(1)(i)(C) (D-95, S813): "Sufficient time shall be at least 10 days from the date of the State agency's initial request for the particular verification that was missing." Once that time has run with a factor unverified the delay is the household's, which is what past_due states; when the agency then denies is application processing. snap_verification_status (CO-1394) cites it; no change. Items: CO-1394.

### OQ-310. Resolved: SNAP: application date and residency exceptions

Body: snap_expedited_postponement_end_date (CO-1393) reads ma_application_date (CO-088), whose meaning names the Medical Assistance application; a PEAK application is one filing for both, so the date is reused rather than duplicated, but a SNAP-only filing date that differs would need its own fact. snap_requires_verification_of_residency (CO-1375) exempts only homeless households; 273.2(f)(1)(vi) and 4.505.3.A (S805, S824) also exempt some migrant farmworker households and households newly arrived in the area where verification cannot reasonably be accomplished, which no supplied fact captures. Items: CO-1375, CO-1393, CO-088.

Resolution (phase-9 fix round, docs/reviews/verification-phase-9.md): Resolved. The residency half is answered by 4.505.3.A (S824) and 273.2(f)(1)(vi) (S805): the new supplied residency_verification_cannot_reasonably_be_accomplished (CO-1586) is read by snap_requires_verification_of_residency (CO-1375). The application-date half is a vocabulary decision: one PEAK filing serves every program, and docs/reviews/vocabulary-reuse.md takes up splitting ma_application_date per program; the new SNAP periods (CO-1617, CO-1618) read the same fact.

### OQ-311. Resolved: SNAP categorical eligibility relief from verification

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.502.A.4 (S819) relieves "households eligible under basic or expanded categorical eligibility" of verifying resources, income, SSN information, sponsored non-citizen information and residency "beyond that gathered by the Public Assistance (PA) program that confers eligibility," unless not already collected and verified by that program, questionable, or unavailable. A basic categorically eligible household's conferring programs (TANF, SSI, OAP, AND, AB) verify these factors; an expanded categorically eligible household's conferring benefit is a TANF-funded informational service that verifies none of them. The requirement items therefore relieve only basic categorically eligible households (CO-1374, CO-1375, CO-1376, CO-1377) and treat both kinds of categorical eligibility as having no resource test (CO-1378). Items: CO-1374, CO-1375, CO-1376, CO-1377, CO-1378.

Resolution (phase-9 fix round, docs/reviews/verification-phase-9.md): Resolved. 4.502.A.4 (S819) relieves categorically eligible households of verifying what the conferring program has "already collected and verified", and 273.2(j)(2)(v) (D-95) does the same federally; a TANF-funded informational service verifies none of these factors, so the volume's reading (relief for basic categorical eligibility only, no resource test for either) stands. The new snap_requires_verification_of_sponsor (CO-1616) follows the same reading for sponsor information.

### OQ-312. Resolved: SNAP actual utility costs and unoccupied homes

Body: 7 CFR 273.2(f)(1)(iii) requires verification of actual utility expenses claimed above the standard utility allowance, and 4.507.B.2 (D-103) requires it for an unoccupied home. The volume models utilities only through utility_allowance_amount (CO-036) and has no fact for a claim of actual utility costs or an unoccupied home, so snap_requires_verification_of_shelter_costs (CO-1379) covers only shelter and utility costs found questionable (4.507.B.1, S832). Items: CO-1379.

Resolution (phase-9 fix round, docs/reviews/verification-phase-9.md): Resolved. Colorado's mandatory standard utility allowance (4.407.31, S1059) says households "cannot claim actual utility expenses", so 273.2(f)(1)(iii)'s verification of actual utilities above the standard (S1058) never arises. The unoccupied-home rule (4.507.B.2, S1060) is read through the new supplied claims_shelter_costs_for_unoccupied_home (CO-1585) by snap_requires_verification_of_shelter_costs (CO-1379).

### OQ-313. Resolved: SNAP accepts a third-party citizenship statement only on the county's finding of 4.505.5.B's two conditions

Body: 4.505.5.B (S826) and 273.2(f)(2)(ii) (S811) accept a signed third-party statement of citizenship only "if the above forms of verification cannot be obtained and the household can provide a reasonable explanation." snap_citizenship_document_is_acceptable (CO-1372) accepts a third_party_citizenship_statement without testing those conditions, which are the worker's judgment and have no fact. 273.2(f)(2)(ii)'s acceptance of citizenship verified by another program is carried by citizenship_verified_by_electronic_data_source. Items: CO-1372.

Resolution (open-question sweep, 2026-11-21): 4.505.5.B (S826) and 7 CFR 273.2(f)(2)(ii) (S811) are clear: a signed third-party statement is accepted "If the above forms of verification cannot be obtained and the household can provide a reasonable explanation". The new supplied snap_third_party_citizenship_statement_is_permitted (CO-2001) records the worker's finding, and snap_citizenship_document_is_acceptable (CO-1372) accepts the statement only when it is true (CO-1372-T2 now states it; new test CO-1372-T4). No household case uses a third-party statement; no case expectation changed. Items: CO-1372, CO-2001.

### OQ-314. Resolved: Colorado Works citizenship verification: every client, or only when questionable

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.3.B.2.b (S872) lists "verification of citizenship or qualified non-citizenship status" among the primary verifications "all participating clients shall provide," while 3.604.3.I.2-3 (D-104) give guidelines for "questionable statement(s) of citizenship" and make a client whose citizenship is in question ineligible until proven. cw_person_pending_verification_count (CO-1424) follows B.2.b and counts citizenship as required for every client who declares it, reading I.2-3 as governing when documents already provided are themselves doubted. If Colorado Works accepts declarations unless questionable (as SNAP, Adult Financial and LEAP do), the citizenship arm should be conditioned on citizenship_attestation_is_questionable. Items: CO-1424.

Resolution (phase-9 fix round, docs/reviews/verification-phase-9.md): Resolved. 3.604.3.B.2.b (S872) says "all participating clients shall provide" verification of citizenship; I.2-3 (D-104) give guidelines for questionable statements, not a questionable-only rule; and 3.604.3.K (S1064) gives thirty days and excludes only the client who cannot provide it. cw_citizenship_verification_is_pending (CO-1645) keeps every client who declares citizenship, and cw_verification_status (CO-1427) now reports members_excluded after the thirty days (review C-9).

### OQ-315. Resolved: Colorado Works and Adult Financial document lists incorporate rules not fetched

Body: 3.604.3.I.1 and I.4.a.1.e (S876, S877) and 3.520.66.E (D-105) also accept "any other document authorized by rules adopted by the Department of Revenue" (1 CCR 204-30), and 3.520.67.C (S886) incorporates the documents in 45 CFR 1626.6. Neither incorporated text was fetched. cw_lawful_presence_identity_document_is_acceptable (CO-1367) and af_citizenship_document_is_primary (CO-1368) accept only the documents the fetched rules name; a document acceptable only under the incorporated rules reads as unacceptable until those texts are fetched and the lists extended. Items: CO-1302, CO-1306, CO-1367, CO-1368, CO-1420, CO-1424, CO-1431.

Resolution (phase-9 fix round, docs/reviews/verification-phase-9.md): Resolved by fetching the incorporated texts. 1 CCR 204-30 Rule 5 and Appendices A and B (D-119, the 06/30/2026 version, unchanged since 08/30/2016 and so the Mar. 2, 2021 edition 3.604.3.I.1 incorporates) list the citizenship and lawful presence documents for Colorado Works and LEAP, and Rule 5 § 1.8 excludes a Road and Community Safety Act license (S1067); Rule 16 (D-120) lists the identity documents Adult Financial incorporates at 3.520.66.E (S1074, S1075); 45 CFR 1626.6 (D-118, S1077) lists the citizenship documents 3.520.67.C incorporates. New items citizenship_document_is_on_revenue_appendix_a (CO-1657), af_citizenship_document_is_acceptable (CO-1673) and af_identity_document_is_acceptable (CO-1674) read them; identity_verification_document_type (CO-1302) gains colorado_road_and_community_safety_act_license_or_id and the Rule 16 types. Rule 5 is repealed in the current 1 CCR 204-30 (effective 10/15/2026, S1072, S1098); see OQ-376.

### OQ-316. Resolved: the extensions of the time to verify are modeled for Colorado Works, LEAP and Medical Assistance

Body: Each program allows more time in some circumstances: Colorado Works to the twentieth day on good cause (3.602.1, D-104) and beyond the 45-day standard at county discretion (3.604.3.B.4, S872); LEAP up to two more weeks on good cause (3.756.12.D, S899); Medical Assistance an extended reasonable opportunity period for a good-faith effort (435.956(b)(2)(ii)(B), S841; 8.100.3.H.10, D-101) and extensions on request (8.100.3.D.2). The due-date items use the base period only. A supplied extension date (or extension granted fact) per program would let each status item honor them. Items: CO-1351, CO-1355, CO-1415, CO-1426, CO-1427, CO-1461, CO-1462.

Phase-9 fix round: 3.604.3.K's thirty days for status elements (S1064) is a period, not an extension, and is now modeled (cw_status_verification_due_date, CO-1656; members_excluded in CO-1427). CCCAP's 60-day new-application rule (3.111.H.5.e.1.b, S891) is likewise a period after closure, outside the initial verification status. The extensions themselves remain unmodeled.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the extension rules cited are clear; each needs an extension-granted fact (and date) per program. Not modeled in this sweep for Colorado Works; the Medical Assistance and LEAP parts belong to those programs' items.

Phase-10 open-question sweep: the LEAP part is resolved. leap_verification_extension_granted (CO-2123) and leap_verification_extension_days (CO-2124, fourteen) carry 3.756.12.D's good-cause extension (S899), and leap_verification_due_date (CO-1461) adds it; leap_verification_status (CO-1462) therefore honours it. The Colorado Works and Medical Assistance extensions belong to the other sweeps.

Phase-10 needs-fact sweep (2026-11-21): the Medical Assistance part is resolved. ma_verification_extended_due_date (CO-2220) carries the extension of time on request (§ 8.100.3.D.2, S1516) and ma_reasonable_opportunity_extended_end_date (CO-2221) the reasonable opportunity period extended for a good faith effort (§ 8.100.3.H.10.a, S1517; 42 CFR 435.956(b)(2)(ii)(B), S841). Both rules leave the length to the site case by case, so each fact is the extended date; ma_verification_due_date (CO-1414) and ma_reasonable_opportunity_period_end_date (CO-1413) take it when it is later (new tests CO-1414-T3, CO-1413-T2), and ma_verification_status (CO-1415) and chp_verification_status (CO-1418) therefore honour them. CHP+'s own due date (CO-2219) takes no § 8.100.3.D.2 extension, since 10 CCR 2505-3 § 130.1 adopts § 8.100.4.B only. If not stated, no extension was granted (each fact's precision says so); no case states them. The Colorado Works extensions are outside this sweep.

Phase-10 open-question sweep (supplied-facts round): the Colorado Works part is resolved. 9 CCR 2503-6 3.602.1.I.2 and 3.604.3.B.4 (D-104, S1629) give the client until the twentieth calendar day after the date of application on good cause and let the county extend the time beyond the forty-five-day maximum. New: cw_verification_good_cause_shown (CO-2370, false when unstated), cw_verification_good_cause_days (CO-2371, 20), cw_application_date (CO-2372) and cw_verification_extended_due_date (CO-2373); cw_verification_due_date (CO-1426) takes the extension when stated, else the later of the eleven days and the twentieth day on good cause (new tests CO-1426-T2 to T4). No case states good cause or an extension; no expectation changed. Only the Medical Assistance extensions remain open, for the Medicaid sweep.

Status (merge reconciliation, 2026-11-21): the three sweeps above ran in parallel, so each says the other programs' extensions remain open. Taken together, the LEAP (CO-2123, CO-2124), Medical Assistance (CO-2220, CO-2221) and Colorado Works (CO-2370 to CO-2373) extensions are all modeled; CCCAP's 60-day rule is a period, not an extension (phase-9 note). Nothing remains open.

### OQ-317. Resolved: Adult Financial income verification is inferred, not stated

Body: 9 CCR 2503-5 has no section that says income "shall be verified" the way 4.506 does for SNAP. 3.520.4.D (S879) directs the county to "verify statements made by the client on the application," and 3.520.5.A (S880) makes SSA and unemployment data verified upon receipt, denies verified status to Department of Labor wage data ("additional verification must be obtained"), and requires the county to "verify potential income" before approval. The volume reads these together as requiring verification of earned income (documents) and unearned income (data or documents) whenever received. Items: CO-1434, CO-1436.

Resolution (phase-9 fix round, docs/reviews/verification-phase-9.md): Resolved. 3.520.5.A.2.a (S880) states that wage data needs "additional verification ... within eleven (11) days ... or the case will be discontinued or denied", and A.3 requires income to be verified for the client, spouse and sponsors. af_earned_income_verification_is_pending (CO-1665) and unearned_income_verification_is_pending (CO-1650) carry it; af_requires_verification_of_earned_income (CO-1434) was retired as a restatement of has_earned_income.

### OQ-318. Resolved: LEAP: a member's missing SSN excludes the member rather than denying the household

Body: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.751.1 (S893) makes a completed application one where "the applicant has provided all required verification," including an SSN or proof of application for each member, but continues "If no SSN is provided for a household member, that member will not be included in the household, but the member's income will be counted." The LEAP counts treat a member's missing SSN as a pending verification like any other (CO-1458, CO-1459), so a household with one member lacking an SSN reads as pending or past due rather than as eligible with that member excluded. Modeling the exclusion would mean filtering leap_household_members on ssn_is_verified, a change to CO-1000 outside this batch. Items: CO-1458, CO-1459, CO-1460, CO-1462.

Resolution (phase-9 fix round, docs/reviews/verification-phase-9.md): Resolved. 3.751.1 Completed Application B (S893) states the consequence: the member without an SSN "will not be included in the household, but the member's income will be counted". The SSN and SAVE arms left the LEAP counts (CO-1458-CO-1460) and the household status (CO-1462); the new outcome leap_member_is_excluded_for_verification (CO-1694) names the member. Filtering leap_household_members (CO-1000) itself belongs to the LEAP eligibility items (OQ-370).

### OQ-319. Resolved: LEAP citizenship documents incorporate 1 CCR 204-30, not fetched

Body: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.753.11 (S895) says "Documents that are acceptable as verification of citizenship can be found at 1 CCR 204-30, Appendix A," which was not fetched. The LEAP pending count accepts any citizenship document received for a questionable claim (CO-1459) and has no acceptability item; once Appendix A is fetched, a leap_citizenship_document_is_acceptable item reading citizenship_verification_document_type should be added. Items: CO-1457, CO-1459.

Resolution (phase-9 fix round, docs/reviews/verification-phase-9.md): Resolved. 1 CCR 204-30 Rule 5, Appendix A (D-119, S1069, S1070) is fetched; leap_citizenship_verification_is_pending (CO-1691) accepts a citizenship document only if citizenship_document_is_on_revenue_appendix_a (CO-1657).

### OQ-320. Resolved: 9 CCR 2503-1 states no verification rule

Body: The phase-9 brief named the general Income Maintenance application rules at 9 CCR 2503-1 as a possible source of verification rules. The current SOS text (D-108, effective 11/01/2018, S900) is "RESERVED FOR FUTURE USE"; its editor's notes record that the former 3.100-3.900 sections were recodified into 9 CCR 2503-2 through 2503-9. Each program's own verification rules were used instead; 9 CCR 2503-8 (administrative procedures) was not fetched. Items: none.

Resolution (Phase-10 open-question sweep): Resolved, no ledger change. The Secretary of State's Income Maintenance list (D-143, retrieved 2026-11-21) shows 9 CCR 2503-1, 2503-2 and 2503-4 "(Reserved for Future Use)" and 9 CCR 2503-8 as "ADMINISTRATIVE PROCEDURES FOR THE COLORADO CHILD CARE ASSISTANCE PROGRAM", not a general verification rule; each program's own verification rules stand.

### OQ-321. Resolved: SSN verification never delays an otherwise eligible person

Body: Every program forbids delaying an otherwise eligible person while SSA verifies a furnished number (7 CFR 273.2(f)(1)(v), S804; 4.505.2.A, S823; 42 CFR 435.910(f), S843; 45 CFR 205.52(c), S878; 3.520.65.A.4, S884). The volume therefore treats the SSN element as satisfied when the person has furnished a number or proof of applying for one (ssn_furnished_or_application_shown) or SSA has confirmed it (ssn_is_verified, CO-1360). A person who furnished neither is pending, which matches each program's rule that a person who refuses or fails to furnish an SSN is ineligible. A later SSA mismatch (4.505.2.B, 3.520.65.B.2) is a change to be resolved, not a pre-certification verification, and is not modeled. Items: CO-1311, CO-1360, CO-1374, CO-1401.

Resolution (phase-9 fix round, docs/reviews/verification-phase-9.md): Resolved. The five texts cited (S804, S823, S843, S878, S884) each forbid delaying an otherwise eligible person while an SSN is verified; the volume's reading stands. LEAP alone excludes the member instead (OQ-318).

### OQ-322. Resolved: CHP+ verification follows Medical Assistance through 130.1

Body: 10 CCR 2505-3 130.1 (S869) requires CHP+ applicants to provide "minimal verification as required in 10 CCR 2505-10-8.100.4.B," and 110.1.C (S870) applies VLP verification and the 8.100.3.H.9 reasonable opportunity period. The CHP+ counts (CO-1416, CO-1417) and status (CO-1418) therefore read the Medical Assistance requirement items, acceptability items, due date and reasonable opportunity period, leaving out the ABD-only elements. 42 CFR 457.380(c)-(d) (S868) let a separate CHIP accept self-attestation of residency and income where Medicaid would not; the fetched Colorado text does not elect that, so the Medical Assistance rules are used unchanged. Items: CO-1416, CO-1417, CO-1418.

Resolution (phase-9 fix round, docs/reviews/verification-phase-9.md): Resolved. 10 CCR 2505-3 130.1 (S869) requires CHP+ applicants to provide the minimal verification of 8.100.4.B. Two corrections follow from the review: CHP+ verifies every declared immigration status (OQ-308), and its earned-income compatibility test uses the CHP+ standard (chp_income_is_reasonably_compatible, CO-1641; chp_requires_verification_of_earned_income, CO-1642).

### OQ-323. Resolved: SNAP postponed verification - Colorado's thirty days stand; the migrant out-of-state exception is a fact

Body: 7 CFR 273.2(i)(4)(iii)(A)-(B) (D-95) postpone verification "until the second month of participation" (applying by the 15th) or "the third month of participation" (after the 15th). Colorado's 4.205.11.B.2.b (S835) terminates participation "when the postponed verification is not completed within thirty (30) calendar days from the date of application." snap_expedited_postponement_end_date (CO-1393) uses Colorado's thirty days for both cases; a household applying after the 15th whose federal postponement runs into the third month is treated as past the postponement after thirty days. Items: CO-1346, CO-1393.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the period is answered. 7 CFR 273.2(i)(4)(iii)(A)-(B) (D-95, S1310) say verification "may be postponed until the second month of participation" and "may be postponed until the third month of participation, if necessary": permissive outer limits, within which Colorado elected thirty calendar days from the date of application for both groups (4.205.11.B.2.b, S835). snap_expedited_postponement_end_date (CO-1393) is right and now cites S1310. Still open: 4.205.11.B.2.b's exception, "Except for migrant households needing out-of-state verification", has no fact and no stated end date of its own; no household case is a migrant household. Items: CO-1393.

CDHS stakeholder review fix round (2026-11-21) (case worker review, design limits): the migrant exception now has a fact, snap_is_migrant_household_needing_out_of_state_verification (CO-2570, S1824), read as false when not stated. For such a household snap_expedited_postponement_end_date (CO-1393) no longer applies the thirtieth-day termination (4.205.11.B.2.b) and reads the federal outer limit of 7 CFR 273.2(i)(4)(iii)(A)-(B) (S1310): through the last day of the second month of participation for an application on or before the fifteenth and of the third month for one after it, the application month counting as the first. The CDHS reviewer notes the volume of such households in Colorado is low. Items: CO-1393, CO-2570.

### OQ-324. Resolved: Medical Assistance citizenship exemption for foster children read through the Title IV payment fact

Body: 42 CFR 435.406(a)(1)(iii)(D) (S844) and 8.100.3.H.1.b.iii-iv (S854) exempt children in Title IV-B foster care and Title IV-E foster care or adoption assistance recipients from documenting citizenship. ma_requires_verification_of_citizenship (CO-1395) reads cw_receives_title_iv_foster_adoption_kinship_payment (CO-841), the Colorado Works fact, which also covers kinship payments and so may exempt a kinship-care child the federal list does not name. A dedicated foster-care fact would be exact. Items: CO-1395.

Resolution (phase-9 fix round, docs/reviews/verification-phase-9.md): Resolved. The new supplied ma_is_in_title_iv_foster_care_or_adoption_assistance (CO-1587) states the 435.406(a)(1)(iii)(D) and 8.100.3.H.1.b.iii-iv group exactly; ma_requires_verification_of_citizenship (CO-1395) reads it instead of the Colorado Works kinship-payment fact. (Moved here in the phase-10 open-question sweep; it had been appended to OQ-358.)

### OQ-350. Resolved: a Colorado Works month with both a new and a current source of earnings is split

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.2.A.2 (S1000) gives a new source of earned income the 100% disregard for the first certification period that the income impacts and a current source the 67% disregard. wages_gross is one figure per person-month, and cw_is_new_earned_income_source (CO-1502) one yes/no, so a person with both a continuing job and a new one in the same month cannot be stated: the supplier must split the earnings or choose. A per-source income model (the vocabulary review's income-fact split, R-1) would carry the two amounts separately. The assumption is one source per person per month. Items: CO-922, CO-1502.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): 3.606.2.A.2 (S1000) is clear; a per-source earnings fact (a new-source amount beside wages_gross) is needed to split a month with both. Not modeled in this sweep.

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep, supplied-facts round): 9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.2.A.2 (S1000) gives a new source the 100% disregard for the first certification period it impacts and a current source 67%. The new supplied cw_new_source_earned_income (CO-2374) states the new source's part of the month's gross earnings; cw_payment_countable_earned_income (CO-922) disregards all of that part and 67% of the rest (new test CO-922-T6: $1,000 of which $400 new counts $198). cw_is_new_earned_income_source (CO-1502) still states a month whose earnings are all new. No case has both kinds of source; no expectation changed.

### OQ-351. Resolved: Adult Financial $20 rollover - the unused part is measured on the unearned income other than SSI

Body: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.533.A.2.f and 3.549.A.2.f (S1002, S1003) apply "the difference between the gross unearned income and the $20.00 deduction" to earned income when "the client's gross unearned income is less than $20.00". A.2.a defines gross unearned income "from all sources", SSI included; A.2.b-d then set SSI aside for the $20 and add it back. af_countable_income (CO-946) reads A.2.f literally, so an SSI recipient whose other unearned income is under $20 has no unused disregard to roll onto earnings (SSI $700 + pension $10: no rollover). The phase-8 review read A.2.f over the non-SSI remainder ($10 rolled to earnings), which is the SSI program's own order (20 CFR 416.1124(c)(12)). The rollover is applied before the $65 and the halving, the order of 20 CFR 416.1112(c). Items: CO-946.

Checked 2026-11-21: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.533.A.2.a-f and 3.549.A.2.a-f (S1002, S1003): A.2.a defines gross unearned income "from all sources" and A.2.f rolls over "the difference between the gross unearned income and the $20.00 deduction" without saying whether SSI set aside under A.2.b is part of it. No published rule settles it.

Resolution (CDHS stakeholder review fix round (2026-11-21), S-5): A.2.b subtracts SSI before A.2.c deducts the $20 "from the remainder", so the remainder is what the $20 is applied to and the part of it the remainder cannot absorb is the unused part A.2.f rolls to earned income; SSI, income based on need, never absorbs the exclusion (20 CFR 416.1124(c)(12), S310: "If you have less than $20 of unearned income in a month and you have earned income in that month, we will use the rest of the $20 exclusion"), and A.2.c.2's "does not receive an unearned income disregard" for an SSI-only client concerns the unearned computation, not the rollover. The CDHS reviewer reads it the same way. af_countable_income (CO-946) now rolls 20 less the non-SSI unearned income; test CO-946-T12 (SSI $700, pension $10, wages $465: 700 + (465 - 10 - 65)/2 = 895, previously 900). Items: CO-946.

### OQ-352. Partly resolved: Adult Financial personal needs allowance - psychiatric and unlicensed facilities are modeled; open: whether income is deducted from the $79

Body: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.532.D-F, 3.543.D-F and 3.548.D-F (S1008-S1010) make an OAP, AND-SO or AND-CS client resident in a listed facility for thirty consecutive days "eligible only for a monthly PNA", at most $79 for every full calendar month. (1) The rule does not say whether countable income is deducted from the $79; af_oap_grant (CO-951) and af_and_grant (CO-952) read the PNA as a grant standard (3.532.C speaks of "the current Personal Needs Allowance (PNA) grant standard") and deduct countable income, with no payment floor - so a resident with income above $79 is paid nothing. The alternative reading pays the regular grant capped at $79. (2) A month only partly in the facility is read through the supplied fact's full-month meaning (af_resides_in_care_facility_thirty_consecutive_days, CO-1510); 3.548.F's rule that an AND-CS grant is not reduced until the month after the first full calendar month is carried by that meaning, not a separate count (corrected in the coverage round, F-4: CO-1510 is now derived from institution_entry_date and is true in the first full calendar month, so af_and_grant, CO-952, applies the AND-CS PNA only from the month after it, entry on or before the first day of the previous month). (3) OAP's list adds a psychiatric facility for a client 65 or older (3.532.D.3), which the fact does not state. (4) A resident of an unlicensed private or uncertified public facility gets neither a PNA nor a grant (3.532.E.2, 3.543.E.2, 3.548.E.2); no fact states it. Items: CO-951, CO-952, CO-1510.

Checked 2026-11-21: (1) 9 CCR 2503-5 3.532.C-F, 3.543.D-F, 3.548.D-F (S1008-S1010) call the PNA a "grant standard" and a "PNA maximum" of $79 and say nothing of income; 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.7.V.3.d.j (D-65) pays a nursing facility resident the difference between reduced SSI and the PNA, which supports the income-deducted reading but does not govern the $79. No published rule settles (1). (3) and (4) are resolved: 3.532.D.3 and 3.532.E.2, 3.543.E.2, 3.548.E.2 (D-80, S1318) are clear. The new supplied af_resides_in_psychiatric_facility_thirty_consecutive_days (CO-2006) gives an OAP client 65 or older the PNA, and af_resides_in_unlicensed_or_uncertified_facility (CO-2007) pays neither a PNA nor a grant in af_oap_grant (CO-951) and af_and_grant (CO-952); new tests CO-951-T6 (psychiatric facility at 66: $79 less $50 = $29) and CO-951-T7 (unlicensed facility: $0). The four case persons who state af_resides_in_care_facility_thirty_consecutive_days (two in CO-07, one each in CO-71 and CO-13) now state both new facts false; no case expectation changed. (1) stays open.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: A facility resident's countable income is subtracted from the $79 personal needs allowance.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.532.C-F, 3.543.D-F and 3.548.D-F (D-80, S1008-S1010) call the allowance a "grant standard" and a maximum of $79 and say nothing of income. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-353. SNAP Standard Medical Expense Deduction: expenses between $165 and $200

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.407.61.A (S1013) uses the $165 SMED "if the total verified medical expenses are greater than thirty-five dollars ($35) and less than or equal to the SMED", and lets the household claim actual expenses "if the total verified expenses, after deducting the first thirty-five dollars ($35), exceed the SMED". Expenses over $165 and up to $200 meet neither sentence. snap_excess_medical_deduction (CO-376) gives such a household the SMED - the greater of $165 and the expenses less $35 - reading the paragraph's first sentence ("eligible for a deduction using either the [SMED] or using actual medical expenses") as a choice the household may make in its favor. Items: CO-376.

Checked 2026-11-21: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.407.61.A (D-82, S1013), unchanged in the 12/30/2025 version (D-133): the SMED applies when expenses are "greater than thirty-five dollars ($35) and less than or equal to the SMED" and actual expenses when they, "after deducting the first thirty-five dollars ($35), exceed the SMED"; Colorado's SMED demonstration terms are not on an allowed host. No published rule settles it.

Class: blocked on a named document
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: A household whose medical expenses are between $165 and $200 gets the standard medical deduction.
Blocked on: Colorado's SNAP Standard Medical Expense Deduction demonstration approval and its terms (FNS's waiver letter to CDHS), which state how the band between the SMED and the SMED plus $35 is treated; neither cdhs.colorado.gov nor www.usda.gov is an allowed host, and fns.usda.gov serves no copy (reclassified in coverage pass 2, C2-8)

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.407.61.A (D-82, S1013; unchanged in D-133) gives the standard deduction when expenses are "less than or equal to the SMED" and actual expenses when they, after the first $35, "exceed the SMED", leaving expenses over $165 and up to $200 in neither. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-354. Colorado Works death reimbursement: how insurance, the estate and responsible persons' resources reduce the benefit

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.7 (S1005-S1007) says insurance proceeds "shall be used to reduce the maximum Death Reimbursement" (F.2.f) and the responsible persons' resources over the SSI limit "shall be used to reduce the State Department funded Death Reimbursement payment" (F.2.c), and requires the estate to be "insufficient to pay all or part" of the expenses (B.2, F.2.b), without an order of application. cw_death_reimbursement_amount (CO-1508) takes the lesser of the $1,500 maximum and the charges the estate leaves unpaid, then subtracts insurance and the excess resources. The Adult Financial burial benefit (3.570.43.D, CO-1230/CO-1232) instead applies insurance, estate and excess resources against the full costs and caps what remains at $1,500; the two readings differ when the charges are well under $1,500 or the offsets are large. Voluntary contributions by the responsible party (F.2.c) also reduce the benefit; coverage review CW-19 models them (burial_contributions_from_responsible_persons, CO-4373, in CO-1508; corrected in coverage pass 2, P3-1). Items: CO-1508.

Checked 2026-11-21: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.7.B.2, F.2.b, F.2.c and F.2.f (S1005-S1007) each say what reduces the Death Reimbursement but not in what order against the $1,500 maximum and the charges. No published rule settles it.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: The death reimbursement is the lesser of $1,500 and the charges the estate leaves unpaid, less insurance and the responsible persons' resources above the limit.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.7.B.2, F.2.b, F.2.c and F.2.f (D-81, S1005-S1007) each say what reduces the payment but not in what order against the $1,500 maximum and the charges. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-355. Adult Financial burial: does the $2,500 combined-charges limit of 3.570.43.F.1 exclude a prepaid plot as A.4 does

Body: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.570.43.A.4 (D-80, `documents/D-80.md:6739-6747`) excludes a prepaid plot of $2,000 or less, or a donated one, from the $2,500 limit; F.1 (`D-80.md:6891`) says "If the combined charges from the provider(s) exceed two thousand five hundred dollars ($2,500), no burial grant payment shall be paid", with no exception. af_burial_eligible (CO-1231) subtracts the plot for both, reading a prepaid plot as no provider charge at death; CO-13 p2 ($2,800 charged with a $600 plot) sits exactly on the difference. Related: burial_services_total_cost (CO-1221) includes the plot, so af_burial_unmet_cost (CO-1230) nets "the full burial costs" including a plot already paid for. The Colorado Works rule states the exemption in its computation steps (3.606.7.F.2.d, S1007) as well as the limit, which supports reading A.4 into F.1. Items: CO-1221, CO-1230, CO-1231.

Checked 2026-11-21: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.570.43.A.4 (D-80:6739-6747) excludes a prepaid plot of $2,000 or less, or a donated plot, from the $2,500 maximum cost limit, and F.1 (D-80:6891) denies payment "If the combined charges from the provider(s) exceed two thousand five hundred dollars ($2,500)" with no exception. No published rule settles whether A.4 reaches F.1.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: A prepaid burial plot is left out of the $2,500 combined-charges limit, as it is left out of the cost limit.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.570.43.A.4 (D-80) excludes a prepaid plot of $2,000 or less, or a donated plot, from the $2,500 cost limit, and F.1 denies payment "If the combined charges from the provider(s) exceed two thousand five hundred dollars ($2,500)" with no exception. No published rule settles whether A.4 reaches F.1.

### OQ-356. SNAP expanded categorical eligibility: gross income "below" or "at or below" 200% of poverty

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 says ECE household gross income must be "below" 200% of the federal poverty level at 4.401.A.1 (`documents/D-82.md:5704`) and "at or below" at 4.206.C.2.a.1 (`D-82.md:2385`). snap_meets_ece_eligibility (CO-340) compares with at or below against snap_bbce_gross_income_limit (CO-339), the reading of the categorical eligibility section itself; a household with gross income exactly at the 200% figure is categorically eligible under 4.206 and not under 4.401. Items: CO-339, CO-340.

Checked 2026-11-21: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.401.A.1 ("below", D-82:5704) and 4.206.C.2.a.1 ("at or below", D-82:2385); the 12/30/2025 version keeps the same pair (D-133:4850 "below", D-133:2019 "at or below"). No published rule settles it.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: Gross income exactly at 200 percent of poverty meets the expanded categorical eligibility limit.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.401.A.1 ("below two hundred percent") and 4.206.C.2.a.1 ("at or below") in D-82, and the same pair in D-133. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-357. Resolved: the Adult Financial bar on Colorado Works reads the closure reasons of 3.520.71.F.2

Body: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.61.F (S711) bars a client "currently receiving or eligible for financial assistance from Colorado Works", and 3.520.71.F.2 (S1011) a client whose Colorado Works case was denied or discontinued "Due to a sanction, demonstrable evidence, or disqualification" or because the client withdrew before exhausting all benefits, until the cause is removed or the client is otherwise ineligible. af_barred_by_colorado_works (CO-1511) reads receipt (receives_tanf_cash) and the person's own cw_eligibility_status - eligible, or sanction_closure (a fourth-level Workforce Development sanction). Not modeled: a closure for demonstrable evidence or an IPV disqualification of the whole case, a withdrawal, and the duty of 3.520.71.F.1 to apply for Colorado Works (the bar reads Colorado Works eligibility directly instead). The bar applies to af_category and AND-SO (and through it the Home Care Allowance's AND-SO route); the Home Care Allowance's other routes and burial assistance do not read it, although 3.500 lists them as Adult Financial programs, because 3.570 states their own requirements. Items: CO-953, CO-1203, CO-1511.

Resolution (open-question sweep, 2026-11-21): 3.520.71.F.2.a-c (S1011) is clear. The new supplied af_has_open_colorado_works_closure_bar (CO-2008) states a Colorado Works denial or discontinuance for demonstrable evidence or a disqualification, or a withdrawal before benefits were exhausted, whose cause still stands; af_barred_by_colorado_works (CO-1511) reads it beside receipt, eligibility and the sanction closure, as false when a case does not state it (the old behavior; no expectation changed); new test CO-1511-T5. F.1's duty to apply is not a separate test, because the bar reads Colorado Works eligibility itself. The Home Care Allowance's other routes and burial assistance keep their own 3.570 requirements, as the body says.

### OQ-358. Resolved: LEAP household of a spouse who has entered a nursing facility

Body: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.751.2.D.1 (D-86, `documents/D-86.md:268`) makes "Husband and wife living together" one household that cannot be classified as separate households. leap_household_members (CO-1000) joins spouses unconditionally over the spouse relationship, and no fact states that a spouse no longer lives in the home, so in the target household for November (CO-02) the grandmother, now a nursing facility resident, stays in her husband's LEAP household and her income counts; she is excluded only from her own vulnerability to heating costs (CO-1004, 3.752.25.B). Whether a spouse in long-term institutional care is still "living together" is not stated in the fetched text; a co-residence fact (OQ-132's missing economic-unit role) would let a case remove her. Items: CO-1000, CO-1009.

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep): Resolved. 3.751.1 defines the household as individuals "living together as one economic unit", 3.751.2.B counts "Each person living at a dwelling", and 3.751.2.D.1 forbids separating only a "Husband and wife living together" (D-86, S1400); the rule states no temporary-absence exception. A spouse who lives in a nursing facility therefore is not in the LEAP household at home. leap_household_members (CO-1000) and leap_household_members_for_income (CO-1009) do not cross a relationship edge to or from a person whose leap_living_arrangement (CO-1283) is institutional_group_care, the 3.752.25.B.1 arrangement (S774) the case already states for her. In the target household the grandmother becomes her own one-person LEAP household (income $2,030, not vulnerable), and the family household is p2, p4 and p5 with p3's income counted ($3,560, 156% FPL for three): its November benefit rises from $450 to $540 (CO-01, CO-02).

### OQ-370. Resolved: LEAP household membership drops a member without an SSN or a verified status

Body: 3.751.1 B (S893) leaves a member with no SSN out of the household with income still counted, and 3.753.17 (S494) does the same for a member who does not meet the status requirement. The verification chapter now reports such a member through leap_member_is_excluded_for_verification (CO-1694) and no longer counts the missing SSN or SAVE response against the household (CO-1458-CO-1462). leap_household_members (CO-1000), which leap_household_size and the benefit read, still filters only on the declared status; adding the SSN and verified-status conditions there is a change to the LEAP eligibility items, left to the eligibility fix round. Until then a household with such a member is sized one person too large. Items: CO-1462, CO-1694, CO-1000.

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep): Resolved. leap_household_members (CO-1000) now leaves out a member for whom leap_member_is_excluded_for_verification (CO-1694) is true: a member who meets the citizenship requirement as declared but has furnished no SSN or proof of application (3.751.1 "Completed Application" B, S893) or whose declared status SAVE has not verified (3.753.15, S896; 3.753.17.A "An alien with no status verification", S1401). leap_household_members_for_income (CO-1009) keeps the member, so the income still counts (3.751.1 B; 3.753.18, S1401), and leap_household_size and the benefit count the household without the member. leap_verification_status (CO-1462) reads not_applicable only when no one in the economic unit meets the citizenship requirement as declared, so a unit whose members all await SAVE is still reported (CO-92 keeps complete). No case expectation changed for the SSN arm: in CO-01/CO-02 the only member without an SSN (p3) is already outside the household for status; CO-08 now states the SSN fact for its four citizens.

### OQ-371. Resolved: SNAP recertification - changed expense sources and outdated information are modeled; an income change is verified only when it is more than $50

Body: 4.502.B.2 (S1056) says a change of "fifty ($50) or more" must be verified and, in the next sentence, that a change of "fifty dollars ($50) or less" need not be; 273.2(f)(8)(i)(A) (S1055) says "more than $50". snap_income_changed_since_last_certification (CO-1619) uses more than $50. A changed source of an expense (4.502.B.3) is not a fact, so the expense items test only the amount; and information that is "incomplete, inaccurate, inconsistent or outdated", or a changed or expired immigration status, is not separately stated, so at recertification immigration status, identity and disability are not asked again and the questionable facts carry the rest. Items: CO-1373, CO-1376, CO-1377, CO-1380, CO-1381, CO-1382, CO-1596, CO-1619.

Checked 2026-11-21: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.502.B.2 (S1056) says both "fifty ($50) or more for each member must be verified" and "changed by fifty dollars ($50) or less, verification is not required"; 7 CFR 273.2(f)(8)(i)(A) (S1055) says "more than $50". No published rule settles the $50 boundary itself. The changed-source and outdated-information parts are NEEDS FACT (a changed-source fact per expense, an outdated-information fact) and are not modeled in this sweep.

Phase-10 open-question sweep (supplied-facts round): the changed-source and outdated-information parts are resolved. 10 CCR 2506-1 4.502.B.1-3 (D-82, S1618) re-verifies an unchanged factor when it is "incomplete, inaccurate, questionable, inconsistent, or outdated" and an expense whose source has changed. New supplied facts: snap_identity_information_is_outdated (CO-2321), snap_immigration_status_information_is_outdated (CO-2322, a changed or expired status included), snap_disability_information_is_outdated (CO-2323), snap_income_information_is_outdated (CO-2324), snap_medical_expenses_information_is_outdated (CO-2325), snap_dependent_care_information_is_outdated (CO-2326), snap_child_support_paid_information_is_outdated (CO-2327) and snap_residency_information_is_outdated (CO-2331), each read as false when not stated; and snap_medical_expenses_source_changed_since_last_certification (CO-2328), snap_dependent_care_source_changed_since_last_certification (CO-2329) and snap_child_support_paid_source_changed_since_last_certification (CO-2330). The recertification arms of CO-1370, CO-1373, CO-1375, CO-1376, CO-1377, CO-1380, CO-1381, CO-1382 and CO-1384 read them (new tests CO-1370-T4, CO-1373-T5, CO-1375-T7, CO-1376-T7, CO-1380-T6 and T7, CO-1381-T4, CO-1382-T4 and T5, CO-1384-T3 and T4). The only recertification case, CO-03, has no verification expectations; no expectation changed. Still open: the $50 boundary itself (checked 2026-11-21, as above); CO-1619 keeps "more than $50".

Resolution (SNAP non-financial coverage round, 2026-11-22; coverage review P2-9, docs/reviews/coverage-snap-nonfinancial.md): the texts settle the $50 boundary, so it was not a state policy choice. 7 CFR 273.2(f)(8)(i)(A) (S1055) both requires verification when "the amount has changed by more than $50" and forbids it: "The State agency shall not verify income if the source has not changed and if the amount is unchanged or has changed by $50 or less"; Colorado's own 4.506 (D-82, S3064) verifies income at recertification "when a household reports that the amount of income has changed more than fifty dollars ($50)". 4.502.B.2's "fifty ($50) or more" cannot require what the federal rule forbids, and its next sentence ("fifty dollars ($50) or less, verification is not required") agrees. snap_income_changed_since_last_certification (CO-1619) already tested more than $50; no answer changes, and the open: reference is removed from CO-1619.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.502.B.2 (D-82, S1056) says both that a change of "fifty ($50) or more" must be verified and that one of "fifty dollars ($50) or less" need not be; 7 CFR 273.2(f)(8)(i)(A) (S1055) says "more than $50". No published rule settles a change of exactly $50.

### OQ-372. Resolved: Medical Assistance renewal: citizenship information and the five-year gap

Body: 435.956(a)(4)(ii) (S1052) forbids re-verifying citizenship at renewal "unless the individual reports a change in citizenship or the agency has received information indicating a potential change"; ma_requires_verification_of_citizenship (CO-1395) reads citizenship_attestation_is_questionable for that information. 8.100.3.H.4.h (S1053) also asks again after a gap of more than five years when the site kept no documents; no fact states the gap or the retention. Renewal on available information and the renewal form's 30 days (435.916(a)(2)-(3), S1050) need no item: the requirement items apply as at application to what the form reports, and ma_verification_due_date already allows 30 days. Items: CO-1395, CO-1566.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the rule is clear (triage: NEEDS FACT) but this sweep did not model it; it needs a fact for the gap in coverage and whether the site kept the documents (§ 8.100.3.H.4.h).

Phase-10 needs-fact sweep (2026-11-21): resolved. New facts ma_previous_eligibility_end_date (CO-2211; if not stated, no earlier period ended) and ma_citizenship_documentation_is_retained (CO-2212), the parameter ma_citizenship_documentation_gap_years (CO-2213, five) and the rule ma_must_resubmit_citizenship_documentation (CO-2214): more than five years from the end of the last period to the application, and the documents not retained (§ 8.100.3.H.4.h.ii, S1053). ma_requires_verification_of_citizenship (CO-1395) now requires documentation at a renewal or later application when it is true (new test CO-1395-T7). No household case is a renewal, so no case states the facts.

### OQ-373. Resolved: renewal verification for Colorado Works, Adult Financial and CCCAP

Body: Only Medical Assistance (CO-1566) and SNAP (CO-1567) carry a renewal fact. Colorado Works requires an SSN applied for at application by the next recertification (3.604.3.B.2.c.1-2, D-104) and verification "at the time of application and redetermination" (3.604.3.K, S1064); Adult Financial accepts the client's statement of resources after application until each twenty-four-month redetermination (3.520.72.D.4, S888); CCCAP closes at redetermination after fifteen days (3.111.H.5.e.2) and verifies child support at each redetermination (3.111.K.2.c). These programs' items describe an initial application. Items: CO-1433, CO-1436, CO-1424, CO-1448.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the rules cited are clear; Colorado Works and Adult Financial need a renewal (redetermination) fact like CO-1566 and CO-1567 and the twenty-four-month resource-statement rule. Not modeled in this sweep; the CCCAP part belongs to the child care items.

Phase-10 open-question sweep (CCCAP part): CCCAP's entry-or-exit income test now reads whether the household is already receiving assistance (cccap_is_receiving_assistance, OQ-133), which covers redetermination for income; the fifteen-day redetermination closure (3.111.H.5.e.2) and the child support re-verification (3.111.K.2.c) still have no redetermination-date fact and stay open with the Colorado Works and Adult Financial parts.

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep, supplied-facts round). Colorado Works: cw_determination_is_redetermination (CO-2375, false when unstated); at a redetermination cw_ssn_verification_is_pending (CO-1647) requires the number itself to be verified, since a client who showed only an application at the initial determination must have the SSN verified before the next recertification (3.604.3.B.2.c.1, D-104, S1630); the status elements are verified at application and redetermination alike (3.604.3.K, S1064), as the items already do. Adult Financial: af_determination_is_redetermination, af_redetermination_is_twenty_four_month and af_has_newly_reported_resources (CO-2376 to CO-2378); af_requires_verification_of_resources (CO-1433) accepts the client's statement at a redetermination other than the twenty-four-month one unless a resource is newly reported or the statement is questionable (3.520.72.D.4, S888). CCCAP: cccap_redetermination_date (CO-2379); cccap_verification_status (CO-1451) reports case_closed instead of past_due when the fifteen-day noticing period runs out at a redetermination (3.111.H.5.e.2, D-87, S1631), and cccap_requires_verification_of_child_support_paid (CO-1446) states the K.2.c re-verification at each redetermination, which its derivation already requires. New tests CO-1647-T3 and T4, CO-1433-T4 to T6, CO-1451-T7. No case is a Colorado Works, Adult Financial or CCCAP redetermination; no expectation changed.


Verification pass V-12 (2026-11-22): the CCCAP part reads the case's determination type, not a date: cccap_verification_status (CO-1451) closes the case at a redetermination by cccap_determination_type (CO-3307). cccap_redetermination_date (CO-2379) was read by nothing and is retired.
### OQ-374. Resolved: Aged, blind and disabled reasonable compatibility compares gross income with the individual SSI rate

Body: ma_abd_income_is_reasonably_compatible (CO-1640) compares the attested monthly gross income (wages, self-employment and unearned income) and the data figure (electronic_data_source_monthly_gross_income, CO-1589) with the SSI individual benefit rate, or 300% of it for a long-term care applicant. The SSI-related test itself counts income after exclusions and uses the couple rate for a couple (ma_meets_ssi_income_standard); the item does not, so results near the line may differ from a countable-income comparison. The 20% threshold carries OQ-302's first half. Items: CO-1589, CO-1640.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the rule is clear (triage: NEEDS FACT) but this sweep did not model it; it needs the attested and data income as countable SSI-method figures and the couple rate.

Phase-10 needs-fact sweep (2026-11-21): resolved. New fact electronic_data_source_monthly_earned_income (CO-2239), the earned part of the data figure, and rules ma_ssi_income_standard (CO-2240: the couple rate for a couple, the individual rate otherwise; ma_meets_ssi_income_standard, CO-609, now reads it) and ma_ssi_countable_income_reported_by_data_source (CO-2241: the § 8.100.5.H.4 disregards applied to the data figures of the person and a spouse living with the person, S308). ma_abd_income_is_reasonably_compatible (CO-1640) compares countable income with the standard for the household size for an aged, blind or disabled applicant, and keeps gross income against 300% of the individual rate for a long-term care applicant, whose special income group test is on gross income (new tests CO-1640-T4 to T6). The 20% base stays with OQ-302. CO-01 and CO-02 p1 and p2 state the data's earned part as 0 (Social Security and pension only); checked by hand: p2's countable figures are both $1,160, above the $994 individual rate (his spouse is institutionalized, so his household size is 1), so the income stays reasonably compatible and no expectation changed.

### OQ-375. Resolved: the AND-SO medical examination's thirty days run from the interview date

Body: 3.541.1.A.1.b (S1080) gives thirty calendar days following the interview to submit the medical examination; the volume has no interview date, so af_verification_status (CO-1438) counts them from verification_request_date, the date the county requested the verification (usually the interview date for Adult Financial). Items: CO-1438, CO-1599, CO-1669.

Resolution (open-question sweep, 2026-11-21): 3.541.1.A.1.b (S1080) counts the thirty calendar days "following the interview". The new supplied af_interview_date (CO-2005) states the date, and af_verification_status (CO-1438) counts the period from it, reading the verification request date when a case does not state the interview (the meaning says so; that is the old behavior, so no expectation changed). New test CO-1438-T6: an interview on September 1 makes the examination past due on October 20 although the request was dated October 1. Items: CO-1438, CO-1599, CO-1669, CO-2005.

### OQ-376. Partly resolved: Department of Revenue Rule 5 repealed - Colorado Works reads the incorporated edition, and a DMV lawful presence match is a document received; open: the edition LEAP reads

Body: The current 1 CCR 204-30 (effective 10/15/2026, D-120) shows Rule 5 withdrawn ("Withdrawal effective December 31, 2026", S1072) and its editor's notes "Rule 5 repealed eff. 10/15/2026" (S1098). 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.3.I.1 incorporates Rule 5, Appendices A and B "(Mar. 2, 2021), no later editions", so Colorado Works still reads the incorporated edition (D-119); 9 CCR 2503-7 3.753.11 and 3.753.14 name Appendices A and B without an edition. The volume reads the incorporated edition for both until CDHS amends its rules.
