Appendix A.C.2 (the applicant's own declaration) and A.D-G are not document types. 3.604.3.H.4 (S1096) lets the county use the DMV to verify lawful presence and identity; cw_lawful_presence_verification_is_pending (CO-1644) accepts a produced document or a SAVE result and does not read an identity match (review C-8), so a DMV lawful-presence match is stated by recording the license as the document received. Items: CO-1644, CO-1657.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the Colorado Works half is resolved: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.3.I.1 incorporates Rule 5, Appendices A and B "(Mar. 2, 2021), no later editions" (D-104), so the repeal of Rule 5 does not change what Colorado Works accepts and cw_lawful_presence_verification_is_pending (CO-1644) reads the incorporated edition (D-119). The DMV lawful-presence match of 3.604.3.H.4 (S1096) has no fact and is not modeled. The LEAP half (3.753.11, 3.753.14 name the appendices with no edition) belongs to the LEAP items and stays open.

Phase-10 open-question sweep (LEAP part): checked 2026-11-21: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.753.11 and 3.753.14 (D-86, D-107) still name 1 CCR 204-30 Appendices A and B without an edition, and the current 1 CCR 204-30 (D-120, S1072, S1098) has Rule 5 withdrawn; no published rule settles which edition LEAP reads, so the volume keeps the incorporated edition.

Open-question classification pass (2026-11-22): the DMV half needs no fact of its own. 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.3.H.4 (S1096) lets the county use the DMV to verify lawful presence and identity, and a DMV match is stated by recording the license as the document received, which cw_lawful_presence_verification_is_pending (CO-1644) already reads; the rule states no separate effect of a DMV match. Only the edition LEAP reads remains.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Energy assistance (LEAP) policy owner
Assumption: LEAP accepts the documents of the 2021 edition of the Department of Revenue's lawful presence list, the edition Colorado Works incorporates.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.753.11 and 3.753.14 (D-86, D-107) name 1 CCR 204-30 Appendices A and B without an edition, and the current 1 CCR 204-30 (D-120, S1072, S1098) has Rule 5 withdrawn. No published rule says which edition LEAP reads.

### OQ-377. Partly resolved: SUN Bucks verification - the exceptions are carried by existing facts; open: no fixed response period

Body: 7 CFR 292.14 (D-117) sets no period for a household selected in the sample: it must respond "by the date determined by the Summer EBT agency" (f)(2)(i), after at least two follow-up attempts (f)(7); verification for cause is completed within 30 days of receipt of the application (a)(1)(ii). sebt_verification_status (CO-1698) therefore has no past-due state. The exception for foster, migrant and runaway children (f)(1) is now read through sebt_other_categorical_basis (CO-1296), and homelessness through sebt_is_homeless_child (final pass, 2026-11-22: this sentence previously said the exception had no fact). Colorado's Plan for Operations and Management, which would state its verification procedures and administrative data sources (292.14(e)(3)), was not fetched (OQ-255). Items: CO-1698, CO-1699.

Phase-10 open-question sweep. The exceptions are carried after all: foster, migrant and runaway children (and FDPIR or another approved program) are within sebt_other_categorical_basis (CO-1296), which sebt_requires_verification_of_income (CO-1699) already reads, and homelessness is snap_is_homeless; CO-1699's precision now says so and records that the same fact also carries Head Start and individual certification, which 292.14(f)(1) does not list, and that (f)(1) speaks of all children in the household. Second-pass review N-9 (2026-11-22): the over-exception is fixed: CO-1699 reads sebt_child_verification_exception_ground (CO-4072), which leaves out Head Start and individual certification (now its own fact, CO-4071), and tests all children of the household. The period stays open: checked 2026-11-21: 7 CFR 292.14(a)(1)(ii), (f)(2)(i), (f)(7) (D-117), C.R.S. 26-2-309 (D-142) and the Secretary of State's rule lists (D-143); no published rule settles the date by which a selected household must respond, so sebt_verification_status (CO-1698) keeps no past-due state.

CDHS stakeholder review fix round (2026-11-21): the CDHS reviewer confirms the response period is in Colorado's Plan for Operations and Management, which was not fetched. The SUN Bucks pending items say so in their due-date note.

Coverage review 2 N-7 (2026-11-22, docs/reviews/coverage2-leap-sunbucks.md): refusal is separate from the response period. 292.14(f)(5) (S1093): "If a household refuses to cooperate with efforts to verify, eligibility for Summer EBT benefits must be terminated." It is now the supplied sebt_household_refused_verification (CO-4854, read as false when not stated), and sebt_benefit_amount (CO-1299) is 0 when it is true, including for an application selected for cause, whose benefits (f)(9) otherwise lets the agency issue. The date by which a household that has not refused must respond stays open.

Class: blocked on a named document
Owner: SNAP policy owner, School meals (CDE) co-approver
Assumption: A household selected for Summer EBT verification has no fixed deadline, so its verification is never reported as past due.
Blocked on: Colorado's Summer EBT Plan for Operations and Management under 7 CFR 292.8, which sets the response period of 292.14(f)(2)(i) (not published on an allowed host; cdhs.colorado.gov refused)

### OQ-378. Resolved: the remaining CCCAP verifications are modeled

Body: Beyond the hours worked and additional care needs now modeled (CO-1684-CO-1687), 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.C.2 (D-106) also lists immunizations (C.2.c, F.2, 3.112.D), the activity schedule when non-traditional hours are requested (C.2.e, 3.112.F), incapacitation by a physician's statement (C.2.g, D.1.c.3), the custody arrangement or parenting schedule (C.2.h), an unrelated caretaker's verification (D.5), and the self-employment weekly schedule and expenses (G.2.a-b). None decides eligibility on facts the volume carries; they are left to the worker. Items: CO-1447, CO-1448, CO-1684.

Phase-10 open-question sweep: left open. The listed verifications need facts no case states (immunization status and provider type for 3.111.F.2, non-traditional hours, custody schedule, an unrelated caretaker's verification); 3.111.D.1.c.3's physician statement is now part of cccap_caretaker_is_incapacitated (CO-1744) and the activity minimum-wage proof is modeled in cccap_caretaker_in_eligible_activity (OQ-162). Not modeled in this sweep.

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep, supplied-facts round): 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.C.2.c, e, h, D.5, F.2 and G.2.a-b (D-87, S1637). New supplied facts: cccap_care_is_from_unrelated_exempt_provider_outside_home and cccap_immunization_record_received (CO-2394, CO-2395: a child not school-aged in care outside the home from an unrelated qualified exempt provider must provide the immunization record, F.2, 3.112.D); cccap_non_traditional_hours_are_requested and cccap_activity_schedule_received (CO-2397, CO-2398: the activity schedule when non-traditional hours are requested in an approved county, C.2.e, and the self-employed caretaker's weekly schedule and expenses, G.2.a-b); cccap_unrelated_caretaker_verification_is_outstanding (CO-2396, D.5) and cccap_custody_schedule_is_outstanding (CO-2399, C.2.h), each stating the requirement and its receipt together because the requirement turns on kinship or a custody finding the relationships do not carry. The provider, hours and outstanding facts are false when not stated. cccap_person_required_verification_count (CO-1447) and cccap_person_pending_verification_count (CO-1448) count them, so cccap_verification_status reports them (new tests CO-1448-T3 to T6). Incapacitation (C.2.g) was already part of cccap_caretaker_is_incapacitated (CO-1744). No household case has care from an unrelated exempt provider, non-traditional hours, a self-employed caretaker, an unrelated caretaker or a custody schedule; no expectation changed.

### OQ-379. Resolved: Long-term care documentation: transfers, joint ownership and sales efforts

Body: ma_asset_transfers_verification_is_pending (CO-1637) reads one supplied fact for the documentation of transfers in the 60-month look-back (8.100.7.A.2.h, F.2.a; S1087, S1088): the transfers made, or a declaration that none was made without fair consideration; the text names the look-back, not the form of the evidence. Verification of joint ownership and the quarterly verification of efforts to sell real property (D-41, 8.100.7) are not modeled. ma_is_long_term_care_applicant (CO-1631) treats every aged, blind or disabled person in a medical institution, an HCBS target group or PACE as a long-term care applicant. Items: CO-1631, CO-1632, CO-1637.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the rule is clear (triage: NEEDS FACT) but this sweep did not model it; it needs facts for joint ownership documentation and the quarterly efforts to sell real property.

Phase-10 needs-fact sweep (2026-11-21): resolved. In the current text both rules are in the aged, blind and disabled resource section, so they apply to every aged, blind or disabled applicant, not only long-term care: excess real property is excluded while reasonable efforts to sell it are unsuccessful, verified quarterly (§ 8.100.5.M.2.b.v, S1514), and an allegation that selling jointly owned property would cause undue hardship to the co-owner requires verification of joint ownership (§ 8.100.5.M.3.h.ii, S1515). New facts ma_excess_real_property_is_for_sale (CO-2242), sale_efforts_verification_document_received (CO-2243), ma_claims_co_owner_hardship_for_real_property (CO-2244) and joint_ownership_verification_document_received (CO-2245); rules ma_requires_verification_of_sale_efforts (CO-2246), ma_sale_efforts_verification_is_pending (CO-2247), ma_requires_verification_of_joint_ownership (CO-2248) and ma_joint_ownership_verification_is_pending (CO-2249). The two pending items are new Medicaid outcomes and count in ma_required_verification_count (CO-1412) and ma_pending_verification_count (CO-1411). CO-01 and CO-02 p1 and p2 state both claims false, and their expectations for the two outcomes (and CO-92's) are false; no existing expectation changed.

### OQ-380. Resolved: SNAP sponsor verification follows the deeming exemptions, and only the sponsored member waits on it

Body: SNAP makes only the sponsored member ineligible until the sponsor's information is provided (4.505.7.B-C, S1061), and the whole household when one sponsor covers it; snap_verification_status reports the household. The exemptions from deeming (indigence, forty qualifying quarters, battered non-citizens) are not modeled, so is_sponsored_non_citizen (CO-1573) is stated true only when deeming applies. Adult Financial's 3.520.69 applies to qualified non-citizens who entered on or after August 22, 1996; af_sponsor_verification_is_pending (CO-1672) reads qualified_status_date for the date of entry. Items: CO-1573, CO-1616, CO-1654, CO-1672.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): 4.505.7.B-C (S1061) and the deeming exemptions are clear; the facts are missing (sponsor indigence, forty qualifying quarters credited from the sponsor and spouse, battered non-citizen). Not modeled in this sweep: the SNAP sponsor-deeming computation itself is not in the ledger, so is_sponsored_non_citizen (CO-1573) keeps meaning "deeming applies".

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep, supplied-facts round): 10 CCR 2506-1 4.305.2.A, C, E (D-82, S1619) state when a sponsor's income and resources are deemed and the eight exemptions. New supplied snap_lives_in_sponsor_household (CO-2332), sponsor_is_organization (CO-2333), snap_is_indigent_non_citizen (CO-2334), snap_battered_non_citizen_sponsor_exemption_applies (CO-2335) and sponsor_has_died (CO-2336), each false when unstated; the forty quarters read qualifying_quarters (CO-004, which already credits a spouse's or parent's quarters) against snap_qualifying_quarters_threshold (CO-103); a non-citizen who needs no sponsor (E.3) or has naturalized (E.6) has no affidavit in force. The new snap_sponsor_deeming_applies (CO-2337) combines them, and snap_requires_verification_of_sponsor (CO-1616) reads it, so is_sponsored_non_citizen (CO-1573) now means only that an affidavit of support was executed. Whose eligibility: 4.505.7.B (S1061) makes only the sponsored non-citizen ineligible until the information is provided, so snap_verification_status (CO-1394) gains members_excluded when the sponsor's information is the only element outstanding (new test CO-1394-T11; the other tests now state the sponsor element false). No household case has a sponsored non-citizen; no expectation changed. The Colorado Works and Adult Financial sponsor verification items (CO-1654, CO-1672) belong with those programs' sponsor deeming (OQ-103, OQ-114).

Hard-coded numbers fix round (audit D-3): the forty quarters are federal_qualifying_quarters_threshold (CO-3033); snap_qualifying_quarters_threshold (CO-103) is retired.

Final verification (docs/reviews/final-snap.md F-2, 2026-11-22): the deeming computation the 2026-11-21 entry recorded as missing is now in the ledger, so the question stays resolved. 10 CCR 2506-1 4.305.2.C-D (S2502) and 7 CFR 273.4(c)(2) (S2503): snap_sponsor_income_after_reductions (CO-3711: each signing sponsor's SNAP earned and unearned income, less 20% of the earnings, snap_sponsor_earned_income_reduction_rate CO-3703, less the gross income limit for the sponsor, the sponsor's spouse and their tax dependents, snap_sponsor_deeming_household_size CO-3710 with the new tax_dependents_claimed_or_claimable_count CO-3705), divided by snap_sponsor_deeming_divisor (CO-3708, the sponsor's non-citizens, non_citizens_sponsored_count CO-3706, plus a sponsored member's citizen children under 18), gives snap_sponsor_deemed_income (CO-3701; the greater of it and the money the sponsor pays, money_received_from_sponsor CO-3707), which snap_person_gross_income (CO-332) adds; snap_sponsor_deemed_resources (CO-3702: the sponsors' resources less $1,500, snap_sponsor_resource_reduction CO-3704) is added in snap_household_countable_resources (CO-343). 10 CCR 2506-1 4.408.B still says $2,000; 4.305.2.D.6 and 273.4(c)(2)(iv) say $1,500, which the volume follows. The federal exemption of a sponsored child under 18 of a sponsored non-citizen (273.4(c)(3)(vi)), absent from Colorado's 4.305.2.E, is applied in snap_sponsor_deeming_applies (CO-2337). New cases CO-116 (deemed income 425, allotment 223) and CO-94 expectations (resources 550 each).

Final verification pass 2 (docs/reviews/final2-snap.md N-2 and N-3, 2026-11-22): the question stays resolved, with two corrections. (1) "Only the sponsored member waits on it" was stated but not applied: snap_verification_status reported members_excluded while the member stayed in the eligible size with the sponsor's income deemed. snap_is_ineligible_pending_non_citizen_verification (CO-4154) now makes the member an ineligible non-citizen (CO-310) while the sponsor's information is outstanding (4.505.7.B-C, S2954; 7 CFR 273.4(c)(5), S2956), with income and resources prorated and the sponsor's deemed amounts left out (CO-3701, CO-3702), and the same for a status document, the forty quarters or a military connection still unverified after the documentation due date (4.505.6.F, I, S2516, S2955; 273.2(f)(1)(ii)(B), S2957). New case CO-141 (CO-116 with the sponsor's information outstanding: no eligible members, allotment 0). (2) The children's exemption recorded above was too narrow: 7 U.S.C. 2014(i)(2)(E) (S2959) and 8 U.S.C. 1631(d)(3) (D-275, S2958), both as amended by Pub. L. 107-171 sec. 4401(b) effective October 1, 2003 (D-276, S2960), exclude every non-citizen under 18 from SNAP sponsor deeming, not only a sponsored child of a sponsored non-citizen (273.4(c)(3)(vi)); snap_sponsor_deeming_applies (CO-2337) now tests age alone, with a statute_override over 4.305.2.E, and no sponsor verification is asked for a child. CO-94's expectations change: deeming applies false and deemed resources 0 for both children (were true and 550).

### OQ-381. Resolved: LEAP vulnerability and heating costs are household elements; zero income is examined for each adult

Body: 3.755.11 (S897) verifies "estimated home heating costs, and vulnerability" for the household: leap_pending_verification_count (CO-1460) counts one pending heating verification however many members are responsible for heating costs (review C-13). The CO-01 and CO-02 cases still state is_responsible_for_heating_costs for the children, because LEAP vulnerability (an eligibility item) reads it; the count no longer depends on it. leap_zero_income_verification_is_pending (CO-1693) reads leap_household_income for "zero income" and asks every adult member (3.755.21.H, S1083). Items: CO-1460, CO-1692, CO-1693.

Phase-10 open-question sweep: left open. The volume has no household-level supplied fact (every fact is a person's or the case's month), so the heating-cost and zero-income elements stay read through the members as described; checked 2026-11-21 against 3.755.11 and 3.755.21.H (D-107, S897, S1083).

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep, supplied-facts round): no household-level fact is needed. 3.752.25.A makes "the household" vulnerable when it is responsible for heating costs, and 3.755.11 (S897) verifies the estimated heating costs and vulnerability once; leap_is_vulnerable_to_heating_cost (CO-1004) now asks whether any member of the economic unit (leap_household_members_for_income) is responsible, so a child who pays nothing toward heat need not state is_responsible_for_heating_costs (new test CO-1004-T6; T1 and T3 now state the unit), and leap_pending_verification_count (CO-1460) already counts the heating element once. 3.755.21.H (S1083) has the county "examine income of all adult members of the household", so leap_zero_income_verification_is_pending (CO-1693) rightly asks each adult. The CO-01 and CO-02 children keep their stated fact, which is now redundant; no expectation changed.

### OQ-382. Resolved: One request fact for Medical Assistance and CHP+

Body: is_requesting_health_coverage (CO-1560) is one fact for both programs because a PEAK health-coverage application is screened for Medical Assistance and CHP+ together; 8.100.3.I.1 (S860) exempts a person "not requesting Medical Assistance yet appear[ing] on the application" from the SSN requirement, and the Medical Assistance and CHP+ statuses read it for not_applicable. A household that requests one program and declines the other would need two facts. The other programs' not_applicable rows read categorical reach (a dependent child or pregnant member for Colorado Works, an eligible child for CCCAP, the Old Age Pension age or a disability basis for Adult Financial, a citizen member for LEAP, SNAP household membership, school age for SUN Bucks) instead of a request fact. Items: CO-1415, CO-1418, CO-1560.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the rule is clear (triage: NEEDS FACT) but this sweep did not model it; it needs separate Medical Assistance and CHP+ request facts in place of is_requesting_health_coverage (CO-1560).

Phase-10 needs-fact sweep (2026-11-21): resolved. New facts ma_is_requesting_coverage (CO-2215) and chp_is_requesting_coverage (CO-2216) state a request for one program; each program reads its own first and, when it is not stated, the shared is_requesting_health_coverage (a PEAK application requests both), as the CCCAP and LEAP request dates do for OQ-300. ma_verification_status (CO-1415), ma_requires_verification_of_ssn (CO-1401, the § 8.100.3.I.1 exemption for a person "not requesting Medical Assistance", S860) and chp_verification_status (CO-1418) read them. New tests CO-1415-T8 and CO-1418-T10. No case requests one program alone, so no case states them and no expectation changed.

### OQ-390. Resolved: supplied income is gross - each program's own exclusions are modeled, the SSI-method exclusions included

Body: Since the vocabulary fix round (docs/reviews/vocabulary-reuse.md R-1) every supplied income fact states the gross amount received, before any program's exclusions; the phase 2 convention that they were countable, post-exclusion amounts is withdrawn. Each program's income item counts the sources its rule counts and applies the disregards it models, but the long exclusion lists are not modeled as rules: SNAP's 7 CFR 273.9(c), Colorado Works' 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.4, Adult Financial's 3.520.786, CCCAP's 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.J (OQ-162), LEAP's 3.752.23 and the SSI exclusions of 20 CFR 416.1112 and 416.1124 (for example interest on countable resources). A case whose household has excluded income must leave it out of the supplied amounts, and a case cannot then be correct for two programs whose exclusions differ. The review's proposal of explicit exclusion inputs per program (snap_excluded_income and the like, each citing its list) is the fix; it was not done in this round because no case or test states excluded income. Items: CO-330, CO-331, CO-908, CO-946, CO-985, CO-1002, CO-641, CO-663.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): for SNAP, Colorado Works and Adult Financial the exclusion lists are stated in the cited rules (7 CFR 273.9(c), 3.605.4, 3.520.786); the fix is per-program excluded-income inputs, which no case yet needs. Not modeled in this sweep.

Phase-10 open-question sweep (supplied-facts round): the review's proposal is adopted for SNAP, Colorado Works, Adult Financial, CCCAP and LEAP. Each program's exclusion list is its own (10 CCR 2506-1 4.405, S1620; 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.4.A, S1621; 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.786, S1622; 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.J, S1623; 9 CCR 2503-7 3.752.23, S1624), so each now has an excluded-income input stating the part of the gross amounts its list excludes, read as 0 when not stated: snap_excluded_earned_income and snap_excluded_unearned_income (CO-2338, CO-2339, subtracted in snap_person_earned_income CO-330 and snap_person_unearned_income CO-331), cw_excluded_earned_income and cw_excluded_unearned_income (CO-2340, CO-2341, in cw_gross_earned_income CO-1716 and cw_countable_unearned_income CO-908), af_excluded_earned_income and af_excluded_unearned_income (CO-2342, CO-2343, in af_countable_income CO-946), cccap_excluded_income (CO-2344, in cccap_household_income CO-985) and leap_excluded_income (CO-2345, in leap_household_income CO-1002). A case with excluded income now states the gross amount and each program's exclusion, so it can be right for every program. New tests CO-330-T3, CO-331-T4, CO-1716-T3, CO-908-T4, CO-946-T11, CO-985-T5, CO-1002-T4. No case states excluded income; no expectation changed. The SSI-method exclusions of 20 CFR 416.1112 and 416.1124 used by the Medicaid SSI-related items (CO-641, CO-663) belong to the Medicaid sweep and keep the question open.

HCPF fix round (2026-11-21, docs/reviews/stakeholder-hcpf.md): the Medicaid half is done as the other programs' was: ma_ssi_excluded_unearned_income (CO-2470, 20 CFR 416.1124) and ma_ssi_excluded_earned_income (CO-2471, 416.1112), each read as 0 when not stated, are subtracted per member in ma_ssi_household_unearned_income_sum (CO-663) and ma_ssi_countable_earned_income (CO-605). ma_ltc_gross_income (CO-641) takes no SSI exclusions by the rule's own terms: § 8.100.7.A (S351) defines the special income standard's gross income as "income before application of deductions, exemptions or disregards appropriate to the SSI program". Items: CO-605, CO-663, CO-2470, CO-2471.

Final cash review (2026-11-22, docs/reviews/final-cash.md FC-2, FC-3 and FC-22): for Colorado Works and Adult Financial the resolution's "excluded-income input" had since become a closed derived sum of six (Colorado Works) and four (Adult Financial) receipt facts, so the other sources of 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.2.C, 3.605.4 and 3.605.5.D (S2604-S2607) and of 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.786.D-P (S2608, and K's 20 CFR 416.1124(c), S2609) were counted. Each is now a neutral receipt fact (general_or_emergency_assistance_received CO-3812, crime_victim_compensation_received CO-3813, child_support_arrears_received CO-3814, workforce_program_payments_received CO-3815, savings_account_distributions_received CO-3816, subsidized_employment_wages CO-3817, short_term_employment_wages CO-3818 with short_term_employment_ended_through_own_fault CO-3819, reverse_mortgage_payments_received CO-3820, foster_care_provider_payments_received CO-3821, infrequent_or_irregular_income_received CO-3822 with af_excluded_infrequent_income CO-3827, va_aid_and_attendance_used_for_uncovered_care CO-3823, in_home_care_allowance_received CO-3824, pass_set_aside_income CO-3825), which cw_excluded_earned_income (CO-2340), cw_excluded_unearned_income (CO-2341), cw_counted_child_support (CO-2553) and af_excluded_unearned_income (CO-2343) read; Adult Financial also excludes interest and dividends (416.1124(c)(22)). The Colorado Works and Adult Financial remainder is closed. Items: CO-2340, CO-2341, CO-2343, CO-2551, CO-2553.

Final pass F-09 and F-11 (CCCAP and LEAP, 2026-11-22): cccap_excluded_income (CO-2344) and leap_excluded_income (CO-2345) are retired. Both programs now read the recurring unearned income of the thirty days before the application (unearned_income_received_prior_thirty_days, CO-3911), which leaves out the receipts each program excludes and that have their own facts (educational aid, loans, reimbursements, home energy assistance, tax refunds and credits, federally excluded payments, lump sums); each program then adds back, by the month received, only what its rule counts (OQ-680, OQ-681).

Coverage round, LEAP (2026-11-22, docs/reviews/coverage-leap-sunbucks.md C-1): the sentence above held only for the receipts the thirty-day figure leaves out; retiring leap_excluded_income (CO-2345) left LEAP counting every other 3.752.23 exclusion. LEAP's list is now carried by leap_excluded_unearned_income (CO-4511: crime victim compensation G, foster care and kinship payments U, reverse mortgages X, housing utility allowances Y, G.I. Bill housing and food allowances Z, PASS income DD, and the claim-expense part of workers' compensation, 3.752.211.D), subtracted in leap_person_unearned_income (CO-3926); by leap_person_one_time_income (CO-4069), which now also leaves out the liquidation of resources (GG), the refugee resettlement grant (AA) and insurance and injury proceeds spent on the costs they cover (3.752.21.C-D); and by leap_person_wages (CO-3925), which leaves out SCSEP wages (CC) and employer health insurance subsidies (HH) and adds in-kind pay (3.752.22.C.4). New neutral receipt facts: CO-4500 to CO-4509. The LEAP remainder is closed. Items: CO-3925, CO-3926, CO-4069, CO-4511.

Coverage review snap-financial (2026-11-22, docs/reviews/coverage-snap-financial.md CF-1): for SNAP the resolution's premise, that snap_excluded_earned_income and snap_excluded_unearned_income state the part of the gross that 4.405 excludes, no longer held once both became closed sums: the receipt facts the cash round added were not read, so SNAP counted crime victim compensation, WIOA and youth project payments, reverse mortgage payments, PASS set-asides, AmeriCorps payments and irregular income in full. Both items now read every 4.405 source that has a neutral receipt fact (S3100-S3110): the cash round's facts (CO-3813, CO-3815, CO-3820, CO-3822 and CO-4010 through the quarter's total, CO-3825, CO-4016) and new ones for the sources no fact stated (CO-4300 to CO-4315: training allowances other than WIOA, WIOA on-the-job training and summer youth wages, VISTA payments and whether the volunteer was on SNAP or public assistance on joining, recoupments and the intentional-violation part, child support turned over to the IV-D agency, need-based charitable cash with its $300 quarterly limit CO-4317, nonprofit or supplier energy assistance, combat pay, SCSEP, a former spouse's share of military retirement, military education deductions, money for a beneficiary living elsewhere, and migrant emergency assistance); the VISTA payments and non-WIOA training allowances that 4.403 counts as earned income are moved from unearned to earned income (CO-330). The 4.405 remainder is closed: G, H and 4.405.2.A.1, A.16, A.17, A.19, A.24 and A.25 are in-kind or vendor payments, not money received (a child-care payment made to the household is a reimbursement, expense_reimbursements_received), A.21's Employment First payments are reimbursements (CO-3363), and 4.405.2.B's Indian payments are in federally_excluded_payments_received. Items: CO-2338, CO-2339, CO-330, CO-331, CO-4319.

### OQ-391. Resolved: the AND-CS grant standard is a State figure, not defined as the SSI federal benefit rate

Body: af_and_cs_grant_standard (CO-291) has the same value as ssi_federal_benefit_rate_individual (CO-235) in both versions (967 and 994). If 9 CCR 2503-5 3.546 defines the AND-CS standard as the SSI federal benefit rate, the parameter should be derived from it; if the state sets it separately, the two only coincide. The vocabulary review (D-7) left this to the cash programs owner; the fetched text does not settle it. Items: CO-291, CO-235.

Resolution (open-question sweep, 2026-11-21): 9 CCR 2503-5 3.546.A-B (D-80, S706) states "The total AND-CS grant standard is $994, effective January 1, 2026" and that it "shall be adjusted as needed to remain within available appropriations"; the rule does not define it as the "SSI benefit standard" it defines separately in 3.510. The two figures coincide in 2025 and 2026 but are set by different bodies, so af_and_cs_grant_standard (CO-291) stays its own dated parameter. No change. Items: CO-291.

### OQ-392. Resolved: Adult Financial counts its own resources and a sponsor's resources over the sponsor's limit

Body: af_countable_resources (CO-947) reads ssi_countable_resources_individual and ssi_countable_resources_couple (CO-800, CO-801), the SSI-method figures of 20 CFR 416.1210, split on plain marriage (3.520.72.A). Adult Financial's rule counts the resources of the client, spouse and sponsors (S454) and has its own exempt resources; the volume assumes the figures agree and does not count a sponsor's resources. An af_countable_resources supplied fact, or the sponsor's resources as a separate fact, would remove the assumption. Items: CO-947.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.72.A-B (D-80) has its own resource list and limits ($2,000 unmarried, $3,000 married, with separate sponsor limits) counting "the client, spouse, and sponsor(s)", and 3.520.77 its own exemptions, so the SSI-method figures are an approximation. An Adult Financial countable-resources fact (or the sponsor's resources as a fact) is needed; not modeled in this sweep.

Resolution (phase-10 open-question sweep, supplied-facts round): 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.72.A and E (S1636). The new af_client_and_spouse_countable_resources (CO-2389) states the client's and spouse's resources as Adult Financial counts and exempts them (3.520.72-77); af_countable_resources (CO-947) reads it, with the SSI-method figure standing in when it is not stated (documented on the fact), and adds, for a sponsored non-citizen, the sponsor's countable resources (af_sponsor_countable_resources, CO-2390) over the sponsor's limit of $2,000, $3,000 (married, spouse not a co-sponsor) or $4,000 (co-sponsor spouse). New tests CO-947-T3 to T5; CO-947-T1 and T2, CO-948-T1 and T2 now state a citizen and CO-953-T2 states no sponsor. No case states the new figures or a sponsor; no expectation changed.

### OQ-393. Resolved: residency - Medicaid's special rules, CCCAP's county residence and SNAP's residency prong are modeled, and SNAP's county of application is not an eligibility condition

Body: is_colorado_resident (CO-505) compares the stated state of residence with Colorado. Not modeled: Medicaid's 42 CFR 435.403(e)-(j) rules for institutionalized persons who cannot state intent, temporary absences and children placed out of state (when modeled they become ma_meets_residency over CO-505); CCCAP's county residence (cccap_requires_verification_of_residency, CO-1440, speaks of 'a verified resident of the county'; no county_of_residence fact exists); and a SNAP residency condition under 7 CFR 273.3, which no SNAP item states, so SNAP eligibility does not read residence at all. Items: CO-505, CO-1440, CO-987.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): the SNAP part is resolved with OQ-3: snap_meets_citizenship_requirement (CO-309) now reads is_colorado_resident for 7 U.S.C. 2015(f)(1), on 4.303.A (S1311). SNAP's county-of-application half, Medicaid's special rules and CCCAP's county residence remain open.

Phase-10 open-question sweep: the CCCAP part is resolved (OQ-159): cccap_meets_county_residency (CO-2107) reads county_of_residence (CO-1979) and cccap_application_county (CO-2106). The Medicaid and SNAP parts are outside this sweep.

Phase-10 needs-fact sweep (2026-11-21): the Medicaid part is resolved. ma_meets_residency (CO-2250) is Medicaid's residency over is_colorado_resident: for a person in an institution who is incapable of indicating intent (ma_is_incapable_of_indicating_intent, CO-2251; § 8.100.3.B.3, S1518; if not stated, no finding of incapacity), Colorado must be the state ma_institutionalized_state_of_residence (CO-2257) gives - the state that arranged an out-of-state placement (CO-2255), otherwise for a person under 21 or incapable before 21 (CO-2252, parameter CO-2256) the parents' or guardian's state at placement (CO-2253), otherwise the institution's state (CO-2254). The conflict between B.2.b and B.2.c with 42 CFR 435.403(h)(4) for a person incapable from 21 on is OQ-472. Temporary absence (B.4) and a home outside Colorado the person intends to return to are already in state_of_residence's meaning. Every Medicaid eligibility item that read is_colorado_resident now reads ma_meets_residency (CO-554, 555, 561, 562, 611, 618, 620, 652, 653, 654, 1150-1152, 1154, 1159, 1161-1163, 1177); CHP+ keeps is_colorado_resident under its own rule. Since incapacity is read as absent when not stated, no case changed. The SNAP part is outside this sweep.

Resolution (open-question classification pass, 2026-11-22): the SNAP county half is answered by 10 CCR 2506-1 (D-82, S2300). 4.303.A has an applicant live in the county where the application is made unless the local office arranges filing in a nearby county; 4.303.C makes a county resident eligible "provided other eligibility requirements are met"; and 4.902.1 lets an office that receives an application belonging to another county process it to completion and transfer the case once the decision is made. The county is where the case is administered, not a condition of eligibility, so SNAP eligibility reads Colorado residence alone (snap_meets_citizenship_requirement, CO-309, through is_colorado_resident), and a household that applied in the wrong county is not denied for it. CO-309 now cites S2300 and says so; no value changes, so no test is added. Every part of this question is now settled.

### OQ-394. Resolved: CCCAP's citizen/legal resident is a citizen, national or qualified alien (3.103.Z and the HHS notice it incorporates)

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.F.1 (S514) requires a child to verify that the child is "a citizen/legal resident". cccap_child_meets_citizenship_requirement (CO-998) reads that as a U.S. citizen or national, a qualified non-citizen, or another lawfully present non-citizen (citizenship_status other_lawfully_present_non_citizen, for example Temporary Protected Status), which keeps the reach the item had when it read Medicaid's lawful_presence_status. A narrower reading (lawful permanent residents and other qualified non-citizens only) would exclude a child with Temporary Protected Status. Items: CO-998.

Phase-10 open-question sweep: checked 2026-11-21: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.F.1 and 3.111.C.2.b (D-87, S1408) and the definitions of 3.103 (D-87); no published rule settles whether "citizen/legal resident" reaches a lawfully present non-qualified non-citizen.

CDHS stakeholder review fix round (2026-11-21): the CDHS/CDEC reviewer would narrow the reading to qualified aliens for CCDF-funded care (8 U.S.C. 1611, 1641), pending CDEC confirmation, and tie it to a funding-source fact. That position rests on program knowledge and on federal text not fetched for this volume (8 U.S.C. 1611 and HHS's PRWORA interpretation for CCDF); the published rule's "citizen/legal resident" (3.111.F.1) does not decide it, so the draft keeps its reading and the question stays with the CDEC.

Resolution (audit fix round, 2026-11-21): 8 CCR 1403-1 3.103.Z (S1910, current SOS text) defines "citizen/legal resident" as a citizen, a current legal resident, or "a person lawfully present in the United States pursuant to Title IV of the Personal Responsibility and Work Opportunity Reconciliation Act", incorporating 63 Fed. Reg. 41658. Title IV makes a non-citizen who is not a qualified alien (8 U.S.C. 1641) ineligible for any Federal public benefit (8 U.S.C. 1611(a), S1911), and the incorporated HHS notice lists the Child Care and Development Fund as a Federal public benefit (S1912). So a child with Temporary Protected Status or another lawfully present but non-qualified status does not meet 3.111.F.1; cccap_child_meets_citizenship_requirement (CO-998) reads is_us_citizen_or_national or is_qualified_non_citizen. CO-998-T3 now expects false; no household case has such a child. The CDHS reviewer's narrower reading is confirmed by the published definition. Items: CO-998.

### OQ-395. Resolved: CCCAP defaults that granted eligibility removed

Body: cccap_is_adult_caretaker (CO-992) reads cccap_is_financially_contributing_to_child (CO-897) as true when it is not stated, and cccap_colorado_works_child_care_eligible (CO-988) reads is_referred_for_colorado_works_child_care (CO-896) as true when it is not stated. The vocabulary review (C-4) asks that defaults which grant eligibility be removed or raised with the child care owner. The volume keeps them and says so in each fact's precision; removing them would make every case that does not state the two facts unknown for CCCAP. The child care owner decides. (The citizenship default of cccap_is_eligible_child, CO-980, was removed in the same round.) Items: CO-992, CO-988, CO-896, CO-897.

Resolution (Phase-10 open-question sweep): Resolved. Both defaults are gone (OQ-134, OQ-135): cccap_colorado_works_child_care_eligible (CO-988) and cccap_is_adult_caretaker (CO-992) read the referral and financially-contributing facts as stated, and the cases state them.

### OQ-396. Resolved: one set of neutral relationship roles; each program's caretaker, parent and sibling is its own derived item

Body: The case-level relationship edges are shared: one caretaker edge is read as SNAP parental control (CO-305), the Medicaid caretaker relative ("a relative ... who assumes primary responsibility", CO-547), the Colorado Works parent or non-parent caretaker (CO-900) and the CCCAP adult caretaker ("legal guardian, or person who is acting in loco parentis", CO-992). A non-relative guardian is a CCCAP caretaker and not a Medicaid caretaker relative. Marriage is stated by marital_status (CO-844) and by the spouse edge, and SNAP's spouse includes persons representing themselves as spouses. The engine cannot declare a new relationship role from a volume (OQ-132), so per-program edges, or the qualifying facts (degree of relationship, guardianship) the rules could test, are not modeled in the vocabulary fix round (review R-15). Items: CO-305, CO-547, CO-900, CO-992, CO-844.

Open-question sweep (2026-11-21): a ledger-design limit, not a text question: the engine cannot declare a per-program relationship role from a volume (OQ-132). Left open.

Phase-10 open-question sweep: LEAP now has its own relationship role (shares_heat_with, OQ-132), so its economic unit no longer depends on the shared family edges alone. Checked 2026-11-21 for the rest: the CCCAP adult caretaker (8 CCR 1403-1 3.103.C, "In loco parentis" 3.103.MMM, D-87) and the Medicaid caretaker relative read the same caretaker edge; no published rule requires the edges to differ, and splitting them is a ledger-design choice.

Resolution (engineering item follow-up pass, 2026-11-21; product-owner decision in docs/plan-colorado.md and same-word audit SW-0 to SW-4, docs/reviews/audit-same-word-different-meaning.md): the engine reads the roles from volume.yaml (OQ-132), so the blocker is gone; the roles added state what is true of the persons, not a program's word: `stepparent`/`stepchild`, `legal_guardian`/`ward`, `foster_parent`/`foster_child`, `other_relative`, `holds_out_as_spouse_with` and `has_primary_responsibility_for`/`in_primary_care_of`; `parent` is the legal (natural or adoptive) parent. The `caretaker`/`dependent` role is retired (its edges become `has_primary_responsibility_for`). Each program's word is its own derived item: Medicaid's parent or caretaker relative, ma_caretaker_relative_of (CO-2606: parent, stepparent or primary responsibility; "related to the dependent child or any adult with whom the dependent child is living and who assumes responsibility", § 8.100.1, S1500); CCCAP's adult caretaker, cccap_adult_caretaker_of (CO-2607: "parent, adoptive parent, step-parent, legal guardian, or person who is acting in 'loco parentis'", 3.103.C, S481); Colorado Works's caretaker, cw_caretaker_of (CO-2608: parent or non-parent caretaker, 3.601, S406; the spouse of a parent stays the optional member of 3.604.2.C.2.a); SNAP's mandatory parent and parental-control edges (CO-305: "natural or adoptive parent(s) or step-parent(s)", a child "(other than a foster child)", S2, S4); community engagement's parent and guardian (CO-1962, CO-1924 now derived from `legal_guardian`, S1207); LEAP's "parent or guardian" (CO-1000, CO-1009). Siblings are derived, never stated: siblings (CO-2609, a shared legal parent) and step_siblings (CO-2610), which MAGI (CO-500, S169) and community engagement (CO-1963, S1207) read together and Colorado Works (CO-2000) reads alone. Step-parents are stated the same way in every case (CO-04, CO-08 `stepparent` in place of `parent`; CO-83 adds the stepfather's `stepparent` edge); every household expectation is unchanged. Still to do in the same-word fix round: holding out as spouses read by SNAP only (SW-6), one co-residence fact for every program (SW-7), foster placement facts (SW-8), and the full and half sibling split for Colorado Works (SW-2). How S1500's "assumes responsibility" and 3.103.C's "loco parentis" relate to `has_primary_responsibility_for` is OQ-540. Items: CO-305, CO-500, CO-547, CO-900, CO-982, CO-992, CO-1924, CO-1962, CO-1963, CO-2606, CO-2607, CO-2608, CO-2609, CO-2610.

### OQ-410. Community engagement: Colorado's look-back months and verification frequency are not published

Body: 42 CFR 435.556(a)(1) and 42 U.S.C. 1396a(xx)(1)(A) (S1211, S1215) let the State plan require community engagement for one to three consecutive months before the month of application, and 435.556(a)(2) for one or more months, consecutive or not, before a renewal; 435.557(d) (S1216) lets the State verify more often than at renewal. No published Colorado rule (the current 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100, D-65, effective 11/14/2026, says nothing of community engagement) or State plan amendment on an allowed host states the choices. The draft uses one month at application (ma_community_engagement_application_lookback_months, CO-1902) and one month at renewal (ma_community_engagement_renewal_required_months, CO-1903), the least the federal text allows, verifies at application and renewal only, and reads the renewal period as the months from the month of the most recent determination's effective date through the month before the determination (CO-1973). Items: CO-1902, CO-1903, CO-1973, CO-1974.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner, State plan coordinator
Assumption: The work requirement looks back one month at application and one month at renewal, and is checked only at those times.

Checked 2026-11-22: 42 CFR 435.556(a)(1)-(2) and 435.557(d) (S1211, S1216) and 42 U.S.C. 1396a(xx)(1)(A) (S1215) leave the look-back months and the verification frequency to the State plan, and 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100 (D-65, effective 11/14/2026) has no community engagement text. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-411. Community engagement: the short-term hardship election, a good faith exemption, and early implementation

Body: 42 CFR 435.555 (S1210) is a State option; 435.559(b) lets a State implement before January 1, 2027; 435.560 (S1213) lets CMS exempt a State that shows a good faith effort, for six months at a time and no later than December 31, 2028. No published Colorado rule or CMS approval on an allowed host states any of these. The draft does not apply the hardship exception (ma_elects_short_term_hardship_exception, CO-1911, false; the hardship items CO-1939-CO-1943 and CO-1971 are modeled so that a yes changes only the parameter) and starts the requirement on January 1, 2027 (CO-1900). If CMS grants Colorado an exemption, CO-1900 needs a later dated value. Items: CO-1900, CO-1911, CO-1971, CO-1974.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner, State plan coordinator
Assumption: Colorado does not use the short-term hardship exception, has no good-faith exemption, and starts the requirement on January 1, 2027.

Checked 2026-11-22: 42 CFR 435.555, 435.559(b) and 435.560 (S1210, S1213) make each a State option or a CMS decision, and 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100 (D-65) has no community engagement text; no CMS approval for Colorado is on an allowed host. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-412. Community engagement: other State choices inside the exclusions and the hours

Body: The federal text leaves these to the State, and Colorado has published none: the optional relatives of the caretaker relative definition, which follow the State's 42 CFR 435.110 elections (435.554(a)(iv), S1207); the list of conditions that identify the medically frail (435.554(c)(5)(ii), S1209), so the five conditions are supplied facts; a minimum time commitment for a drug or alcohol treatment program (435.554(c)(8)); computing work hours from income divided by the minimum wage when hours are not documented (435.552(e)(2)(i)-(ii), S1204); and not seeking further information to verify a mandatory exception (435.557(g)(1), S1216). (Phase-10 needs-fact sweep, 2026-11-21: the first of these is published after all. Colorado's 435.110 election is its definition of caretaker relative, "a person who is related to the dependent child or any adult with whom the dependent child is living and who assumes responsibility for the dependent child's care" (§ 8.100.1, S1500), which takes 435.4's option (3); 435.554(a)(iv) applies the same election, and CO-1962 cites it. See OQ-51.) The draft reads the caretaker edge as the 435.4 relatives, supplies the medically frail conditions, applies no minimum time, counts documented hours only, and does not model verification of the requirement (435.557). (Final verification P3-2, 2026-11-22: the noncompliance procedures of 435.558 were modeled in audit fix round 4, CO-3161 to CO-3165, recorded in OQ-65; the earlier clause saying they were not is struck.) Items: CO-1962, CO-1964, CO-1965, CO-1967.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner, State plan coordinator
Assumption: The medically frail conditions are entered case by case, a treatment program needs no minimum time, only documented hours count, and the State's verification choices are not applied.

Checked 2026-11-22: 42 CFR 435.554(c)(5)(ii) and (c)(8), 435.552(e)(2) and 435.557(g)(1) (S1204, S1207, S1209, S1216) leave each to the State, and 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100 (D-65) has no community engagement text. No published rule settles them.

### OQ-413. Resolved: community engagement - the mandatory groups, the relatives, the 1115 branch, the noncompliance procedure and the undocumented adult

Body: (1) "Described in" a mandatory group of 1902(a)(10)(A)(i)(I)-(VII) (435.553(a)(3)) is read through SSI, TANF and title IV-E receipt and Colorado's MAGI Parents and Caretaker Relatives, Pregnant and Children categories (CO-1970); subclause (V) has no Colorado category. (2) The family caregiver's relatives (435.554(c)(3)(i)(B)) are read from the volume's relationship edges, which have no uncle, aunt, cousin, nephew or niece role (CO-1963; OQ-396). (3) The 1115 demonstration branch of the applicable individual (435.551(b)) is not modeled: Colorado has no expansion-like 1115 adult coverage in the fetched text. (4) The status is evaluated at the determination; the 30-day noncompliance notice period and continued coverage of 435.558 are outside this volume's scope. (5) Whether an undocumented adult who would be in the Adults group but does not meet community engagement is still emergency-only (CO-510 reads ma_magi_category, which is now none for that person) is not addressed by the text. Items: CO-1963, CO-1970, CO-1974, CO-551.

Engineering item follow-up pass (2026-11-21), part (2) resolved: the new `other_relative` role states an aunt, uncle, cousin, nephew, niece or other relative by blood, adoption or marriage, and ma_is_family_caregiver_for_community_engagement (CO-1963) reads 435.554(c)(3)(i)(B)'s relative ("by blood, adoption, or marriage", S1207) as parent, child, stepparent, stepchild, grandparent, grandchild, spouse, siblings (CO-2609), step_siblings (CO-2610) or other_relative; a person with primary responsibility who is not a relative is no longer read as one (same-word audit SW-5). Parts (1), (3), (4) and (5) stay open.

Audit fix round 4 (2026-11-21), parts (1), (3), (4) and (5) resolved, fixed in full at the product owner's direction. (1) The mandatory-group reading, subclause by subclause of 1902(a)(10)(A)(i) (S2104): (I) is TANF and title IV-E receipt and the section 1931 Parents and Caretaker Relatives category; its plans under titles I, X and XIV were repealed for the States from January 1, 1974 (Pub. L. 92-603 sec. 303, S2106), and title XVI cash is the federal SSI program read under (II). (II) is SSI receipt and, newly, SSA's continued SSI status without a payment, which includes the section 1619(b) "qualified severely impaired individual" ((II)(bb); retains_ssi_status_without_payment, CO-1112). (III), (IV), (VI) and (VII) are pregnant women and children under 19, already deemed under 435.553(a)(1) (under 19 for part of the month) and (a)(4) (pregnant or postpartum, a specified excluded individual), so ma_is_described_in_mandatory_group_i_to_vii (CO-1970) no longer reads the Pregnant and Children categories, which also reached optional income levels above the mandatory ones. (V) is empty: "No individual shall be a qualified family member for any period after September 30, 1998" (42 U.S.C. 1396d(m)(2), S2105). (3) The section 1115 branch of 435.551(b) (1902(xx)(9)(A)(i)(II), S2108) has no Colorado population: Colorado's only section 1115 demonstration in CMS's record is "Expanding the Substance Use Disorder Continuum of Care" (D-168, S1713), whose expenditure authorities are for services to people covered under the State plan, and CMS reads 1902(xx)(9)(A)(i)(II) not to describe "section 1115 demonstrations that ... include section 1115(a)(2) expenditure authority only for specific services (versus eligibility) for groups covered under the State plan" (91 FR 33353-33354, S2107); the demonstration's continuous eligibility authority for children up to three and people leaving incarceration expired June 30, 2026, unimplemented. So the applicable individual is the 435.551(a) Adults group alone, as ma_community_engagement_status (CO-1974) reads it; a new demonstration covering adults outside the State plan would add the branch. (4) The noncompliance procedure of 435.558 and 1902(xx)(6) (S2108, S2109) is modeled: the date on the notice of noncompliance (CO-3161), a showing of later receipt (CO-3162), the five-day receipt and 30-day showing period as parameters (CO-3163, CO-3164), and the period's last day (CO-3165). ma_community_engagement_status (CO-1974) reports notice_required (the facts do not show the requirement met and no notice has been sent) and notice_period (the 30 days are running) before not_met; ma_meets_adult_group_community_engagement_condition (CO-1975) keeps an enrolled beneficiary covered through those months and, after a period with no showing, through the month after the month it ended, the latest month of disenrollment (435.558(d)(2)(ii); OQ-593 for an earlier one); an applicant is pending (ma_magi_eligibility_status, CO-554, pending_community_engagement) and is denied only once the period ends. CO-73's p2 changes from not_met / ineligible_community_engagement to notice_required / pending_community_engagement. (5) An undocumented adult who would be in the Adults group but does not meet community engagement is not emergency-only. 42 U.S.C. 1396b(v)(2)(B) pays for emergency services to a non-citizen who "otherwise meets the eligibility requirements for medical assistance under the State plan" (S2110), and the Adults group is "subject to subsections (k) and (xx)" (1902(a)(10)(A)(i)(VIII), S2104); 42 CFR 435.119(d) makes "eligibility under this section ... subject to the community engagement requirement" (S1200), and 435.119(b)'s criteria do not include citizenship or immigration status, which 435.406 states separately, so such an adult is "eligible to enroll ... under § 435.119" for 435.551(a) and the requirement is part of what the person must otherwise meet. CMS's preamble lists citizenship and immigration status among the bases of eligibility for the group and calls community engagement a new condition of eligibility (S2111). The draft's existing result therefore stands: ma_is_emergency_medicaid_only (CO-510) reads ma_meets_requirements_other_than_status (CO-1150), which reads ma_magi_category (CO-551), which places a person in the Adults group only when the condition holds; during the noncompliance period the applicant is pending, as any applicant is. Items: CO-1970, CO-1974, CO-1975, CO-554, CO-3161, CO-3162, CO-3163, CO-3164, CO-3165.

### OQ-430. Resolved: OAP for a qualified non-citizen - the statute's entry-date or five-year-bar condition applies (C.R.S. 26-2-111(2)(c))

Body: C.R.S. 26-2-111(2)(c)(I) (D-59, S1316) says a qualified alien "shall not be granted the old age pension" unless the alien meets 26-2-111.8(2)(a) (entry before August 22, 1996) or (2)(b) (the five-year bar) and (2)(c) (sponsor deeming), except, under (2)(c)(II)-(III), an alien abandoned or mistreated by the sponsor, one without a sponsor who would suffer significant financial hardship, one whose sponsor can no longer meet the alien's needs, or one also eligible for SSI. 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.68.A (D-80, S1317) states the five-year bar for "AND" only, and 3.520.61.C's citizenship prong is likewise stated for AND. af_meets_citizenship_requirement_oap (CO-929) follows the rule (citizen, national or qualified non-citizen, no bar), because the rule is what counties administer and (2)(c)(II) leaves its exceptions to "rules of the state department", which state none for OAP. Following the statute instead would add the bar to CO-929 with facts for the four exceptions. Checked 2026-11-21: the version of 9 CCR 2503-5 effective 2026-03-02 (D-80) and C.R.S. 26-2-111 (2024, D-59); no published rule reconciles them. Items: CO-929.

Resolution (CDHS stakeholder review fix round (2026-11-21), S-4): C.R.S. 26-2-111(2)(c)(I) (S1316) says a qualified alien "shall not be granted the old age pension" unless 26-2-111.8(2)(a) or (b) is met; a rule's silence ("for AND only", 3.520.68.A, S1317) cannot narrow a statutory bar, and the volume already follows the governing statute over a Colorado rule that has not caught up (SNAP, OQ-1). The CDHS reviewer's position is the same, to be confirmed by CDHS Legal. af_meets_citizenship_requirement_oap (CO-929) now requires meets_public_assistance_citizenship_requirement (CO-905: citizen or national, or a qualified non-citizen who entered before August 22, 1996, is federally exempt or has held qualified status five years), or a qualified non-citizen who receives SSI ((2)(c)(III)) or for whom the county finds a sponsor-related or significant financial hardship ((2)(c)(II), af_oap_qualified_alien_hardship_exception, CO-2572, read as false when not stated). CO-929 carries a statute_override note. CO-71's p3 (an LPR since 2023 with no sponsor and no income) now states the hardship finding, so his OAP and OAP health care expectations are unchanged. Items: CO-929, CO-2572.

### OQ-470. CHP+ copayment bands: ratios between 150% and 151%, and between 200% and 201%

Body: 10 CCR 2505-3 § 320.1 (S1504) states the copayment bands in whole percentages of the federal poverty level: less than 101% (A), "between 101% and 150%" (B), "between 151% and 200%" (C) and "between 201% and 260%" (D). A family whose income ratio is above 150% but below 151%, or above 200% but below 201%, falls in no band. chp_copayment_income_band (CO-2228) reads B as up to and including 150% and C as up to and including 200%, so those ratios fall into the next band up (C and D). The rule also leaves open whether "between 101% and 150%" includes exactly 150%; the draft includes it in B. Checked 2026-11-21: § 320.1 (D-66) and § 330 (D-66); no published rule settles the gaps. Items: CO-2228, CO-2225, CO-2226, CO-2227.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: CHP+ policy owner
Assumption: Income at exactly 150 or 200 percent of poverty falls in the lower copayment band, and a ratio just above it falls in the next band up.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-3 § 320.1 and § 330 (D-66, S1504) state the bands in whole percentages ("between 101% and 150%", "between 151% and 200%", "between 201% and 260%"). No published rule settles the ratios between them.

### OQ-471. Resolved: an income trust user's patient payment keeps the family allowance (42 U.S.C. 1396r-5(d)(1))

Body: The income trust rules of 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.7 (trusts, 6.a.i.c, S1508) say "The only deductions from the monthly trust distribution to the Long Term Care institution are the allowable deductions which are permitted for Medical Assistance-eligible persons who do not have income trusts. Allowable deductions include only the following: i) Personal need allowance ii) Spousal income payments iii) Approved PETI payments". The first sentence allows every deduction a person without a trust receives, which § 8.100.7.T.1 and 42 U.S.C. 1396r-5(d)(1) make include the family allowance for dependent family members; the list that follows omits it. ma_patient_payment (CO-658) keeps the family allowance for a trust user, reading the first sentence and the federal requirement over the list. Checked 2026-11-21: § 8.100.7 (D-65) states both sentences; no published rule settles which governs. Items: CO-658, CO-2238.

Class: resolved by text on file (was "needs Legal"; coverage pass 6, non-MAGI F6-11)
Owner: Legal, LTSS policy owner
Assumption: A person with an income trust still gets the family allowance for dependent family members in the patient payment.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.7 (D-65, S1508) first allows every deduction a person without a trust receives and then lists only the personal needs allowance, spousal income and PETI payments; § 8.100.7.T.1 and 42 U.S.C. 1396r-5(d)(1) include the family allowance. The readings: (a) the first sentence and the statute govern, the reading in force; (b) the list is exhaustive. No published rule reconciles them.

Resolution (coverage pass 6, non-MAGI F6-11, 2026-11-24; docs/reviews/coverage6-medicaid-nonmagi.md): the text on file decides it. 42 U.S.C. 1396r-5(d)(1) (D-47, S5033): after an institutionalized spouse is determined eligible, "there shall be deducted from the spouse's monthly income the following amounts in the following order", and (C) is "A family allowance, for each family member"; the statute names no exception for income placed in a trust, and the State rule's first sentence allows every deduction a person without a trust receives (S1508). A State rule cannot narrow a deduction the Act requires (the section supersedes any inconsistent provision in determining an institutionalized spouse's eligibility, 1396r-5(a)(1), S3615, and a State plan must follow the Act), so the list's omission is not a reading open to the State: the family allowance is deducted for a trust user, the reading in force (ma_patient_payment, CO-658; ma_family_allowance, CO-657, already takes the federal floor). No item changes. The question is closed; Legal may still advise HCPF to correct the list in the rule.

### OQ-472. Residency of an institutionalized person who became incapable of indicating intent at or after 21

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.3.B.2.b (S1518) makes the state of residence of an institutionalized person who became incapable of indicating intent at or after age 21 "the state in which the person was living when he or she became incapable of indicating intent", or, if that cannot be determined, when first so determined. B.2.c says that "upon placement in another state, the new state is the state of residence unless the current state of residence is involved in the placement", and 42 CFR 435.403(h)(4) (D-33) makes it "the State in which the individual is physically present, except where another State makes a placement". For a person who became incapable in one state and was then placed privately in an institution in another, B.2.b gives the first state and B.2.c and the federal rule the second. ma_institutionalized_state_of_residence (CO-2257) follows B.2.c and the federal rule (the institution's state, ma_institution_state), because the federal definition binds the State plan. Checked 2026-11-21: § 8.100.3.B.2-3 (D-65) and 42 CFR 435.403(e), (h) (D-33); no published rule reconciles them. Items: CO-2257, CO-2250, CO-2254.

Class: state policy choice (reclassified from "needs Legal" in coverage pass 4, 2026-11-23, docs/reviews/coverage4-medicaid-magi-chp.md section 4: both texts are on file (§ 8.100.3.B.2.b-c, 42 CFR 435.403(h)(4)); reconciling the State's rule with the federal residency rule a state plan must follow is the State's (HCPF amends B.2.b or confirms the reading in force), with Legal advising; no unfetched document would decide it)
Owner: Legal, Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: A person who became unable to state intent at or after 21 and was then placed privately in another state's institution is a resident of the state where the institution is.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.3.B.2.b-c (D-65, S1518) and 42 CFR 435.403(h)(4) (D-33, "physically present"): B.2.b gives the state the person lived in when the incapacity began, B.2.c and the federal rule the state of the institution. The readings: (a) the federal rule and B.2.c govern, the reading in force; (b) B.2.b governs. No published rule reconciles them.

### OQ-473. Ineligible child allocation: only from an ineligible parent's income, or also from applicant spouses with children

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.5.H.3.a.i (S1506) disregards the Ineligible Child Allocation "from the ineligible parents' gross income", and H.2.c defines an ineligible parent or spouse as one "who is not applying or eligible for SSI". H.3.a.iii adds that "No allocations are allowed for applicant/recipient spouses who do not have children in the home", which implies that applicant or recipient spouses who do have children in the home receive allocations from their own income. The draft takes the allocation only from the income of a spouse living with the person who is not applying for or eligible for SSI (ma_ssi_child_allocation_from_spouse_unearned and _earned, CO-2278, CO-2279), as H.3.a.i says; a single applicant parent, or a couple both applying for or eligible for SSI, gets no allocation. Checked 2026-11-21: § 8.100.5.H.1-4 (D-65), which incorporates 20 CFR 416.1112 only, not the SSI deeming rules; no published rule settles it. Items: CO-2278, CO-2279.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner, LTSS policy owner
Assumption: The allocation for an ineligible child is taken only from the income of a spouse who is not applying for or eligible for SSI.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.5.H.1-4 (D-65, S1506): H.3.a.i takes the allocation "from the ineligible parents' gross income", and H.3.a.iii implies that applicant spouses with children in the home get one too, without saying from what; H.4.e incorporates 20 CFR 416.1112 only. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-490. Resolved: Colorado Works disregards on a disqualified member's earnings - 3.605.1.A.6 governs status and SSN disqualifications, 3.604.2.M the others

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.2.M (S1004) says a disqualified or ineligible required member's income "must be considered when determining eligibility without applying income disregards", and 3.606.2's introduction (S1628) applies the disregards to "cases with eligible members that have earned income (not including disqualified individuals)". 3.605.1.A.6 (S1628) deems the "net income" of a required member disqualified for citizenship, non-citizen status, lawful presence or SSN reasons, where "Net income equals gross earned income minus employment disregards and employment incentives plus any unearned income". The two readings differ for exactly the members M.4 and M.6 name. cw_countable_income (CO-910) and cw_payment_countable_income (CO-923) follow M and 3.606.2 (gross earnings, no disregard) for every member cw_is_disqualified_member (CO-928) marks, two provisions against one. Checked 2026-11-21: 3.604.2.M, 3.605.1.A.6 and 3.606.2 (D-81, effective 2025-07-01); no published rule reconciles them. Items: CO-910, CO-923, CO-928.

Resolution (CDHS stakeholder review fix round (2026-11-21), S-5): 3.605.1.A.6 (S1628) is the specific provision for required members disqualified for citizenship, non-citizen status, lawful presence or SSN reasons and deems their "net income", "gross earned income minus employment disregards ... plus any unearned income"; the same section calls the $90 "the employment disregard" (3.605.1.A.4.a, S1808). 3.604.2.M's "without applying income disregards" then governs the other disqualified members (an intentional program violation, a fleeing felon). cw_countable_income (CO-910) and cw_payment_countable_income (CO-923) now count a status- or SSN-disqualified member's earnings less $90 (cw_is_disqualified_for_status_or_ssn, CO-2557) and an IPV or fleeing-felon member's earnings gross; test CO-910-T5 ($1,000 of earnings: 910). The payment figure does not give the member the 100% or 67% payment disregard, since 3.606.2 excludes disqualified individuals. The CDHS reviewer asks that CBMS budgeting for an ineligible non-citizen parent confirm it. Items: CO-910, CO-923, CO-928, CO-2557.

### OQ-491. Resolved: Adult Financial first-month proration - the day of eligibility counts, by 3.581.D's table, and the cents are dropped

Body: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.532.B.2, 3.543.B.2 and 3.548.B.2 (S1635) prorate a client's first grant "according to the number of days remaining in the month" when the client is determined eligible on a day other than the first. Whether the day of eligibility itself is a remaining day is not said; read inclusively, a client eligible on the 10th of a 31-day month gets 22/31, read exclusively 21/31. af_oap_grant (CO-951) and af_and_grant (CO-952) count the day of eligibility, as SNAP's stated formula counts the day of application (10 CCR 2506-1 4.207.2.C, S1615: days in the month plus one, less the date) and as "If determined eligible on the first of the month" receiving the whole grant implies. The rule also states no rounding; the prorated grant is left unrounded. Checked 2026-11-21: 3.532.B, 3.543.B and 3.548.B (D-80, effective 2026-03-02); no published rule settles either point. Items: CO-951, CO-952.

Resolution (engineering item follow-up pass, 2026-11-21): both points are settled by 9 CCR 2503-5 3.581.D-E (D-80, re-fetched 2026-11-21; S1825), which this question's earlier checks had not found. 3.581.D has the county "Determine the number of days for which the client is eligible for assistance" and multiply the monthly grant payment by the decimal figure its table gives for that number of days, so the day of eligibility is a day for which the client is eligible (22 days for a client eligible from October 10) and the figure is the table's (.72329 for 22 days), not days over days in the month (22/31). 3.581.E: "All payments, including partial payments, shall have any cents dropped to the nearest dollar, except in cases where SSI income reduces the grant." New items af_partial_month_payment_factor_table (CO-2602), af_partial_month_payment_factor (CO-2603) and af_grant_is_reduced_by_ssi_income (CO-2604); af_oap_grant (CO-951) and af_and_grant (CO-952) apply the factor to the monthly payment and drop the cents unless SSI income reduces the grant. CO-951-T8 changes from 604.6451612903226 (22/31 of 852) to 616 (852 x .72329 = 616.24308, cents dropped). No household case states an eligibility date or an Adult Financial grant with cents, so no case expectation changes. Items: CO-951, CO-952, CO-2602, CO-2603, CO-2604.

### OQ-510. The October 2026 federal payment limit is applied by determination date, not by the month of service

Body: 42 U.S.C. 1396b(v)(5) (S1700) limits payment "beginning on October 1, 2026" for medical assistance furnished to an individual, which reads as a limit on services furnished from that date; CMS SHO #26-001 (S1704) applies it to applicants determined eligible and to enrollees on and after October 1, 2026. ma_meets_citizenship_requirement (CO-509) is a per-person test, so it applies the limit when the determination date is on or after October 1, 2026 (ma_alien_payment_restriction_start_date, CO-2401). A retroactive month before October 2026 on an application determined later (a refugee adult applying in November 2026 for September) is then judged by the October 2026 rule, although the statute would pay for September's services. Reading status per month would need CO-509 and its readers to be per-month. Items: CO-509, CO-2400, CO-2401.

Hard-coded numbers fix round (audit E-1): the switch is a dated yes/no parameter, ma_alien_payment_restriction_applies (CO-3034), false before October 1, 2026 and true from it, rather than a comparison of the determination date with ma_alien_payment_restriction_start_date (CO-2401, retired). The question, determination date or month of service, stands.

Class: state policy choice (reclassified from "needs Legal" in coverage pass 4, 2026-11-23, docs/reviews/coverage4-medicaid-magi-chp.md section 4: the statute (S1700) and CMS's implementing letter (SHO #26-001, S1704) are on file and support the determination-date reading in force; how Colorado applies the limit to a retroactive month before October 2026 is the State's implementation choice, with Legal advising; no unfetched document would decide it)
Owner: Legal, Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The October 2026 limit on federal payment for some non-citizens is applied to every month of a determination made on or after October 1, 2026, including earlier months of retroactive coverage.

Checked 2026-11-22: 42 U.S.C. 1396b(v)(5) (S1700) limits payment "beginning on October 1, 2026" for assistance furnished, and CMS SHO #26-001 (S1704) applies it to applicants determined eligible and enrollees on and after that date; no Colorado rule addresses retroactive months. The readings: (a) by determination date, the reading in force; (b) by month of service, which would pay for September 2026 services on a later application. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-511. Children with Disabilities Buy-In premiums: whether the public health emergency waiver has ended

Body: § 8.100.6.Q.1.g.vii (S1728) says the Department "will continue to waive premiums until the last day of the twelfth month following the end of the COVID-19 Public Health Emergency" (May 11, 2023, so May 31, 2024) and "will notify all members as to when required premiums will resume". ma_cbwd_premium (CO-621) reads the waiver as ended and charges the schedule. The HCPF reviewer [K] asked to confirm whether premiums are being charged; the Department's notice is on hcpf.colorado.gov, which this session cannot fetch. Items: CO-621.

Class: blocked on a named document
Owner: Premium and finance owner
Assumption: The pandemic waiver of buy-in premiums for children has ended, and premiums are charged on the schedule.
Blocked on: HCPF's notice to members that Children with Disabilities Buy-In premiums resume, which 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.6.Q.1.g.vii promises (hcpf.colorado.gov refused)

### OQ-512. "The MAGI needs standard for his/her family size" in the needy persons under 21 groups

Body: § 8.100.4.H.1.a and d (S1717) admit Psych <21 and Alternatives to Foster Care youth "whose household income is less than the MAGI needs standard for his/her family size"; § 8.100 does not define the term. ma_needy_young_person_category (CO-2419) reads it as the highest MAGI income standard among the groups whose demographic criteria the person meets (ma_magi_highest_income_standard_pct, CO-539: 142% for a child under 19, 133% for an adult of 19 or 20), compared strictly with the MAGI household income. HCPF's Eligibility Policy section would know the standard CBMS applies. Items: CO-2419.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The needs standard for a young person's family size is the highest MAGI income limit of the groups whose age and other conditions the person meets.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.4.H.1.a and d (D-65, S1717) use "the MAGI needs standard for his/her family size", and neither the definitions of § 8.100.1 nor any other paragraph of § 8.100 (D-65) defines it. No published rule settles it.

### OQ-513. Resolved: hospital presumptive eligibility for adults is the Adult Group, 19 through the end of the month the person turns 65 (state plan page S21)

Body: § 8.100.3.R.1.c and R.2.d (S1721, S1722) open hospital presumptive eligibility to "An applicant aged 19-65" at or below 133% FPL. The Adults group the category stands for ends at 65 (42 CFR 435.119(b)(1); ma_adult_upper_age_limit, CO-199), so ma_hospital_presumptive_eligibility_category (CO-2439) reads the range as 19 through 64. Read literally, a 65-year-old would qualify for a presumptive period in a group whose full determination excludes them. Items: CO-2439.

Resolution (coverage round, 2026-11-22; coverage review F-13): Colorado's hospital presumptive eligibility state plan amendment CO-13-051-MM (D-316, approved 07/21/2014), page S21, lists the groups for which hospitals determine presumptive eligibility: the "Adult Group, if covered by the state" is selected and "Eligibility groups for individuals age 65 and over" is not (S3363; "Individuals above 133% FPL under Age 65, if covered by the state" is also selected, a group Colorado does not cover). R.2.d's "aged 19-65" is therefore the Adult Group as Colorado covers it: from 19 through the end of the month the person turns 65 (§ 8.100.1, S3350; the coverage round's reading of the Adults group, ma_is_under_adult_upper_age_through_birthday_month, CO-4550). ma_hospital_presumptive_eligibility_category (CO-2439) reads CO-4550, so a person whose 65th birthday fell earlier in the determination month is in the adult arm and a person past that month is not (CO-2439-T15, T16). The reviewer's note that the above-133% group was unselected is not borne out by the page image; it does not change the answer. Items: CO-2439, CO-4550.

Checked 2026-11-22: Colorado State Plan Amendment CO-13-051-MM, page S21 (D-316, medicaid.gov, transcribed from the page image); 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.1 and § 8.100.3.R.1.c, R.2.d (D-65, S3350, S1721, S1722); 42 CFR 435.1110 and 435.119(b)(1) (D-35, D-31).

### OQ-514. Funding source and CBMS aid codes

Body: The HCPF review's C-12 [K] asked for the funding stream (Title XIX, Title XXI, state-only) CBMS needs for aid codes and HCPF needs for budget. ma_funding_source (CO-2456) states it from the volume's own outcomes and the funding the statutes name (C.R.S. 25.5-2-104(4) and 25.5-5-201(6)(c) for state-only coverage, S1729, S1730; CMS SHO #26-001 for CHIP and emergency services, S1703, S1731). The aid codes themselves, and how CBMS orders a person who qualifies through more than one route, are HCPF operational choices not published in a fetchable source. Items: CO-2456.

Class: blocked on a named document
Owner: Medicaid policy owner, Premium and finance owner
Assumption: The funding stream is stated from the program each result belongs to and the funding the statutes name; the codes the eligibility system assigns are placeholders.
Blocked on: HCPF's CBMS aid code table and its order of precedence for a person eligible through more than one route (not published on an allowed host; hcpf.colorado.gov refused)

### OQ-515. The date Medicaid, CHP+ and the Medicare Savings Programs start using each year's poverty guidelines

Body: federal_poverty_guideline_annual_table (CO-181) and its companion (CO-182) date each HHS table from January 1 (2025 from S1710, 2026 from S55). HHS publishes in mid-January, and the HCPF reviewer says [K] HCPF applies the new guidelines on the date its annual memo sets (F-1); the memo is on hcpf.colorado.gov, which this session cannot fetch. A January determination may therefore use a figure HCPF had not yet adopted. Items: CO-181, CO-182.

Class: blocked on a named document
Owner: Medicaid policy owner, CHP+ policy owner
Assumption: Each year's federal poverty guidelines are used from January 1.
Blocked on: HCPF's annual memo setting the date Medical Assistance, CHP+ and the Medicare Savings Programs adopt the new poverty guidelines (hcpf.colorado.gov refused)

### OQ-516. Resolved: 2025 spousal impoverishment and Medicare Savings Program figures loaded from CMS's bulletin of November 15, 2024

Body: The HCPF review (F-2) asked for 2025 versions of the indexed figures so that a 2026 application's retroactive months in late 2025 have a figure. The 2025 SSI federal benefit rates, substantial gainful activity amounts (S1711) and poverty guidelines (S1710) are now versions. CMS's 2025 SSI and Spousal Impoverishment Standards chart (the 2025 CSRA minimum and maximum, the MMMNA maximum and the home equity limit) and the 2025 Medicare Savings Program resource limits were not found at a fetchable URL (medicaid.gov serves only the current chart and a 2021 one at the addresses tried; medicare.gov serves the current limits), so ma_csra_minimum, ma_csra_maximum, ma_mmmna_cap, ma_home_equity_limit and the MSP resource limits (CO-827, CO-828, CO-830, CO-831, CO-807, CO-808) still begin on 2026-01-01 and a 2025 month has no value for them. Items: CO-807, CO-808, CO-827, CO-828, CO-830, CO-831.

Resolution (final verification pass 3, P2-1, 2026-11-22): CMS's CMCS Informational Bulletin of November 15, 2024, "2025 SSI, Spousal Impoverishment, and Medicare Savings Program Resource Standards", is on medicaid.gov (https://www.medicaid.gov/federal-policy-guidance/downloads/cib11152024.pdf, fetched 2026-11-22, D-280). It states the 2025 QMB, SLMB and QI resource standards, $9,660 and $14,470 (S3006), and its 2025 chart the Maximum Monthly Maintenance Needs Allowance 3,948.00, the community spouse minimum and maximum resource standards 31,584.00 and 157,920.00, and the minimum home equity limit 730,000.00 (S3007). Each of the six parameters has a 2025 version from 2025-01-01 to 2026-01-01. No household case changed (none has a 2025 month that reads them). Items: CO-807, CO-808, CO-827, CO-828, CO-830, CO-831.
