The other reading fails a client whose resources equal the limit exactly. Items: CO-948.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: Countable resources equal to the Adult Financial resource limit meet it.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.71.A.1 ("below the resource limit") and 3.520.72.A (D-80, rule version 12428, effective 03/02/2026) against C.R.S. 26-2-111(2)(a) (D-59). No published rule settles it. Coverage round: 3.510 "Eligible client" ("a client whose countable resources are below the resource limit", D-80) uses the same word; C.R.S. 26-2-111(2)(a)(I) and (4)(d) apply the SSI resource eligibility requirements, which support the reading taken.

### OQ-680. CCCAP: the date the prior thirty days run back from, the self-employment month, and the count of a lump sum

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.H.4 bases the expected monthly income "Generally" on "the income received in the prior thirty (30) day period", and H.5.a.1 and H.5.b say the income "received during the prior thirty (30) day period shall be used in determining eligibility" (S2704, S891); H.5.a.3 asks a self-employed caretaker for "documentation listing his/her income and work-related expenses for the prior thirty (30) day period". The rule does not say what the thirty days are prior to: the application (or redetermination) date, or the date the county determines eligibility. The volume reads them as the cccap_prior_income_period_days (CO-5151) days before cccap_application_or_redetermination_date (CO-5150), the date the income is reported and verified "at Application and Re-determination" (H.5): the day before that date is in them and the date itself is not. From the earnings and receipt records (CO-5100, CO-5000): (1) a pay or a recurring receipt stated with its date is counted when its date is in the thirty days (cccap_person_earned_income CO-3915, cccap_person_gross_wages CO-5152, cccap_other_countable_receipts CO-4464, cccap_counted_recurring_receipts CO-5721, with the deductions and the child support figure placed the same way, CO-4463, CO-5725; coverage pass 4 C4-2); (2) a pay or receipt stated with its month and no date is read for the month determined, standing for the thirty days, so a case that states calendar-month amounts is read as having the same income each month; one stated with neither (a stream) is the pay or payment of the days its source ran (docs/conventions.md, "A record's time"): it stands for the thirty days when its source ran through all of them (no job_start_date or start_date after their first day, no job_end_date or end_date before their last), is not read when its source ended before the thirty days or began on or after the application or redetermination date, and a source that began or ended inside them is placed by its dated pays or receipts, so a stream without them cannot determine (coverage pass 5, C5-1: before, a stream was read for the month whatever its source's dates, so a job or a benefit that ended before the thirty days was counted); (3) self-employment's receipts, costs and hours are read for the month determined, whether or not dated (cccap_self_employment_gross_receipts CO-5156, cccap_self_employment_business_expenses CO-1709, cccap_self_employment_net_earnings CO-1710, cccap_self_employment_hours CO-6404), H.4.c and I.2 averaging a business's income and H.5.a.3's thirty-day documentation being read as the month's (coverage pass 4 C4-7), a business's stream by its job dates as in (2); (4) H.4.i lets a nonrecurring lump sum be "included as income in the month received or averaged over a twelve (12) month period, whichever is most beneficial for the recipient": the volume counts one twelfth of a receipt paid once in the month being determined, the more beneficial count for that month, and nothing for one received in an earlier month (counting it in its own month is then the more beneficial), by the month received whether or not dated (cccap_one_time_receipts_averaged CO-5722). Pay under an annual contract is prorated under H.4.b (OQ-1221); irregular income is tested under J.28 by the quarter of each record (OQ-713). The county's case-by-case choice of another period (H.4.a, H.4.d-h, H.5.a.1, H.5.b) is the county's figure, cccap_county_determined_monthly_income (CO-3914). The other readings: the thirty days before the date the county determines eligibility; a business's dated records placed in the thirty days as pay is. Items: CO-985, CO-3914, CO-3915, CO-3916, CO-3917, CO-4463, CO-4464, CO-4076, CO-5150, CO-5151, CO-5152, CO-5156, CO-1709, CO-1710, CO-5721, CO-5722, CO-5725, CO-6404, CO-984.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: The thirty days end the day before the application or redetermination date; a pay or recurring receipt stated with its date is counted when dated in them, one stated without a date is the month determined's; self-employment is read for the month determined; a lump sum or prize counts at one twelfth in the month received.

Consequence the owner signs with the assumption (coverage pass 7, R7-5; docs/reviews/coverage7-care-energy.md): thirty days hold five paydays of a weekly earner for two of every seven application dates, and three of a biweekly earner for one of every seven, and every pay dated in them is counted, as H.4's general rule says. Worked example (probe CW5): $500 a week, paydays 2026-10-16 to 2026-11-13, an application on 2026-11-15: five paydays, $2,500 (four paydays: $2,000; 52/12 weeks: $2,166.67). A $700-a-week earner applying on a date whose thirty days hold five paydays is $3,500 against the entry level of $3,336.16 for two (ineligible_income) and, a week later, with four, $2,800 (eligible_low_income). H.4's exception ("when the prior thirty (30) day period does not provide an accurate indication of anticipated income ... a different period of time may be applicable") is the county's to apply: the volume reads it as the county's own figure, cccap_county_determined_monthly_income (CO-3914), which replaces the thirty days' count when the case states it; it is never applied by default.

Checked 2026-11-23 (coverage pass 4, docs/reviews/coverage4-care-energy.md X4-3): 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.H.4-5, I.2, I.20, J (D-87, rule version 12658, effective 2026-08-14): "prior thirty (30) day period" has no stated anchor, H.5.a.3's thirty-day documentation is not tied to the income counted, and "whichever is most beneficial" is not tied to a month. 3.124.D (the fee review) uses the same words. A state policy choice for the CCCAP owner.

History: until coverage pass 4 this question named the retired thirty-day facts (wages_gross_received_prior_thirty_days CO-3910, unearned_income_received_prior_thirty_days CO-3911) and read every receipt by its month; coverage pass 4 found a dated receipt read by calendar month while a dated pay was read in the thirty days (C4-2), and the self-employment month unrecorded (C4-7); the body above restates the reading on the records items. Coverage pass 5 (docs/reviews/coverage5-care-energy.md, C5-1) found point (2) silent on a stream whose own dates end before, or begin after, the thirty days, which the items counted; point (2) now states the reading of a stream (review households CJ1, CJ2, CE1: CO-3915-T19, CO-5721-T9, CO-5156-T5). Checked again 2026-11-23: 3.111.H.4, H.5.a.1, H.5.b (D-87) count income "received" in the prior thirty days, which a source that ended before them or began after them does not give; the class is unchanged.

### OQ-681. LEAP: the income verification period, "any four weeks of the eight", one month's unearned income, and the self-employment month

Body: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.751.1 (S2712) defines the income verification period as "from the date of application to the same date of the prior month (approximately thirty (30) calendar days from the date of application)". 3.752.22.A counts unearned income "received in the income verification period, not to exceed one month's income"; B counts earned ongoing income as "the countable gross income in any four (4) weeks of the eight (8) weeks prior to the application date", made monthly by adding four weekly, two bi-weekly or two semi-monthly amounts (C.1-2); C.3 uses the actual income of the period for income terminated as of the application date (a), new earned income (b), and unemployment or other unearned income, not counted when the first check has not been received "as of the income verification period" (c) (S2713). 3.755.21.I.1, as amended effective November 1, 2026 (S2719), takes self-employment income "received in the full month prior to the application date". The volume reads, from the earnings and receipt records (CO-5100, CO-5000):

(1) The period starts on the same date of the prior month (leap_income_verification_period_start, CO-5002; OQ-886 for a date the prior month lacks) and ends on the application date. A pay of a job that began or ended, and a receipt or pay received once, is counted when dated in the period, both ends included (leap_wages_in_income_verification_period CO-3912, leap_one_time_receipts_in_period CO-5009, CO-5121's pays of a frequency C does not convert). A job stated as a stream (no date) whose job_end_date is before the period's first day, or whose job_start_date is after the application date, pays nothing in the period and is not read (C.3.a: the actual income received in the period, none; C.3.c: no first check "as of the income verification period"), as a receipt stream is under (4); a dated pay is read by its date, so a last paycheck received in the period after the job ended counts; and a job stated as a stream that began or ended inside the period is read from its dated pays, so a stream without them cannot determine (coverage pass 6, R6-1: CO-3912, leap_scsep_wages CO-5122).
(2) "Any four weeks" is each job's average pay of the pays dated in the eight weeks before the application date, converted by the job's schedule (four weekly, two bi-weekly, two semi-monthly, one monthly) and added over the jobs (leap_ongoing_dated_wages CO-5121, leap_scsep_wages CO-5122, the record's job: coverage pass 4 L4-1), which equals any four weeks when pay is regular; a pay stated as a stream converts its per-pay amount (leap_ongoing_wage_streams CO-5120).
(3) "The full month prior" is the calendar month before the month of the application (leap_self_employment_income CO-1712 at month_before, leap_self_employment_gross_receipts CO-5126).
(4) A recurring receipt stated with its date is counted when dated in the period after its first day through the application date (leap_counted_recurring_receipts CO-5003, leap_counted_service_payments CO-5004, leap_social_security_whole_dollars CO-5005, leap_ssi_whole_dollars CO-5006, leap_cash_assistance_receipts CO-5007, leap_receipts_set_aside CO-5008, leap_boarder_payments CO-5124; coverage pass 4 L4-3): the period is then one month long, which is how the volume reads "not to exceed one month's income" (a monthly source paid on the application date's day of the month is counted once), and a check first paid after the application date is not counted (C.3.c). A receipt stated with its month and no date is read in the month of the application, the month leap_household_income (CO-1002) reads, as 3.752.21.G-I read several of them ("in the month received"). A receipt stated with neither (a stream) is the payment of the days its source ran: it is read in the month of the application when its source ran through the whole period (start_date before the period's first day or not stated, end_date after the application date or not stated), is not counted when its end_date is before the period's first day (C.3.a: the actual income received in the period, none) or its start_date is after the application date (C.3.c: no first check "as of the income verification period"), and a source that began or ended in the period is read from its dated receipts, as a job that began or ended is under (1), so a stream without them cannot determine (coverage pass 5, L5-1). A business's stream is read in the month of (3) only when its job dates cover that month. The verification of unearned income (leap_requires_verification_of_unearned_income, CO-4526) takes off the declared gifts and support exactly as these items count them (coverage pass 5, L5-6).

The other readings: the same-date boundary counted at both ends for recurring receipts too, with a cap of one month's income per source (a source's pays beyond one month left out); for a source paid weekly or bi-weekly, one month's income as four weekly or two bi-weekly amounts, as C.1 converts pay "paid less than monthly" (review household U3: five weekly $200 child support pays in the period count $1,000 under (4), $800 under this reading); the four weeks the county chooses; the month back from the application date for self-employment. Items: CO-5002, CO-3912, CO-5120, CO-5121, CO-5122, CO-3925, CO-5003, CO-5004, CO-5005, CO-5006, CO-5007, CO-5008, CO-5009, CO-5124, CO-3926, CO-1002, CO-1712, CO-1711, CO-5126, CO-4526.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Energy assistance (LEAP) policy owner
Assumption: The period from the same date of the prior month through the application date (both ends for pays of a job that began or ended and for receipts paid once; after the first day for recurring receipts stated with their dates); each job's average dated pay of the eight weeks times four, two or one; the calendar month before the application month for self-employment; recurring receipts stated without a date in the month of the application.

Checked 2026-11-23 (coverage pass 4, docs/reviews/coverage4-care-energy.md X4-3): 9 CCR 2503-7 3.751.1, 3.752.21-23, 3.755.21 (D-86, version effective 11/01/2025) and the amendments effective 11/01/2026 (D-173), which change 3.755.21.I.1 and not 3.752.22.A-C. Neither defines "any four (4) weeks", "one month's income" or "full month prior" further, nor says whether the period's first day is in it. A state policy choice for the LEAP owner.

History: until coverage pass 4 this question read the period as the thirty days before the application date, the period the retired thirty-day facts were stated for (CO-3910, CO-3911), point (2) from one pay schedule per person (CO-3922), one-time receipts through the retired CO-4069, and recurring receipts by the month of the application whether or not dated (second-pass review N-8 moved prizes and lump sums into the period). Coverage pass 4 found point (1) contradicting CO-5002 and point (4) placing a dated receipt after the application date in the count (L4-3, X4-3); the body above restates both on the records items. Coverage pass 5 (docs/reviews/coverage5-care-energy.md, L5-1, L5-6) found point (4) keeping a stream in the month of the application without its source's dates, and its list of items omitting the verification sibling CO-4526; point (4) now states both. Checked again 2026-11-23 against 3.752.22.A, C.3.a, C.3.c (D-86) and 3.755.21.I.1 (D-173): the class is unchanged.

Coverage round (2026-11-22, docs/reviews/coverage-leap-sunbucks.md C-16): 3.755.21.J as amended (D-173) counts an LLC or S-corporation owner's draws and personal expenses paid from the business as gross earned income, verified by a statement of "the total amount of draws in the full month prior to the application date". The volume states them as wages (leap_self_employment_income, CO-1712, precision), read like other wages under point (2), or for the period when they are irregular; reading them for the calendar month before the application, as point (3) reads self-employment, is the alternative. The text ties "full month prior" to the verification statement, not to the income counted, so the Assumption is unchanged.

### OQ-682. CCCAP: which counties provide Colorado Works Child Care for diversion households

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.116.H (S2708): "Counties that provide Colorado Works Child Care for households approved for Colorado Works state diversions require the same eligibility as outlined in this rule section." The rule provides the route; each county elects it. The current Colorado Works rule (9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.8, D-81) has one kind of diversion payment, and the volume reads 3.116.H's "state diversions" as it (receives_tanf_diversion_payment, CO-3931). The counties' elections are county policy (county plans and procedures under 3.104), published by CDEC and the counties, not on any host this volume can reach. cccap_county_provides_cw_child_care_for_diversion_table (CO-3932) has no version; a diversion household's Colorado Works Child Care route is unknown until it is loaded. Items: CO-988, CO-3931, CO-3932.

Class: blocked on a named document
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: No county is assumed to provide it; the table is empty (unknown, with a no-version warning) for a diversion household.
Blocked on: the counties' elections to provide Colorado Works Child Care to Colorado Works diversion households under 8 CCR 1403-1 3.116.H (county child care plans and CDEC's county policy listing; cdec.colorado.gov refused)

Checked 2026-11-22: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.116.H and 3.104 (D-87, version effective 2026-08-14) name no county; 9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.8 (D-81) describes the diversion payment, not a county's child care election.

### OQ-720. VA Aid and Attendance in a State Veterans Home: who are the "dependents" (§ 8.100.5.K)

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.5.K (S2900) uses the VA pension's Aid and Attendance and Unusual Medical Expenses portion as patient payment "For a veteran or surviving spouse of a veteran in a State Veterans Home with no dependents", and not "for a veteran or surviving spouse of a veteran in a State Veterans Home with dependents" (K.1.b). The rule does not define dependents. The volume reads them as a spouse or a dependent child, as § 8.100.7.V.3.d.ii (S1231), the other Long-Term Care rule about a VA pensioner, does ("has no spouse or dependent child"; "the widow(er) of a veteran with no dependent children"): ma_va_aid_and_attendance_is_patient_payment (CO-4103) requires the person to be unmarried (is_married) and to have no child who is a dependent child under the Medical Assistance rules (ma_is_dependent_child). The VA's own dependency rules (a dependent parent, a child in school to 23) are not read. Final verification pass 2, F2-2. Items: CO-4103, CO-658.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: LTSS policy owner, Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: A veteran or surviving spouse has dependents when married or when a child of the person is a dependent child under the Medical Assistance rules.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.5.K and § 8.100.7.V.3.d.ii (D-65, version effective 11/14/2026; § 8.100.5.K re-read in the Secretary of State's Word text fetched 2026-11-22). § 8.100.1 defines "Dependent Child" and "Dependent Relative" ("one who is claimed as a dependent by an applicant for federal income tax purposes"), but not "dependents"; a reading by the second (tax dependents) is the alternative, and no published rule chooses.

### OQ-721. Patient payment: the two months of Part B premium deduction - from admission or from Long-Term Care eligibility

Body: Two paragraphs of 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.7.V count the months in which a Medicare Part B premium the person pays is deducted in the patient payment from different starting points. V.3.b.ii.8.a (S2401): "Medicare Part B Premium expenses, if applicable, are deductible only for the first and second month in the Nursing Facility", and V.1.e.i (S2400): "Review the AP-5615 form for Medicare part B premium deduction allowances for the first two months of admission"; but V.1.e.ii (S2400): "If client is not on the Buy-In program, adjust AP-5615 form for the Medicare premium deduction for the first two months of Long-Term Care institution eligibility." They differ for a resident who entered the facility before becoming eligible (private pay, or an application made after admission): counted from admission, she may have no deduction at all; counted from eligibility, she has it in her first two eligible months. The volume counts from the month of entry (institution_entry_date), as V.3.b.ii.8.a and V.1.e.i say: ma_patient_payment (CO-658) with ma_nf_part_b_premium_deduction_months (CO-3612). Final verification pass 2, P2-1 (CO-112: December's payment is 1,018.24 counted from admission, 815.34 counted from eligibility). The six months of the home maintenance allowance (V.3.g.ii) raise the same choice and take the same reading, from the month of entry (ma_home_maintenance_allowance, CO-3618; final verification pass 3, P3-4). Items: CO-658, CO-3612, CO-3618.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: LTSS policy owner, Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The Part B premium is deducted for the first and second month counted from the month the person entered the Nursing Facility, whether or not she was eligible then.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.7.V.1.e and V.3.b.ii.8 (D-65, version effective 11/14/2026; the Secretary of State's Word text re-fetched 2026-11-22 reads the same). No published rule says which governs.

### OQ-730. Resolved: SNAP deems the income and resources of a sponsor's spouse who did not sign Form I-864A (8 U.S.C. 1631(a)(2) over 7 CFR 273.4(c)(2))

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.305.2.C (S2971) deems "the income and resources of the sponsor and the sponsor's spouse, if they have executed USCIS Form I-864 or I-864A", as 7 CFR 273.4(c)(2) and (c)(2)(i) do (S2503). 4.305.2.D.1 (S2971) then says "The spouse's income and resources shall be counted even if the sponsor and spouse were married after the signing of the agreement", which presupposes a spouse who signed nothing; 8 U.S.C. 1631(a)(2) (D-275, S2970) attributes "the income and resources of the spouse (if any) of the person" who executed the affidavit, and 7 U.S.C. 2014(i)(1) (D-2) that of "the sponsor's spouse if such spouse is living with the sponsor", neither conditioned on a signature. The volume follows C and the federal regulation: snap_sponsor_income_after_reductions (CO-3711) and snap_sponsor_deemed_resources (CO-3702) read the persons who signed (sponsors, CO-2713), and a non-signing spouse only enters the size of the sponsor's household (snap_sponsor_deeming_household_size, CO-3710). The other reading counts the income and resources of every spouse of the sponsor, or of a spouse living with the sponsor, as D.1 and the statutes read. Final verification pass 2 P2-2. Items: CO-3710, CO-3711, CO-3702.

Resolution (SNAP non-financial coverage round, 2026-11-22; coverage review C-14, docs/reviews/coverage-snap-nonfinancial.md): this was not a state policy choice. The texts are two federal statutes and a federal regulation, and a State cannot choose between them: 8 U.S.C. 1631(a) (D-275, S2970) applies "Notwithstanding any other provision of law" and deems "(2) The income and resources of the spouse (if any) of the person" who executed the affidavit of support under INA section 213A, with no signature condition; 7 U.S.C. 2014(i)(1) (D-2, S3071) deems those of "the sponsor's spouse if such spouse is living with the sponsor"; 4.305.2.D.1 (S2971) counts a spouse married after the agreement was signed. The volume applies the statute over the regulation, as it did for 2014(i)(2)(E) and 1631(d)(3) over 273.4(c)(3)(vi) in final verification pass 2 N-3 (CO-2337). snap_sponsor_income_after_reductions (CO-3711) and snap_sponsor_deemed_resources (CO-3702) now add the income and resources of each spouse of a sponsor who did not sign (sponsor_spouses_not_cosigning, CO-2714), with a statute_override naming 4.305.2.C and 273.4(c)(2); a spouse declared in the case is read as living with the sponsor (CO-2714), so 2014(i)(1)'s condition is met as well. New tests CO-3711-T4 and CO-3702-T5. Households CO-94 and CO-116 now state the non-signing spouse's (zero) income; no expectation changed. The open: reference is removed from CO-3702, CO-3710 and CO-3711.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.305.2.C and D.1 (D-82, version effective 2026-10-01) state both sentences and no later paragraph reconciles them; 7 CFR 273.4(c)(2) (D-9) conditions the spouse's deeming on Form I-864 or I-864A; 8 U.S.C. 1631(a)(2) (D-275, law.cornell.edu, fetched 2026-11-22) and 7 U.S.C. 2014(i)(1) (D-2) do not. No CDHS or FNS text in an allowed source chooses between them.

### OQ-710. CCCAP: whether Child Welfare Child Care needs a CCCAP eligible child

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.120.D (S2853) leaves eligibility for Child Welfare Child Care to "the Child Welfare division using the criteria outlined in ... 12 CCR 2509-4, rule section 7.302 (July 31, 2023)"; 3.120.F says the county "shall not provide Child Welfare Child Care utilizing the Child Care Development Fund". The program-wide definition 3.103.UU (S2853) makes an "eligible child" a child under thirteen (or under nineteen with additional care needs) who needs care for part of the day and lives with the caretaker, and adds that "Any child served through the Colorado Works program or the Low-Income Child Care program must be a citizen/legal resident" - naming neither Child Welfare nor Protective Services Child Care. The volume reads the age and need parts as applying to Child Welfare Child Care (the definition is the program's, and 3.120.A-C describe care for children of the same kind) but not the citizenship part (the sentence names only Colorado Works and Low-Income, and the federal citizenship condition attaches to the Child Care Development Fund, which 3.120.F excludes): cccap_eligibility_status (CO-989) finds a Child Welfare Child Care household eligible only when it has a child for whom cccap_is_child_of_eligible_age_needing_care (CO-4056) holds. Protective services households need a full eligible child, citizenship included (3.119.F makes the child's citizenship, age and identity a condition of the care). The other reading: 7.302's criteria alone decide, with no CCCAP age or need test. 12 CCR 2509-4 7.302 (version of July 31, 2023) was not fetched; it is CDHS's child welfare rule and would settle whether it states its own ages. Items: CO-989, CO-4056.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: A Child Welfare Child Care household needs a child under thirteen (under nineteen with additional care needs) who needs part-day care; the child's citizenship is not tested.

Checked 2026-11-22: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.103.UU, 3.111.F, 3.118-3.120 (D-87, version effective 2026-08-14, re-fetched from the Secretary of State on 2026-11-22). Neither names the ages that apply to Child Welfare Child Care; 3.120.D defers to 12 CCR 2509-4 7.302. Coverage round (F-16): 12 CCR 2509-4 7.302 (D-305, rule version 12039, effective 07/01/2025, fetched from sos.state.co.us 2026-11-22; 7.302 is "[Rev. eff. 4/1/12]", so it reads as the incorporated 07/31/2023 edition) states no ages: 7.302.1 (S3259) requires "that there are no other child care options available and the child is eligible for Program Area 4, 5, or 6", and 7.302.2.G says to "Follow State rules for the Colorado Child Care Assistance Program (CCCAP)", which supports reading the CCCAP eligible-child ages. Its 7.302.2.E ("Assess a client fee when applicable") is overridden by 8 CCR 1403-1 3.124.I.4, which exempts Child Welfare Child Care from the parent fee (CO-1290). A state policy choice for the CCCAP owner, to settle with the Child Welfare division.

### OQ-711. CCCAP: whether one child's unexplained absences end the case or that child's authorization

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.114.A.5 (S2854) terminates "child care authorizations and cases" when "the child has had twenty-two (22) or more unexplained absences from authorized care within a thirty (30) day period and two (2) failed documented attempts to contact the adult caretaker or teen parent have been made"; B.11 says the same at redetermination and 3.117.A.3 bars the automatic transition from Colorado Works. The rule speaks of "the child" and of both authorizations and cases, and does not say whether a second child in good attendance keeps the case open. The volume reads it as ending the absent child's authorization: cccap_eligibility_status (CO-989) finds the household ineligible when every eligible child has the finding (cccap_child_has_unexplained_absences_finding, CO-4055), at a change, a redetermination or a transition, and not at an application. The other reading: any one child's absences close the whole case. Items: CO-989, CO-4055.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: One child's absences end that child's authorization; the case ends when no eligible child is left without them.

Checked 2026-11-22: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.114.A.5, B.11, C-D, 3.117.A.3 (D-87, version effective 2026-08-14, re-fetched 2026-11-22). No other section says how a multi-child household is treated.

### OQ-712. CCCAP: whether a permitted fee increase during the eligibility period lifts the cap altogether

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.124.C (S2850): "During the twelve (12) month eligibility period the household parent fee cannot be assessed higher than the parent fee determined at the most recent Colorado Works Child Care referral or low-income application or re-determination"; 3.124.B: "Increases in parent fees beyond what is outlined in rule subsections (1)-(4) below shall only go into effect at Low-Income Child Care re-determination or at the end of the twelve (12) month Colorado Works Child Care period", B.1, B.3 and B.4 being circumstances that can raise the fee (income regained after a temporary loss, more care authorized, leaving a top-quality-rated provider); 3.117.F caps a transitioning household's fee at its last Colorado Works Child Care referral and lets it be revised "as outlined in rule section 3.124(B)". C reads as absolute and B as allowing those increases; the rule does not say whether a B.1, B.3 or B.4 change lets the whole fee be reassessed or only the part that change causes. The volume lets the formula fee stand when the county records one of those circumstances (cccap_parent_fee_increase_basis, CO-4051) and otherwise caps it at the fee last determined (cccap_parent_fee_within_period_cap, CO-4052). The other reading: the fee rises only by what the circumstance itself adds (for B.4, the 20% reduction lost), other income changes still waiting for the redetermination. Items: CO-1290, CO-4050, CO-4051, CO-4052.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: A recorded B.1, B.3 or B.4 circumstance lets the fee be reassessed by the formula; without one the fee is the lesser of the formula and the fee last determined.

Checked 2026-11-22: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.124.A-C, J, R, T and 3.117.E-F (D-87, version effective 2026-08-14, re-fetched 2026-11-22). None apportions an increase.

### OQ-716. CCCAP: from when a protective services household's verification periods run

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.119.B (S2852) allows "up to sixty (60) days to provide verification of the child(ren)'s income", G sixty days for immunization, and F.1-2 "up to six (6) months" for a child's citizenship, age and identity when the Child Welfare worker cannot attest at the referral; F.3 ends the care if they are not verified "within six (6) months of the referral". B and G name no starting day. The volume counts each from the county's request (the program's own request date, else the shared checklist date, OQ-300), the day it counts the fifteen-day and homelessness periods from (CO-1450, CO-3909), and counts six months to the same day of the month (cccap_protective_services_verification_due_date, CO-4061); where the request is made at the referral, as the referral process implies (3.118.E), the two agree. Elements 3.119 does not name (an activity schedule, a custody schedule, an unrelated caretaker's verification) keep the fifteen days. The other readings: the sixty days from the referral; six months as 183 days. Items: CO-4061.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: Every period runs from the county's request; six months end on the same day of the sixth month after it.

Checked 2026-11-22: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.H.5.e, 3.118.E, 3.119.B, F, G (D-87, version effective 2026-08-14, re-fetched 2026-11-22). None states the first day of the sixty days.

### OQ-713. CCCAP: irregular income under $90 a quarter, measured by the quarter of each record

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.J.28 (S2858) excludes "Irregular income in the certification period that totals less than ninety dollars ($90) in any calendar quarter, such as slight fluctuations in regular monthly income and/or that which is received too infrequently or irregularly to be reasonably anticipated". CCCAP counts the income received in the prior thirty days (3.111.H.4-5; OQ-680), which can straddle two months and, near a quarter's start, two quarters; J.28 measures by calendar quarter. The volume tests each irregular pay or receipt (a record marked irregular, CO-5100, CO-5000) against the calendar quarter of its own date: a record dated in the thirty days against the quarter they begin in when its month is on or before cccap_prior_income_period_first_quarter_end (CO-6401; that quarter's total is cccap_irregular_income_in_first_quarter_of_period, CO-6402), else against the quarter they end in (CO-6403); a record stated without a date against the quarter of the month determined. A quarter's total is the irregular income, earned and unearned, placed by each record's own month or date (cccap_irregular_receipts_in_quarter CO-5724 over cccap_irregular_receipts CO-5723; cccap_irregular_earnings_in_quarter CO-6151 over cccap_irregular_earnings CO-6150). The record is excluded when its quarter's total is under the limit (cccap_irregular_income_quarterly_limit, CO-4075) and counted otherwise: earned (cccap_person_earned_income CO-3915, cccap_other_countable_receipts CO-4464), unearned (cccap_excluded_irregular_income CO-4076 takes it out of cccap_counted_recurring_receipts CO-5721). "Slight fluctuations in regular monthly income" are left to the stated amounts, which a county-determined monthly figure (CO-3914) already averages. The other readings: the quarter containing the month determined for every record (the reading before coverage pass 4, C4-1: a pay drawn from the previous quarter was tested against a quarter that does not hold it); "certification period" read as the twelve-month eligibility period, so a quarter not yet ended is projected. This was filed under OQ-162 (county plans) before the second-pass review. Items: CO-3915, CO-4464, CO-4076, CO-5721, CO-5723, CO-5724, CO-6150, CO-6151, CO-6401, CO-6402, CO-6403, CO-985.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: An irregular pay or receipt is excluded when the calendar quarter of its own date (of the month determined, when undated) holds less than $90 of irregular income, earned and unearned.

Checked 2026-11-23 (coverage pass 4, docs/reviews/coverage4-care-energy.md C4-1, X4-3): 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.H.4-5, J.28 (D-87, rule version 12658, effective 2026-08-14). The rule does not reconcile the thirty-day income period with the calendar quarter, nor say what "in the certification period" adds. History: until coverage pass 4 the assumption read the month's irregular receipt against the quarter of the month determined, and named the retired infrequent_or_irregular_income_received (CO-3822) and infrequent_or_irregular_income_in_quarter (CO-4077).

### OQ-714. LEAP: a household member other than the applicant who is serving a fraud penalty

Body: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.751.563 (S2859): "Individuals who are found guilty of committing fraud, pursuant to section 26-1-127, C.R.S., in a prior program year shall be ineligible to participate in LEAP in the subsequent program year for the first violation, two program years for the second violation, and permanently for the third violation." 3.754.1.U (S2715) makes the applicant's conviction a factor for denial of the household (leap_applicant_is_serving_fraud_penalty, CO-3929). For any other member the rule makes the individual ineligible "to participate" but does not say how the household is affected: whether the member is left out of the household count with the income still counted (as 3.753.17 does for a member who does not meet the citizenship requirement), left out entirely, or the household denied. The volume models only the applicant's penalty; a penalized member who is not the applicant stays in the household and its count. Items: CO-3929, CO-1007.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Energy assistance (LEAP) policy owner
Assumption: Only the applicant's penalty affects the household (denial); another member's penalty is not applied.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.751.56-3.751.563, 3.753.17, 3.754.1.U (D-86, version effective 11/01/2025) and the amendments effective 11/01/2026 (D-173), which do not touch these sections. No section states the household effect of a non-applicant's penalty.

### OQ-700. Colorado Works: personal care and home care allowances exempt for every member, or only in deeming

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.4.A.5 (S2605) reads: "Personal care or home care allowances paid to a recipient or non-recipient spouse, parent, stepparent, or child, from a federal, state, or local government program for in-home supportive services (attendant, chore, housekeeping) shall be exempt as income in determining the amount of attributable non-recipient spouse, non-recipient parent, or non-recipient stepparent income. However, it shall be classified as employment income in determining the attendant's own eligibility for assistance." The sentence names the allowance paid to "a recipient" among the persons it is paid to, but states the exemption only "in determining the amount of attributable non-recipient ... income", the deeming of 3.605.6. Read literally, an allowance paid to a member of the assistance unit for services to that member is not exempt, though it pays for the services and not the family's needs; read as a list of whose allowances are exempt, it is exempt for every member. The volume takes the second reading: cw_excluded_unearned_income (CO-2341) exempts in_home_care_allowance_received for every person, so the unit member's allowance and a deemed person's allowance are both left out; the attendant's pay for the services is wages, as the last sentence says, and is counted. The other reading would count an allowance received by a unit member. Items: CO-2341.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: A personal care or home care allowance paid to any member, recipient or not, is exempt; the attendant's pay is employment income.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.4.A.5 and 3.605.2.D.3 (D-81; coverage review P3-2: first cited as 3.605.6, which D-81 does not have, 3.605 ending at 3.605.5; rule version 12030, effective 07/01/2025, the Secretary of State's current version). No other paragraph of 3.605 treats an allowance paid to a recipient; the Adult Financial counterpart, 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.786.E (S2608), exempts the allowance "paid to the client" outright.

### OQ-740. Children with Disabilities Buy-In: how the § 8.100.5.H disregards and allocations apply to a family household

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.6.Q.1.b (S3000) tests "Household income ... after income disregards" under four rules: the MAGI household (§ 8.100.4.E), § 8.100.5.F's income rules, its F.6 exemptions, and § 8.100.5.H's allocations and disregards. § 8.100.5.H (S1506, S3002) is written for an SSI-related individual or couple: one $20 general disregard "per couple" (H.4.a.iii), "the first $65 of all earned income" (H.4.b), child support to an "applicant/recipient child" reduced by one third (H.4.c), and allocations to ineligible children and parents deducted from a parent's income before it is deemed to an applicant child (H.3). The rule does not say how these apply to a MAGI household of parents and children compared with the poverty level for the household's size. The volume (CO-4200 to CO-4203) counts every member's § 8.100.5.F income (not only the members MAGI's tax-dependent rule of § 8.100.4.C.1.f would count, since Q.1.b takes only the household from § 8.100.4), applies one $20 and one $65-plus-one-half to the household's pooled income, reduces the child support of each blind or disabled child of the Buy-In's age by one third, applies the student earned income exemption to such a child, and takes no H.3 allocation, since the household's size already enters the comparison and an allocation would count the same members twice. Alternatives: a disregard for each earner or each couple in the household; H.3's allocations from the parents' income as in SSI deeming, compared with the poverty level for the child alone; or MAGI income with the § 8.100.5.H disregards. Final verification pass 3, F3-1. Items: CO-4200, CO-4201, CO-4202, CO-620, CO-621.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner, Premium and finance owner
Assumption: The household's pooled § 8.100.5.F income takes one $20 general and one $65-plus-one-half earned income disregard, with no ineligible child or parent allocation, and is compared with the poverty level for the MAGI household's size.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.6.Q.1.b, § 8.100.5.F and § 8.100.5.H.1-4 (D-65, version effective 11/14/2026; re-read in the Secretary of State's PDF fetched 2026-11-22). No published rule says how the per-person and per-couple disregards and the allocations apply to a family household; HCPF's Buy-In guidance is on hcpf.colorado.gov, which is refused.

### OQ-800. LEAP: which households are "public assistance households" whose applications are accepted before November 1

Body: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.752.1 (S3308) reads: "Preference shall be given to application forms received from public assistance households (such as Colorado Works, Old Age Pension (OAP), Aid to the Needy Disabled (AND), Aid to the Blind (AB), and Supplemental Social Security Disability Benefits.) Such applications received prior to November 1st shall be accepted and may be processed; however, eligibility shall not be effective until November 1st." The list is introduced by "such as", and its last entry names no program: "Supplemental Social Security Disability Benefits" joins the words of Supplemental Security Income (SSI) and Social Security Disability Insurance (SSDI). 3.751.1's "Public Assistance Income" (S3309), defined "for purposes of verifying income", lists Colorado Works, OAP, AND and AB (each "both the SSI-supplement and State-only groups"), Non-Categorical Refugee Assistance, and SSDI only "for clients on another state program, such as a Medicaid waiver or buy in program". The volume (leap_is_public_assistance_household, CO-4518) reads a household with a member who receives Colorado Works basic cash, an Adult Financial grant (OAP or AND), SSI, or SSDI on the member's own record, and accepts its application filed before November 1 for the program year that begins that November (leap_is_eligible, CO-1007; coverage review C-3). Alternatives: SSI only, or SSDI only; SSDI only under 3.751.1 F's condition; adding NCRA from 3.751.1 E. Aid to the Blind and NCRA have no receipt fact in the volume. Items: CO-4518, CO-1007, CO-4517.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Energy assistance (LEAP) policy owner
Assumption: A household with a member receiving Colorado Works, OAP, AND, SSI or SSDI is a public assistance household; its application received before November 1 is effective November 1.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.751.1 and 3.752.1 (D-86, rule version 12221, effective 11/01/2025, the Secretary of State's current version re-fetched 2026-11-22) and the amendments effective 11/01/2026 (D-173), which do not touch 3.751.1's definition or 3.752.1. Neither defines "public assistance household" or the program 3.752.1 calls "Supplemental Social Security Disability Benefits".

### OQ-801. LEAP: a foster or kinship child living with a guardian, a member or not

Body: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.751.2.D.2 (S490) says children under eighteen "living in the same dwelling as the parent or guardian, unless emancipated" cannot be classified as a separate household, and 3.752.23.U (S3300) excludes "Payment received as foster care/kinship income; foster care/kinship individuals are not considered LEAP Household members". A child in kinship guardianship has a legal guardian (D.2) and is a kinship individual (U). The volume reads U as the specific rule: leap_household_members (CO-1000) and leap_household_members_for_income (CO-1009) leave out a person whose child_welfare_payment_type is IV-E or State-funded foster care, IV-B services in foster care, or IV-E or State kinship guardianship, so the child adds nothing to the household's size and income limit, and the payment is not income (leap_excluded_unearned_income, CO-4511). The other reading joins a ward under D.2 and excludes only the payment. Adopted children (adoption assistance) are legal children, and their adoption subsidy is counted (3.752.21.M). Coverage review C-7. Items: CO-1000, CO-1009.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Energy assistance (LEAP) policy owner
Assumption: A foster or kinship child is not a LEAP household member, even with a legal guardian in the household; the foster or kinship payment is not income.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.751.1 "Household", 3.751.2 and 3.752.23.U (D-86, rule version 12221, the Secretary of State's current version re-fetched 2026-11-22); D-173 does not amend them. No paragraph reconciles U with D.2.

### OQ-780. Adult Financial: an absence to care for a family member injured in military duty - 180 days in place of 90, or in addition

Body: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.64.E (S3201) presumes a client is no longer a resident after "a period of ninety (90) or more consecutive days" out of the State (E.2, except for documented medical treatment) and, in E.4, when the client "leaves the State to care for an immediate family member injured in the line of military duty for a period of one hundred eighty (180) or more consecutive days". Read literally, E.2's ninety days would reach that client first, and E.4 would add nothing; E.4 has effect only if it replaces E.2's ninety days for this absence. The volume reads E.4 as the period for that absence: af_is_presumed_nonresident_by_absence (CO-4401) applies E.2 to every other absence and E.4's 180 days to an absence to care for an injured family member (is_out_of_state_caring_for_family_member_injured_in_military_duty, CO-4404). The other reading presumes non-residence for that client at ninety days. Items: CO-4401, CO-4404.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: A client out of the State to care for an immediate family member injured in the line of military duty is presumed not a resident only at 180 consecutive days.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.64.E.1-4 (D-80, rule version 12428, effective 03/02/2026, the Secretary of State's current version, re-fetched 2026-11-22) and 3.520.64.F-G. No paragraph says how E.2 and E.4 relate.

### OQ-781. Resolved: Adult Financial donated work hours: which "current minimum wage standard"

Body: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.784.B (S3211) values countable donated work hours at "The going rate in the community for similar work; or, 2. The current minimum wage standard, whichever is greater." The rule does not say whether the standard is the federal minimum wage (29 U.S.C. 206(a)(1), $7.25 an hour), Colorado's (Colo. Const. art. XVIII, sec. 15 and C.R.S. 8-6-109, adjusted each year by the Colorado Department of Labor and Employment) or a local one (Denver's). af_donated_work_income (CO-4443) reads the federal minimum wage (federal_minimum_wage, CO-160), the only minimum wage this volume carries; the Colorado figure for 2026 is published by the Department of Labor and Employment (cdle.colorado.gov), which is not an allowed host. Where the going rate is stated and is above the minimum wage, as it usually is, the choice does not matter. Items: CO-4443, CO-160.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: The statewide Colorado minimum wage (colorado_minimum_wage, CO-4730) is the floor.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.510 and 3.520.784 (D-80, rule version 12428, effective 03/02/2026). Neither defines the minimum wage standard. If the state reads Colorado's, the figure must come from the Department of Labor and Employment's annual minimum wage order, which no allowed host serves.

Resolution (coverage review 2 of Adult Financial, G-8 (3), 2026-11-22): the Department's orders are filed with the Secretary of State, so the side claim above was wrong. The COMPS Order, 7 CCR 1103-1 Rule 3.1 (D-332, S3508, effective 02/01/2026), requires that "all employees ... shall be paid not less than the Colorado minimum wage, as specified for the applicable year in the PAY CALC Order", and the 2026 PAY CALC Order, 7 CCR 1103-14 Rule 1.2.1(A) (D-330, S3506, effective 01/01/2026; D-331, S3507, effective 02/01/2026), states the full Colorado minimum wage as "$15.16 per hour". For work done in Colorado the federal $7.25 is not a wage any covered employer may pay, so the "current minimum wage standard" of 3.520.784.B.2 is read as the statewide Colorado minimum wage, the new dated parameter colorado_minimum_wage (CO-4730); a local minimum wage (COMPS Rule 3.2) is not a statewide standard and is not used. af_donated_work_income (CO-4443) reads it; its tests CO-4443-T1 and T2 are re-pinned (T2: 40 hours at a $14 going rate, 40 x 15.16 = 606.40). No household case states donated work, so no case expectation changes.

### OQ-782. Adult Financial: interest and dividends - countable under 3.520.785.B.10, or excluded under the SSI exclusions 3.520.786.K incorporates

Body: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.785.B.10 (D-80) lists as countable unearned income "Dividends and interest received on financial accounts, savings bonds, leases, etc.", while 3.520.786.K incorporates 20 CFR 416.1124 (S2609), whose (c)(22) excludes "Interest and dividend income from a countable resource" and (c)(9) and (c)(20) the interest on excluded burial funds and other excluded resources. The two paragraphs conflict for interest and dividends on a countable resource (a savings account, a bond). af_excluded_unearned_income (CO-2343) follows K: all interest and dividends (taxable_interest_and_dividend_income, tax_exempt_interest) are excluded. Under B.10 they would be counted, dollar for dollar against the grant. Coverage review P2-1. Items: CO-2343, CO-3831.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: Interest and dividends are excluded, as the SSI exclusions of 3.520.786.K exclude them.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.785.B.10 and 3.520.786.K (D-80, rule version 12428, effective 03/02/2026, re-fetched 2026-11-22) and 20 CFR 416.1124(c)(9), (20), (22) (D-207). Neither paragraph says it governs the other.

### OQ-783. Aid to the Needy Disabled under eighteen: the rule pays AND-CS (and blind AND-SO) from age zero; the statute requires eighteen

Body: C.R.S. 26-2-111(4)(b) (S442) makes "eighteen years of age or older" a condition of aid to the needy disabled. 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.61.A (D-80) sets the ages at "eighteen (18) through fifty-nine (59) years of age for AND-SO (unless diagnosed with blindness, then age zero (0) through 59 years of age); age 0 through 59 years of age for AND-CS", and 3.549.E deems parents' income to an unemancipated AND-CS child. The volume follows the rule: af_and_cs_eligible (CO-950) and af_and_so_eligible (CO-1203) admit children (af_and_so_min_age with the blind exception), against the principle OQ-430 states that a rule's silence cannot narrow a statutory bar. The rule's reading is plausible: AND-CS is the Colorado supplement to SSI, which pays disabled and blind children, and Aid to the Blind (26-2-111(5)) has no age floor. Coverage review P2-3. Items: CO-950, CO-1203, CO-287, CO-288.

Class: needs Legal
Owner: Legal, Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: The rule's ages govern: AND-CS from age zero, AND-SO from eighteen, or from zero if blind.

Checked 2026-11-22: C.R.S. 26-2-111(4)(b) (D-59, 2024 edition, re-fetched 2026-11-22) and 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.61.A, 3.540, 3.546 and 3.549.E (D-80, rule version 12428, effective 03/02/2026). No statute or rule reconciles them.

### OQ-770. Colorado Works: ten or eleven days to supply requested information

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.602.1.K.1.c (S3169) lists as a basis for denial that "The client does not supply information or otherwise fails to cooperate with the county department within ten (10) calendar days of the request for information unless good cause is granted". 3.602.1.I.2 (S1629) allows "eleven (11) calendar days to provide the missing verifications", to the twentieth day after application on good cause, and 3.604.3.A.2 (S871) makes the due date on the written request "eleven (11) calendar days from the date the verification was requested in writing". The rule does not say whether K.1.c's ten days are a separate, shorter period for information other than verification or a leftover of an earlier version. The volume reads eleven days for every requested verification (cw_verification_response_days, CO-1351; cw_verification_due_date, CO-1426), since I.2 and A.2 state the period the county must put on the request, and K.1.c lists bases for a denial "such as, but not limited to". Items: CO-1351, CO-1426. Coverage review CW-15.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: The client has eleven calendar days from the written request (to the twentieth day after application with good cause); K.1.c's ten days are not applied separately.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.602.1.I.2, 3.602.1.K.1.c and 3.604.3.A.2 (D-104 and D-81, rule version 12030, effective 07/01/2025, the Secretary of State's current version on 2026-11-22). No other paragraph sets a period for information that is not a verification.

### OQ-771. Colorado Works: the month before the month of application

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.1.A.1 (S412) says that "for purpose of determining need, all countable gross income received in the month prior to, if available, and in the month of application shall be used to determine eligibility of members of the assistance unit". 3.606.1.E and H then compare "the countable gross earned and unearned income" with the need standard, and 3.606.1.J.4 says "If the budgetary unit's income equals or exceeds the need standard, the assistance unit is not eligible", without saying how two months are combined: whether each month is tested, the two are averaged, or the prior month only informs the estimate of the application month's income ("if available"). The volume tests the income of the month being determined (cw_countable_income, CO-910, and its parts read the month's facts), which at application is the application month; the prior month is not read. Items: CO-907, CO-908, CO-910, CO-913, CO-1716. Coverage review CW-16.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: Eligibility at application rests on the countable income of the application month; the prior month's income is evidence for that estimate, not a second test.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.1.A.1-2, 3.605.1.B, 3.606.1.E, H and J (D-81, rule version 12030, effective 07/01/2025, still current on 2026-11-22). No paragraph states how the two months are combined.

### OQ-772. Colorado Works: a tribal member who is eligible under a Tribal Family Assistance Plan

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.2.J (S3170) says "Members of an Indian Tribe not eligible for assistance under a Tribal Family Assistance Plan are eligible for Colorado Works." It states who is eligible, not that a tribal member who is eligible under a tribal TANF plan is barred, and no other paragraph states that bar; 3.604.1.A.3 bars a client who received TANF from another State in the same month (received_tanf_from_another_state_this_month, CO-842), and a tribal plan is not a State. The volume applies no bar for eligibility under a tribal plan, and states no fact for it. Items: CO-921. Coverage review CW-21.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: A tribal member is not barred from Colorado Works because the member is eligible under a Tribal Family Assistance Plan; duplicate receipt is left to the county's coordination with the tribe.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.1.A and 3.604.2.J (D-81, rule version 12030, effective 07/01/2025, still current on 2026-11-22). No paragraph of 9 CCR 2503-6 states the negative.

### OQ-790. CCCAP: an education or training time limit reached during the eligibility period

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.114.B.3 (S3251) ends care when a caretaker "exceeds time limited low-income eligible activity time limits" "at re-determination only", and 3.125.Q (S3252) lets a county discontinue care before the redetermination only for six listed reasons, none of them a time limit. 3.132.B.3 (S3260) says care "is typically authorized for twelve (12) consecutive months except ... When the adult caretaker(s) or teen parent(s) are participating in time limited activities such as job search or education/training", so the authorization for the activity may end before the redetermination. Whether a household whose caretaker reaches the 104-week or 52-week limit during the period keeps an eligible status with no authorized care, or moves to job search, is not stated. Items: CO-984.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: The household stays eligible until its redetermination (the time limit is not applied at a change during the period); how long care is authorized for the activity is the authorization, which this volume does not model.

Checked 2026-11-22: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.G.4.a-c, 3.114.A-B, 3.125.Q and 3.132.B (D-87, rule version 12658, effective 2026-08-14, re-fetched from sos.state.co.us 2026-11-22). None says whether eligibility or only the authorization ends when the limit is reached during the period.

### OQ-760. SNAP: a self-employed household's or an LLC owner's statement of income accepted unless questionable, against the federal verification of gross nonexempt income

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.506.C (S831) reads: "A household's statement of self-employment income shall be accepted unless questionable." 4.403.F (S3121) reads: "Client statement of LLC and S-Corporation income is acceptable, unless questionable." 7 CFR 273.2(f)(1)(i) (S801) requires that gross nonexempt income "shall be verified for all households prior to certification", with an exception only where attempts to verify fail because the person or organization providing the income does not cooperate. If FNS approved a Colorado waiver of 273.2(f)(1)(i) for self-employment and owner income, the State text stands; if not, the federal text governs and the income is verified at application whether or not questionable. The volume follows the State text: snap_requires_verification_of_earned_income (CO-1376) asks for self-employment income and an LLC or S-corporation owner's income (wages_from_owned_llc_or_s_corporation, CO-4341) only when questionable. Coverage review snap-financial CF-16 and CF-10. Items: CO-1376.

Class: blocked on a named document
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Blocked on: FNS's approval of a Colorado waiver of 7 CFR 273.2(f)(1)(i) for self-employment and LLC or S-corporation owner income, if one exists (FNS publishes no complete waiver list on fns.usda.gov; CDHS's correspondence is on cdhs.colorado.gov, refused)
Assumption: The household's statement of self-employment income, and an LLC or S-corporation owner's statement of the owner's income, are accepted unless questionable, as the State rule says.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.506.C and 4.403.F (D-82, rule version 12718, effective 10/01/2026, re-downloaded 2026-11-22) and 7 CFR 273.2(f)(1)(i) (D-95). The FNS waivers page (fns.usda.gov, redirecting to fna.usda.gov/snap/waivers, fetched 2026-11-22) publishes only a five-row state administration waiver list (snap-state-administration-waivers-05122025.xlsx), with no Colorado entry and none on verification; its state waivers database link returns 404. CDHS's waiver correspondence is on cdhs.colorado.gov, which is refused.

### OQ-810. MAGI annualized income (§ 8.100.4.C.4.a): which year is annualized

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.4.C.4.a (S3356) determines an applicant ineligible on monthly income, whose household has earned income from self-employment, seasonal employment or commission-based employment, "using annualized self-employment, seasonal employment, and commission-based employment income". The rule does not say which twelve months are annualized (the calendar year, the twelve months from the application, or the year before). The volume takes the member's expected income from self-employment, seasonal and commission employment for the calendar year of the determination (expected_annual_self_employment_seasonal_commission_income, CO-7403), the year the premium tax credit methodology of C.5 also uses (expected_annual_income, CO-4556). Which income is annualized is not open: coverage pass 6, C6-07 (2026-11-24), settled by the text on file. C.4.a's own words annualize "self-employment, seasonal employment, and commission-based employment income" (S3356), and C.1.d.i counts a lump sum "only in the month received" (S166); the volume had annualized the whole income of a member with such earnings, which spread a lump sum over the year (SE-LUMP: a business netting $1,000 a month and a $6,000 lump sum in November, 18,000 / 12 = 1,500, 112.8%, eligible; by the text 7,000 - 1,000 + 12,000 / 12 = 7,000, 526.3%, and C.5 18,000 / 15,650 = 115.0%: ineligible_income). Only that income is annualized now (ma_annualizable_earnings_in_month, CO-7404; ma_annualized_household_income, CO-4559), for each member who has such earnings (ma_has_annualizable_earnings, CO-6451: a business record, or pay stated seasonal_or_commission on the earnings records, CO-5100; the person-level fact has_self_employment_seasonal_or_commission_earnings, CO-4557, has no reader since coverage pass 4, C4-05); the member's other income stays the month's. The annualized figure is reached only when the monthly figure is higher (ma_budget_period_household_income_pct_fpl, CO-4567). Coverage review F-04. Items: CO-4556, CO-4557, CO-4559, CO-4561, CO-4567, CO-4975, CO-7403, CO-7404.

Coverage pass 3 (C3-08, 2026-11-22): a third reading the rule leaves open is whose ineligibility triggers C.4.a. The volume takes the lower of the monthly and annualized figures for every test (ma_budget_period_household_income, CO-4975, read by CO-4567 and, since C3-02, by limited Family Planning's CO-1153), which is C.4.a's order for each test taken alone. It also applies the annualized figure where the monthly figure already makes the applicant eligible under another test, with two effects the owner should know: (1) the CHP+ copayment band (10 CCR 2505-3 § 320.1, income "at the time of eligibility determination") is set on the annualized figure (RV3-CPB: $4,328 this month, 240.0% for two, band_d on the monthly figure; $34,624 a year, 160.0%, band_c), and (2) the MAGI category can change, for example Adults on the monthly figure against Parents and Caretaker Relatives on the annualized one, which then sets Transitional Medical Assistance's months (§ 8.100.4.I.1.a). The other reading ("Applicants who are found financially ineligible" as the applicant, once eligible under any MAGI test on the monthly figure, keeping it for every test) would keep band_d and the Adults category in those cases.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner, Eligibility operations owner
Assumption: The annualized self-employment, seasonal and commission income is one twelfth of the member's expected income from that work for the calendar year of the determination, for every member with such earnings; the member's other income and other members' income stay monthly (C6-07, by the text); and each income test takes the lower of the monthly and annualized figures.

Coverage pass 4 (2026-11-23): the CHP+ copayment band is no longer part of this question. The copayment amounts are 10 CCR 2505-3 § 320.1's own text (D-66), which sets the band by "income, at the time of eligibility determination", and C.4.a annualizes only for "Applicants who are found financially ineligible based on current or previous monthly household income" (S3356): an enrollee eligible for CHP+ on the month's income has the band of the month's income (chp_copayment_income_band, CO-2228, reads ma_magi_monthly_household_income_pct_fpl, CO-6452, first). CO-387 (RV3-CPB) expects band_d and passes; it is off conformance/known-failures.json. Also since pass 4 (C4-05): each test reads the month's figure first, so the lower-of-two figure decides only a test the month's figure fails; whether a member has such earnings is read from the earnings records (ma_has_annualizable_earnings, CO-6451), and an unstated expected annual income is cannot determine, no longer the month's income. Coverage pass 5 (C5-08, C5-18, 2026-11-23): the seasonal_or_commission field of a pay record is required where read (it had default false, which contradicted this question's statement that an unstated seasonal or commission statement is cannot determine): a pay record MAGI counts that does not state it, in a household the month's figure leaves over a standard, is cannot determine.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.4.C.4 and C.4.a (D-65, rule version effective 11/14/2026, S3356); 42 CFR 435.603(h) (D-34), which lets a State base current monthly income on a prorated portion of reasonably predictable income without fixing the period; Colorado's MAGI verification plan (D-317), which does not mention annualizing. No published rule states the period. Not found (coverage review 2, 2026-11-22): Colorado's MAGI-based income methodology state plan page (template S10), which records the State's 435.603(h) budget-period elections; medicaid.gov's SPA downloads co-13-0030 to co-13-0060 (suffixes -mm, -mm1 to -mm4) hold only co-13-0045-mm1 (D-315, no S10), and a web search for the page could not be run (search service out of credits).

### OQ-811. § 8.100.4.C.5 read through 42 CFR 435.603(i): the premium tax credit income replaces the MAGI figure

Body: § 8.100.4.C.5 (S3357) says an applicant who does not meet the MAGI financial requirements but meets all others "shall be found eligible" if income under the premium tax credit methodology is below 100% of the poverty level. 42 CFR 435.603(i) (S3358), which C.5 implements, says Medicaid financial eligibility "will be determined in accordance with 26 CFR 1.36B-1(e)" - the tax-method income replaces the MAGI figure. The volume reads C.5 as 435.603(i): ma_magi_household_income_pct_fpl (CO-504) takes the premium tax credit figure (CO-4561) when it is below 100% and below the MAGI figure, and every category tests it against its own standard. Every MAGI standard but the Parents and Caretaker Relatives 68% is above 100%, so the readings differ only for a parent or caretaker relative between 68% and 100% on the tax method who is outside the Adults group (entitled to Medicare, or whose dependent child lacks minimum essential coverage): eligible under C.5's words, not under the substitution. The tax-method figure uses the guideline of the preceding open enrollment period (26 CFR 1.36B-1(h)), not the one MAGI uses. A person who neither files nor is claimed has no premium tax credit household and C.5 does not reach that person. Coverage review F-05. Items: CO-504, CO-4560, CO-4561, CO-4564.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The premium tax credit household income replaces the MAGI figure in each category's test (42 CFR 435.603(i)); C.5 does not make a person eligible in a category whose standard that income exceeds.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.4.C.5 (D-65, S3357); 42 CFR 435.603(i) (D-34, S3358); 26 CFR 1.36B-1(d), (e), (h) (D-318, S3359); 45 CFR 155.410(e) (D-319, S3360); Colorado's MAGI state plan amendment CO-13-0045-MM1 (D-315), whose pages do not address 435.603(i). No published Colorado text says which group a person found eligible under C.5 is placed in. Not found (coverage review 2, 2026-11-22): Colorado's state plan page S10 (MAGI-based income methodologies), which would record a 435.603(i) election; the search is the one recorded on OQ-810.

### OQ-812. Medicaid sponsor deeming (§ 8.100.3.K.1): which income of the sponsor counts

Body: § 8.100.3.K.1 (S3352) makes the "Income or resources of an alien sponsor or an alien sponsor's spouse ... countable to the sponsored alien" until citizenship or 40 qualifying quarters, except for a pregnant woman or a child (K.1.a). It states no measure of the sponsor's income and no allowance for the sponsor's own household, as SNAP (10 CCR 2506-1 4.305.2.D), Colorado Works (9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.1.A.5) and Adult Financial (9 CCR 2503-5 3.534.D) each do for their programs. ma_sponsor_deemed_income (CO-4551) counts each sponsor's and sponsor's spouse's own MAGI-based income for the month (ma_person_magi_income, CO-502), with no allowance, adds it to the sponsored person's own MAGI household income, and leaves out a sponsor already in that household. It applies 8 U.S.C. 1631(e) (indigence: no more than the support actually provided, for twelve months) and 1631(f) (battered spouse or child), which K.1 does not restate. "A child" is read as under 19 through the birthday month. Coverage review F-03. Items: CO-4551, CO-4552, CO-503.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner, Legal
Assumption: The sponsor's and the sponsor's spouse's own MAGI-based monthly income counts in full to a sponsored adult who is not pregnant, added to the adult's own household income, with 1631(e) and (f) applied. The sponsor's income must be known: a sponsor who is not part of the case has an income the case does not state, so the answer cannot be determined until it is. The same income counts when the person is tested under the premium tax credit methodology, since the federal deeming rule applies notwithstanding any other provision of law.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.3.K.1 and K.1.a (D-65, S3352); 8 U.S.C. 1631(a), (b), (e), (f) (D-275, S2970, S3353-S3355). Neither text states a measure of income or an allowance for Medicaid; no published Colorado text does.

Coverage pass 5 (C5-06, 2026-11-23): a sponsor who is not a person of the case (no sponsored_by relationship) leaves ma_sponsor_deemed_income (CO-4551) cannot determine, where before it was 0 (SPONSOR-OUT, CO-817); and ma_premium_tax_credit_household_income_pct_fpl (CO-4561) adds twelve times the deemed income of the determination month (8 U.S.C. 1631(a), S2970).

### OQ-813. CHP+ income reasonable opportunity period: 90 days (§ 430.3.A.i) or 30 days (§ 130.1 through § 8.100.4.C.2)

Body: 10 CCR 2505-3 § 430.3.A.i ends a pregnant woman's CHP+ enrollment span if she "Fails to provide a reasonable explanation or paper documentation when self-attested income is not reasonably compatible with income information from an electronic data source, by the end of the 90-day reasonable opportunity period" (S1715). § 130.1 applies the minimal verification of 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.4.B to CHP+, whose income reasonable opportunity period is "30 calendar days" (§ 8.100.4.C.2, S864), and the parallel Medicaid rule for a pregnant woman says "by the end of the 30day reasonable opportunity period" (§ 8.100.4.G.5.a.i). The CHP+ text names a 90-day period for income that no other CHP+ paragraph sets. chp_verification_due_date (CO-2219) gives every CHP+ income documentation request ma_income_reasonable_opportunity_days (30) days; the prenatal span item (chp_is_in_prenatal_coverage_period, CO-2407) leaves the A.i exception to the verification items, so a pregnant woman whose income documentation is 31 to 90 days late is reported past due under the 30-day reading and still enrolled under the 90-day one. Coverage review F-22. Items: CO-2219, CO-2407.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: CHP+ policy owner, Eligibility operations owner
Assumption: CHP+ income documentation is due 30 days after the request for every enrollee, as § 130.1 applies § 8.100.4.C.2; § 430.3.A.i's "90-day" is read as the period of § 8.100.4.C.2 that § 130.1 adopts.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-3 §§ 130.1 and 430.3.A.i (D-66, rule version effective 08/30/2026, S1715); 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.4.C.2 and G.5.a.i (D-65, S864); 42 CFR 457.380 (D-98, S868), which leaves the period to the State. Colorado's MAGI verification plan (D-317) states a 90-day reasonable opportunity period for citizenship documentation, not for income. No published text reconciles the two figures.

### OQ-820. Home equity limit outside long-term care

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.5.M.2.a.ii (S3403) requires the home's equity to be below the indexed limit for the home to be exempt, M.2.a.vi counts a home over the limit that the person intends to return to, and M.3.f counts "an individual's home if the individual's equity interest in the home exceeds the equity value limit". All three sit in the general aged, blind and disabled resource rules, so read literally they apply to every ABD category, not only long-term care. 42 U.S.C. 1396p(f) (S369) is a long-term care rule ("with respect to nursing facility services or other long-term care services"), and 42 CFR 435.601(b)(2) (D-34) has the agency apply the SSI methodology, under which the home is excluded at any value (20 CFR 416.1212). The volume applies the limit to long-term care only: ma_home_equity_disqualifies (CO-650) is read only by the long-term care items, and ma_home_is_countable_resource (CO-4622) does not test it. Coverage review NM-16. Items: CO-650, CO-4622.

Class: state policy choice, with Legal advising (reclassified from "needs Legal" in coverage pass 6, non-MAGI F6-11, 2026-11-24: the texts that bear on it are all on file, the State's rule (§ 8.100.5.M.2.a.ii, vi, M.3.f), 42 U.S.C. 1396p(f) and 42 CFR 435.601(b)(2), (d); no document not on file would decide it. the reading in force follows 435.601(b)(2) and 1396p(f), the State's text reads wider, and which the State means is its own to settle by amending M.2.a or confirming the reading in force, with Legal advising on the federal limit (435.601(d)(2)-(3): no more restrictive than the SSI methodologies))
Owner: Medicaid policy owner, LTSS policy owner
Assumption: The home equity limit applies to long-term care (institutional, HCBS and PACE) only; for the other aged, blind and disabled categories the home is exempt at any equity value unless another rule of § 8.100.5.M.2.a makes it countable.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.5.M.2.a.ii, vi and M.3.f (D-65, version effective 11/14/2026), 42 U.S.C. 1396p(f)(1)(A) (D-46) and 42 CFR 435.601(b)(2) (D-34). The state text states the limit without restricting it to long-term care; whether Colorado's SSI-related methodology may be more restrictive than SSI's for the home is a legal question.

Checked 2026-11-24 (coverage pass 6, non-MAGI F6-11): the same texts; 42 CFR 435.601(b)(2), (d)(1)-(3) (D-51, S4754). Reclassified, the assumption unchanged.

### OQ-821. Transfer penalty: how the partial month of a penalty period shows in the monthly outcome

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.7.F.2.d says "The period of ineligibility shall also include partial months, which shall be calculated by multiplying 30 days by the decimal fractional share of the partial month." ma_transfer_penalty_days (CO-659) computes the days, but ma_transfer_penalty_applies (CO-660), which the monthly outcome ma_ltc_eligibility_status (CO-652) reads, covers whole months only, so a transfer worth less than one month of the divisor never stops long-term care in any month, and the last partial month of a longer penalty is reported eligible. The rule does not say whether a month with some penalty days is a month of ineligibility, an eligible month whose long-term care is not paid for those days, or something else. Coverage review NM-21. Items: CO-659, CO-660, CO-652.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: LTSS policy owner
Assumption: A month that is only partly within the penalty period is reported eligible; the penalty days in it are counted separately, and the facility's payment for that month reflects them.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.7.F.2.c-e (D-65, version effective 11/14/2026). The rule counts the days but says nothing about the month's eligibility status.

### OQ-822. Adult Financial grants other than OAP-A in the aged, blind and disabled income, and the § 8.100.5.F.6 exemptions in the long-term care 300% test

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.5.F.6.y (S3003) exempts "General Assistance, SSI, OAP-A and cash assistance under the Temporary Assistance to Needy Families (TANF) funds". The current Adult Financial grants are OAP (A and B, one category in this volume, OQ-111), Aid to the Needy Disabled (AND-SO and AND-CS) and Aid to the Blind. The SSI-related income (ma_ssi_household_unearned_income_sum, CO-663) reads no Adult Financial grant, so OAP-B, AND and AB grants are not counted; the long-term care gross income (ma_ltc_gross_income, CO-641) counts them, as income before any exemption (§ 8.100.7.A.1). The rule's program names predate the current ones. F.6.y names the "Long Term Care" categories as well, while § 8.100.7.A.1 (S351) defines the long-term care gross income as income "before application of deductions, exemptions or disregards appropriate to the SSI program"; the two texts give different answers for a Colorado Works or Adult Financial grant in the 300% test, which matters only when the grant brings other income near three times the FBR. Coverage review NM-24; coverage pass 2 N2-5. Items: CO-663, CO-641.

Coverage pass 3 C3-10 (fix round 2026-11-23): the same choice reaches every receipt F.6 exempts "for the purpose of determining eligibility for the Long Term Care and Aged, Blind, and Disabled Medical Assistance categories", not only the cash grants: retroactive SSI and Title II lump sums (F.6.c, l), foster care payments (F.6.o), income set aside under a plan to achieve self-support (F.6.q), decennial Census wages (F.6.z) and student grants and scholarships (F.6.aa-cc) are part of the unearned or earned income facts ma_ltc_gross_income (CO-641) sums, so the 300% test counts them while the SSI-related tests exempt them (ma_ssi_excluded_unearned_income, CO-2470; ma_ssi_excluded_earned_income, CO-2471). Loan proceeds (F.6.a) and income tax refunds and credits are not this question: they are not income at all (20 CFR 416.1103(d), (f), S2209), so they are not part of A.1's "income before ... exemptions", and CO-641 now subtracts them (stored case CO-485).

Narrowed in coverage pass 7 (non-MAGI F7-6, docs/reviews/coverage7-medicaid-nonmagi.md): the question is only the exemptions Colorado's own text gives. Where a federal statute bars counting a payment as income in any federal or federally assisted program, Colorado has no choice, and the 300% test and the patient payment leave it out (ma_ltc_federally_excluded_unearned_receipts, CO-7720): LEAP (F.6.n; 42 U.S.C. 8624(f)(1), S5241, "shall not be considered income or resources ... for any purpose under any Federal or State law"), Domestic Volunteer Service Act payments below the minimum wage (F.6.m in part; 42 U.S.C. 5044(f)(1), S5242) and the payments the receipt kind federally_excluded_payment names (F.6.h, i, r, s, v, x; S5246). AmeriCorps allowances stay here: 42 U.S.C. 12637(d) (S5244) excepts programs "as provided under the Social Security Act".
