Title IV student aid is barred by 20 U.S.C. 1087uu (S5243), but the receipt kind educational_assistance does not separate it from other grants and scholarships, so it is listed as a known limit (docs/reviews/known-limits.md, NM-KL-3), not decided here.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: Every Adult Financial grant is exempt for the aged, blind and disabled income tests of the SSI method: OAP-B with OAP-A as one OAP program, and AND and AB as the "General Assistance" F.6.y names. The long-term care 300% test counts every cash grant, Colorado Works and Adult Financial alike, and every other receipt F.6 exempts that is income (retroactive lump sums, foster care payments, PASS income, Census wages, student grants), reading § 8.100.7.A.1's "income before application of deductions, exemptions or disregards" over F.6's "Long Term Care"; loan proceeds and tax refunds, which are not income, are not counted, nor are the payments a federal statute bars from income in any federally assisted program (LEAP energy assistance, payments to volunteers under the Domestic Volunteer Service Act below the minimum wage, and the payments federal statutes exclude from income).

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.5.F.6.y (D-65, version effective 11/14/2026) and § 8.100.6.K (OAP-A and OAP-B both named). No published rule maps the current Adult Financial programs onto F.6.y's names. Checked 2026-11-23: § 8.100.5.F.6 (lead-in, a-cc) against § 8.100.7.A.1 (D-65, version effective 11/14/2026); 20 CFR 416.1103 (D-207). Neither paragraph refers to the other, and no rule says which governs the 300% test. Checked 2026-11-24 (coverage pass 7): 42 U.S.C. 8624(f) (D-5240), 5044(f) (D-5241), 12637(d) (D-5243) and 20 U.S.C. 1087uu (D-5242), fetched; the three that bind Medicaid are taken out of the question (CO-7720).

### OQ-823. In-kind support and maintenance: a flat one third of the FBR, or the presumed maximum value of the shelter received

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.5.H.1.b (S1506) says "For a person living in the household of another and not paying shelter costs, one third of the Federal Benefit Rate (FBR) is counted as in-kind income", while § 8.100.5.F.5.k.ii (S306) says the amount "should not be greater than one third of the Federal Benefit Rate (FBR). Use the Presumed Maximum Value (PMV) of 1/3 of the recipient's portion of the rent ... Use one third of the FBR if an amount is not declared". ssi_in_kind_support_and_maintenance (CO-802) takes the lesser of the shelter others pay and one third of the FBR, F.5.k's reading. Under H.1.b, a person living all month in another's household and paying nothing would count the full one third whatever the shelter is worth (20 CFR 416.1131's one-third reduction). Coverage review NM-25. Items: CO-802.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: In-kind support and maintenance is the shelter others provide, up to one third of the FBR, and one third of the FBR when the amount is not declared (F.5.k.ii).

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.5.F.5.k and H.1.b (D-65, version effective 11/14/2026). The two paragraphs state different measures for the same person and neither refers to the other.

### OQ-830. Colorado Works minor parent living with an unrelated caretaker who joins the unit: does the county require approval of the setting

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.2.N.3.a (S3450) requires a county approved setting when an unmarried, non-emancipated minor parent "lives with an unrelated non-parent caretaker who chooses not to be a member of the assistance unit"; N.3.b says that when the unrelated caretaker "chooses to be a member of the assistance unit, the county department may choose if it is necessary to approve the setting before grant payments are provided per county policy." cw_minor_parent_living_arrangement_met (CO-906) accepts a relative or a parent without approval (N.2.a, N.3.c), and an unrelated caretaker only when the caretaker is a member of her unit; otherwise it reads county_approved_living_arrangement (CO-845). Coverage pass 2 CW2-1. Items: CO-906.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: An unrelated caretaker who has joined the minor parent's unit meets the living arrangement without a separate county approval; the county's approval is read only when an unrelated caretaker has not joined.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.2.N.1, N.2.a and N.3.a-c (D-81, version effective 2025-07-01, S3153, S3450). N.3.b leaves the approval to "county policy"; no state rule sets a default.

### OQ-831. Colorado Works second- and third-level sanction in a partial initial month: 25% of the full-month grant or of the partial payment

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.608.4.C.1 (S760) makes the second and third sanctions "twenty five percent (25%) of an assistance unit's grant payment", and D requires a client who "reapplies for benefits anytime within the calendar month that they are serving a sanction" to serve it "by having a reduction in benefits". 3.609.2.A.1.e (S3156) says "Subtract from authorized grant amount for the partial month any appropriate deductions", without saying how a percentage deduction is measured. For a $488 grant and an 11-day initial month (.36164), 25% of the full-month grant leaves $54 and 25% of the partial payment leaves $132. cw_sanction_reduction (CO-1265), read by cw_grant_amount (CO-914). Coverage pass 2 CW2-3.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: In the month of the initial payment the twenty five percent is taken of the partial month payment (the grant times the 3.609.2.A.1 partial month figure), since C.1 measures it against the unit's "grant payment".

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.608.4.C.1, D and 3.609.2.A.1.e (D-81, version effective 2025-07-01, S760, S3156). Neither paragraph names the base of a percentage deduction in a partial month.

### OQ-845. Old Age Pension: "a member of a household that is receiving" Colorado Works - the household or the assistance unit

Body: C.R.S. 26-2-111(2)(d)(I) (D-59) says "A person who is a member of a household that is receiving public assistance under the Colorado works program ... shall not be eligible to receive public assistance pursuant to this subsection (2)" (the Old Age Pension). 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.71.F.1 (D-80) lets a grandparent or other specified caretaker who is not a parent stay out of the Colorado Works case ("is not required to be a member of the TANF/Colorado Works case") and says the Colorado Works funds received for a child "are not used in determining the specified caretaker's eligibility". af_barred_by_colorado_works (CO-1511) bars only a person who receives Colorado Works cash (receives_tanf_cash), whose own Colorado Works status is eligible (3.520.61.F: "eligible for"), or who is closed out of it by a sanction, a disqualification or demonstrable evidence while otherwise eligible (3.520.71.F.2), so an OAP grandparent living with a grandchild on Colorado Works keeps OAP. That reads "household" as the Colorado Works assistance unit, as (4)(c)(I) defines it for AND; (2)(d) has no such definition, and read literally as everyone in the home it would bar the grandparent. Coverage review 2 of Adult Financial, G-6. Items: CO-1511, CO-953.

Class: needs Legal
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: "Household" in 26-2-111(2)(d)(I) is the Colorado Works assistance unit: the bar reaches a person who is a member of the Colorado Works case, not a relative in the same home whom 3.520.71.F.1 leaves out of it.

Checked 2026-11-22: C.R.S. 26-2-111(2)(d)(I) and (4)(c)(I) (D-59); 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.61.F and 3.520.71.F.1.a-c (D-80, rule version 12428, effective 03/02/2026). Neither text defines "household" for (2)(d).

### OQ-835. ABAWD exception for living with a child: the physical household (Colorado) or the SNAP household (statute)

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.311.A.4 (S749) excepts from the ABAWD time limit an individual "Residing in the same physical household with an individual who is under the age of eighteen (18). The minor individual does not have to be included in the same SNAP household". 7 CFR 273.24(c)(4) (S3050) excepts a person "residing in a household where a household member is under age 18, even if the household member who is under 18 is not himself eligible for SNAP benefits", and 7 U.S.C. 2015(o)(3)(C) as amended by Pub. L. 119-21 sec. 10102 (S127, S128) speaks of "a parent or other member of a household with responsibility for a dependent child under 14". In the Act and the regulation, "household" is the SNAP household (7 U.S.C. 2012; 7 CFR 273.1), and a member under 18 who is "not himself eligible" is still a member of it; Colorado's text reaches a child who is in another SNAP household under the same roof, such as a roommate's child. snap_lives_with_child_under_abawd_dependent_child_age (CO-4250) follows Colorado's physical-household reading, with the statute's age of 14. Elsewhere the volume applies the statute where Colorado's rule is broader (OQ-1, OQ-533, CO-072); whether Colorado may keep the broader exception, or whether it is superseded by the amended 2015(o)(3)(C), is a legal reading. Answers differ only for a person living with a child under 14 who is in a different SNAP household. Coverage pass 2 N2-P2-1. Items: CO-4250, CO-414.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner, Legal
Assumption: Colorado's physical-household reading: a person living in the same home as a child under 14 is excepted whether or not the child is in the person's SNAP household.

Class (coverage pass 4 for SNAP, S4-16, 2026-11-23): reclassified from "needs Legal", which is not one of the stopping rule's two classes. Both texts are on file and the answer waits on no document: it is which of two texts the State applies to a case they treat differently, Colorado's broader rule or the statute's narrower one, a choice the State makes on Legal's reading. Elsewhere the volume applies the federal text where Colorado's rule is broader (CO-3700, CO-119, CO-1243, CO-308); the owner either confirms Colorado's reading, the assumption here, or adopts the statute as a statute override on CO-4250.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.311.A.4 (D-82, version effective 2026-10-01, S749); 7 CFR 273.24(c)(4) (D-202, S3050); 7 U.S.C. 2015(o)(3)(C) as amended (D-3, D-5; S127, S128). No text reconciles the two; FNS's implementation memoranda for sec. 10102 are on www.usda.gov, not an allowed host.

### OQ-850. CCCAP medical leave at a redetermination: does D.9's "unable to care for their children" apply, or is any verified medical leave a temporary break

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.D.9 (S2700) names as an eligible household caretakers "with an open and active Low-Income Child Care case who are participating in a low-income eligible activity and go on temporary verified medical leave and are unable to care for their children". 3.112.C.2 (S2701) makes "Any temporary absence from low-income eligible activities ... due to extended verified medical leave" a temporary break, with no condition on caring for the children, and 3.103.WWW (S3530) includes leave "to care for a family member". 3.114.B.5 (S3251) ends care at the redetermination for a caretaker no longer in an eligible activity. At a change during the period 3.125.Q.2 (S3252) keeps any temporary break, so CO-984 counts the leave there (coverage pass 2 C2-2); at a redetermination the two paragraphs give different tests. Items: CO-984.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: At a redetermination medical leave is an eligible activity only when the caretaker is unable to care for the children (D.9, the paragraph that states the eligible household); at a change during the period any verified medical leave counts (3.112.C.2, 3.125.Q.2).

Checked 2026-11-22: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.103.WWW, 3.111.D.9, 3.112.C.2, 3.114.B.5 and 3.125.Q.2 (D-87, version effective 2026-08-14). Neither D.9 nor 3.112.C refers to the other.

### OQ-863. LEAP: does 3.752.23.AA's "one-time resettlement grant" cover the Colorado Refugee Services Program's Interim Cash Payment

Body: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.752.23.AA (S3302) excludes "A one-time resettlement grant received under the refugee admissions program." The volume states a month's resettlement receipt in one neutral fact, refugee_resettlement_payments_received (CO-4367: "refugee resettlement funds or reception and placement money (from a resettlement agency), or ... the Interim Cash Payment of the Colorado Refugee Services Program"), which Colorado Works exempts as a whole (9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.4.B.28-29, S3163, two paragraphs). LEAP's paragraph names one grant under the refugee admissions program: reception and placement money is that grant; whether the State's Interim Cash Payment is too, LEAP's rule does not say, and it does not mention the Colorado Refugee Services Program. Coverage review 2 N-6 found the fact unread by LEAP, so a resettlement receipt stated as the month's income was counted. Items: CO-4511.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Energy assistance (LEAP) policy owner
Assumption: LEAP excludes all the resettlement money a newly arrived refugee receives, reception and placement money and the State's Interim Cash Payment alike; a grant the case states both as the month's receipt and as a lump sum in the thirty days is excluded once.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.751.1 and 3.752.23 (D-86, version effective 2025-11-01) and the amendment effective 2026-11-01 (D-173) define neither "resettlement grant" nor "refugee admissions program"; 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.4.B.28-29 (D-81) lists the two receipts separately. No published LEAP text settles it.

### OQ-860. Colorado Works military allotment for persons outside the unit: which need standard, and whether persons in the home are included

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.3.B.10 (S3580) counts "The military allotment received by the non-recipient spouse, parent, or stepparent on behalf of individuals not in the assistance unit ... to the extent that such income exceeds the need standard concerning those persons not in the budget unit." The Standards of Assistance Chart (3.606.1.F, S414) has three rows (No Caretaker, One Caretaker, Two Caretakers) keyed by the number of children, and B.10 does not say which row prices "those persons". Persons in the home outside the unit (a stepparent's own children, for example) also have their needs deducted from the stepparent's or parent's income under 3.605.1.A.4.a, so applying B.10 to them as well would deduct the same needs twice. The draft reads it this way: cw_unearned_income_deductions (CO-4369) takes off, for a non-recipient (outside every unit, or a disqualified member), up to the No Caretaker need standard for the number of persons living elsewhere the allotment is paid for (military_allotment_received_for_persons_outside_home, CO-4836; military_allotment_persons_outside_home_count, CO-4837); an allotment for persons in the home is counted with the other unearned income, and A.4.a's needs deduction covers them. Coverage pass 3 CW3-4. Items: CO-4369, CO-4836, CO-4837.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: The need standard for the persons an allotment is paid for is the No Caretaker row for that number of persons, and B.10 is applied only to persons living outside the home.

Checked 2026-11-22: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.3.B.10, 3.605.1.A.4.a and 3.606.1.F (D-81, rule version 12030, effective 07/01/2025). None names the row or says how B.10 and A.4.a combine.

### OQ-867. Rent from an exempt home in the SSI method: J.1's three deductions or the SSI method's ordinary and necessary expenses; gross or net rent in the 300% test

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.5.J.1 (S3619) counts "Net rental income from an exempt home or a life estate interest in an exempt home ... after the following allowable deductions": property taxes and insurance, routine maintenance, and a professional manager's fee. 20 CFR 416.1121(d) (S3620), the SSI method the aged, blind and disabled groups follow, deducts from all rents "ordinary and necessary expenses", interest on debts and other taxes among them. The volume subtracts rental_property_expenses_paid (CO-4458, 416.1121(d)'s list: taxes, insurance, mortgage interest, repairs and maintenance, owner-paid utilities, management fees) from rents stated as rental_income_received, in ma_ssi_excluded_unearned_income (CO-2470), for every rental property. For an exempt home that allows mortgage interest and utilities, which J.1 does not name. The long-term care gross income (CO-641) counts rents before expenses, reading § 8.100.7.A.1's "income before application of deductions". First-pass coverage review NM-27 (J.1). Items: CO-2470, CO-641.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: Rents count net of 416.1121(d)'s ordinary and necessary expenses in the SSI method, an exempt home included (J.1's three deductions are among them, and 42 CFR 435.601(b) does not let the state use a stricter methodology than SSI's); the 300% test counts gross rents.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.5.J.1 and § 8.100.7.A.1 (D-65, version effective 11/14/2026); 20 CFR 416.1121(d) (D-53). J.1 speaks only of an exempt home and does not say whether its list is exclusive; no Colorado text says how other rents are counted.

### OQ-871. CCCAP: a caretaker who leaves the household during the eligibility period

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.125.EE.1 (S3635) acts at once on "Changes to an adult caretaker or teen parent's qualifying low-income eligibility activity" and on a composition change only when "an additional child [is] requesting care"; EE.2 holds every other change "until the adult caretaker or teen parent's re-determination". 3.125.Q.2 (S3252) lets a county discontinue care before the redetermination when "The adult caretaker(s) or teen parent(s) is no longer in a qualifying low-income eligible activity". When a working caretaker leaves during the period and the caretaker who stays was excused only because the other worked (incapacitated, 3.111.D.1.c.3, S1409), the text does not say whether the departure is a composition change held to the redetermination (EE.2) or whether Q.2 reads "the adult caretaker(s)" for the household as it now stands, whose remaining caretaker is in no activity (3.111.E: an incapacitated single caretaker not in an activity is not eligible). The draft reads Q.2 for the household as it stands: the caretaker who left is no longer stated, and the remaining caretakers are tested (cccap_household_caretakers_in_eligible_activity, CO-991), so probe Y-1c is ineligible_activity at the change. A caretaker who joins during the period is the other way round: he was never in an activity, so Q.2's "no longer" does not reach him and EE.2 holds the change (CO-4910). Coverage pass 3 C3-1. Items: CO-991, CO-4910.

Probe hand-check (docs/reviews/probe-handcheck-cash-food-care.md, CO-338; fix round 2026-11-23): two further paragraphs bear on the departure and the body did not name them. 8 CCR 1403-1 3.114.A lists the reasons a county "must terminate child care authorizations and cases during the eligibility period", and its A.3 reads "Adult caretaker(s) or teen parent(s) is not involved in a low-income eligible activity and their job search period has expired"; 3.111.G.3.a makes job search child care available "for no fewer than thirteen (13) weeks of child care for each instance of non-temporary cessation of activity (per rule section 3.112(C))", and G.3.b starts it the day the change is reported. Read together, a household whose working caretaker leaves would keep care as job search for the remaining caretaker for at least 13 weeks, and close only when that period expires (A.3), rather than at the change. Against that, G.3.a offers job search to caretakers "that met the eligibility criteria on the most recent eligibility determination", which the remaining caretaker met only as excused (D.1.c.3) and not by an activity of her own, and the cessation is the departed caretaker's, not hers. The draft keeps the reading below; the other reading would give probe Y-1c (stored CO-338) eligible_low_income through a 13-week job search period.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: A caretaker's departure during the period is acted on at the change when the caretakers who remain are not in an activity or excused from one (Q.2 read for the household as it stands).

Checked 2026-11-22: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.D.1, 3.111.E, 3.125.EE and 3.125.Q (D-87, version effective 2026-08-14). None says whether a caretaker's departure is a change in composition (EE.2) or in the household's activity (EE.1.b, Q.2). Checked 2026-11-23: 3.111.G.3.a-c and 3.114.A.3 (D-87, same version). G.3 ties job search to a "non-temporary cessation of activity" of a caretaker who met the criteria at the last determination and does not say whether an excused caretaker whose co-caretaker left has ceased an activity; 3.114.A.3 closes only after the job search period, so the answer turns on G.3, which neither paragraph settles.

### OQ-872. CCCAP: is a caretaker involuntarily out of the home counted in the household's size and income

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.D.1.b (S1409) qualifies "Households with two adult caretakers or teen parents, when one adult caretaker or teen parent is involuntarily out of the home. Such a household shall be considered a household with one adult caretaker or teen parent." 3.103.LLL (S3636) says the household "includes" the children "in the home" and "the adult caretaker(s) or teen parent(s)", and D.1.a keeps only a caretaker "voluntarily out of the home temporarily" as "part of the household". D.1 is a list of qualifying compositions for the activity test and does not say in terms whether "considered a household with one adult caretaker" also sets the size the income guidelines and fee are read for, or whether the absent caretaker's income counts. The draft reads D.1.b and D.1.a together: the involuntarily absent caretaker is not a household member (cccap_household_members, CO-982), so he is not tested for county residency or the asset declaration, his documents and income are not read, and he is not counted in the size (probe Y-8c: household of 2, fee 47). Read the other way, he would be counted in the size (fee 20 for 3 at $2,000) and his income would count, while residency, assets and his activity would still not be tested. Coverage pass 3 C3-2. Items: CO-982.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: A caretaker involuntarily out of the home is outside the CCCAP household for every purpose: size, income, residency, assets and verification.

Checked 2026-11-22: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.103.LLL, 3.111.A, 3.111.D.1.a-b and 3.111.H.5.c (D-87, version effective 2026-08-14). None says whether D.1.b's one-caretaker household reaches size and income.

### OQ-873. CCCAP: a diversion payment that is not cash assistance

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.103.S (S3638): "All state diversion payments of less than four (4) consecutive months are not cash assistance. For the purpose of child care, county diversion payments are not cash assistance." 3.111.J.26 (S2706) excludes "Public cash assistance grants including ... (TANF)/Colorado Works", so a state diversion paid over four or more consecutive months is excluded (cccap_excluded_unearned_receipts, CO-4463, reading tanf_state_diversion_payment_months, CO-4911). 3.111.I lists countable income ("Each of the following are considered countable income") and does not name a diversion payment; J does not exclude one that is not cash assistance. The draft counts a shorter state diversion and a county diversion as unearned income, as it counts any unearned receipt that J does not exclude; read the other way, I is a closed list and they are not income at all. Coverage pass 3 C3-6. Items: CO-4463, CO-4911, CO-5720, CO-5722.

Probe hand-check (docs/reviews/probe-handcheck-cash-food-care.md, CO-345; fix round 2026-11-23): even counted as income, a one-time county diversion payment is a non-recurring lump sum. 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.H.4.i: "Non-recurring lump sum payments, including lump sum child support payments, may be included as income in the month received or averaged over a twelve (12) month period, whichever is most beneficial for the recipient." cccap_person_unearned_income (CO-3916) averages only lump_sum_received (CO-3367), whose meaning includes "a similar one-time payment"; a one-time payment the case states only in the other unearned income facts is counted in full in the month. Stored CO-345 states its 1,500 county diversion that way: counted in full the household is at 3,500, over 185% of the guideline (3,336.16), ineligible_income; averaged it would be 2,000 + 125 = 2,125, eligible_low_income. So a second question sits under this one: whether CCCAP must average every one-time payment whatever fact the case states it in (which would need a program reading of "non-recurring" over the other unearned facts), or only a payment the case states as a lump sum.

Receipt records, migration phase 2 (2026-11-23; docs/reviews/receipts-migration-2-cccap-sunbucks.md): the second question is settled by the text once the payment's form is a fact of its own. H.4.i reaches "Non-recurring lump sum payments, including lump sum child support payments", whatever their source, and a receipt record states whether a payment is paid once (frequency once) apart from its kind, so CCCAP averages every counted receipt paid once (cccap_one_time_receipts_averaged, CO-5722); no fact the case chose decides it any more. A diversion is a non-recurring payment by its own rule: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.8.A (S762) calls it a "non-recurring cash payment" ("Specify the need(s) for and the specific type(s) of non-recurring cash payment"; "All diversion clients who receive a one-time cash payment"). Stored CO-345 now states its county diversion as one payment in November 2026 and expects the averaged figure, 2,000 + 1,500 / 12 = 2,125, eligible_low_income. The first question stays open: whether a diversion that is not cash assistance is income at all.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: A diversion payment that is not cash assistance under 3.103.S is counted as unearned income. A diversion paid once, like any counted receipt paid once, is averaged over twelve months under 3.111.H.4.i (receipt records; before them, only when the case stated it as a lump sum).

Checked 2026-11-22: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.103.S, 3.111.I and 3.111.J (D-87, version effective 2026-08-14). Neither I nor J names a diversion payment, and S defines cash assistance without saying how a payment that is not cash assistance is treated. Checked 2026-11-23: 3.111.H.4.h-i (D-87, same version). H.4.i lets a non-recurring lump sum be averaged "whichever is most beneficial" but does not define "non-recurring" or say whether a diversion payment is one; 3.103.S calls a county diversion payment not cash assistance without saying whether it recurs.

### OQ-875. SNAP: a foster child 18 or older who applies apart from the foster household - Colorado's exception or the federal bar

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.304.3.B.3 (S3475) says the foster child "may not apply for benefits separately from the foster family unless they are over eighteen (18) and no longer under the parental control of the foster parents". 7 CFR 273.1(c)(4) (S3651) says foster care individuals "cannot participate in the Program independently of the household providing the foster care services", with no age exception. snap_foster_child_applies_separately (CO-4689) reads Colorado's exception and takes 18 or older as standing for "over eighteen and no longer under the parental control of the foster parents" (the age at which parental control ends for SNAP, snap_parental_control_age). A foster youth of 18 to 20 still placed in a foster home by a governmental program who applies separately is eligible on Colorado's text and barred on the federal one.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner, Legal
Assumption: Colorado's exception, with age 18 or older standing for "over eighteen and no longer under parental control".

Class (coverage pass 4 for SNAP, S4-16, 2026-11-23): reclassified from "needs Legal", which is not one of the stopping rule's two classes. Both texts are on file and the answer waits on no document: it is which of two texts the State applies, Colorado's exception or the federal bar, a choice the State makes on Legal's reading. Elsewhere the volume applies the federal text where Colorado's rule is broader (CO-3700, CO-119, CO-1243, CO-308); the owner either confirms Colorado's exception, the assumption here, or adopts 273.1(c)(4) as a statute override on CO-4689.

Checked 2026-11-22: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.304.3.B.3 (D-82, version effective 2026-10-01, re-downloaded 2026-11-22); 7 CFR 273.1(c)(4) (D-7, eCFR as of 2026-11-18). Neither text reconciles the two; no FNS waiver or Colorado state plan provision for 273.1(c)(4) is on an allowed host.

### OQ-885. LEAP: money a non-citizen receives from a sponsor

Body: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.752.21 (S2714) counts "payments from any other source, which is considered to be a gain or benefit to the applicant or recipient", and no paragraph of 3.752.23 (S1624, S2208, S3300, S3302, S3595) names money from a sponsor. Before receipt records LEAP never read it: money_received_from_sponsor (CO-3707) was stated outside the unearned income facts LEAP summed, so a sponsor's cash was left out of LEAP income by silence. The counting table (leap_receipt_treatment_table, CO-5001) keeps that reading, as not_counted_open_question, until the LEAP owner decides.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Energy assistance (LEAP) policy owner
Assumption: Money from a sponsor is not counted (the reading before receipt records), pending this answer; 3.752.21's "any other source" suggests it is.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.752.21, 3.752.211, 3.752.23 (D-86, version effective 11/01/2025, and the amendments effective 11/01/2026, D-173). Neither names sponsor support.

### OQ-886. LEAP: the income verification period when the prior month has no same date

Body: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.751.1 (S2712): "The income verification period is from the date of application to the same date of the prior month (approximately thirty (30) calendar days from the date of application)". An application on March 29, 30 or 31 has no same date in February, nor one on the 31st of a month after a 30-day month. leap_income_verification_period_start (CO-5002) takes the application date less the days of the prior month, which lands a day or two into the application month (March 30: March 2), so a receipt of the last days of February is outside the period.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Energy assistance (LEAP) policy owner
Assumption: The application date less the number of days in the prior month (March 30 gives March 2); the alternative reading is the prior month's last day (February 28).

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.751.1 and 3.752.22.A (D-86, version effective 11/01/2025). Neither says which date stands for a missing same date.

### OQ-887. Adult Financial: a retroactive SSI award paid in a lump sum

Body: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.785.B.1.a (S1622) counts unearned income "in the month received"; 3.520.786.K (S2608) incorporates the SSI unearned income exclusions of 20 CFR 416.1124, and 3.533.A.2 adds the full SSI back after the $20 disregard is taken from unearned income other than SSI. Neither says whether a retroactive SSI award (or a retroactive Adult Financial grant) paid as one sum is SSI for 3.533.A.2 or unearned income other than SSI in the month received. Before receipt records the ledger counted it with the other unearned income, because retroactive_ssi_or_public_assistance_lump_sum_received (CO-3920) was part of the lump sum, itself part of the other unearned income facts; the counting table (af_receipt_treatment_table, CO-5016) keeps that reading.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: A retroactive SSI or Adult Financial lump sum is counted, in the month received, as unearned income other than SSI.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.785, 3.520.786 and 3.533 (D-85); 20 CFR 416.1123(d) and 416.1124 as K incorporates them. 416.1123(d) treats retroactive SSI as income in its own months for SSI; the Colorado rule does not adopt it for the Adult Financial grant.

### OQ-888. Adult Financial: money a non-citizen receives from a sponsor

Body: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.785 (S1622) counts gross unearned income and 3.520.786 (S2608) lists what it exempts; a sponsor's cash support to the non-citizen is not named in either. Before receipt records the ledger never read it for Adult Financial: money_received_from_sponsor (CO-3707) was stated outside the unearned income facts, so it was left out by silence. Deeming from a sponsor (3.534.D, 3.544.G, 3.549.G) counts the sponsor's own income, not what the sponsor pays. The counting table (af_receipt_treatment_table, CO-5016) keeps the reading, as not_counted_open_question, until the owner decides.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: Money a sponsor pays the non-citizen is not counted as the client's unearned income (the reading before receipt records), pending this answer.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.785, 3.520.786, 3.534.D, 3.544.G and 3.549.G (D-85). None names sponsor support paid to the client.

### OQ-900. Medicaid: a recorded beneficiary deed and the Medicare Savings Programs: against which assistance is "subject to estate recovery" judged

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.5.M.2.a.xiv (S3403): "The principal place of residence, which is subject to estate recovery, becomes a countable resource upon the execution and recording of a beneficiary deed"; M.3.e counts "Any real property that is subject to a recorded beneficiary deed and on which an estate recovery claim can be made." 42 U.S.C. 1396p(b)(1) (S3751) allows recovery only from the estate of an institutionalized individual ((A)) or one who was 55 or older when the assistance was received ((B)), and (B)(ii) never reaches "medical assistance for medicare cost-sharing or for benefits described in section 1396a(a)(10)(E)" (QMB, SLMB, QI-1, QDWI). The rule does not say whether "subject to estate recovery" is judged against the assistance being determined or against the person (whose estate is reachable for other Medicaid once 55). Coverage pass 3 C3-5 and the probe hand-check of CO-402: a 76-year-old QMB applicant with a beneficiary deed on her home. Judged against the assistance being determined, nothing a Medicare Savings Program pays can be recovered, so the home stays exempt and she is QMB; judged against the person, the home counts and she is refused QMB on its equity while her full-Medicaid test (where recovery is possible) refuses her too. A person under 55 who is not institutionalized has no recoverable estate on either reading (ma_home_is_subject_to_estate_recovery, CO-5203), and the text settles that half. Items: CO-4622, CO-5203, CO-5205, CO-5206, CO-613, CO-614, CO-615, CO-616.

Class: state policy choice, with Legal advising (reclassified from "needs Legal" in coverage pass 6, non-MAGI F6-11, 2026-11-24: both readings rest on text on file, § 8.100.5.M.2.a.xiv and M.3.e against 42 U.S.C. 1396p(b)(1) and 42 CFR 435.601(b)(2); no unfetched document would decide which assistance 'subject to estate recovery' is judged against. It is the State's reading of its own rule, which HCPF settles by amending M.2.a.xiv or confirming the reading in force)
Owner: Legal, Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: "Subject to estate recovery" is judged against the assistance being determined: a home that counts only because of a recorded beneficiary deed is left out of the Medicare Savings Program resources (and out of a deeming parent's resources), and counts for the full-coverage aged, blind and disabled groups when the person is 55 or older or lives in a medical institution.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.5.M.2.a.xiv and M.3.e (D-65, version effective 11/14/2026); 42 U.S.C. 1396p(a)(1)(B), (b)(1) (D-46). Neither the rule nor the statute says against which assistance the beneficiary-deed paragraph is judged; no HCPF memo on an allowed host addresses it.

Checked 2026-11-24 (coverage pass 6, non-MAGI F6-11): the same texts; no HCPF memo on an allowed host. Reclassified, the assumption unchanged.

### OQ-901. Adult Financial: an Old Age Pension client who is a patient in a private psychiatric hospital - barred by C.R.S. 26-2-111(1)(d) or paid under the rule

Body: C.R.S. 26-2-111(1)(d) (S3213) grants no assistance payment unless "The person is not an inmate of a public institution, except as a patient in a public medical institution, or is not a patient in any institution for tuberculosis or mental diseases ... but the provisions of this paragraph (d) shall not be applicable to or in any way affect the class of old age pension recipients provided for in subsection (2)(a)(III)". 26-2-111(2)(a)(III) describes an old age pension recipient who is "an inmate of an institution, not penal in character, maintained by the state or by a municipality therein or county thereof". Read literally, the proviso spares only that class, so an OAP client of 60 or older who is a patient in a private psychiatric hospital (not maintained by the state, a municipality or a county) is barred. 9 CCR 2503-5 bars only correctional inmates and fleeing felons (3.520.61.B, S1010) and pays an OAP client in a psychiatric facility a personal needs allowance (3.532.D.3), with no distinction between public and private facilities. af_and_is_barred_as_institution_patient (CO-4446) applies (1)(d) to Aid to the Needy Disabled only (coverage review F-13, statute override), and af_category (CO-953) pays OAP in any psychiatric facility. Adult Financial coverage pass 3 H-4 (the same class of finding as coverage pass 2's G-6). Items: CO-4446, CO-953.

Class: needs Legal
Owner: Legal, Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: The (1)(d) bar is not applied to the Old Age Pension at all: an OAP client who is a patient in any psychiatric or tuberculosis institution, public or private, is paid under 3.520.61.B and 3.532.D.3.

Checked 2026-11-23: C.R.S. 26-2-111(1)(d) and (2)(a)(III) (D-59, leg.colorado.gov text); 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.61.B and 3.532.D.3 (D-80, SOS version in force). The rule does not cite (1)(d) or say why its psychiatric-facility allowance reaches private facilities; no Colorado court opinion or CDHS rule history on an allowed host reconciles the two.

### OQ-950. Medicaid: the whole month for an age condition in the groups Colorado's effective-date rules do not name

Body: Time audit T-1 (docs/reviews/time-audit.md) measures each Medicaid age condition in the month evaluated, met if it is met on any day of the month. For the MAGI groups 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.4.G.1 says so ("eligible ... at any time during a calendar month shall be eligible for benefits during the entire month", S3351); for the Aged, Blind and Disabled categories § 8.100.5.C.1 makes eligibility effective "the first day of the month the person becomes eligible" (S3900), and § 8.100.5.C.3 gives the Children's Buy-In the whole month. 42 CFR 435.915(b) lets the State make eligibility effective on the first day of a month for a person "eligible at any time during that month" (S3901), an option. Three groups read an age and are named by neither Colorado rule: the Breast and Cervical Cancer Program ("has not yet attained the age of 65", ma_bccp_eligibility_status, CO-1152), PACE (55 or older, ma_pace_eligible, CO-654, which also requires an Aged, Blind or Disabled category) and the IMD exclusion for an institutional resident aged 21 to 64 (ma_meets_imd_requirement, CO-1179). The draft reads them as the other groups are read: an age condition is met for the month if it is met on any day of it. Read the other way (the age on the first day of the month, or on the date coverage starts), a woman whose 65th birthday falls in the month would leave BCCP for that month, a person turning 55 would enter PACE the next month, and a resident turning 21 or 65 would be excluded or covered from the next month.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner, LTSS policy owner
Assumption: An age condition of BCCP, PACE and the IMD exclusion is met for a month if it is met on any day of the month, as § 8.100.4.G.1 and § 8.100.5.C.1 read for the other groups.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.4.G.1, § 8.100.5.C.1 and C.3 (D-65, rule version effective 11/14/2026); 42 CFR 435.915(a)-(b) (D-35). The BCCP excerpts cited by CO-1152 (S608) and the PACE and IMD excerpts state the ages but no effective-date rule for a month in which the age is reached. Items: CO-1152, CO-654, CO-1179.

### OQ-951. Colorado Works and Adult Financial: the age in an application month earlier than the determination month

Body: Time audit T-1 also asked about the cash programs. cw_is_dependent_child (CO-901: "under 18", 26-2-703(9), C.R.S.; 9 CCR 2503-6 3.601) and af_is_oap_age (CO-940: sixty or older, 26-2-111(2)(a)(I); the birth-month transition of 3.532.B.3) are per-person items, so each is evaluated once per case: CO-901 on the determination date, and CO-940 on the determination date with its birth-month rule anchored to the determination month. Neither program's fetched text says on which date an age condition is measured for an application month that precedes the determination month (Adult Financial prorates the first month from the day the client is determined eligible, 3.532.B.2), or for a later month of a case, where a birthday is a change in circumstances handled under the programs' change rules. A child who turns 18 between the application date and the determination is therefore not a dependent child in the application month, and an applicant who turns 60 after applying is OAP age in the application month. 3.532.B.3 settles only the transition of an AND or AND-SO client: "from the first day of the birth month". No stored household crosses such a birthday.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: (narrowed by coverage pass 4, CA4-15) Each age condition is measured in the month evaluated: an "under N" or "N or younger" limit on the month's first day, an "N or older" limit on its last day, as docs/operators.md "Ages in a month" reads them; Adult Financial's first month is prorated from the eligibility date (3.542.B), and Colorado Works' unit takes in a child who is a dependent child in any month from the application month through the determination month, and is built for each month from the members of that month, the unit's size and standards, the minor-parent rules, the adult member and Adult Financial's AND-CS child and spouse's children each taking the age on the month's first day (coverage pass 6). Open: which day of the month an age is read on, which neither program's text names.

Checked 2026-11-23: 26-2-703(9), C.R.S. and 9 CCR 2503-6 3.601 (the dependent child, S401, S406); 9 CCR 2503-5 3.530 and 3.532.B.2-3 (D-80: the first month "prorated according to the number of days remaining in the month"; the AND client's transition "from the first day of the birth month"). None states the date an age is measured on for a month other than the determination month. Items: CO-901, CO-940.

Revisited 2026-11-24 (coverage pass 6, CA6-4, docs/reviews/coverage6-cash.md): the review found the assumption carried only by the eligibility test's dependent child (CO-921, CO-6807); the unit's counts, standards and income, the minor-parent items and two Adult Financial deeming items read a person-scope test or the determination date's age. They now read each age in the month evaluated (the first day for "under N", the complement for "18 or older"), so the assumption holds throughout; a child is a dependent child of a month only from the month of birth (CA6-3). The day remains the open choice. Items added: CO-6807, CO-7303, CO-7304, CO-7305, CO-906, CO-909, CO-924, CO-926, CO-927, CO-2351, CO-3881, CO-4352, CO-2384, CO-2392.

Revisited 2026-11-24 (cash pattern sweep, PS-T1 to PS-T7, docs/reviews/pattern-sweep-cash.md): seven more readers still took the determination date's age in a month they evaluate: the OAP psychiatric facility's sixty-five (af_oap_grant, the last day, as the OAP age), the work-eligible adult and minor parent and the work requirement's child under six (the first day, as the unit), the lawful presence and school attendance verifications (the first day) and the AND-SO verifications (the last day, as af_and_so_eligible). They now read the age in the month evaluated, so the assumption holds in every cash reader of an age in a month; the burial decedent's minority is the age at death (3.570.42), not a month's. The day remains the open choice. Items added: CO-951, CO-919, CO-920, CO-1420, CO-1422, CO-1668, CO-2584.

### OQ-952. Resolved: a retroactive month's SSI-method resources and couple are those of that month (8.100.3.E.1; 20 CFR 416.1207(a))

Body: Time audit T-8. ssi_countable_resources_individual (CO-800) is per person: it counts the holdings a case states once (liquid_resources_value, the vehicles, the burial funds, retroactive_benefit_payment_unspent_value), and its readers (ssi_countable_resources_couple, CO-801; ma_ssi_countable_resources_individual, CO-4623; af_burial_responsible_person_resources, CO-1225) are per person too. So every month a determination evaluates, a retroactive month included, is judged on the determination date's holdings, and § 8.100.5.F.6.c's nine-month exclusion of a retroactive SSI or Social Security lump sum (S3404) is counted to the determination month. A retroactive month is to be one in which the person "met all applicable eligibility requirements" (§ 8.100.3.E.1), resources included. A person whose savings, or whose unspent lump sum's exclusion, differed in a retroactive month from the determination date is judged on the wrong figure in that month: for example a lump sum received eleven months before the determination is counted in every retroactive month, though it was still excluded in the earliest of them. Stating the holdings by month would make the resource facts and their readers per month, the change the non-MAGI fix round owns.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The holdings a case states are the determination date's and stand for every month the determination evaluates; the lump-sum exclusion is counted to the determination month.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.3.E.1-2 and § 8.100.5.F.6.c (D-65); neither says on which date a retroactive month's resources are valued, and no fetched Colorado text adopts SSI's first-moment-of-the-month valuation for retroactive months. Items: CO-800.

Resolved (coverage pass 4, non-MAGI, N4-7, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/coverage4-medicaid-nonmagi.md): the premise is gone and the text decides it. The holdings records are per month (holdings, CO-5300: `month`, "The month the value is as of (its first moment, 20 CFR 416.1207(a))", S4501), and 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.3.E.1 (S217) gives retroactive coverage only where the person "met all applicable eligibility requirements" in the month; 416.1207(a) values resources "as of the first moment of the month". So each month a determination evaluates is judged on its own month's holdings: ma_ssi_countable_resources (CO-608) and ma_meets_ssi_resource_standard (CO-610) are per month, from ma_ssi_countable_resources_in_month (CO-5322); the couple (ma_ssi_couple_members, CO-3626; ma_ssi_household_size, CO-604) is the month's, with institution status measured against the month (ma_excluded_from_ssi_household, CO-661; ma_is_institutionalized_spouse_in_institution, CO-5200; § 8.100.7.C.2.b; 42 U.S.C. 1396r-5(b)(1)); and F.6.c's nine-month exclusion of a held retroactive payment is counted to each month (CO-5320). Stored households CO-760 (RETRO-RES) and CO-761 (RETRO-COUPLE). ssi_countable_resources_individual (CO-800) stays the determination month's figure for the programs that adopt it at one date.

### OQ-953. Cash programs: whether the first day counts in "N consecutive days"

Body: Time audit T-6. Colorado Works counts a client temporarily out of the State as in the home if the absence does "not exceed ninety (90) consecutive days", and a resident of an institution after "at least thirty (30) consecutive days" (9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.2.O, S2602); Adult Financial limits the personal needs allowance to a resident of "at least thirty (30) consecutive days" (3.532.D, 3.543.D, 3.548.D; S1008 to S1010) and presumes non-residence after "thirty (30) or more consecutive days" out of the country and "ninety (90) or more" out of the State (9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.64.E, S3201); the Home Care Allowance reads a thirty-day absence or stay. None says whether the day of departure or entry is one of the N days. The codex counts the days elapsed since it (days_between), so the day itself is not counted: a client who left on August 1 has been away ninety days on October 30 and is within the Colorado Works limit through that day. Counted the other way, October 30 would be the 91st day and the client would be out of the home a day earlier, and each "at least thirty days" would be met a day earlier. The readers are cw_meets_residency (CO-904), cw_is_institution_resident (CO-3804), cw_assistance_unit_members_for_size (CO-2351), af_resides_in_psychiatric_facility_thirty_consecutive_days (CO-2006), af_resides_in_care_facility_thirty_consecutive_days (CO-1510), af_hca_eligibility_status (CO-1217) and af_is_presumed_nonresident_by_absence (CO-4401); each states the reading in its precision.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: A period of N consecutive days is counted from the day after the absence or stay began, so the day of departure or entry is not counted.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.2.O (D-81); 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.64.E, 3.532.D, 3.543.D, 3.548.D (D-80). None defines how consecutive days are counted, and neither volume has a general rule on computing periods of days. Items: CO-904, CO-3804, CO-2351, CO-2006, CO-1510, CO-1217, CO-4401.

### OQ-970. Partly resolved: AmeriCorps payments are MAGI income and special pay is earnings; open: whether workforce and vocational training allowances are MAGI income

Body: 42 CFR 435.603(e) (S165) counts "income calculated using the same financial methodologies used to determine modified adjusted gross income as defined in section 36B(d)(2)(B) of the Code", and 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.4.C.1 (S166) lists what it counts and excludes without naming these payments. Before receipt records the ledger stated each of them as part of the non-taxable other unearned income (americorps_payments_received, workforce_program_payments_received with vocational_training_allowance_received, uniformed_services_special_pay_received, all part of other_unearned_income_nontaxable, CO-030), so ma_person_magi_income (CO-502) left them out. The federal tax treatment is not that simple: an AmeriCorps living allowance and most training allowances are taxable compensation, and hostile fire or imminent danger pay is excluded only for service in a combat zone. Medicaid's MAGI counting table (ma_magi_receipt_treatment_table, CO-5500) keeps the earlier reading, as not_taxable_income, until the owner decides.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The four kinds are not MAGI-based income (the reading before receipt records).

Checked 2026-11-23: 42 CFR 435.603(e) (D-34) and 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.4.C.1.a-d (D-40). Neither names these payments; the Code sections that would decide them (26 U.S.C. 61, 112, 3121) are not on file. Items: CO-5500.

Coverage pass 4 (C4-11, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/coverage4-medicaid-magi-chp.md). Fetched for this pass from Cornell LII: 26 U.S.C. 61(a) (D-4430, S4631), 42 U.S.C. 12637(d) and 29 U.S.C. 3241(a)(2) (D-4439, S4640); 26 U.S.C. 112 was already on file (D-4340); IRS Publication 525, Taxable and Nontaxable Income (irs.gov, fetched 2026-11-23, not stored), names neither AmeriCorps payments nor WIOA training allowances.
- Uniformed services special pay: no longer part of this question. It is earnings (OQ-1020), and MAGI's earnings row leaves it out for a combat zone (26 U.S.C. 112; OQ-1024, settled for MAGI); the receipt row is in_earnings.
- AmeriCorps: settled. 26 U.S.C. 61(a) makes gross income "all income from whatever source derived" "Except as otherwise provided", and no Code section on file provides otherwise for an AmeriCorps living allowance, stipend or award. 42 U.S.C. 12637(d) keeps such allowances out of income for need-based programs "other than as provided under the Social Security Act", and Medicaid is a Social Security Act program whose income rule is MAGI (42 U.S.C. 1396a(e)(14)(A), S4630). ma_magi_receipt_treatment_table (CO-5500) counts americorps (counted_no_rule_excludes; the owner signs). Before, not counted.
- Workforce and vocational training allowances: still open, and a genuine state choice. 61(a) counts them unless the Code excludes them, and 29 U.S.C. 3241(a)(2) does not reach a Social Security Act program; but Colorado's own rule says "Training allowances granted by WIA to a dependent child or a caretaker relative of a dependent child to participate in a training program is exempt" (10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.3.K.8.b, S4107), and 8.100.3.K applies to MAGI except where 8.100.4 says otherwise. Whether that State exemption stands for MAGI against 42 U.S.C. 1396a(e)(14)(B)'s bar on income disregards (S4630), and whether the State counts the allowances of other persons and of non-WIOA vocational programs as 61(a) income, is the State's reading of its own rule against the federal one (Medicaid policy owner with Legal); no fetched text reconciles the two.

Class: state policy choice (training allowances only)
Owner: Medicaid policy owner, Legal
Assumption: Workforce and vocational training allowances are not MAGI-based income (the reading before receipt records, and K.8.b's exemption for the persons it names).

Checked 2026-11-23 (coverage pass 4): 26 U.S.C. 61(a) (D-4430); 42 U.S.C. 12637(d), 29 U.S.C. 3241(a)(2) (D-4439); 42 U.S.C. 1396a(e)(14)(A)-(B) (D-26); 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.3.K.8.a-c (D-65); IRS Publication 525 (irs.gov). Items: CO-5500.

### OQ-971. Partly resolved: the kinds the text on file settles (N4-1, N4-3); open: whether 8.100.5.F.6.j or F.6.y reaches six kinds of receipt no paragraph on file names

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.F.5 (S3001) counts unearned income "including, but not limited to" its list, and 8.100.5.F.6 (S3404, S3617, S3003, S4751) exempts named receipts "For the purpose of determining eligibility for the Long Term Care and Aged, Blind, and Disabled Medical Assistance categories". Where Colorado's text names nothing, 42 CFR 435.601(b)(2) (S4754) has the agency "apply the financial methodologies and requirements of the cash assistance program that is most closely categorically related to the individual's status", for these groups SSI's (20 CFR 416 Subpart K). Six kinds remain that no paragraph of F.5, F.6 or Subpart K on file names: kinship_care_payment (a payment to a kinship caregiver who is not a certified foster parent; a certified kinship foster parent's payment is a payment "for providing foster care", stated as foster_care_payment and exempt under F.6.l), colorado_works_supportive_services ("a payment and/or service in addition to basic cash assistance or diversion that is based on an assessed need", 9 CCR 2503-6 3.601, S4753: F.6.y names TANF "cash assistance"), refugee_resettlement, migrant_farmworker_emergency_assistance, early_childhood_research_payment and military_allotment. Subpart K excludes none of them (416.1124(c)(8) reaches foster care for "an ineligible child who was placed in your home by a public or private nonprofit child placement or child care agency", which a kinship caregiver who is not a foster parent is not paid as), so the SSI methodology counts them; the open point is Colorado's own text: whether F.6.j, "Assistance from other agencies and organizations", which the ledger reads as General Assistance, emergency assistance and charity, or F.6.y reaches these payments from an agency or organization. Worked example (the review's DIVERSION-aged mechanics): an aged person with Social Security 800 and a 300 supportive services payment in the month: counted, 800 + 300 - 20 = 1,080 > 994, not aged-eligible; exempt under F.6.j, 780, aged.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: Each of the six kinds is counted (the reading before receipt records), pending the owner's reading of F.6.j and F.6.y.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.F.4-6, L, P (D-65); 9 CCR 2503-6 3.601 (D-81); 20 CFR 416.1103, 416.1121, 416.1123, 416.1124, 416.1157 (D-53, D-207); 42 CFR 435.601 (D-51). F.6.j does not say which agencies or organizations; no paragraph on file names the six kinds. Items: CO-5510.

Resolved in part (coverage pass 4, non-MAGI, N4-1 and N4-3, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/coverage4-medicaid-nonmagi.md): the question as first written said Colorado's text "neither adopts Subpart K's exclusions by reference nor lists these payments"; it does list several, and 42 CFR 435.601(b)(2) brings the SSI methodology where it is silent (as OQ-820 and OQ-867 already read it). Settled by the text: reverse_mortgage, not income (8.100.5.L.1, S4750); reimbursement, not income (F.6.w, S4751; 416.1103(a)(5), (b)(3), S4757); housing_utility_allowance, exempt (F.6.g, S4751; 416.1124(c)(14), S4758; OQ-1281 for a program outside the Acts); colorado_works_diversion, exempt (F.6.y; S4753); energy_assistance_other, exempt (F.6.j; 416.1124(c)(10), 416.1157(c), S4758); resource_liquidation, not income (P.1, S4752; 416.1103(c), S4042; with OQ-995's two kinds); property_or_food_tax_refund and crime_victim_compensation, exempt (416.1124(c)(1), (c)(17)); in_home_care_allowance and vocational_training_allowance, not income (416.1103(a)(3), (b)(1), S4757); uniformed services special pay, exempt as earnings (416.1124(c)(19); ma_ssi_earnings_treatment_table, CO-5107). Where Colorado's own paragraph counts what Subpart K does not (interest from a countable resource, a gift to a child with a life-threatening condition, a State's annuity for a veteran), the question is OQ-1280. The receipt's irregular field (416.1124(c)(6)), the death-benefit and claim-expense parts (416.1121(e), 416.1123(b)(3)) and a veterans benefit's amount for a dependent (416.1123(e)(1)) are read (CO-5511, CO-6731).

### OQ-972. Medicaid SSI method: whether money a sponsor gives the non-citizen is counted on top of the sponsor's deemed income

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.F.5 (S3001) counts "Gifts and inheritances" and unearned income generally; cash a sponsor gives the non-citizen is not named in 8.100.5.F.6. Before receipt records the ledger never read it for Medicaid: money_received_from_sponsor (CO-3707) was stated outside the unearned income facts, so it was left out by silence; Medicaid's sponsor deeming (ma_sponsor_deemed_income, CO-4551) counts the sponsor's own income under 8.100.3.K.1. The SSI-method counting table (ma_ssi_receipt_treatment_table, CO-5510) keeps that reading, as not_counted_open_question; MAGI leaves it out as a gift (not taxable income).

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: Money a sponsor pays the non-citizen is not counted as the person's SSI-method unearned income (the reading before receipt records), pending this answer.

Checked 2026-11-23 (re-read in coverage pass 4, non-MAGI N4-14): 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.F.5.c ("Gifts and inheritances") and F.5.j.vii ("Alimony and support payments") name money a sponsor gives the non-citizen, so the text counts it as unearned income; what no paragraph on file says is whether it is counted as well as the sponsor's income deemed under 8.100.3.K.1 (ma_sponsor_deemed_income, CO-4551) and 20 CFR 416.1166a (D-53), which deems the sponsor's earned and unearned income and says nothing of a payment the sponsor makes to the non-citizen. The open point is only that double counting. Items: CO-5510.

### OQ-973. Medicaid: whether 8.100.5.K reaches a VA housebound allowance

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.K (S2900) leaves out "The portion of the pension payments for Aid and Attendance (A&A) and Unusual Medical Expenses (UME), as determined by the VA" when determining eligibility, and uses it as patient payment under K.2. The receipt kind va_aid_and_attendance (receipts, CO-5000) means "VA Aid and Attendance or housebound allowance", a kind the receipts share with the other programs; a household states the VA's Unusual Medical Expenses portion as a receipt of that kind too (the translation of va_aid_and_attendance_amount does). K does not name the housebound allowance, which the VA pays on the same pension for a veteran confined to the home. The SSI method's counting table (ma_ssi_receipt_treatment_table, CO-5510) treats the whole kind as not income.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The housebound allowance is treated as K treats Aid and Attendance (not income; patient payment under K.2), since the receipt kind does not separate them.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.K (D-65). It names Aid and Attendance and Unusual Medical Expenses only. Items: CO-5510, CO-5530.

### OQ-974. Medicaid spousal impoverishment: whether a community spouse's or family member's child support and cash assistance are part of their income

Body: The community spouse monthly income allowance and the family allowance subtract the community spouse's and each dependent family member's own income (10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.7.R.2, S1756; § 8.100.7.T.1.c, S1736; 42 U.S.C. 1396r-5(d)(1)(C), (d)(3)). Before receipt records ma_community_spouse_income (CO-2467) and ma_family_allowance (CO-657) summed Social Security, pensions, wages, self-employment, other unearned income and tax-exempt interest (and, for the community spouse, unemployment), and left out child support received and Colorado Works and Adult Financial cash, which had their own facts. The texts on file say "income" without listing it. ma_spousal_unearned_receipts (CO-5531) keeps the earlier reading; long-term care gross income (CO-641) counts both.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: LTSS policy owner, Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: A community spouse's or family member's child support and Colorado Works or Adult Financial cash are not part of their income for the allowances.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.7.R, T (D-65); the federal text the family allowance follows, 42 U.S.C. 1396r-5(d)(1)(C). Neither lists the income counted. Items: CO-5510, CO-5531.

### OQ-985. Colorado Works: a kinship care payment received for a related child

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.4.B.11 (S2606): "A child receiving foster care or kinship care funds under Title IV of the Social Security Act shall be excluded from the assistance unit and his or her income shall be exempt from consideration for Colorado Works eligibility and payment." The volume reads a foster care provider's payment as exempt under it (foster_care_payment: excluded, as cw_excluded_unearned_income did before receipt records), but a kinship care payment made to the caretaker for a related child in the caretaker's care was never among the exempt sources: before receipt records it sat in the caretaker's other unearned income (kinship_care_payments_received, CO-4503, was read only by CCCAP), so Colorado Works counted it by silence. The text does not say whether B.11's "his or her income" reaches a kinship payment the caretaker receives for the child, nor whether a payment not funded under Title IV (a State kinship payment) is exempt. The counting table keeps the reading before receipt records (kinship_care_payment: counted_no_rule_excludes) until the owner decides. Items: CO-5650.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: A kinship care payment the caretaker receives for a related child is counted as the caretaker's unearned income (the reading before receipt records), pending this answer.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.3 and 3.605.4 (D-81, version effective 2025-07-01). B.11 names "foster care or kinship care funds under Title IV" as a child's income; no paragraph names a kinship payment received by the caretaker.

### OQ-986. Colorado Works: its own diversion and supportive services payments as income to the unit

Body: A Colorado Works diversion payment (9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.8) and a Colorado Works supportive services payment are Colorado Works' own payments; docs/conventions.md rule 6 says a program does not count its own grant as income, and the counting table so treats basic cash assistance (colorado_works_cash: not_income_to_unit; 3.609.2.G for a retroactive one). 3.605.4 does not name either payment, and 3.606.8 does not say how a diversion payment is treated in a later basic cash assistance determination (the unit is then usually within a period of ineligibility, A.4). Before receipt records both sat in the other unearned income facts (tanf_diversion_payment_received, CO-4850, and tanf_supportive_service_payments_received, CO-4452, were read only by CCCAP), so Colorado Works counted them by silence. The counting table keeps that reading (counted_no_rule_excludes) until the owner decides. Items: CO-5650.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: A Colorado Works diversion or supportive services payment received in the month is counted as unearned income (the reading before receipt records), pending this answer.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.3, 3.605.4, 3.606.8 and 3.609.2.G (D-81). None names a diversion or supportive services payment as income or as exempt.

### OQ-987. Colorado Works: emergency assistance to a migrant farmworker and need-based cash from a charity

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.4.A.3 (S2605) exempts "Emergency Assistance received on a one-time basis in cash or in kind from other agencies and organizations", and B.9 (S4004) "Assistance from other agencies or organizations that are provided for items not included in the need standard or do not duplicate a component of the need standard in total". Emergency assistance paid to a migrant or seasonal farmworker in the job stream (migrant_farmworker_emergency_assistance) and cash a charitable organization gives on the basis of need (charitable_need_based_cash) are receipt kinds of their own; whether either is exempt under A.3 (when received once) or B.9 (when it pays for items outside the need standard) turns on facts the receipts do not state. Before receipt records both sat in the other unearned income facts and Colorado Works counted them by silence; the counting table keeps that reading (counted_no_rule_excludes) until the owner decides. Items: CO-5650.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: Both are counted as unearned income (the reading before receipt records), pending this answer.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.4.A.3, B.9 and B.10 (D-81). Neither paragraph names a migrant farmworker's emergency assistance or a charity's need-based cash, and B.9 depends on what the assistance pays for.

### OQ-990. CCCAP: kinds of receipt a paragraph of 3.111.J names only in part, or may name

Body: CCCAP's counting table (cccap_receipt_treatment_table, CO-5720) gives each kind of receipt one treatment. For five kinds the paragraph of 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.J that bears on it names only part of what the kind holds, or may reach it. J.12 (S2207) excludes "Low-Income Energy Assistance Program (LEAP) benefits"; energy_assistance_federal holds LEAP and any other federally funded energy assistance. J.14 excludes "Earned Income Tax Credit (EIC) payments"; income_tax_refund holds every income tax refund or credit. J.27 (S2706) excludes "Reimbursements for expenses paid related to a settlement or lawsuit"; reimbursement holds any reimbursement of expenses that is not a gain. These three are excluded whole, as the ledger excluded them before receipt records (unearned_income_prior_thirty_days, CO-3913, subtracted energy_assistance_received, tax_refunds_and_credits_received and expense_reimbursements_received). J.20 (S4021) excludes Carl D. Perkins assistance "made available for attendance costs", which a vocational_training_allowance may be; J.25 excludes "Loans from private individuals as well as commercial institutions", which a reverse_mortgage may be read as. These two are counted because no rule excludes them (3.111.I names neither). 3.111.I says what is "considered countable income" and J what is not; neither says how a receipt that I does not name and J names only in part is treated.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: Federally funded energy assistance, income tax refunds and credits, and reimbursements of expenses are excluded whole (J.12, J.14, J.27, the reading before receipt records); a vocational training allowance and reverse mortgage payments are counted because no rule excludes them.

Checked 2026-11-23: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.I and J (D-87, version effective 2026-08-14). No paragraph treats non-LEAP energy assistance, income tax refunds other than the EIC, reimbursements unrelated to a settlement, vocational training allowances outside WIA or Perkins attendance costs, or reverse mortgage payments. Items: CO-5720.

### OQ-991. CCCAP: General Assistance and emergency assistance, and J.26's "public cash assistance grants including"

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.J.26 (S2706) excludes "Public cash assistance grants including Old Age Pension (OAP), Aid to the Needy Disabled (AND), and Temporary Assistance to Needy Families (TANF)/Colorado Works", and 3.103.S (S3638) defines cash assistance as "payments, vouchers, and other forms of benefits designed to meet a household's ongoing basic needs". County General Assistance and one-time emergency assistance are public payments the list does not name; read as illustrations ("including"), J.26 may exclude General Assistance, which meets basic needs, and perhaps not emergency assistance received once. Before receipt records the ledger counted both (general_or_emergency_assistance_received, CO-3812, was part of the other unearned income CO-3913 counted); the counting table keeps that reading and marks both rows counted because no rule excludes them.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: General Assistance and emergency assistance are counted (the reading before receipt records): J.26 is read as naming its grants.

Checked 2026-11-23: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.103.S, 3.111.I and J.26 (D-87, version effective 2026-08-14). Neither names General Assistance or emergency assistance. Items: CO-5720.

### OQ-992. CCCAP: money a non-citizen receives from a sponsor

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.I (S4020) lists what is "considered countable income" and J (S1623) what is not; neither names a sponsor's cash support to a non-citizen. Before receipt records the ledger never read it for CCCAP: money_received_from_sponsor (CO-3707) was stated outside the unearned income facts CCCAP summed. On OQ-873's reading (a receipt J does not exclude is counted) it would be counted; on the reading that I is a closed list it would not. The counting table (cccap_receipt_treatment_table, CO-5720) keeps the reading before receipt records, as not_counted_open_question, until the owner decides, as LEAP (OQ-885) and Adult Financial (OQ-888) do.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: Money a sponsor pays the non-citizen is not counted (the reading before receipt records), pending this answer.

Checked 2026-11-23: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.I and J (D-87, version effective 2026-08-14). Neither names sponsor support. Items: CO-5720.

### OQ-993. SUN Bucks: whether a Colorado Works diversion or supportive service payment is "assistance under TANF"

Body: 7 CFR 292.2 (S792) makes a child categorically eligible for Summer EBT when "one or more children in that household are receiving assistance under SNAP, TANF, or FDPIR". Colorado Works pays basic cash assistance, diversion payments and supportive service payments from TANF funds. 9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.8.A (S762) says a diversion addresses "a specific crisis situation or episode of need", is "not designed to meet clients' basic ongoing needs", is a "non-recurring cash payment", and that its receipt "does not count toward their Federal TANF assistance time limit", which suggests it is not TANF "assistance"; neither 292.2 nor 292.12(d) defines "assistance" or says whether the federal definition (45 CFR 260.31, not on file) applies. SUN Bucks read only tanf_cash_income (basic cash assistance) before receipt records, and its table of receipts (sebt_receipt_treatment_table, CO-5730) keeps that reading.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner, School meals (CDE) co-approver
Assumption: Only Colorado Works basic cash assistance is assistance under TANF for 292.2; a diversion or supportive service payment does not make a child categorically eligible.

Checked 2026-11-23: 7 CFR 292.2, 292.6, 292.12(d) (D-88); 9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.8.A (D-81). None defines TANF "assistance" for Summer EBT. Items: CO-5730, CO-5731.

Re-checked 2026-11-23 (coverage pass 4 S4-1, docs/reviews/coverage4-care-energy.md): the earlier line said 45 CFR 260.31 was not on file; it is, as D-60 (an eCFR fetch), excerpted as S4603. 260.31(b)(1) excludes from TANF "assistance" "Nonrecurrent, short-term benefits that ... Are designed to deal with a specific crisis situation or episode of need; ... Are not intended to meet recurrent or ongoing needs; and ... Will not extend beyond four months", which is a Colorado Works diversion as 3.606.8.A describes it and supports the assumption for diversions. It does not settle the question, for two reasons. First, 260.30 says its definitions "apply under parts 260 through 265 of this chapter" (45 CFR, the TANF regulations), and 7 CFR 292.2 neither adopts them nor refers to 45 CFR 260.31, so the definition does not reach Summer EBT by its own terms. Second, read into 292.2 it would not give the assumption whole: 260.31(a)(3) counts as assistance "supportive services such as transportation and child care provided to families who are not employed", so a Colorado Works supportive service payment to a family with no one employed would be TANF assistance where the assumption says it is not. The question stays a choice for the owners: adopting 260.31 for 292.2 (diversions excluded, supportive services to families not employed included) or the assumption above (basic cash assistance only).

### OQ-995. Partly resolved: a savings withdrawal and cash value taken are resources that changed form; open: whether a Medicare premium refund is income

Body: Receipt records, migration phase 3 gives three receipts that were parts of other receipts kinds of their own: savings_account_distribution (a withdrawal from an IRA, 401(k), health, education or development account the person owns), medicare_premium_refund (a lump-sum refund of Part B premiums) and insurance_cash_value (cash value taken out of a life or disability insurance policy). 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.F (S3001) counts unearned income and F.6 lists what is exempt; neither names these three. 20 CFR 416.1103(c) (S4042) says "Receipts from the sale, exchange, or replacement of a resource are not income but are resources that have changed their form", which may reach a withdrawal from an account the person owns and cash value taken from a policy; 416.1103 names no premium refund, and (a)(6) makes only the direct payment of medical insurance premiums by someone else not income. The ledger reads money obtained by converting a resource (resource_liquidation) as counted because no rule on file excludes it (OQ-971). Before receipt records the three were counted as part of the Social Security, pension or other non-taxable income that held them; the counting table (ma_ssi_receipt_treatment_table, CO-5510) keeps that reading, counted because no rule on file excludes them, until the owner decides.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: A Medicare Part B premium refund is counted as SSI-method unearned income in the month received (the reading before receipt records), pending this answer.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.F.5-6 (D-65); 20 CFR 416.1103 (D-207). Neither names a savings account withdrawal, a premium refund or cash value taken. Items: CO-5510.

Resolved in part (coverage pass 4, non-MAGI, N4-2, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/coverage4-medicaid-nonmagi.md): a withdrawal from an account the person owns (savings_account_distribution) and cash value the person takes out of a policy the person owns (insurance_cash_value) are the conversion of a resource the person holds (the account, 8.100.5.N.2; the policy's cash surrender value, M.2.f-g), which 8.100.5.P.1 (S4752, "The net proceeds from the sale of exempt or non-exempt resources are considered available resources") and 20 CFR 416.1103(c) (S4042, "Receipts from the sale, exchange, or replacement of a resource are not income but are resources that have changed their form") settle: not income; what is kept is a resource from the month after (P.1-3). The counting table (CO-5510) reads both as not_income, with resource_liquidation. Still open, as a state policy choice: the Medicare premium refund, which neither 416.1103 nor F.6 names (416.1103(a)(6) makes only another's direct payment of the premiums not income).

### OQ-996. Resolved: SNAP: a payment for meals only or for a room only is a boarder's or a roomer's self-employment income

Body: Receipt records, migration phase 3 gives board_only_payment (payments from persons who take regular meals in the home without lodging) and room_only_payment (payments from lodgers for a room only) kinds of their own; before, both were stated as part of the other taxable unearned income. 10 CCR 2506-1 4.403.2 (S2963) treats "payments from a boarder" as self-employment income less the cost of doing business, added to earned income, and the ledger reads a boarder as a person paying for room and meals (room_and_board_payments_received, CO-4161; snap_boarder_income, CO-4165). The text does not say whether a payment for meals only, or for a room only, is a boarder's payment under 4.403.2 or unearned income. SNAP's counting table (snap_receipt_treatment_table, CO-5010) keeps the reading before receipt records: both counted as unearned income because no rule excludes them.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: A payment for meals only or for a room only is counted as unearned income (the reading before receipt records), pending this answer.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.403.2 (D-82); 7 CFR 273.1(c)(3)(ii), (c)(5)-(6) (S1815, D-7), which say who boarders and roomers are, not how a meals-only or room-only payment is counted. Items: CO-5010.
