Resolution (coverage pass 4 for SNAP, 2026-11-23, finding S4-9 and the class check S4-16 of docs/reviews/coverage4-snap.md): the text on file settles it, so the question was not a state policy choice. 7 CFR 273.1(c)(3)(ii) (S1815, D-7): "All other individuals or groups of individuals paying a reasonable amount for meals or meals and lodging must be considered boarders", so a person paying for meals only is a boarder; 273.1(c)(5) (S1815): roomers are "Individuals to whom a household furnishes lodging for compensation, but not meals"; and 273.9(b)(1)(ii) (S4728, D-13), the sentence the Checked line above missed: "Payments from a roomer or boarder, except foster care boarders, shall also be considered self-employment income." Both payments are earned income (self-employment), less the cost of doing business: for boarders, meals only included, the greater of the maximum allotment for the number of boarders and the documented costs (10 CCR 2506-1 4.403.2.B, S2963); for a roomer, the documented costs of providing the room (273.11(b)(1); 4.403.11.B, S4729). SNAP reads both from the boarder records (earnings, boarder_payments with provides board_only or room_only; snap_boarder_income, CO-4165; snap_room_and_board_payments, CO-5117), and its receipt rows for board_only_payment and room_only_payment are read_from_earnings (CO-5010). Worked examples (stored households CO-750, CO-751): a roomer paying $600 adds $600 of earned income, less the 20% deduction; a meals-only boarder paying $400 adds $400 - $306 (the one-person maximum allotment, FY 2027) = $94. Items: CO-5010, CO-5101, CO-4165, CO-5117, CO-5118, CO-5110.

### OQ-1020. Earnings: whether special pay, and payments for a room only or meals only, are earnings in every program

Body: Earned-income records, step E0 (docs/design-earned-and-resource-records.md 2.3, decision D2) draws the boundary between the two lists of records: earnings (CO-5100) are money or in-kind value paid to the person for work the person performed, as an employee or in self-employment; every other payment is a receipt (CO-5000). Three receipt kinds fall on the earnings side: uniformed_services_special_pay (pay for service; with combat_zone, also the combat-zone pay stated today as part of wages, CO-4310), board_only_payment and room_only_payment (boarder income; room and board is already a statement of its own, CO-4161). The texts on file treat them as earned in some programs and are silent in others: Colorado Works counts board, room, and room and board payments as earned income (9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.2.E.4.c-e, S3159), Medicaid counts a board payment as earned income (10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.3.K.10.c), SNAP treats a boarder's payments as self-employment income (10 CCR 2506-1 4.403.2, S2963; OQ-996 for meals only and room only), CCCAP counts "hazard duty pay" (8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.I.1) and boarders and lodgers (I.18, S3256) as income, and LEAP, Adult Financial and the SSI method name none of them as earned or unearned. Until each program's E1 track reads its earnings table, the three kinds stay in receipts, with each program's receipt row unchanged, and a household that states one states it in both lists, both written by the translator from the receipt (the transition rule); the earnings row of each program carries the receipt row's reading. E2 retires the three receipt kinds.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Energy assistance (LEAP) policy owner, Cash programs policy owner, Child care policy owner (CDEC), Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The three kinds are earnings in every program, each program counting them as its receipt row counts them today until its owner signs its earnings row.

SNAP's half settled by the text (coverage pass 4 for SNAP, 2026-11-23, S4-9, S4-16): special pay is pay for service (7 CFR 273.9(b)(1)(i), S41), read from earnings since step E1; a payment for meals only or a room only is a boarder's or a roomer's self-employment income (7 CFR 273.1(c)(3)(ii), (c)(5), S1815; 273.9(b)(1)(ii), S4728), read from the boarder records (OQ-996, resolved). SNAP's receipt rows for the three kinds are read_from_earnings; the question stays open for the other programs only.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.2.E.4 (D-81); 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.3.K.10 (D-65); 10 CCR 2506-1 4.403.2 (D-82); 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.I (D-87); 9 CCR 2503-7 3.752.21-23 (D-86); 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.78 (D-80). Items: CO-5100, CO-5000, CO-5001, CO-5010, CO-5016, CO-5650, CO-5720, CO-5730, CO-5500, CO-5510, CO-5101, CO-5102, CO-5103, CO-5104, CO-5105, CO-5106, CO-5107.

### OQ-1021. Resolved: SNAP counts wages an employer pays to a third party for the household

Body: The earnings kind employer_vendor_payment (earnings, CO-5100) states money an employer pays to a third party for an expense of the person's household, as part of the person's compensation, from funds the employer would otherwise pay the person (employer_vendor_payments_received, CO-4455, today). 7 CFR 273.9(c)(1)(vii)(B) (S4101) counts such a payment: "the wages are garnished or diverted by the employer and paid to a third party for a household expense, such as rent. This vendor payment is counted as income", while a payment "in addition to paying the household its regular wages" is excluded. No SNAP item reads CO-4455, so SNAP counts none of it. The earnings table (snap_earnings_treatment_table, CO-5101) keeps that reading (not_counted_text_counts) so step E0 changes no answer; the SNAP E1 track decides, as a cited difference, whether the row is counted. If the text applies as written, today's reading is a wrong answer (a P1 candidate): a household whose employer pays part of its wages to the landlord has that part left out of SNAP income.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: Not counted (today's reading) until the SNAP E1 track rewires SNAP's earned income.

Checked 2026-11-23: 7 CFR 273.9(c)(1) (D-13); 10 CCR 2506-1 4.405 (D-82), which lists no vendor-payment exclusion of its own. Items: CO-5101, CO-4455.

### OQ-1022. Resolved: Colorado Works: wages from WIOA on-the-job training are exempt

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.4.B.16 (S2606) exempts "Income received through the Workforce Innovation and Opportunity Act, including supportive services through that program", and B.13 training allowances. Today WIOA on-the-job training wages are stated as part of wages_gross (wioa_on_the_job_training_wages, CO-4301, read only by SNAP) and Colorado Works counts them (cw_gross_earned_income, CO-1716, subtracts work-study, subsidized and short-term wages only). Whether wages an employer pays for WIOA on-the-job training are "income received through" WIOA is not settled by the text; subsidized employment, which 3.605.2.C (S2604) does not consider, may cover them. The earnings table (cw_earnings_treatment_table, CO-5104) keeps today's reading (counted_text_excludes) so step E0 changes no answer.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: Counted as earned income (today's reading) until the owner decides.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.2.C, 3.605.4.B.13-16 (D-81). Items: CO-5104, CO-2340.

### OQ-1023. Resolved: Medicaid SSI method: in-kind pay, boarder payments, and WIA wages of a dependent child

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.3.K (S4107), the general income rules the SSI-related method relies on, says three things the ledger does not read today: earned income is "payment in cash or in kind for services performed" and earned in-kind income is compensation "in shelter or other items in lieu of wages" (K.3-4), but no SSI-method item reads in_kind_compensation_value (CO-4506); board, room, and room and board payments are earned income above the one-person food stamp allotment per boarder and the documented costs (K.10.c-e), but no SSI-method item reads room_and_board_payments_received (CO-4161) or the payments for a room or meals only; and WIA wages paid to a dependent child applying for or receiving Medical Assistance are exempt for up to six months a calendar year (K.8.c), but the SSI method counts every WIOA on-the-job training wage as part of wages_gross. The earnings table (ma_ssi_earnings_treatment_table, CO-5107) keeps today's readings (not_counted_text_counts, counted_text_excludes) so step E0 changes no answer; the Medicaid E1 track decides each as a cited difference. If K.3-4 and K.10 apply to the SSI method as written, today's reading is a wrong answer (a P1 candidate) for an SSI-method household with in-kind pay or boarders.

Resolved (earned-income records, Medicaid and CHP+ track E1, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/records-earnings-medicaid.md): the text settles each part, and the Medicaid E1 track fixed the three rows as cited differences. 8.100.5.F.1 (S3001) sends the ABD programs to 8.100.3 for general income types, and 8.100.3.K is written for Medical Assistance as a whole. (1) Pay in kind: K.3-4 (S4107) and 20 CFR 416.1110(a)(3) (S4342) make it earned income; the SSI table counts in_kind_pay at its stated value (row counted). (2) Boarders: K.10.c-e count board, room, and room and board payments as earned income to the extent they exceed the one-person food stamp allotment per boarder (meals, room and board) and the documentable costs; ma_ssi_boarder_earned_income (CO-5177) reads each record's boarders and provides and the boarder provision costs (rows counted_above_boarder_allowance, deducted_from_boarder_payments), foster care payments exempt (F.6.l). Before, the SSI method counted payments for a room or meals only in full as unearned income and room and board payments not at all. (3) WIOA wages: K.8.c exempts them for a dependent child (8.100.1, S4341) applying for or receiving Medical Assistance for up to six months a calendar year (ma_ssi_excluded_wioa_wages, CO-5179; row exempt_dependent_child_six_months); K.8.a counts a caretaker relative's. Two narrower points the text leaves open are recorded apart: a domestic or agricultural worker's in-kind pay (OQ-1120). Items: CO-5107, CO-5170, CO-5171, CO-5177, CO-5178, CO-5179, CO-2471.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: Today's readings, until the Medicaid E1 track rewires the SSI method's earned income.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.3.K.3-4, K.8, K.10 and 8.100.5.F.4 (D-65). Items: CO-5107.

### OQ-1024. Partly resolved: the Medicaid MAGI half is settled (26 U.S.C. 112); open: Adult Financial, combat-zone pay stated as part of wages

Body: The earnings kind uniformed_services_special_pay (CO-5100) states special pay from a uniformed service for hazardous or combat duty, which today is stated two ways: as a receipt of that kind (37 U.S.C. 310 pay), and as combat_zone_pay_received (CO-4310), part of wages_gross, which only SNAP reads (4.405.2.A.12 excludes combat pay). Adult Financial's receipt table excludes the 310 pay (3.520.786, 20 CFR 416.1124(c)(19)) and Medicaid's MAGI receipt table does not count it (not_taxable_income), but both count combat-zone pay stated inside wages_gross. Each earnings table carries the receipt row's reading (excluded, not_taxable_income); the Adult Financial and Medicaid E1 tracks confirm it against the text, or keep the wages reading for pay that is not 310 special pay. No stored household or rule test states either.

Resolved for Medicaid MAGI (earned-income records, Medicaid and CHP+ track E1, 2026-11-23): 26 U.S.C. 112 (S4340, D-4340) leaves out of gross income the compensation for active service of an enlisted member for any month served in a combat zone (a), and of an officer up to the maximum enlisted amount, which includes the officer's own 37 U.S.C. 310 special pay (b), (c)(5)(B); MAGI follows the federal tax figure (42 CFR 435.603(e), S165). The MAGI earnings row for uniformed_services_special_pay is excluded_in_combat_zone: special pay with combat_zone is not counted, and special pay outside a combat zone is compensation for services, counted (8.100.4.C.1.a.v, S166). The Medicaid receipt row for the kind is in_earnings (no longer read there). Wages paid for a month served in a combat zone, which 112 also excludes, are OQ-1123 (the earnings record has no field for them). The Adult Financial half stays with the cash programs owner. Items: CO-5106, CO-5160.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner, Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The earnings row carries the receipt row's reading.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.786 (D-80); 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.4.C (D-65); 10 CCR 2506-1 4.405.2.A.12 (D-82). Items: CO-5103, CO-5106, CO-4310.

### OQ-1050. Medicaid spousal impoverishment: whether a community spouse's or family member's VA Aid and Attendance is income for the monthly income and family allowances

Body: The community spouse monthly income allowance is the amount by which the community spouse's minimum monthly needs exceed "his/her income from sources other than the institutionalized spouse", calculated "by using the gross income less mandatory deductions for FICA and Medicare tax" (10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.7.R.2, S1756); the family allowance is reduced "by the monthly income of that family member" (8.100.7.T.1.c, S1736). Both are deducted after the institutionalized spouse is determined eligible (42 U.S.C. 1396r-5(d)(1)-(2), S4201: "After an institutionalized spouse is determined or redetermined to be eligible ..."; "the amount of monthly income otherwise available to the community spouse"). 8.100.5.K (S2900) says the Aid and Attendance and Unusual Medical Expenses portion "shall not be considered as income when determining eligibility" and then speaks of post-eligibility only for the institutionalized veteran's patient payment (K.1-2). Two readings: (A) K's exclusion is for eligibility only, so in this post-eligibility figure the community spouse's or family member's portion is gross income and is counted; (B) the portion is not income at all: 8.100.5.F.5.j.vi (S3001), the income section for the ABD and long-term care programs, lists only "Veterans benefits other than Aid and Attendance (A&A) and Unusual Medical Expenses (UME)" as unearned income, and 20 CFR 416.1103(b)(1) (S4200) gives "Cash given you by the Department of Veterans Affairs to purchase aid and attendance" as not income; neither is limited to eligibility. Worked example: a community spouse with $1,500 Social Security and a $400 Aid and Attendance portion, minimum monthly needs of $2,100 (CO-655-T2), the institutionalized spouse's income sufficient: reading A gives income $1,900 and a monthly income allowance of $200; reading B gives income $1,500 and an allowance of $600. A dependent family member living with the community spouse (a dependent parent who is a veteran's surviving spouse) with a $400 portion and no other income: under A the family allowance is one third of the MMMNA standard less $400; under B, the full third. The ledger follows B (it did before receipt records too, since va_aid_and_attendance_amount was not among the facts the spousal sums read).

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: Reading B: VA Aid and Attendance and Unusual Medical Expenses are left out of the community spouse's and the family members' income, as for eligibility.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.F.5, 8.100.5.K, 8.100.7.R.2, 8.100.7.T.1 (D-65); 42 U.S.C. 1396r-5(b)(2), (d)(1)-(2) (D-47); 20 CFR 416.1103 (D-207). None says how the community spouse's Aid and Attendance is treated in the allowances; no CMS or HCPF guidance on file addresses it. Items: CO-5531 (read by CO-2467 and CO-657).

### OQ-1051. Colorado Works: what "the applicant's or recipient's own home" covers in the VA Aid and Attendance exemption

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.4.A.6 (S2605): "VA Aid and Attendance is exempt income to the applicant or recipient to determine eligibility for public assistance in the applicant's or recipient's own home, if the VA Aid and Attendance is used for medical supplies and medical or attendant care not covered by Medicare or Medicaid, or other health insurance programs. The remainder is deducted from the assistance grant." The rule does not define "own home". The ledger states it as a neutral fact, lives_in_own_home (CO-5940): a dwelling where the person lives as a member of the household that owns or rents it, the person's own household or a family the person lives with as one of its members; not an institution or care facility, not another household's home as a guest, boarder or lodger, and not a shelter, a temporary stay or no fixed dwelling. Two cases the text leaves open: (1) a veteran parent who lives with the children as members of the grandparents' household in the grandparents' house: read as own home (the exemption applies); the other reading (only a home the person or the person's spouse owns or rents) would count the whole portion; (2) a veteran staying as a guest in another household's home or in a shelter: read as not own home (the whole portion is counted); the other reading (any private dwelling as opposed to a facility, residence_setting_type private_dwelling) would exempt the part used for uncovered care. Worked example: $400 Aid and Attendance with $150 spent on attendant care no insurance covers, beside $700 Social Security: counted unearned income $950 where the exemption applies, $1,100 where it does not (CO-5651-T10, T11).

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: The meaning of the own-home fact above: a member of the household whose home it is lives in the person's own home; a guest, a boarder, a resident of a facility and a person in a shelter or with no fixed dwelling do not.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.4.A.6 (D-81, rule version 12030, the text matches S2605); a search of the whole of 9 CCR 2503-6 (D-81) finds "own home" only in A.6, and C.R.S. 26-2-703 (D-59, S401) defines "assistance unit" and "dependent child" but not a home. Items: CO-5940, CO-5651.

### OQ-1060. Medicaid long-term care: the transfer-penalty divisor and the nursing facility personal needs allowance (S1228) are not checked against HCPF OM 25-073

Body: Excerpt S1228 states the 2025 and 2026 transfer-penalty divisor ($10,475.11 and $10,814.64) and nursing facility personal needs allowance ($108.73 and $110.36). It is not a quotation: the product owner supplied the figures on 2026-11-21 from two summaries of HCPF Operational Memo OM 25-073 (D-125). The verification pass of 2026-11-23 tried to fetch the memo and every other allowed source that could state the figures. hcpf.colorado.gov refused the memo itself, the 2025 memo OM 24-054 and the 2025 memo series index (HTTP 403 from the site's CloudFront, on a direct request and through a page fetcher). www.medicaid.gov answered, but CMS's 2026 SSI and spousal impoverishment standards (cib04272026.pdf, D-72) chart only the federal standards (SSI, MMMNA, community spouse resource standards, home equity) and state no Colorado personal needs allowance or private-pay rate. www.sos.state.co.us answered, but the current 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100 (D-65) defines the divisor (§ 8.100.7.F.2.c.i, S648) and the indexed base allowance from $75 in 2015 (§ 8.100.7.V.3.d.i.2, S361) without stating the year's figures. www.ecfr.gov, www.law.cornell.edu, www.govinfo.gov and www.federalregister.gov answered; federal law sets only the method (42 U.S.C. 1396p(c)(1)(E)(i)(II), fetched from law.cornell.edu on 2026-11-23: "the average monthly cost to a private patient of nursing facility services in the State ... at the time of application") and no Federal Register notice publishes a State's figures. The figures cannot be derived either: the PNA is indexed to the statewide nursing facility per diem rate, which no allowed host publishes. No figure was changed. This narrows OQ-212 to the one named document.

Class: blocked on a named document
Owner: LTSS policy owner
Assumption: The 2025 and 2026 transfer penalty divisor and nursing facility personal needs allowance are the figures the product owner supplied from the operational memo, unverified; every rule resting on them stays Draft.
Blocked on: HCPF Operational Memo OM 25-073, "2026 Social Security Cost of Living Adjustments" (hcpf.colorado.gov refused, HTTP 403 on 2026-11-23); for the 2025 figures also OM 24-054 (refused the same day)

Checked 2026-11-23: D-65 (§ 8.100.7.F.2.c.i, V.3.d.i), D-72 (CMS 2026 standards). Items: CO-832, CO-887, CO-1987, CO-649, CO-659, CO-658, CO-3616, CO-1977, CO-651.

### OQ-1061. Medicaid long-term care: the income-trust regional private-pay rates (S1230) are not checked against HCPF OM 25-073

Body: Excerpt S1230 states the 2025 and 2026 regional average private-pay rates that cap an income-trust user's gross income under § 8.100.7.G.6.a.i.a) (S1229): Region I $11,522.44 (2025 $11,556.20), Region II $10,633.36 ($10,397.93), Region III $9,780.17 ($9,290.77), Region IV $11,322.60 ($10,655.56). It is not a quotation: the product owner supplied the figures on 2026-11-21 from two summaries of HCPF Operational Memo OM 25-073 (D-125). The verification pass of 2026-11-23 tried the same hosts as OQ-1060: hcpf.colorado.gov refused (HTTP 403); www.medicaid.gov (CMS's 2026 standards, D-72), www.sos.state.co.us (the current § 8.100.7.G, D-65, which says only that HCPF "shall calculate the average rates for such regions on an annual, calendar-year basis"), www.ecfr.gov, www.law.cornell.edu, www.govinfo.gov and www.federalregister.gov answered, and none of them states a regional rate. The county lists in S1230 match the rule text (S1229); only the dollar figures are unverified. No figure was changed. This narrows OQ-99 to the one named document.

Class: blocked on a named document
Owner: LTSS policy owner
Assumption: The 2025 and 2026 regional income-trust ceilings are the figures the product owner supplied from the operational memo, unverified; every rule resting on them stays Draft.
Blocked on: HCPF Operational Memo OM 25-073, "2026 Social Security Cost of Living Adjustments" (hcpf.colorado.gov refused, HTTP 403 on 2026-11-23)

Checked 2026-11-23: D-65 (§ 8.100.7.G.6.a.i.a)), D-72. Items: CO-3022, CO-1985, CO-642, CO-651, CO-3310.

### OQ-1160. LEAP: earned pay of a frequency 3.752.22.C does not convert, and pay received once

Body: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.752.22.B (S2713) counts earned ongoing income as "the countable gross income in any four (4) weeks of the eight (8) weeks prior to the application date", and C converts the pay of a household member "paid less than monthly": four weekly, two bi-weekly or two semi-monthly amounts. C.3 reads "actual income received in the income verification period" for a job that began or ended. The text does not say how pay made less often than monthly (every other month, quarterly, twice a year, annually) or pay received once (a bonus, an odd job) is counted, and 3.752.23 excludes no irregular earned income. With earnings records (CO-5100) each such pay is a record of its own, so the question can be answered by date. The volume reads (1) a pay of one of those frequencies stated as a stream (no date) at the amount the record states for the month determined, as a program whose text gives no conversion counts the amount received in the month (design 2.4); (2) one stated with its date at its amount when received in the income verification period, as 3.752.22.A counts income received once; (3) an odd job stated beside a converted pay schedule as its own record, counted in its month (CO-194). The other readings: half of what was received in the eight weeks (four weeks of the eight); the pay averaged over its period (a quarterly pay divided by three); or not counted as ongoing income at all. Items: CO-5120, CO-5121, CO-5122, CO-3925.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Energy assistance (LEAP) policy owner
Assumption: A stream of such pay counts at its stated monthly amount; a dated pay of that kind, or one paid once, counts when received in the income verification period; an odd job beside regular pay counts in its month.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.751.1, 3.752.21-23 (D-86, rule version 12221, re-fetched from the Secretary of State on 2026-11-23, unchanged: "any four (4) weeks of the eight (8) weeks prior to the application date"; C.1-2 name weekly, bi-weekly and semi-monthly pay only) and the amendments effective 11/01/2026 (D-173), which do not change 3.752.22. No paragraph settles it.

### OQ-1162. LEAP: payments from boarders, at their gross or less the cost of the room and meals

Body: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.752.21 (S4100) counts as unearned income "payments from any other source, which is considered to be a gain or benefit to the applicant or recipient", and no paragraph of 3.752.23 excludes a boarder's payment; before earnings records LEAP counted a lodger's payment for a room only or meals only (receipts counted because no rule excludes them) and not a payment for room and board. leap_boarder_payments (CO-5124) now counts all three, at the gross payment. Whether the "gain or benefit" is the gross payment or the payment less what the room and meals cost the household (as other programs deduct a boarder's costs: Colorado Works 3.605.2.E.4, SNAP 4.403.F) the LEAP text does not say; business_cost_boarder_provision is not deducted (a row for the owner's signature in leap_earnings_treatment_table, CO-5102). Items: CO-5124, CO-5102, CO-3926.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Energy assistance (LEAP) policy owner
Assumption: The gross payment counts; the cost of providing the room or meals is not deducted.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.752.21, 3.752.211, 3.752.23 (D-86, re-fetched 2026-11-23); none names boarders, lodgers or a room-and-board cost.

### OQ-1163. CCCAP Child Welfare Child Care: whether "from birth to the age of thirteen" reaches a child of thirteen

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.103.UU (S2853) defines an eligible child as "a child, from birth to the age of thirteen (13) years", and 3.111.F (S482) "from birth to the age thirteen (13) years"; neither says whether a child of thirteen is included (T-9 hand check A-8, docs/reviews/t9-threshold-handcheck.md). For Low-Income and Colorado Works child care, paid from the Child Care and Development Fund, 45 CFR 98.20(a)(1)(i) (S4420) settles it: a child must "Be under 13 years of age", so cccap_is_child_of_eligible_age_needing_care (CO-4056) reads "under thirteen" (CO-4056-T7). Child Welfare Child Care "is not funded through the Child Care Development Fund" (3.120.F, S2853), so the federal limit does not bind it, and its eligible child rests on the state words alone. The volume reads them the same way for it. Items: CO-4056, CO-989.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: A child is an eligible child by age until the thirteenth birthday, in Child Welfare Child Care as in the CCDF-funded programs.

Checked 2026-11-23: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.103.UU, 3.111.F, 3.120.F (D-87, rule version 12658, re-fetched from the Secretary of State on 2026-11-23, unchanged) and 45 CFR 98.20(a)(1) (D-442, eCFR as of 2026-11-19). No state text names the thirteenth birthday.

### OQ-1100. SNAP expedited service: whether a household is still in its postponement period on the thirtieth day after application

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.205.11.B.2.b (S835; the current text, D-82, rule version 12718, reads the same) says that "when the postponed verification is not completed within thirty (30) calendar days from the date of application, the local office shall terminate the household's participation on the thirtieth (30th) calendar day without providing a notice of adverse action". "Within thirty calendar days from the date of application" can include the thirtieth day after it, so that verification completed that day is in time; but participation is terminated "on the thirtieth calendar day", so on that day the household may already be out of its postponement. The text does not say whether the thirtieth day is the application date plus 29 days or plus 30, nor whether the household is still postponed on it. snap_expedited_postponement_end_date (CO-1393) is the application date plus snap_expedited_postponed_verification_days (30), and snap_verification_status (CO-1394) reads a determination date on or before it as postponed_expedited; rule test CO-1394-T14 (the T-9 boundary, A-5 of docs/reviews/t9-threshold-handcheck.md) fixes that reading at equality. 7 CFR 273.2(i)(4)(iii)(A)-(B) (S1310) postpones verification to the second or third month of participation and names no day.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: The postponement runs through the application date plus thirty days, inclusive: a household that completes the postponed verification on that day is in time, and its verification is still postponed on that day.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.205.11.B.2.b (D-82, rule version 12718; S835 from D-103); 7 CFR 273.2(i)(4)(iii)(A)-(B) (S1310); OQ-323 (thirty days rather than the federal months; it does not settle the day). Items: CO-1393, CO-1394.

### OQ-1101. SNAP self-employment: whether a business that does not say it is the household's annual support, or that its business has changed, is averaged

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.403.12 (S3114) averages "Self-employment income which represents a household's annual support" over twelve months (A), averages income "intended to meet the household's needs for only part of the year" over the period it covers (B), and turns to anticipated earnings when the household "has experienced a substantial increase or decrease in business" (A, C). Before earned-income records, snap_self_employment_is_averaged (CO-4329) read self_employment_is_annual_support and self_employment_has_changed_substantially as no when not stated. The earnings records (CO-5100) declare both fields required where read (design decision D3: no rule text gives them a value), so from step E1 a business record that does not state annual_support makes SNAP's self-employment income cannot determine, and one that is annual support must state changed_substantially. Six stored households of other programs (CO-136, CO-182, CO-322, CO-382, CO-383, CO-387) and the generated business households are now cannot determine for SNAP (harness class SE-D4-1); the one SNAP household with a business (CO-447) states that its farm is not annual support. Whether 4.403.12.A's "shall be averaged ... If the averaged amount does not accurately reflect ... the local office shall calculate ... on the basis of anticipated earnings" gives changed_substantially a default of no (averaging the rule, a substantial change the exception) is a reading for the owner; a default would be declared on the field by the integration owner (CO-5100), not by SNAP's reader.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: Both are required where read, as the earnings records declare them: a business that does not say whether it is the household's annual support, or an annual-support business that does not say whether it has changed substantially, cannot be determined.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.403.11-4.403.12 (D-82, S1152, S3114); 4.402.2.A.1 (S3113); 7 CFR 273.11(a)(1) (D-15). Items: CO-4329, CO-4349, CO-1702, CO-3722, CO-5100.

### OQ-1120. Medicaid SSI method: a domestic or agricultural worker's pay in kind

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.3.K.3-4 (S4107) makes pay in kind for services earned income for Medical Assistance, and the SSI method now counts in_kind_pay as earned income (ma_ssi_earnings_treatment_table, CO-5107, OQ-1023 resolved). 20 CFR 416.1110(a)(3) (S4342), which the SSI method follows elsewhere, says "if you are a domestic or agricultural worker, the law requires us to treat your in-kind pay as unearned income". The difference matters: earned income gets the $65 and one-half disregard (8.100.5.H.4.b), unearned income only the $20. Worked example: a farm worker given housing worth $500 a month in place of wages, no other income: as earned income (500 - 20 - 65) / 2 = 207.50 countable; as unearned income 500 - 20 = 480. Colorado's text does not carve out domestic or agricultural workers, and the earnings record does not say whether the person is one.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: Pay in kind is earned income for every worker, as 8.100.3.K.3-4 says.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.3.K.3-4, 8.100.5.F.1, F.4 (D-65); 20 CFR 416.1110(a)(3) (D-53). Items: CO-5107, CO-5170.

### OQ-1121. Resolved: Medicaid MAGI counts boarder payments net of the documentable costs of providing the room or meals

Body: MAGI counts payments from boarders as rents (10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.4.C.1.b.ii, "Rents") and, for meals, compensation for services (a.v) (S166), at their gross amount, as the MAGI receipt table counts rents (rental_income, CO-5500): ma_magi_earnings_treatment_table (CO-5106) row counted, and the boarder provision cost row not_deducted_open_question. MAGI follows the federal tax figure (42 CFR 435.603(e), S165), where rent and boarders' payments are reported net of the expenses of providing them; the gross reading overstates income by those expenses. Worked example: two boarders paying $900 a month for room and board, $250 a month of food and utilities for them: gross $900; net $650. Before E1, MAGI counted payments for a room only or meals only in full (as receipts) and room and board payments not at all.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: Gross, as rents are counted in the MAGI receipt table.

Checked 2026-11-23: 42 CFR 435.603(e) (D-34); 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.4.C.1.a-b (D-40). Neither says whether the costs of providing a room or meals are deducted; the Code's rules on rental expenses are not on file. Items: CO-5106, CO-5160.

Resolved (coverage pass 4, C4-03, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/coverage4-medicaid-magi-chp.md): the text on file, and the Code section fetched for this round, settle it; it was not a policy choice. 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.3.K.10.c-e (S4107, D-65), which applies to MAGI since 8.100.4 does not carve it out (K.7 and K.11.a.i.1 name MAGI where they do), counts board, room, and room and board payments as earned income "to the extent that the payment ... exceeds ... documentable expenses directly related to the provision" of them; and 26 U.S.C. 62(a)(4) (S4632, D-4431, fetched from Cornell LII 2026-11-23), which 8.100.4.C.1 incorporates, takes "The deductions ... which are attributable to property held for the production of rents" out of adjusted gross income. The food stamp allotment per boarder of K.10.c and e is an income disregard, which 42 U.S.C. 1396a(e)(14)(B) (S4630, D-26) bars for MAGI, so only the costs come off. ma_magi_boarder_earnings (CO-6450) counts the month's boarder payments less the month's business_cost_boarder_provision records, never below 0; CO-5106's rows are counted_net_of_provision_costs and deducted_from_boarder_payments. Worked example (B-BOARD, CO-502-T8): wages 1,100 + (900 - 250) = 1,750, 131.6% for one: adult, eligible (before: 2,000, 150.4%, ineligible). Items: CO-5106, CO-5160, CO-6450, CO-502.

### OQ-1122. Medicaid SSI method: an ineligible child's own income for the Ineligible Child Allocation

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.H.3.a.i (S3405) reduces the allocation "by any of the ineligible child's own income", and H.4.c says only that the one-third child support reduction does not apply. 20 CFR 416.1161(c) (D-53) reduces it by the child's income less what 416.1161(a) excludes (which includes the exclusions of 416.1112 and 416.1124 that apply to an ineligible parent or spouse) and, for a student, less the student earned income exclusion of 416.1112(c)(3). The ledger reads the child's gross income (ma_ssi_ineligible_child_allocation_generated, CO-6050), as before. Worked example: an ineligible child of 16, a student, earning $400 a month: Colorado's words give an allocation of 497 - 400 = 97; 416.1161(c) gives 497 (the student exclusion takes the $400).

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The child's gross income, as H.3.a.i reads.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.H.3.a.i, H.4.c (D-65); 20 CFR 416.1161(a), (c), 416.1163(b) (D-53). Items: CO-6050, CO-2277, CO-4633.

### OQ-1123. Resolved: Medicaid MAGI leaves out compensation for a month served in a combat zone, basic pay included (26 U.S.C. 112)

Body: 26 U.S.C. 112(a)-(b) (S4340) leaves out of gross income all compensation for active service for a month during any part of which the member served in a combat zone (an officer's up to the maximum enlisted amount), basic pay included, not only the special pay. MAGI follows the federal tax figure (42 CFR 435.603(e), S165). The earnings record states combat_zone only on uniformed services special pay (CO-5100), so a service member's basic pay for such a month is counted. Worked example: an enlisted member deployed to a designated combat zone in October, basic pay $2,800 and hostile fire pay $225: the federal tax figure counts neither; the ledger counts $2,800. Fixing it needs a field on the wages record (paid for a month served in a combat zone), a request to the integration owner (docs/reviews/records-earnings-medicaid.md, R-3).

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: Wages are counted; only special pay stated as paid in a combat zone is left out.

Checked 2026-11-23: 26 U.S.C. 112 (D-4340); 42 CFR 435.603(e) (D-34); CO-5100's record fields. Items: CO-5106, CO-5160.

Resolved (coverage pass 4, C4-02, 2026-11-23): 26 U.S.C. 112(a)-(b) (S4340, D-4340, on file) decides it, so it was not a policy choice; the block was a missing record field. The earnings record now has combat_zone_compensation (CO-5100; request R-3 of docs/reviews/records-earnings-medicaid.md): the pay is compensation for active service for a month during any part of which the member served in a combat zone (all of an enlisted member's; an officer states the part up to the maximum enlisted amount as its own record), a fact of the federal return, false unless shown (D3). ma_magi_wage_earnings (CO-5160) leaves it out of every pay it counts. Worked example (COMBAT-FAM, stored household CO-720): an enlisted member's basic pay of 3,800 for a combat-zone month and 225 of hostile fire pay are both out of gross income, so the spouse's household of four has MAGI 0 and the spouse is a parent or caretaker relative, eligible (before: 3,800, 138.2%, ineligible). Items: CO-5100, CO-5106, CO-5160.

### OQ-1124. Resolved: a former foster care youth in Colorado's foster care on the 21st birthday is covered (foster care ends at 21, 12 CCR 2509-3 7.203.41.A)

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.4.H.2.a (S1718) extends Medical Assistance to "individuals that were formerly in foster care ... on their 18th, 19th, 20th or up to their 21st birthday and were receiving Medical Assistance"; "up to" can stop before the 21st birthday. 42 CFR 435.150(b)(3) (S214, S4343) covers youth in foster care and enrolled in Medicaid "upon attaining: (i) Age 18; or (ii) A higher age at which the State's or such Tribe's foster care assistance ends under title IV-E of the Act", which reaches a youth in care on the birthday on which that assistance ends. Whether Colorado's title IV-E foster care assistance ends at 21 is not in any text on file. ma_is_former_foster_care_category (CO-549) reads a youth whose foster care ended at 21 as covered (CO-549-T95).

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: A youth in foster care on the 21st birthday is covered (the federal "upon attaining ... a higher age at which ... foster care assistance ends").

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.4.H.2.a (D-65); 42 CFR 435.150(b)(3) (D-31, eCFR re-read 2026-11-23). The age at which Colorado's title IV-E foster care assistance ends is in the Colorado Department of Human Services' rules, not on file. Items: CO-549.

Resolved (coverage pass 4, C4-12, 2026-11-23): the Colorado Department of Human Services' rules are served by the Secretary of State (an allowed host). Fetched: 12 CCR 2509-3 § 7.203.4-7.203.41 (D-4433, S4641, rule version 12365), the Foster Youth in Transition Program, Colorado's foster care after 18: "An eligible youth is an individual who: A. Is at least eighteen but less than twenty-one years of age or such greater age of foster care eligibility as required by federal law"; the closure rules list "The child reaches his/her 21st birthday"; and 12 CCR 2509-4 § 7.304.4.A (rule version 11000) continues placement services after 18 through that program. Colorado's foster care therefore ends when the youth attains 21, the "higher age at which the State's ... foster care assistance ends" of 42 CFR 435.150(b)(3)(ii) (S4343), and a youth in foster care and enrolled in Medicaid on the 21st birthday is a former foster care youth, as ma_is_former_foster_care_category (CO-549) already reads it (CO-549-T95); 8.100.4.H.2.a's "up to their 21st birthday" reaches the same youth. The rule does not name the funding (title IV-E or State), which 435.150(b)(3)(ii) names; the age is the same for the program either way. No item changes. Items: CO-549.

### OQ-1140. Adult Financial: combat-zone pay that is not 37 U.S.C. 310 special pay

Body: Earned-income records, step E1 Adult Financial, narrowing OQ-1024 for Adult Financial. 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.786.K (S2608) exempts the unearned income 20 CFR 416.1124 excludes, and 416.1124(c)(19) (S2803) is "Special pay received from one of the uniformed services pursuant to 37 U.S.C. 310". 37 U.S.C. 310 (fetched from law.cornell.edu on 2026-11-23) is special pay for duty subject to hostile fire or imminent danger, at most $225 a month; it does not reach every other pay a member receives because of a deployment to a combat zone. 3.520.782.A counts "gross monetary wages received for services performed as an employee", and no paragraph of 3.520.786 names other combat-zone pay. The earnings kind uniformed_services_special_pay (CO-5100) holds both: 37 U.S.C. 310 pay, and pay received because the member is deployed to a designated combat zone (the field combat_zone, which restates combat_zone_pay_received, CO-4310). The text settles the first: Adult Financial excludes a record of the kind not stated as combat-zone pay (af_earnings_treatment_table, CO-5103: excluded_except_combat_zone). For a record stated as combat-zone pay it cannot tell how much is 310 pay. Worked example: a member with $2,000 of basic pay and $500 of pay received for a combat-zone deployment, $225 of it hostile fire pay: counting the $500 gives gross earned income $2,500, excluding the 310 part gives $2,275, excluding the whole record gives $2,000. A field or kind that separates 310 pay inside combat-zone pay is a request to the integration owner (docs/reviews/records-earnings-cash.md).

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: Special pay stated as received because of a combat-zone deployment is counted as wages, as before earnings records; special pay not so stated is hostile fire or imminent danger pay and is exempt.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.782.A, 3.520.786 (D-80); 20 CFR 416.1124(c)(19) (D-207); 37 U.S.C. 310 (law.cornell.edu, not on file). No stored household or rule test outside CO-5130 states either. Items: CO-5103, CO-5130, CO-2342, CO-4012.

### OQ-1141. Colorado Works: subsidized employment wages when the client was terminated or quit through the client's own fault

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.2.C (S2604): "Income received from short-term employment such as temporary employment (ninety days or less) and subsidized employment shall not be considered to determine eligibility as long as the client has not been terminated or has terminated the employment due to a fault of their own." 3.605.4.B.35 (S2606) exempts "Wages earned through subsidized employment programs including employment, apprenticeships, on-the-job training, and transitional jobs" with no proviso. Read with C, a subsidized job the client lost or left through the client's own fault would have its wages considered; read with B.35, they are exempt whatever the reason. Worked example: a client with $500 of wages from a subsidized transitional job that ended in October through her own fault, and no other income: under C the $500 is earned income ($500 - $90 = $410 at application, against a need standard of $331 for one caretaker and one child: ineligible); under B.35 nothing is counted (eligible). The ledger has read B.35 since final cash review FC-2 (cw_earnings_treatment_table, CO-5104: subsidized_employment and wioa_on_the_job_training excluded) and reads C's proviso for a temporary job only (temporary_job, ended_through_own_fault).

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: Wages from subsidized employment are exempt whatever the reason the job ended, as 3.605.4.B.35 says; the own-fault proviso is applied to temporary jobs only.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.2.C, 3.605.4.B.35 (D-81, re-fetched from the Secretary of State 2026-11-23). Neither paragraph refers to the other. Items: CO-5104, CO-2340.

### OQ-1142. Colorado Works: which certification period a new source of earned income first impacts

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.2.A.2.a (S1000): "A new source of earned income shall receive a one hundred percent (100%) earned income disregard for the first certification period that the income impacts according to section 3.606.3." A certification period is six months from the first month the unit is found eligible (3.606.1.E); a new source must be reported by the tenth of the following month (3.606.3.B.2), and a change that decreases the grant does not take effect until redetermination unless it is a negative change of 3.606.3.C (3.606.3.A.2). So the period a new job "impacts" may be the current one (a negative change, e.g. income over the need standard) or the next one (a decrease held until redetermination). The earnings records state each job's start date (job_start_date, CO-5100), and the ledger reads a job begun on or after the application date as a new source (cw_new_source_earnings, CO-5141); no supplied fact states the certification periods or when a reported change takes effect, so whether a month is in the first certification period the new income impacts stays the county's statement (cw_is_new_earned_income_source, CO-1502). Worked example: a unit certified April to September 2026 whose mother starts a $400-a-month job on July 6 beside a continuing $600 job: if the job first impacts the October-March period, October counts 1,000 - (400 + 600 x 0.67) = $198; if it impacted the April-September period, October is after it and counts 1,000 - 1,000 x 0.67 = $330.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: The county states for each month whether it falls in the first certification period a new source impacts; the job's start date says which pay is the new source.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.1.E, 3.606.2.A, 3.606.3.A-C (D-81). None says, for a decrease held until redetermination, whether the period that "the income impacts" is the period the job began in or the next. Items: CO-922, CO-1502, CO-5141.

### OQ-1180. Medicaid: whether a person whose only earnings are a boarder's payments is employed for the buy-ins and Transitional Medical Assistance

Body: Three Medicaid tests turn on employment: the Working Adults Buy-In ("Individuals must be employed", 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.6.P.1.e, S317), the Children's Buy-In's exclusion of an employed child of 16 to 18 (8.100.6.Q.1.e, S318), and Transitional Medical Assistance ("At least one Parent/Caretaker Relative must continue to be employed", 8.100.4.I.1.b.i, S1719). They read ma_has_earned_income (CO-6200), which the records E1 integration made from the earnings records in place of has_earned_income (CO-1356): pay for services as an employee, in cash or in kind (8.100.3.K.3-4, S4107), or a business's gross receipts. 8.100.3.K.10.c-e (S4107) makes "board, room, and room and board payments" self-employment income, but none of the three paragraphs says whether taking in a boarder or lodger is being employed. Worked example: a disabled adult of 40 whose only income is $600 a month from a lodger for room and board: employed, the Working Adults Buy-In is open to her (income after the disregards within 450% of the poverty level); not employed, it is not.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: Not employed: a boarder's payments alone do not meet the employment tests, as the volume read them before earnings records (wages and business receipts counted, room and board payments did not).

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.3.K.3-4, K.10 (S4107), 8.100.6.P.1, Q.1 (S317, S318), 8.100.4.I.1.b.i (S1719). None defines "employed". Items: CO-6200, CO-618, CO-620, CO-2425.

### OQ-1181. Resolved: Earnings records: the frequency of a pay stated with its date but no frequency

Body: The earnings record (CO-5100) gives `frequency` the default monthly. Under decision D3 (docs/design-earned-and-resource-records.md, section 7) a field takes a default only where the rule's own text gives one; no rule says a pay is monthly unless stated. Where a rule converts dated pays by their schedule, a missing frequency matters: LEAP averages the pays of each schedule over the eight weeks before application and multiplies by the pays per month (3.752.22.B-C; leap_ongoing_dated_wages, CO-5121), so four weekly pays of $500 stated with their dates but no frequency are read as four monthly pays, $500 a month, where the weekly reading gives $2,000 (request R-6 of docs/reviews/records-earnings-care-energy.md). Making `frequency` required where a dated pay is read would make such a household cannot determine instead. No stored household states a dated pay without its frequency; nine rule tests do (CO-330-T7, CO-4930-T5, CO-5113-T9, CO-3915-T2, CO-3915-T10, CO-5152-T3, CO-5152-T5, CO-3912-T6, CO-3912-T8), each for a rule that reads the pay at its amount (CCCAP's thirty days, the pay of a job that ended, SNAP's pay after the application date), where the frequency is not read. A dated pay the earnings translator writes (SNAP's pay after the application date) states no frequency, and the translator notes each one.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Data steward, Energy assistance (LEAP) policy owner, SNAP policy owner
Assumption: A dated pay with no frequency is a monthly pay (the schema's default); a household that states dated weekly or bi-weekly pays states their frequency.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.752.22.B-C (D-86); 10 CCR 2506-1 4.402.A.2 (D-82). Neither says what schedule to assume for a pay whose schedule is not reported. Items: CO-5100, CO-5121.

Resolved (coverage pass 4, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/coverage4-care-energy.md L4-2 and section 4): the question was misclassified. It is not a choice the State's text leaves open but the codex's own data default, and product-owner decision 4 of 2026-11-23 with design decision D3 already answer it: a field takes a default only where the rule text gives one, and the body above records that none does for a dated pay. CO-5100's `frequency` keeps monthly for a stream (a record with no date, whose amount is by its meaning the month's) and is required on a dated record (`required_if_dated`, docs/operators.md "Receipt records"): a rule that reads the schedule of a dated pay that states none cannot determine, naming `earnings[<i> <kind>].frequency` (leap_ongoing_dated_wages CO-5121 and leap_scsep_wages CO-5122, LEAP 3.752.22.B-C; SNAP's dated-pay conversion, 10 CCR 2506-1 4.402.A.2). Household L2 of the review (four weekly $500 pays, dated, no frequency) is stored as CO-711 and expects cannot determine; CO-5121-T14 and T15 test it. The three SNAP rule tests that stated dated pays without a frequency (CO-330-T7, CO-4930-T5, CO-5113-T9) now state them monthly, two jobs' pays, with their answers unchanged; the other six named above read no frequency. The earnings translator writes SNAP's pay after the application date as the month's pay (frequency monthly), read at its amount as before. Class: no longer open; the assumption above is withdrawn.

### OQ-1230. Resolved: Medicaid MAGI counts the taxable part of a pension or annuity, and of an income tax refund, the record states

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.4.C.1 (S166) counts gross income "pursuant to 26 U.S.C. § 61" (S4631), and MAGI follows the federal tax figure (42 CFR 435.603(e), S165). Two kinds of receipt hold money the Code counts and money it does not, and MAGI reads the receipt's amount: a pension or annuity is counted in full (ma_magi_receipt_treatment_table, CO-5500, row counted; 8.100.4.C.1.b.vi), although the part that returns the person's own investment in the contract (26 U.S.C. 72(b)(1), S4637) and a pension for injuries or sickness resulting from active service in the armed forces (104(a)(4), S4638) are not gross income; and an income tax refund is not counted at all (row not_taxable_income), although a State income tax refund is gross income to a person who deducted the tax in an earlier year to the extent the deduction reduced the tax (111(a), S4639, read the other way). Since coverage pass 4 (C4-01) the receipt record has the field that states the federal figure, taxable_amount (CO-5000), and MAGI reads it for savings account distributions and insurance cash value, whose kinds hold mostly non-taxable money. Reading it for pensions and refunds too would make the field required on every pension and refund record any MAGI household states (about thirty stored households and as many rule tests state a pension, none its taxable part), so a pension record without it would be cannot determine. Worked example: a retired service member receives a 1,600 military disability pension excluded under 104(a)(4) and has no other income: the ledger counts 1,600 (1,600 / 1,330 = 120.3% of the guideline for one); the tax figure is 0 (0%), which meets every MAGI standard. A filer who itemized last year and receives a 400 State refund: the ledger counts 0, the tax figure up to 400.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner, Data steward
Assumption: A pension or annuity is counted at its full amount, and an income tax refund is not counted, as before; a household whose pension is partly or wholly excluded, or whose refund is taxable, is answered on that reading until the owner decides whether the application asks every pension and refund for its taxable part or keeps the gross reading.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.4.C.1.b.vi-vii, d (D-65, D-40); 42 CFR 435.603(e) (D-34); 26 U.S.C. 61(a), 72(b)(1), 104(a)(4), 111(a) (D-4430, D-4436, D-4437, D-4438, fetched from Cornell LII 2026-11-23). The Code settles what is taxable; no Colorado text says whether the State's intake asks for the taxable part of a pension or a refund, and asking is a change to every program's statement of a pension. Coverage pass 4, C4-09 (docs/reviews/coverage4-medicaid-magi-chp.md). Items: CO-5500, CO-5501, CO-5000.

Resolved (coverage pass 5, C5-09, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/coverage5-medicaid-magi-chp.md): the class failed the pass-5 check; the Code on file decides the figure, and reading the field where it is stated needs no change to any program's statement of a pension. 26 U.S.C. 61(a)(8) and (10) (S4631) make annuities and pensions gross income, and the Code takes a part out only as 72(b)(1) (S4637) and 104(a)(4) (S4638) provide, which the return states; a refund of tax is gross income only as a recovery under 111(a) (S4639), to the extent the earlier deduction reduced the tax, which only the return states. So ma_magi_receipt_treatment_table (CO-5500) counts a pension at the taxable_amount its record states, else its whole amount (counted_taxable_amount_where_stated), and an income tax refund at the taxable_amount its record states, else nothing (counted_stated_taxable_amount_only); taxable_amount (CO-5000) says what its absence means (when_absent), so no pension or refund record becomes cannot determine. Worked examples (CO-5501-T12 to T16): the 1,600 military disability pension stated with taxable_amount 0 counts 0; a civil service annuity of 2,000 with 300 of basis recovery counts 1,700; a State refund of 400 stated taxable counts 400. Not a state policy choice: whether the application asks for the taxable part is intake, and an unasked part keeps the reading the Code gives the kind.

### OQ-1231. CHP+: whether the guaranteed newborn year covers the month of the first birthday

Body: 10 CCR 2505-3 § 430.5 (S1716) guarantees "coverage for one year" to a child born to a mother enrolled in CHP+ at the birth; the year ends on the day before the first birthday. CHP+ eligibility is effective on the first day of a month (§ 430.1, S1714) and every CHP+ period the rule states is in months (§ 430.2, § 430.3), but no paragraph says whether a guarantee that ends partway through a month covers that month. chp_is_guaranteed_newborn (CO-2410) is evaluated per month since coverage pass 4 (C4-07) and reads the child as guaranteed in a month when the child is under one on its first day, so the month of the first birthday is covered and the month after it is not; the other reading (under one on the last day of the month) ends the guarantee with the month before the birthday month. Worked example: a child born 2025-10-20 to a CHP+ mother, family income 300% of the guideline: October 2026 is eligible_newborn on the first reading, ineligible_income on the second.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: CHP+ policy owner
Assumption: The month of the first birthday is covered (the guarantee reaches its first days, and coverage is by the month); the month after it is not.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-3 § 430.1, § 430.2, § 430.3, § 430.5 (D-66, rule version effective 08/30/2026). None states how a guarantee that ends within a month is applied to that month; Medicaid's whole-month rule (10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.4.G.1) is not adopted by 2505-3. Coverage pass 4, C4-07. Items: CO-2410.

### OQ-1232. CHP+: whether a pregnant applicant under 19 is a Prenatal Care Program client

Body: 10 CCR 2505-3 § 110.1.A (D-66) makes a person eligible who is "less than 19 years of age" (A.1) or "a pregnant woman" (A.2), and § 50.19 defines "Woman" as "a female who is 19 years in age or older". A pregnant applicant of 17 is therefore a CHP+ child, and chp_eligibility_status (CO-707) reports her eligible_child (the child test comes first, on the child's band). chp_prenatal_is_eligible (CO-706) has no age test, so the ledger also reads her as a Prenatal Care Program client, which § 330.4 exempts from cost sharing (chp_is_exempt_from_cost_sharing, CO-1990) and § 430.3 gives an enrollment span through the twelfth month after the birth (chp_is_in_prenatal_coverage_period, CO-2407). On § 50.19's words she is not a "woman", so she would pay the § 320.1 copayments of her family's band and keep the child's 12-month continuous eligibility (§ 430.2) instead of the postpartum span. Federal CHIP rules on cost sharing for pregnancy-related services (not on file) may bear on it. Worked example: a pregnant 17-year-old in a family at 180% of the guideline: band_c copayments on § 50.19's reading; none under the ledger's.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: CHP+ policy owner
Assumption: A pregnant applicant under 19 who meets the prenatal tests is also a Prenatal Care Program client (exempt from cost sharing, with the postpartum span), as the ledger read before coverage pass 4.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-3 § 50.19, § 110.1.A, § 320.1, § 330.4, § 430.2, § 430.3 (D-66, rule version effective 08/30/2026). § 330.4 names "Prenatal Care Program clients" without an age, and no paragraph says whether a pregnant person under 19 is one. Coverage pass 4, C4-15. Items: CO-706, CO-1990, CO-2407.

### OQ-1325. Medicaid: whether the SUD continuum of care demonstration lifts the IMD exclusion of § 8.100.3.G.1.c for a stay for substance use treatment

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.3.G.1.c (D-65, S659) makes it a condition of Medical Assistance that the person "Not be a patient in an institution for tuberculosis or mental disease, unless the person is under 21 years of age or has attained 65 years of age". Since coverage pass 5 (C5-04) ma_magi_eligibility_status (CO-554) and ma_family_planning_status (CO-1154) report a patient of 21 to 64 in an institution for mental diseases (ma_meets_imd_requirement, CO-1179) as ineligible_institution. § 8.100.3.G.1.b.ii and 10 CCR 2505-3 § 120.1.C incorporate the CMS Demonstration Approval of October 14, 2025 for the 1115 demonstration "Colorado Expanding the Substance Use Disorder (SUD) Continuum of Care", but only for Reentry Demonstration Initiative services to inmates. Section 1115 SUD demonstrations commonly authorize federal payment for short-term residential and inpatient SUD treatment in an institution for mental diseases; whether Colorado's approval does, and whether the State reads it as lifting G.1.c's eligibility condition for such a stay (rather than only paying for the service), is not in any text on file: G.1.c names no exception for it. Worked example: an adult of 40 in a 60-bed residential substance use treatment facility that is an institution for mental diseases, income within the Adults standard: ineligible_institution on G.1.c's words; eligible if the demonstration lifts the condition for the stay.

Class: blocked on a named document
Owner: Medicaid policy owner, Legal
Assumption: A patient aged 21 to 64 in an institution for mental diseases is not eligible while a patient, whatever the treatment, as the rule is written.
Blocked on: CMS's Demonstration Approval of October 14, 2025 for Colorado Expanding the Substance Use Disorder Continuum of Care, its special terms and conditions (medicaid.gov refused the automated fetch)

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.3.G.1.b.ii, G.1.c (D-65, rule version effective 11/14/2026); 10 CCR 2505-3 § 120.1.C (D-66). Neither text applies the demonstration to G.1.c. Coverage pass 5, C5-04. Items: CO-554, CO-1154, CO-1179.

### OQ-1200. Holdings: whether the Medicare Savings Program has any resource rule of its own beyond the SSI method and the beneficiary-deed home

Body: Resource records, step H0 (docs/design-earned-and-resource-records.md 3.3, decision D7) gives the Medicare Savings Program no holdings counting table of its own: ma_msp_countable_resources (CO-5206) reads the SSI-method couple figure (ma_ssi_countable_resources, CO-608) less the equity of a home counted only because it has a recorded beneficiary deed (ma_home_equity_counted_only_by_beneficiary_deed), so from step H1 it reads the SSI method's table (ma_ssi_holding_treatment_table, CO-5303) through that figure. Whether the MSP rules (10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.6, the resource limits of S1735) count or exclude any kind of holding differently from the SSI method, other than that home, is not settled by a text the volume has; the design records it as an owner confirmation, not an assumption made silently.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The Medicare Savings Program counts every kind of holding as the SSI method does, except the home counted only by a beneficiary deed (today's reading).

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.M (S1514, S1515, S3400-S3403, S3421) and the MSP resource limits (S1735); no MSP-specific exclusion was found. Items: CO-5300, CO-5303, CO-5206.

### OQ-1201. Holdings: whether Colorado Works, LEAP and SUN Bucks have no resource test, and CCCAP keeps its asset declaration

Body: Resource records, step H0 (docs/design-earned-and-resource-records.md 1.2 and 3.3, decision D10) builds holdings counting tables for SNAP, Adult Financial and the Medicaid SSI method only. No item of Colorado Works, LEAP or SUN Bucks reads a resource fact: Colorado Works reads holdings only through the death reimbursement, which adopts the SSI method (9 CCR 2503-6 3.606.7.F.2.c, as Adult Financial's burial assistance does, 9 CCR 2503-5 3.570.43.D.3.b), and CCCAP asks the household to declare that its assets are within the limit (cccap_declares_assets_within_limit, CO-1741; OQ-54), which stays the declaration it is. The design records each as an owner confirmation: that 9 CCR 2503-6 (Colorado Works) and 9 CCR 2503-7 (LEAP) set no resource test the volume has missed, that SUN Bucks has none, and that CCCAP's rule asks for the declaration rather than a count.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner, Energy assistance (LEAP) policy owner, School meals (CDE) co-approver, Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: Colorado Works, LEAP and SUN Bucks have no resource test, and CCCAP reads the household's declaration, not its holdings (today's reading).

Checked 2026-11-23: the volume's items for the four programs read no resource fact other than CO-1741 (CCCAP) and the death reimbursement's adopted SSI method (Colorado Works); 9 CCR 2503-6 and 9 CCR 2503-7 as fetched (D-81, D-86) were not searched end to end for a resource test in this step. Items: CO-5300, CO-1741.

### OQ-1202. SNAP holdings: burial spaces beyond one plot per member, and the kinds no SNAP item reads

Body: Resource records, step H0 sets SNAP's holdings counting table (snap_holding_treatment_table, CO-5301) to today's reading. Two kinds of rows need the SNAP owner. (1) A finding: 7 CFR 273.8(e)(2) (S2216) and 10 CCR 2506-1 4.410.D.4 (S2967) exclude one burial plot per household member; burial spaces beyond the needs of the person's family, the only burial spaces a fact states today (burial_spaces_value_beyond_family_needs, CO-3857, read by Adult Financial only), are counted by neither text's exclusion, yet no SNAP item reads them (row not_counted_text_counts). No stored household or rule test states them. (2) Kinds no SNAP item reads and no text on file names: investment personal property (gems, collectibles), a revocable burial trust (not a funeral agreement under D.4; an irrevocable one is inaccessible under 4.410.F.1 and F.3, S2968), a trust interest the person can revoke, an annuity, a life estate (rows not_counted_no_rule_found); and money SNAP counts as liquid because no rule excludes it: loan proceeds and replacement proceeds still held, other resources (counted_no_rule_excludes), the mortgages and promissory notes the person holds, which pass the real property tests today (counted_with_real_property), and personal property other than household goods and effects (equipment), which no fact states (excluded_household_goods). The SNAP H1 track changes a row only as a cited difference.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: Each row as today's items read it, until the SNAP owner signs it or the SNAP H1 track changes it with a citation.

Checked 2026-11-23: 7 CFR 273.8(c)(1), (d), (e)(2) (D-191, D-12); 10 CCR 2506-1 4.409-4.410 (D-82). Items: CO-5301, CO-4176, CO-074. Corrected (coverage pass 4 for SNAP resources, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/coverage4-snap-resources.md, SR-3, SR-16): 7 CFR 273.8 is on file in full twice (D-12, retrieved 2026-09-18; D-191, as in force 2026-11-19), (c)(2) included (S4820); the line "not on file in full" below was wrong.

Resolution in part (resource records, step H1 SNAP, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/records-holdings-snap-af.md): (1) settled by the text: a burial space beyond the burial needs of the immediate family is counted, at its equity, unless that is $1,500 or less (7 CFR 273.8(e)(2), S2216; 10 CCR 2506-1 4.410.D.1, D.4, S4690, S2967; F.2, S3119): row counted_beyond_one_plot_per_member, snap_countable_burial_spaces (CO-5313). Whether a space for the immediate family is one household member's excluded plot is OQ-1250. Money from a loan still held is cash on hand or money in an account (4.409.A, S4690) and 4.405.F.1 (S4691) excludes loans from income only: row counted. Land used only to produce food for the household is real property (4.409.B) that no paragraph of 4.410 exempts: counted, once stated not for sale (snap_real_property_not_exempt, CO-5310). (2) Stays open, with its assumption (as recorded at step H1; resolved below except the life estate): investment personal property (4.409.B defines non-liquid resources as real property; 7 CFR 273.8(c)(2), not on file in full [corrected: on file in full, D-12 and D-191], names personal property), a revocable burial trust, a revocable trust, an annuity and a life estate (not_counted_no_rule_found); insurance or home-sale proceeds held for replacement and other resources (counted_no_rule_excludes; insurance settlements are lump sums counted as resources, 4.405.E, S4691, and relocation reimbursements are excluded, 4.410.J.4, S3650, stated as held_excluded_payment); the mortgages and notes the person holds (counted_with_real_property; 4.410.G does not apply its $1,500 test to negotiable instruments, which the record does not tell apart); personal property other than household goods (excluded_household_goods).

Resolution of part (2) (coverage pass 4 for SNAP resources, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/coverage4-snap-resources.md, SR-3, SR-16): settled by the text on file, so not a state policy choice. 7 CFR 273.8(c)(2) (S4820) includes "Nonliquid resources, personal property, ... and any other property, provided that these resources are not specifically excluded under paragraph (e)", (e) excludes "only the following", and 10 CCR 2506-1 4.408.1.B (S4823) values non-liquid resources other than real property "less verified encumbrances". Investment personal property is counted at its equity, inaccessible at $1,500 or less (4.410.F.2; row counted_at_equity; snap_countable_other_nonliquid_resources, CO-6900). A burial fund the person can withdraw is money in an account (273.8(c)(1)), counted unless irrevocable (F.1; row counted_unless_irrevocable; CO-4176). Replacement proceeds still held and other resources are counted by (c)(1)-(2) (rows counted; no answer changes). The mortgages and notes the person holds are counted (c)(1)-(2), with the $1,500 tests except for a negotiable instrument (4.410.G: "does not apply to negotiable financial instruments"; 273.8(e)(18), S4826): the record states it (negotiable_instrument; CO-5311). Personal property other than household goods: the kind joins goods and equipment, a field request (SR-13; the row stays excluded_household_goods for the owner's signature). A revocable trust and an annuity: 273.8(c)(2) counts them, but Colorado's 4.410.F.3 and 273.8(e)(8) differ on when a trust is inaccessible: OQ-1307. Stays open: the life estate (not_counted_no_rule_found), whether a life estate or remainder interest can be sold not being settled on file.

### OQ-1203. Adult Financial holdings: investment personal property, and the kinds no Adult Financial item reads

Body: Resource records, step H0 sets Adult Financial's holdings counting table (af_holding_treatment_table, CO-5302) to today's reading. (1) A finding: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.77.B (S4507) exempts household goods and personal effects but "does not include effects that the client holds because of the value or as an investment, which are countable as personal property"; no Adult Financial item reads investment_personal_property_value (CO-4642), so Adult Financial counts none of it (row not_counted_text_counts). Eight stored households state CO-4642, each as 0; one rule test (CO-4623-T3, Medicaid's) states it. (2) Rows counted because 3.520.72.A (S1636) counts every resource not otherwise specified and no exclusion was found on file: an education savings account, a state or local earned income tax credit held apart from a refund (3.520.77.K names refunds and the PTC rebate), a government payment a federal statute excludes from resources, prorated income still held, other resources; rows not counted because no item reads them and no text on file names them: an annuity, a life estate; and personal property other than household goods and effects (equipment, livestock), which 3.520.72.B.3-4 (S2213) counts when not exempt and no fact states today.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: Each row as today's items read it, until the owner signs it or the Adult Financial H1 track changes it with a citation.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.72-3.520.77 (D-80). Items: CO-5302, CO-4000, CO-4412.

Resolution in part (resource records, step H1 Adult Financial, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/records-holdings-snap-af.md): (1) the finding is fixed as the text reads: personal property held because of its value or as an investment is counted at its appraised value less liabilities (3.520.77.B, S4507; 3.520.742.A.3, B, S4692): row counted, af_countable_personal_property (CO-5345). (2) Settled by the text: an education savings account is an investment account 3.520.72.B.5 (S2213) counts ("other retirement or investment accounts and investment vehicles"): row counted. Stays open, with its assumption: a state or local earned income tax credit held apart from a refund, a government payment a federal statute excludes, prorated income still held and other resources (counted_no_rule_excludes); an annuity and a life estate (not_counted_no_rule_found); personal property other than household goods and investments (excluded_household_goods; 3.520.72.B.3-4 counts it when not exempt).

Resolution in part (coverage pass 4, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/coverage4-cash.md, CA4-11 and CA4-12): two more parts are settled by the text and leave this question. Personal property other than household goods and personal effects (equipment, livestock, tools, a mobile home or recreational vehicle not lived in) is counted at its appraised value less liabilities (3.520.72.B.3-4, S2213; 3.520.742.B, S4692: "i.e., vehicles, farm equipment and livestock"), unless the county finds it cannot be sold or it is used for self-support: the holdings record states which it is (household_goods_or_personal_effects, CO-5300), row counted_unless_household_goods, af_countable_personal_property (CO-5345). An annuity the person can assign or surrender is an investment vehicle convertible to cash (3.520.72.B.5, S2213; 3.510 "Convertible asset", S4796) and is counted at its value, row counted_if_revocable, af_cash_and_convertible_holdings (CO-5340). A life estate is not negotiable (3.520.741.D.1), so not counting it follows the text. Stays open, with its assumption: a state or local earned income tax credit held apart from a refund, a government payment a federal statute excludes, prorated income still held and other resources (counted_no_rule_excludes).

### OQ-1204. Partly resolved: part (1), the earned income tax credit, loans, disaster and relocation payments still held, is settled by the text; open: (2), the kinds no rule on file settles

Body: Resource records, step H0 sets the SSI method's holdings counting table (ma_ssi_holding_treatment_table, CO-5303) to today's reading. (1) Findings: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.F.6 (S4506) exempts "as either income or resources" for the ABD and long-term care categories a bona fide loan (a), relocation payments, toward a replacement dwelling for up to 6 months (f), major disaster and emergency assistance (k), Civil Liberties Act payments (r), the Earned Income Tax Credit as a resource "for the month it is received and for the following month" (u) and payments to victims of Nazi persecution (x). No SSI-method item subtracts the part of liquid_resources_value (CO-3390) that is such money still held: ssi_countable_resources_individual (CO-800) subtracts only the retroactive SSI or Social Security payment (F.6.c). So a federal refund carrying the credit, a state or local credit, loan proceeds, and disaster, relocation, Civil Liberties Act or Nazi persecution payments still held are counted (rows counted_text_excludes for held_income_tax_refund, held_eitc, held_loan_proceeds, held_excluded_payment, held_replacement_proceeds). One stored household states federal_tax_refunds_held_value above 0 and one states excluded_government_payments_held_value; neither is an SSI-method case (docs/reviews/holdings-h0.md). (2) Rows counted because 20 CFR 416.1201(a) (S4500) counts what the person owns and could convert to cash and no exclusion was found on file: an education savings account, business cash (8.100.5.J.3, S3402, excludes property used in a trade or business essential to self-support up to $6,000 with a 6% return; whether business cash is such property is open), prorated income still held, other resources; and personal property other than household goods and effects (8.100.5.M.3.i, S4505, counts a mobile home or trailer not the home and not income-producing), which no fact states today.

Resolved, part (1) (resource records, Medicaid track H1, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/records-holdings-medicaid.md): the text settles each row, and the track set them as cited differences (ma_ssi_holding_treatment_table, CO-5303; ma_ssi_holdings_countable_in_month, CO-5320). held_loan_proceeds: excluded (8.100.5.F.6.a, S4506: "A bona fide loan", exempt "from consideration as either income or resources", with no time limit). held_excluded_payment: excluded (F.6.f, k, r, s, v, x, S4506, S4668: relocation, major disaster and emergency assistance, Civil Liberties Act, Agent Orange, Radiation Exposure Compensation and Nazi persecution payments; 20 CFR 416.1237(a), S4663, excludes disaster assistance from SSI resources too). held_income_tax_refund: a federal refund, the earned income tax credit paid with it included, is left out for twelve months from receipt (26 U.S.C. 6409, S4664, D-4441: "Notwithstanding any other provision of law, any refund ... shall not be taken into account as resources for a period of 12 months from receipt" in any State program financed in part with Federal funds; this is longer than F.6.u's two months and 20 CFR 416.1235's nine, S4661, and governs a federal refund; ma_federal_tax_refund_resource_exclusion_months, CO-6500); a State or local refund stays counted (no text on file excludes it). held_eitc (a State or local credit received apart from a refund): left out in the month it is received and the next (F.6.u: "EIC shall also be exempt as resources for the month it is received and for the following month"); whether F.6.u's "Earned Income Tax Credit" reaches a State or local credit is OQ-1242. held_replacement_proceeds: proceeds held toward replacing real property are left out for three months from receipt, the window every source the Medical Assistance text names at least has (the sale of an exempt home used to buy another within three months, 8.100.5.P.4, S4669; a relocation payment toward a replacement dwelling for up to six months, F.6.f); proceeds toward personal property stay counted, and the longer windows of a relocation payment and of insurance (20 CFR 416.1232(a), S4662, nine months, which no Colorado text on file adopts) need the source stated: OQ-1240. A refund, credit or proceeds whose date of receipt is not stated cannot be placed in its window: cannot determine (decision 4). Part (2), settled in part by the Medicaid H1 track (docs/reviews/records-holdings-medicaid.md, section 5): an education savings account and other resources are counted by the text (8.100.5.M.1, S301: "Resources are defined as cash or other assets or any real or personal property that an individual or spouse owns"; E.3, S4670; no exemption of M.2 or F.6 names them; rows counted). Open for the owner: business cash (whether it is J.3 "Property used in a trade or business", S3402, which M.1 names apart from cash), prorated income still held (E.1, S4673, calculates such income monthly; no text says whether what is held for the months it covers is a resource), and personal property other than household goods and effects (M.3.i, S4505).

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: Each row as today's items read it, until the owner signs it or the Medicaid H1 track changes it with a citation.
