Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.F.6, J, M, N (D-65); 20 CFR 416.1201 (D-4400). Not on file: 20 CFR 416.1235 (tax refunds), 416.1232 (replacement of excluded resources), 416.1237 (disaster assistance). Items: CO-5303, CO-800, CO-4623.

### OQ-1205. Adult Financial vehicles: whether "the vehicle with the highest value" means the highest market value or the highest equity

Body: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.77.A (S2214): "The vehicle with the highest value is exempt if it is used for the transportation of the client or a member of the household." The holdings record states each vehicle's market value (`value`) and what is owed on it (`amount_owed`). Two readings: (A) the highest equity, value less amount owed, which is what P58 `largest` returns when its value is the equity, and which matches the SSI method's practice of excluding the one automobile in the way most advantageous to the person (20 CFR 416.1218(b)(1), S2215); (B) the highest market value, whose equity is then excluded, which P58 cannot express without a key argument (the record with the highest market value, larger equity winning a tie). Worked example: car X worth $9,000 owing $8,000 (equity $1,000) and car Y worth $6,000 owing nothing (equity $6,000), both used for transportation: under A the countable vehicle equity is $1,000 (Y excluded); under B it is $6,000 (X excluded), $5,000 more against the resource limit. transportation_vehicle_equity_value (CO-3395) mixes the two ("the equity value of the ... highest-valued vehicle"). Raised by the fact-graph review of design decision D5 (P58), 2026-11-23.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: Reading A, the highest equity; the Adult Financial H1 track writes the vehicle exclusion with P58 over equity floored at 0, and adds a key argument to P58 only if the owner chooses B.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.77.A (D-80); 20 CFR 416.1218(b)(1) (S2215). No definition of "value" for vehicles in 3.520.77. Items: CO-5302, CO-3395.

Note (resource records, step H1 Adult Financial, 2026-11-23): the assumption is implemented: af_countable_vehicles (CO-5343) counts the vehicles' equity, each floored at 0, less the largest such equity of a vehicle used for transportation (P58 largest); rule test CO-5343-T2 is the worked example (1,000 counted under A; B would count 6,000). Items: CO-5343.

### OQ-1220. SUN Bucks: the Summer EBT benefit level for summer 2027

Body: 7 CFR 292.15(e) (S794) sets the Summer EBT benefit per child at a published level that "will be adjusted in subsequent years to reflect changes in the cost of food as measured by the Thrifty Food Plan"; FNS publishes each summer's level as a notice, "Summer Electronic Benefits Transfer for Children Program; <year> Benefit Levels". sebt_monthly_benefit_table (CO-1244) holds the levels the volume has fetched, through summer 2026. Every determination made from July 2026 is for summer 2027 (sebt_summer_year, CO-2581), which has no row, so the benefit (CO-1299) is cannot determine, with a lookup-miss warning on CO-01, CO-02 and CO-13 (coverage pass 4 S4-3, docs/reviews/coverage4-care-energy.md). That is the right answer until the figure is published: a lookup miss is never filled with the last year's level (docs/conventions.md, Effective dating). Items: CO-1244, CO-1299.

Class: blocked on a named document
Owner: School meals (CDE) co-approver, SNAP policy owner
Assumption: None: the summer 2027 benefit cannot be determined until the notice is published and its figure loaded.
Blocked on: the FNS notice "Summer Electronic Benefits Transfer for Children Program; 2027 Benefit Levels" in the Federal Register (not yet published).

Checked 2026-11-23: the Federal Register's document search (federalregister.gov, terms "Summer Electronic Benefits Transfer" and "Benefit Levels") lists the 2026 notice (document 2026-07843, published 2026-04-22) and the 2025 notice (2025-03760, 2025-03-10), and no 2027 notice.

### OQ-1221. CCCAP: "the term of the contract" for pay under an annual contract

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.H.4.b (S2704): "For contract employment in cases, such as in some school systems, where the employees derive their annual income in a period shorter than a year, the income shall be prorated over the term of the contract, provided that the income from the contract is not earned on an hourly or piecework basis." The earnings record states such pay with the contract's pay for the year (annual_contract_pay, CO-5100, a contract renewed on an annual basis). The volume reads "the term of the contract" as the contract year, twelve months (cccap_annual_contract_months, CO-6406), so the annual pay is spread over every month, the months without pay included: review household CK1 (stored as CO-719), $40,500 paid $4,500 a month September to May, counts $40,500 / 12 = $3,375 a month (coverage pass 4 C4-4). The other reading is the months the contract has the employee work and pays (nine), which gives $4,500 in a month of the school year and nothing in summer; it would not prorate anything in the school year, which the "shall" seems to rule out, but the text does not name twelve. Items: CO-6406, CO-3915, CO-5152.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: The term of an annual contract is twelve months: one twelfth of the contract's annual pay is counted in each month.

Checked 2026-11-23: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.H.4.b (D-87, rule version 12658, effective 2026-08-14); 3.103 defines neither "contract employment" nor "term of the contract". 10 CCR 2506-1 4.403.3.A (S3115) averages the same income "over a twelve (12) month period" for SNAP, which supports the reading but is not CCCAP's text.

### OQ-1222. CCCAP: Title I (VISTA) payments below the minimum wage to a client who was not receiving CCCAP when joining VISTA

Body: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.I.32 (S4601) counts "wages of AmeriCorps volunteers in service to America (vista) workers" as earned income: "Vista payments are excluded if the client was receiving CCCAP when they joined vista. If the client was not receiving CCCAP when they joined vista, the vista payments shall count as earned income." 3.111.J.11 (S4601) excludes payments under "any other program under Title I (VISTA) when the value of all such payments adjusted to reflect the number of hours such volunteers are serving is not equivalent to or greater than the minimum wage". For VISTA pay at or above the minimum wage only I.32 speaks: the receipt kind dvsa_title_i_payment_at_or_above_minimum_wage is excluded or counted as earned by received_cccap_when_joined_vista (CO-6407; cccap_person_earned_income, CO-3915; coverage pass 4 C4-5). For VISTA pay below the minimum wage (dvsa_title_i_volunteer_payment) both speak: J.11 excludes it, and I.32 counts it for a client who was not receiving CCCAP when joining. The volume keeps the J.11 exclusion (cccap_receipt_treatment_table row excluded, as before coverage pass 4): an exclusion that names the payment by its value is read as the more specific text. The other reading counts it as earned income under I.32 unless the client was receiving CCCAP when joining. Items: CO-5720, CO-3915, CO-6407.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: A Title I (VISTA) payment below the minimum wage is excluded (J.11) whether or not the client was receiving CCCAP when joining; one at or above the minimum wage is excluded or counted as earned income by I.32.

Checked 2026-11-23: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.I.32, J.11 (D-87, rule version 12658, effective 2026-08-14). Neither paragraph refers to the other, and 3.111 states no order between the inclusions and the exclusions.

### OQ-1241. Medicaid parental deeming: whether two deeming parents exclude one automobile between them

Body: 20 CFR 416.1218(b) (S4660) excludes one automobile "In determining the resources of an individual (and spouse, if any)", and 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.M.2.c (S4504) one automobile "used for transportation for the individual or a member of the individual's household". Resource records, Medicaid track H1, applies the couple's one automobile to the couple's own figures (ma_ssi_countable_resources, CO-608; ma_institutionalized_spouse_countable_resources, CO-647; ssi_countable_resources_couple, CO-801; ma_spousal_resources_at_snapshot, CO-888): each spouse's own figure excludes that spouse's automobile, and the couple adds back the smaller. The resources of a child's ineligible parents (416.1202(b)(1), S3414: "any resources, not otherwise excluded under this subpart, of his or her ineligible parent") are each parent's own figure (ma_ssi_parental_resources_deemed, CO-4627), so two parents who each own a car used for transportation have both excluded. Whether 416.1218(b)'s "individual (and spouse, if any)" reaches two parents whose resources are deemed to a child, so one automobile is excluded between them, is not settled by the text on file. Worked example: parents with 1,000 and 2,000 in the bank and cars of 6,000 and 4,000 equity: as stated today, 3,000 - 3,000 (the couple limit) = 0 deemed; with one automobile between them, 3,000 + 4,000 - 3,000 = 4,000 deemed.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: Each parent excludes the parent's own automobile, as before the records.

Checked 2026-11-23: 20 CFR 416.1202(b)(1), 416.1218(b) (D-54, D-4440); 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.M.2.c (D-65). Items: CO-4627, CO-5321.

### OQ-1243. Resolved: decision 4 decides how the Medicaid holdings readers read an unstated record field

Body: The holdings schema (CO-5300) declares most fields "required where read" (decision D3: a field with no default the rule states is required). Resource records, Medicaid track H1 reads these fields as the scalar facts' readers read the facts they restate, so that no stored household's answer turns to "cannot determine" on a field the old facts never stated: a real property record's income_producing, produces_for_household_use, used_in_trade_or_business, ma_sale_efforts_unsuccessful and co_owner_would_lose_housing; a home record's intends_to_return, intent_in_writing, outside_colorado, held_in_trust_or_entity and beneficiary_deed (lives_in, when not stated, from residence_setting_type); a revocable burial record's accrued_interest (none); a trust, annuity or life estate record's ma_found_available_value (none found). Each is an exclusion or a finding the person or the Department has to establish (10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.E.3, S4670: resources are available when owned and convertible), so "not so" is the reading the rule's structure gives; but decision 4 asks for "cannot determine" unless the rule says otherwise, and the schema does not declare these defaults. Either the integration owner declares each default on the field (the rule gives it), or the owner accepts "cannot determine" for a record that does not state it and the stored households and rule tests state each field. The strict fields are read strictly: value, amount_owed (for property), used_for_transportation, face_value, irrevocable, source_date, compensation_received, ma_transfer_exempt, net_annual_return for business property.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The reading the scalar facts' readers gave (each field "not so" when not stated), until the owner or the integration owner decides.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.E.3-4, J, M.2-3 (D-65); docs/design-earned-and-resource-records.md 7 (D3), docs/plan-colorado.md (decision 4). Items: CO-5320, CO-4620, CO-4621, CO-4622, CO-5204, CO-5322.

Resolved (coverage pass 4, non-MAGI, N4-13 and N4-14, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/coverage4-medicaid-nonmagi.md): not a state policy choice. Decision 4 of 2026-11-23 (docs/plan-colorado.md) says missing information is "cannot determine" unless the rule says otherwise, and that each supplied fact states once whether it is required or what its rule-given default is. Applied field by field: the seven fields an exclusion or exemption rests on (income_producing, produces_for_household_use, used_in_trade_or_business, ma_sale_efforts_unsuccessful, co_owner_would_lose_housing, intends_to_return, intent_in_writing) take a default the rule gives, false, declared once on the holdings schema (CO-5300), from 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.E.3 (S4670: resources are available when owned, convertible to cash and not legally restricted, so an exclusion applies only when shown); the fields that take an exemption away (outside_colorado, held_in_trust_or_entity, beneficiary_deed) and the parts a rule needs stated (accrued_interest of a revocable burial fund, ma_found_available_value of a trust, annuity or life estate) are required where read, so a record that leaves them out cannot be determined. The stored households and rule tests that read them state them (the home records of fifteen stored households and of four of this round's, with the scalar facts they translate; each scalar line commented).

### OQ-1244. Medicaid transfer look-back: counted from the month of application or from the day 60 months before the date of application

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.7.F.1.d (S365, S4671) defines the look-back period as "the number of months prior to the month of application for long-term care services", and F.2.a says "the look-back date is 60 months prior to the date of application"; 42 U.S.C. 1396p(c)(1)(B)(i) (S368) speaks of a look-back date. Resource records, Medicaid track H1 places a transfer in the look-back by its month (ma_uncompensated_transfers_in_lookback, CO-5325: a disposed_date in or after the month 60 months before the month of the application), as F.1.d defines the period. Read by the day, a transfer earlier in that first month than the application's day would fall outside it. Worked example: an application on 2026-10-20 and a transfer on 2021-10-05: in the look-back by the month (2021-10), outside it by the day (before 2021-10-20).

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The look-back counted in months from the month of application (F.1.d).

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.7.F.1.d, F.2.a (D-65); 42 U.S.C. 1396p(c)(1)(B) (S368). Items: CO-5325, CO-252.

### OQ-1240. Medicaid SSI method holdings: proceeds held to replace property, by their source (narrowed: a condemnation award, and the 416.1232 extensions)

Narrowed in the field-effect sweep fix round, non-MAGI Medicaid (FS-MA-9, 2026-11-23). The holdings record states proceeds_source (CO-5300), and the text settles three sources: the sale of the home three months (8.100.5.P.4, S4669); a relocation payment six months (F.6.f, S4506; ma_relocation_payment_exclusion_months, CO-7150); cash from insurance or any other source received to repair or replace an excluded resource that is lost, damaged or stolen nine months, 20 CFR 416.1232(a) (S4662), which the Medical Assistance text does not name and 42 CFR 435.601(b)(2) (S4754) brings in as the SSI methodology, as coverage pass 4 brought 416.1123 and 416.1124 where Colorado's text names nothing (ssi_replacement_cash_exclusion_months, CO-7151; Medicaid's finding ma_replaces_lost_excluded_resource, required where read); other such cash is counted (P.1, S4945). Still open: (a) a condemnation award: no text on file names it, and a taking is not a loss, damage or theft; the assumption keeps the three months toward real property and counts it toward personal property; (b) the good-cause and declared-disaster extensions of 416.1232(b)-(c) (S4944): no field states the finding, so the nine months are never extended. Worked example (a): 20,000 awarded in June for land taken for a road, held to buy other land, determined in October: counted (fourth month); left out through December if a relocation window applied. (b): fire insurance for the home received in January, rebuilding delayed by a contractor's default, determined in November: counted (tenth month); left out up to nine more months with good cause.

Original body: The holdings kind held_replacement_proceeds (CO-5300) is money the person holds and has stated in writing will restore or replace property the person owned: fire or casualty insurance, the sale of the home, a relocation payment, a condemnation award. The Medical Assistance text gives such money different windows by its source: the proceeds of the sale of an exempt home are excluded when used within three months to buy another home (10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.P.4, S4669); relocation payments to a displaced homeowner toward a replacement dwelling are exempt for up to six months (F.6.f, S4506); no Medical Assistance text on file names insurance proceeds, while the SSI rule excludes cash received to repair or replace a lost, damaged or stolen excluded resource for nine months (20 CFR 416.1232(a), S4662). The record states the property replaced (replacement_property) and the date received (source_date), not the source. Resource records, Medicaid track H1 leaves out proceeds toward real property for the three months every named source at least has (ma_replacement_proceeds_exclusion_months, CO-6501) and counts proceeds toward personal property. Open: (1) whether the holdings record gets a field for the source (a request to the integration owner, docs/reviews/records-holdings-medicaid.md), so a relocation payment is left out for six months; (2) whether insurance proceeds for an excluded resource are left out (416.1232's nine months; no Colorado text on file adopts it). Worked example: 9,000 of fire insurance received in June to rebuild the home, determined in October: counted today (fourth month); under 416.1232 left out through March.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: A condemnation award toward real property left out for three months from receipt, toward personal property counted; the nine months of 416.1232(a) never extended.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.F.6.f, P.1, P.4 (D-65); 20 CFR 416.1232 (D-4440, D-54); 42 CFR 435.601(b)(2) (S4754). Items: CO-5303, CO-5320, CO-6501, CO-7150, CO-7151.

### OQ-1242. Medicaid SSI method holdings: whether 8.100.5.F.6.u's earned income tax credit reaches a State or local credit

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.F.6.u (S4506): "The Earned Income Tax Credit (EIC). EIC shall also be exempt as resources for the month it is received and for the following month." A federal credit is paid as a federal refund and is left out for twelve months by 26 U.S.C. 6409 (S4664), whatever F.6.u says (the holdings kind held_income_tax_refund, paid_by federal). The holdings kind held_eitc is a State or local credit received apart from a refund; resource records, Medicaid track H1 leaves it out in the month received and the next by F.6.u. Whether F.6.u, which names "The Earned Income Tax Credit (EIC)" (the federal credit's name), reaches a State or local credit is not settled by the text on file; if not, a State or local credit held is counted in the month after receipt. 20 CFR 416.1235 (S4661) is about the federal credit (section 32 of the Internal Revenue Code) only.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: F.6.u reaches a State or local earned income tax credit (left out in the month received and the next).

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.F.6.u (D-65); 26 U.S.C. 6409 (D-4441); 20 CFR 416.1235 (D-4440). Items: CO-5303, CO-5320.

### OQ-1250. Resolved: SNAP holdings: whether a burial space for the immediate family is a household member's one excluded plot

Body: 7 CFR 273.8(e)(2) (S2216) excludes "one burial plot per household member" and 10 CCR 2506-1 4.410.D.1 (S4690) "Household goods, personal effects, including one burial plot per household member". The holdings record of a burial space (burial_space, CO-5300) says whether it is for the person, the spouse or another member of the immediate family, or a brother or sister of one of them (for_immediate_family, the Adult Financial and SSI test, 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.77.I, S2619), not whether the person it is for is a member of the SNAP household, nor whether a member already has an excluded plot. Step H1 SNAP counts a space not for the immediate family (OQ-1202 resolved in part; snap_countable_burial_spaces, CO-5313). Worked example: a household of one, a widow, owns her own plot ($1,800) and a plot for her adult son, who lives in another state ($1,800): under the text the son's plot is not a household member's and counts ($1,800 is more than $1,500, 4.410.F.2), so her resources are $1,800 more; read as for the immediate family, both are excluded.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: A burial space for the immediate family is one household member's excluded plot (not counted); a case cannot say that it holds a second plot for a member, or a plot for a relative outside the household (a field request to the integration owner: whether the space is for a member of the person's SNAP household).

Checked 2026-11-23: 7 CFR 273.8(e)(2) (D-191); 10 CCR 2506-1 4.410.D.1, D.4, F.2 (D-82). No paragraph on file defines a member's plot. Items: CO-5313, CO-5301, CO-5300.

Resolution (coverage pass 4 for SNAP resources, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/coverage4-snap-resources.md, SR-7; the class check found the text settles it): "one burial plot per household member" (7 CFR 273.8(e)(2), S2216; 4.410.D.1, S4690) is the one plot of a member of the SNAP household; what was missing was a field, not a choice. The record now names whom a space is for (burial_for, for_person: a person of the case, by the case's id), and snap_countable_burial_spaces (CO-5313) excludes, for each member of the person's SNAP household, the largest space for that member; a space for anyone else, a relative outside the household included, is counted when it is worth more than $1,500. The worked example: the widow's own $1,800 plot is excluded and her son's $1,800 plot is counted. for_immediate_family is no longer read by SNAP. Items: CO-5313, CO-5301, CO-5300.

### OQ-1251. SNAP holdings: whether money a federal statute excludes keeps its exclusion when commingled

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.409.D (D-82): "Exempt monies that are kept in a separate account and that are not commingled in an account with non-excluded funds shall retain their resource exclusion for an unlimited period of time. Those excluded shall retain their exemption for six (6) months from the date they are commingled. After six (6) months from the date of commingling, all funds in the commingled account shall be counted as a resource." The holdings records state money SNAP excludes by its source (a held refund or credit, prorated income, an excluded government payment) as its own record beside the account it sits in, and no field says whether it is kept apart or since when it has been commingled; SNAP excludes each such record for as long as its own paragraph says (twelve months for a federal refund, 4.410.J.17). Worked example: a $2,000 disaster payment (4.410.J.3) deposited on 2026-03-01 into a checking account that holds $500 of wages: on 2026-10-01 it has been commingled seven months, so under D all $2,500 counts; read by its own paragraph, $500 counts.

Class: state policy choice (with Legal)
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: Each excluded holding keeps the exclusion its own paragraph gives, whether or not it is commingled (the reading before step H1); a field request to the integration owner (the date the money was commingled, or that it is kept apart) if the owner reads D as applying to it.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.409.D, 4.410.C, J (D-82). Not on file: the federal provision behind 4.409.D [corrected below: on file]. Items: CO-4176, CO-5301.

Narrowed (coverage pass 4 for SNAP resources, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/coverage4-snap-resources.md, SR-16, SR-17): the federal provision behind 4.409.D is 7 CFR 273.8(g), on file in D-12 and D-191 (S4825): "All other excluded moneys which are commingled in an account with nonexcluded funds shall retain their exemption for six months from the date they are commingled. After six months from the date of commingling, all funds in the commingled account shall be counted as a resource", and (g) keeps the exclusion of prorated student and self-employment income for the period it is prorated over. The text settles the six-month rule for money excluded only by 273.8(e) and 4.410. What stays open is narrower: whether money a federal statute excludes outright, on its own terms (a federal income tax refund for twelve months under 26 U.S.C. 6409, 4.410.J.17; disaster assistance under the Stafford Act, 4.410.J.3), keeps that statute's exclusion when commingled, or loses it after six months under (g) (273.8(e)(11): "Resources which are excluded for SNAP purposes by express provision of Federal statute"); a question of law for the owner with Legal. Separately, the record has no commingling date (a field request to the integration owner: the date the money was commingled with counted money, or that it is kept apart), so even the settled six-month rule cannot be read: until it is, each excluded holding keeps its own paragraph's exclusion. The worked example's disaster payment (a statute's exclusion) is the open part.

### OQ-1252. SNAP holdings: a holding shared with an ineligible student, a non-household member living with the household

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.409.C (S2968): "Nonexempt liquid and non-liquid resources owned jointly by separate households shall be considered available in their entirety to each household, unless it can be demonstrated by the applicant household that it does not have access to such resources." A spouse in an institution that provides most meals, or an ineligible student, is not a member of the SNAP household, so a joint account with that spouse is owned jointly with a separate household. The holdings record names the co-owner as the spouse (co_owners spouse, a person of the case), and SNAP counts the person's share (snap_countable_liquid_resources, CO-4176); before step H1 the household stated the other owners' share apart (joint_liquid_resources_other_owners_share_value, CO-4340) when the owner lived outside the home. Worked example: a husband at home and his wife in a nursing facility share a $6,000 account (share 0.5 each): by the record the husband's SNAP household counts $3,000; under 4.409.C, unless he shows he cannot reach it, $6,000.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: The person's share of a holding owned with an ineligible student who is a member of the purchase-and-prepare unit is counted (the student's own share is not, 4.411.2.C); a co-owner of the case outside the person's SNAP household is a separate household (resolved in part, below).

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.409.C (D-82); 7 CFR 273.8(d) (S3129). Items: CO-4176, CO-5310, CO-5300.

Resolved in part (coverage pass 4 for SNAP resources, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/coverage4-snap-resources.md, SR-8; the class check found the text settles the spouse in an institution): 4.409.C and 273.8(d) count a holding owned jointly with a separate household in its entirety, and whether a co-owner of the case is a member of the person's SNAP household is derivable: a spouse (co_owners spouse) from the spouse relationship and the household's members, another person of the case from the record (co_owner, naming the person). A co-owner of the case who is not a member of the person's SNAP household is a separate household, so the co-owners' part is added (snap_countable_liquid_resources, CO-4176; snap_real_property_not_exempt, CO-5310; snap_countable_real_property, CO-5311; snap_countable_other_nonliquid_resources, CO-6900): the worked example counts $6,000. Stays open: an ineligible student is a non-household member living with the household (4.411.2.C, S2508), whose resources are not available to the household, but who is not a separate household in 4.409.C's words; a holding shared with an ineligible student who buys and prepares with the household is read at the person's share (the student is in snap_household_members), as before.

### OQ-1253. Narrowed: Adult Financial holdings: the interest on the exempt $1,500 of a revocable burial trust or contract whose principal is over $1,500

Body: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.77.G.3 (S2619): "A revocable burial trust is exempt if the value of the trust does not exceed one thousand five hundred dollars ($1,500). Any interest on the exempt $1,500 is also exempt. Any amount over $1,500 is a countable resource." H.2.b says the same of a revocable prepaid burial contract. The holdings record states a burial fund's or contract's value and the interest accrued in it (accrued_interest, CO-5300), but the rule does not say how the interest is split between the exempt $1,500 and the rest when the principal is over $1,500, and Adult Financial has never read the interest (af_countable_burial_holdings, CO-5344, counts the value over $1,500, as af_person_countable_resources_other_than_life_insurance did before step H1). Worked example: a revocable trust of $1,500 that has earned $120 of interest, value $1,620: if the interest on the exempt $1,500 is exempt, nothing counts; read as today, $120 counts. A trust of $3,000 principal with $200 of interest: interest in proportion to principal gives $100 on the exempt $1,500, so $1,600 counts; read as today, $1,700.

Narrowed (field sweep fix round, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/field-sweep-snap-cash-nonmagi.md, FS-AF-5): a trust or contract whose principal (value less accrued_interest) is $1,500 or less has all its interest on the exempt $1,500, so G.3 and H.2.b exempt it whole: the first worked example ($1,620, $120 of interest) counts 0, read by af_countable_burial_holdings (CO-5344). What stays open is the split when the principal is over $1,500 (the second worked example).

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: With a principal over $1,500, the value over $1,500 counts, interest included, until the owner says how the interest on the exempt $1,500 is measured.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.77.G.3, H.2 (D-80). No paragraph on file allocates the interest. Items: CO-5344, CO-5302.

### OQ-1254. Resolved: Adult Financial transfers: several resources transferred in one month, and the first month of the thirty-six

Body: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.76.D.1-3 and E (S2614, S2806) compute the months of ineligibility for each resource transferred: "Divide the uncompensated value by the TWFC monthly penalty value and round down to the nearest whole number"; "depending on the amount of the uncompensated value for each resource transferred". A.3 counts a transfer "within thirty-six (36) months prior to the application date". Since step H1 each transfer is a holdings record (CO-5300), but the divisor (af_twfc_monthly_penalty_value, the client's program's grant standard) and the application date (af_application_date) are facts of the person, which the receipt operators do not let a sum over the records read (docs/operators.md, P55: a condition or value inside the sum reads only the record's fields, the month, case facts and parameters). So (1) when two or more resources transferred in one month are not returned, the months are cannot determine (af_twfc_penalty_months, CO-3840): rounding their total overstates them by up to one month per extra resource; and (2) in the month thirty-six months before the application month, when transfers fall on both sides of the application's day of the month, the value within the thirty-six months cannot be read and the months are cannot determine. Worked example: an OAP client (standard $1,032) gives $3,000 and $2,200 away in December 2025: 2 + 2 = 4 months; the month's total, 5,200 / 1,032, would give 5.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: none (resolved).

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.76.A.3, D, E (D-80); docs/operators.md (P55, P58). Items: CO-3840, CO-4008, CO-3841.

Resolution (cross-list records round, 2026-11-24; docs/reviews/cross-list-records.md): the text decides both cases, and the operators now read them. P60 (docs/operators.md: `that holding's owner`, `["of", ["owner"], fact]`) lets a condition or value inside a sum over the person's own holdings read a fact of the person whose list it is, the one person's facts a record may be read with; P55's rule is otherwise unchanged. af_twfc_penalty_months (CO-3840) now sums, over the resources transferred in the month and not returned in whole in that month (D.6), each one's uncompensated value divided by the client's TWFC monthly penalty value read at the determination month (D.2) and rounded down (D.3), at most 36 (E), cut at its own month of return when returned in whole later (D.6.a), and counts in the month thirty-six months before the application month only the resources transferred on or after the application's day of the month (A.3). The worked example gives 2 + 2 = 4 (CO-3840-T11); transfers on October 3 and 20 against an application on October 15 give the October 20 transfer alone (CO-3840-T14, T19). The periods of a month's resources follow one another (D.5) as the months' periods already do in af_is_in_twfc_penalty_period (CO-3841), which adds each month's figure.

### OQ-1260. SNAP: foster care payments when some foster individuals placed with the person are members and others are not

Body: 7 CFR 273.9(b)(2)(ii) (S4725) counts "foster care payments for children or adults who are considered members of the household", and 273.9(c)(15) (S4725) excludes "Governmental foster care payments received by households with foster care individuals who are considered to be boarders"; a foster individual is a member only "at the request of the household providing the foster care" (273.1(c)(4), S3651). Coverage pass 4 (S4-6) reads the payments by membership: snap_foster_care_payments_are_counted (CO-6601) is true when a foster individual placed with the person buys food and prepares meals with the person. A receipt record (foster_care_payment) or a difficulty of care payment (foster_care) does not say which foster individual it is paid for, and a record field cannot name a person a rule tests for membership inside a receipt sum (docs/operators.md, Receipt records). Where a household includes one foster child and not another, the payments for the one are counted and for the other excluded under the text, but the records cannot say which payment is which. Worked example: $600 a month for each of two foster children, one included: by the text $600 counted; as read here $1,200.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: The payments are counted in full when at least one foster individual placed with the person is a member, and excluded when none is.

Checked 2026-11-23: 7 CFR 273.1(c)(4) (D-7); 7 CFR 273.9(b)(2)(ii), (c)(15) (D-13); 10 CCR 2506-1 4.304.3.B.1 (D-82, S3475). None says how payments for several foster individuals are divided. Items: CO-6601, CO-5010, CO-5101, CO-5011, CO-5113.

### OQ-1261. SNAP: educational assistance and the G.I. Bill housing allowance - Colorado excludes all education assistance, the federal rule counts room and board

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.405.1 (S4732): "All education assistance including grants, scholarships, fellowships, work-study, veteran's educational benefits, and any other money received specifically for educational expenses are exempt from consideration as income." 7 CFR 273.9(c)(3)(i) (S4733) excludes educational assistance, "veterans' educational benefits and the like", but (c)(3)(ii)(C)(9): "Normal living expenses which are room and board are not excludable." SNAP's receipt table excludes educational_assistance in full (Colorado's text) and counts gi_bill_housing_food_allowance because no rule was read to exclude it (the federal reading of a room-and-board allowance). The two rows follow different texts. Worked example: a veteran student with a $1,800 monthly G.I. Bill housing allowance and no other income: counted (today), gross $1,800; excluded under 4.405.1, gross $0. Coverage pass 4 S4-15.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner, Legal
Assumption: Each row keeps its reading: educational assistance excluded in full (4.405.1), the G.I. Bill housing or food allowance counted (273.9(c)(3)(ii)(C)(9)).

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.405.1 (D-82); 7 CFR 273.9(c)(3) (D-13). The volume applies the federal text where Colorado's rule is broader (CO-3700, CO-119, CO-1243, CO-308); whether that reaches 4.405.1 is the choice. Items: CO-5010.

### OQ-1262. SNAP: the day of the month an age is taken on for a month evaluated

Body: Coverage pass 4 (S4-11) makes snap_is_elderly (CO-301) per month: prospective budgeting computes the allotment "based on anticipated income and circumstances during the issuance month" (10 CCR 2506-1 4.402.1.A, S4734), so an application month before the determination month, or a later month, reads its own age. No SNAP text on file says on which day of a month the age is taken. CO-301 reads 60 or older on the last day of the month, so a person who turns 60 during the month is elderly for that month (the status the age grants is met on any day of the month, as Medicaid reads its qualifying ages, docs/operators.md "Ages in a month"); snap_is_in_abawd_age_range (CO-412) reads the first day; snap_student_earnings_are_excluded (CO-2569) reads the day before the month, which 4.405.C sets. Worked example (AGE60): a stepfather born 1966-11-02, applied 2026-10-12: elderly in November (60 on November 30), not in October; read on November 1 he would not be elderly until December, and the household's November allotment would be $743, not $808.

Coverage pass 4 S4-11's remaining ages (OQ-1263, resolved by making them per month) take the same reading of the day: an age that grants a status or an exemption is met when it is met on any day of the month ("under N" on the first day, "N or older" on the last day: a child under 22 or under 18 in the household, CO-305; a child under 6 or 12 cared for, CO-401, CO-408, CO-409; the youth exemption from 16 through 17, CO-405; the student exemptions of 17 or under and 50 or over, CO-410; the non-citizen and military dependent under 18 or 22, CO-3713, CO-4260; the exemption from sponsor deeming and the citizen child under 18, CO-2337, CO-3708; the work registration exemptions under 16 and 60 or older, CO-406, CO-2318), and an age that withholds eligibility or imposes a requirement only when it holds on every day (the ineligible student 18 through 49, CO-411; the work registrant 16 through 59, CO-406; the foster child under 18 barred from applying apart, CO-4689), as docs/operators.md (Ages in a month) reads the Medicaid ages. Worked example (stored household CO-780): a college student who turns 18 on November 15 is not an ineligible student in November (17 on November 1) and is one from December; read on the last day he would be one in November.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: 60 or older on the last day of the month evaluated; for every other SNAP age read in a month, an age that grants a status or an exemption is met when it is met on any day of the month (under an age on the first day, at or over an age on the last day), and an age that withholds eligibility or imposes a requirement only when it holds on every day of it.

Checked 2026-11-23: 7 CFR 271.2 (D-6, S6: "Is 60 years of age or older"); 10 CCR 2506-1 4.000.1, 4.402.1.A (D-82); 4.304.1, 4.306, 4.310.3, 4.305.B.3.f, 4.304.3.B.3 (D-82); 7 U.S.C. 2015(e) (S133), 7 CFR 273.5(b)(1) (S134). None names a day of the month for an age; 4.405.C (the student earnings exclusion "until the month following the month in which the student turns eighteen", CO-2569) is the only SNAP age the text places in a month. Items: CO-301, CO-6603, CO-305, CO-401, CO-405, CO-406, CO-408, CO-409, CO-410, CO-411, CO-2318, CO-2337, CO-3708, CO-3713, CO-4260, CO-4689.

### OQ-1263. Resolved: SNAP: ages and statuses read on the determination date for an application month before the determination month

Body: Coverage pass 4 (S4-11, S4-19) found that SNAP's per-person items are evaluated once per case, on the determination date (time contract month-v1), so an application month earlier than the determination month, or a later month, takes the determination date's ages and statuses, though prospective budgeting reads "anticipated income and circumstances during the issuance month" (4.402.1.A, S4734) and a household whose BCE or ECE circumstances change is re-evaluated (4.206.D, S4735). The fix round made per month the Expanded Categorical Eligibility test and the category (CO-339 to CO-342), the elderly member (CO-301, CO-303, CO-6603) and the readers of the household's elderly or disabled member that are per month (the gross income test, the shelter deduction, the net income with disqualified members). Still read on the determination date: household composition (snap_household_members, CO-305: under 22 living with a parent), the ineligible student and its exemptions (CO-411, CO-410, CO-408, CO-409, CO-401: 18 to 49, a child under 6 or 12), the non-citizen child and military-connection ages (CO-3713, CO-4260), sponsor deeming (CO-2337, CO-3708), voluntary quit (CO-2318), the separate elderly and disabled household (CO-2304), the foster child (CO-4689), and the ABAWD limit (CO-2564, S4-19: the countable months are per month, the limit's verdict per person). Making these per month changes the scope of CO-305 and the chain of per-person items that read it, the resource test's items among them (CO-343, CO-4158 to CO-4160), which the holdings track is moving now. Worked examples: a son who turns 22 between the application month and the determination is a mandatory member in the application month and not after it; a student who turns 50 after applying is an ineligible student in the application month.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: These ages and statuses are taken on the determination date for every month a determination evaluates; a birthday after the determination is a change in circumstances (4.604).

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.206.D, 4.304.1, 4.306, 4.311.3, 4.402.1.A (D-82). The text reads circumstances by issuance month; the day each age is taken on is OQ-1262's. Items: CO-305, CO-411, CO-410, CO-408, CO-409, CO-401, CO-3713, CO-4260, CO-2337, CO-3708, CO-2318, CO-2304, CO-4689, CO-2564.

Resolution (coverage pass 4 for SNAP, S4-11 and S4-19 finished, 2026-11-23; docs/reviews/coverage4-snap.md, section 7): not a state policy choice but a wrong answer, since the text reads the allotment on "anticipated income and circumstances during the issuance month" (4.402.1.A, S4734) and the time contract (month-v1, docs/operators.md "Ages in a month") measures a month-scoped age in the month evaluated. The household (snap_household_members, CO-305) is built for each month and every item listed above is per month, with the chain that reads them: the eligible members and size (CO-311, CO-312, CO-313, CO-2302, CO-2565, CO-4169), the participation status (CO-2571), the non-citizen findings (CO-306 to CO-310, CO-4154), sponsor deeming (CO-2332, CO-3702), the categorical, striker and FDPIR tests (CO-336 to CO-338, CO-3504, CO-4266, CO-3714, CO-3719), the care and foster items (CO-065, CO-4690) and the resource test (CO-343, CO-1740, CO-4158; CO-344 and CO-4158 read the month's elderly or disabled member, CO-6603). The separate elderly household (CO-2304) and the ABAWD limit (CO-2564) read the month evaluated, and the voluntary quit's period (CO-2318) is tested against the month evaluated. The day each age is taken on stays OQ-1262's. Worked examples: a son who turns 22 on November 15 is a mandatory member through November (CO-305-T11 to T13); a student who turns 50 on November 15 is an ineligible student in October and not in November (CO-411-T8, T9); stored household CO-780: a grandmother who turns 60 on November 10 and asks to be a separate household, and her grandson, a college student who turns 18 on November 15, applied 2026-10-12, determined 2026-11-05: October {mother, grandmother, grandson} $408 (before: the grandmother separate in October too, $249 and $197), November {mother, grandson} $387 and the grandmother $306, December the grandson an ineligible student, $131 (before: $387). Items: as listed above.

### OQ-1280. Medicaid SSI method: income Colorado's paragraphs count that 20 CFR 416.1124 does not (interest on a countable resource, a gift to a child with a life-threatening condition, a State's annuity for a veteran)

Body: For three kinds of receipt Colorado's own text names the money as counted unearned income while SSI's methodology does not count it: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.F.5.e (S3001) counts "Interest or dividend payments received from any resources", 20 CFR 416.1124(c)(22) (S2609) does not count "Interest and dividend income from a countable resource"; F.5.c counts "Gifts and inheritances", (c)(21) (S2803) does not count gifts from a 501(c)(3) organization to or for a child under 18 with a life-threatening condition, up to $2,000 a calendar year; F.5.j.vi counts "Veterans benefits other than Aid and Attendance (A&A) and Unusual Medical Expenses (UME)", (c)(24) does not count "Any annuity paid by a State to a person (or his or her spouse) based on the State's determination that the person is" a veteran and blind, disabled or aged. 42 CFR 435.601(b)(2) and (d)(2) (S4754) have the agency apply the SSI methodology and allow one less restrictive, but no more restrictive, than SSI's for the aged, blind and disabled groups. The counting table (ma_ssi_receipt_treatment_table, CO-5510) follows the Colorado paragraph for each, as it did before receipt records; the same conflict for the home equity limit is OQ-820. Worked example (the review's INTEREST): an aged person with Social Security 1,010 and 5 of bank interest: by F.5.e, 1,010 + 5 - 20 = 995 > 994, not aged-eligible; by 416.1124(c)(22), 990, aged. Coverage pass 4, non-MAGI N4-12.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: Colorado's paragraph governs (each is counted), as before receipt records, until the owner decides whether the State's SSI-related methodology follows 416.1124(c)(21), (22) and (24).

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.F.5.c, e, j.vi and F.6 (D-65); 20 CFR 416.1124(c)(21), (22), (24) (D-53, D-207); 42 CFR 435.601(b)(2), (d)(1)-(3) (D-51). Colorado's paragraphs name the money; no paragraph on file says whether the State's methodology for these groups is less restrictive than SSI's on these points, and no document on file (a state plan income methodology page) would settle it. Items: CO-5510.

### OQ-1281. Medicaid SSI method: a housing program's utility allowance paid under an Act 8.100.5.F.6.g does not list

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.F.6.g (S4751) exempts "The value of any assistance paid with respect to a dwelling unit" when paid under the federal housing Acts it lists (the United States Housing Act of 1937, the National Housing Act, section 101 of the Housing and Urban Development Act of 1965, Title V of the Housing Act of 1949, section 202(h) of the Housing Act of 1959, and HUD's Experimental Housing Allowance Program); 20 CFR 416.1124(c)(14) (S4758) lists the same Acts. The receipt kind housing_utility_allowance (receipts, CO-5000) is "A utility allowance paid to the person by a subsidized housing program", a kind the receipts share with every program, and does not say under which Act. The counting table (CO-5510) reads it as paid under the Acts listed (exempt). A utility allowance from a State- or locally-funded housing program under none of them is not named by F.6.g; F.6.j ("Assistance from other agencies and organizations") or 416.1124(c)(2) (State-funded assistance based on need) may reach it. Coverage pass 4, non-MAGI N4-1.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: Every subsidized housing program's utility allowance is exempt, read as paid under the Acts F.6.g lists.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.F.6.g, j (D-65); 20 CFR 416.1124(c)(2), (14) (D-53). Neither says how a non-federal program's allowance is treated beyond F.6.j's general words. Items: CO-5510.

### OQ-1282. Medicaid SSI method: work-study pay other than the federal College Work-Study Program, or of a graduate student

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.F.6.aa (S4674) exempts "Any grant or loan to an undergraduate student for educational purposes made or insured under any programs administered by the Commissioner of Education (...), Pell Grant Program, the PLUS Program, the BYRD Honor Scholarship programs, and the College Work Study Program." The earnings kind work_study (earnings, CO-5100) is "Pay for work under a federal or State work-study program for students", and the record does not say whether the student is an undergraduate. The SSI method's earnings table (ma_ssi_earnings_treatment_table, CO-5107) now holds the kind exempt (coverage pass 4, non-MAGI N4-5). Pay from a State work-study program, or a graduate student's work-study pay, is not named by F.6.aa; it would otherwise be counted as wages, with the student earned income exclusion of 20 CFR 416.1112(c)(3) for a student under 22.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: Every work-study pay is exempt, read as the College Work Study Program F.6.aa names.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.F.6.aa-cc (D-65). No paragraph on file names a State work-study program or limits the earnings rule by the student's level. Items: CO-5107.

Reclassified 2026-11-23 (coverage pass 4, CA4-19): the text decides the count, per resource transferred (3.520.76.D.3: "Divide the uncompensated value by the TWFC monthly penalty value and round down"; E: "for each resource transferred"), so this is not a state policy choice; what is open is how the operators let af_twfc_penalty_months read the divisor and the application date inside the sum over the records.

### OQ-1290. Adult Financial: a qualified Roth IRA distribution, counted by its taxable amount

Body: Coverage pass 4 CA4-3 (docs/reviews/coverage4-cash.md). 9 CCR 2503-5 3.510 (S4790): "“Income” means any financial gain by means of money payment or in-kind payment." 3.520.73.B.1.a (S3208) counts a retirement account as a resource at its gross value less the taxes due "regardless of whether the client, spouse of the client, or sponsor(s) of the client is receiving any monthly income from the account", so money paid from a retirement account is the account's income, while a withdrawal from the person's own health savings, education savings or individual development account for its purpose moves the person's own money and is no gain. The receipt kind savings_account_distribution holds both, and the record states the part the person's federal return counts (taxable_amount, CO-5000), the one statement of the split Medicaid MAGI reads too. Adult Financial counts that part (row counted_taxable_amount): a traditional IRA or 401(k) distribution is counted, an HSA, education account or IDA withdrawal for its purpose is not. A qualified Roth IRA distribution is paid from a retirement account but its taxable amount is 0 (26 U.S.C. 408A(d)(1)), so it is not counted; no Adult Financial text names a Roth distribution. Worked example: an OAP client with $600 of Social Security takes $500 a month from a Roth IRA: read as modeled, countable income 600 - 20 = 580; if a Roth distribution is the account's income, 1,080.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: A savings account distribution counts at its taxable amount: a qualified Roth IRA distribution is not counted.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.510 ("Income"), 3.520.73.B.1, 3.520.785, 3.520.786 (D-80). No paragraph names a distribution from a Roth IRA or a savings account. Items: CO-5016, CO-5017.

### OQ-1291. Adult Financial: "the term of the contract" for pay under an annual contract

Body: Coverage pass 4 CA4-8. 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.781.G.2.b (S4792): "For contract employment, such as in some school systems, where the employees derive their annual income in a period shorter than a year, the income shall be prorated over the term of the contract, provided that the income from the contract is not earned on an hourly or piecework basis". The earnings record states such pay with the contract's pay for the year (annual_contract_pay, CO-5100, a contract renewed on an annual basis). The volume reads "the term of the contract" as the contract year, twelve months (af_annual_contract_months, CO-6802), so the annual pay is spread over every month, the months without pay included: review household A10b, a $24,000 school cafeteria contract paid September to May, counts $2,000 in July. The other reading is the months the contract pays (nine), which gives $24,000 / 9 in a month of the school year and nothing in summer. SNAP (10 CCR 2506-1 4.403.3) and CCCAP (OQ-1221) read twelve months.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: The term of an annual contract is twelve months: one twelfth of the contract's annual pay is counted in each month.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.510 and 3.520.781.G.2.b (D-80); neither defines "contract employment" or "term of the contract". Items: CO-6802, CO-5130.

### OQ-1292. Adult Financial: whether exempt income uses up the $60 of infrequent or irregular income in a quarter

Body: Coverage pass 4 CA4-18. 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.786.O (S2608) exempts "Infrequent or irregular income of less than $60 in a calendar quarter." af_infrequent_income_quarter_total (CO-4011) adds every receipt and pay of the quarter stated as irregular, the kinds the counting tables exclude included (an income tax refund marked irregular, work-study pay), so exempt money can use up the $60 and make a small gift countable. The text does not say whether the $60 is measured on all infrequent income or only on the income that would otherwise count; 20 CFR 416.1124(c)(6) (S2609), which K incorporates, applies its own $60 after the other exclusions ("infrequent or irregular income ... if such income does not exceed $60 in a calendar quarter", read in the order of 416.1124(b)). Worked example (review probe A19): an OAP client with $600 of Social Security receives a $40 irregular gift in October and an $800 income tax refund in November marked irregular: read as modeled, the quarter's total is 840, the gift is counted, countable income 620, grant $412; read over countable income only, the total is 40, the gift is exempt, 580, grant $452.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: Every receipt and pay stated as irregular counts toward the quarter's $60, exempt kinds included (today's reading), until the owner decides.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.786.O and K (D-80); 20 CFR 416.1124(b), (c)(6) (D-207). Items: CO-4011, CO-3827, CO-4012.

### OQ-1293. Adult Financial: kinship care payments, adoption subsidies and Medicare premium refunds counted because no rule excludes them

Body: Coverage pass 4 CA4-23. Adult Financial's receipt table (af_receipt_treatment_table, CO-5016) counts kinship_care_payment, adoption_subsidy and medicare_premium_refund because no paragraph of 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.785-786 names them. 3.520.786.N (S2608) exempts "Child support payments made to the client, client’s spouse, or sponsor(s) for another person", and M exempts "Payments received for providing foster care"; a kinship care payment or an adoption subsidy is paid to the caretaker for a child, as support for another person is, but is not child support. A lump-sum refund of Medicare Part B premiums the person paid returns the person's own money, which may be no "financial gain" (3.510 "Income", S4790), or a gain because the premiums were paid from income already counted. The text on file settles none of the three.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: Each is counted as unearned income of the person it is paid to (today's reading), until the owner signs the row or excludes it.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.510, 3.520.785, 3.520.786 (D-80); 20 CFR 416.1124 (D-207) names none of them. Items: CO-5016.

### OQ-1294. Adult Financial: the cost of a home used for the business and a business vehicle

Body: Coverage pass 4 CA4-20. 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.783.B.1 (S1156): "Cost of doing business expenses include, but are not limited to, the rent of business premises, wholesale cost of merchandise, utilities, interest, taxes, labor and upkeep of necessary equipment". Adult Financial's earnings table (af_earnings_treatment_table, CO-5103) does not deduct business_cost_home_space or business_cost_vehicle (not_deducted_no_rule_found): "not limited to" leaves both open, and Colorado Works' own rule names the room cost (9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.2.E.1.a.1). Worked example: a client earns $900 a month from a home business with $200 of supplies and $150 of the home's costs for its room: read as modeled, net profit 700; deducting the room, 550.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: Neither cost is deducted (today's reading), until the owner signs the rows or deducts them.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.783.B.1-3, D (D-80). Items: CO-5103, CO-1707.

### OQ-1295. Colorado Works: the exception for contract employment in 3.605.2.B

Body: Coverage pass 4 CA4-21. 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.2.B (D-81): "With the exception of contract employment, wages that are paid to an employee for a period for which services were rendered are considered available when paid rather than when earned, except that wages held at the request of the employee are considered income in the month they would otherwise have been paid." The rule makes contract employment an exception but does not say how its wages are counted: "when earned" (the opposite of "when paid"), or prorated over the contract, as SNAP (4.403.3), CCCAP (3.111.H.4.b) and Adult Financial (3.520.781.G.2.b) read their own words. The earnings record states the contract's yearly pay (annual_contract_pay, CO-5100); cw_earned_pay (CO-5140) does not read it and counts every pay when paid. Worked example: a school aide's $18,000 contract paid $2,000 a month September to May: read as modeled, $2,000 in each month of the school year and nothing in summer; prorated, $1,500 every month.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: Contract pay is counted when paid (today's reading), until the owner says how the exception is read.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.601, 3.605.2.B (D-81); neither defines contract employment. Items: CO-5140, CO-5104.

### OQ-1305. Resolved: SNAP: one funeral agreement per member excluded up to $1,500 (Colorado) or in full (federal)

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.410.D.4 (S2967): "One bona fide pre-purchased funeral agreement per household member ... shall be excluded provided that the agreement does not exceed one thousand five hundred dollars ($1,500) in equity value; the equity value over one thousand five hundred dollars ($1,500) is counted as a resource." 7 CFR 273.8(e)(2) (S2216; D-12, D-191) excludes "one burial plot per household member, and the value of one funeral agreement per household member", with no limit, and 273.8(a) (S4820) requires the State agency to "apply the uniform national resource standards of eligibility to all applicant households"; (e) lists "only the following" exclusions. Worked example (coverage pass 4 for SNAP resources, FUN2): a household resource-tested at $3,000 with $2,000 in the bank and one $3,000 agreement for a member: under 4.410.D.4, 2,000 + 1,500 = 3,500, over the limit; under 273.8(e)(2), 2,000, eligible.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner, Legal
Assumption: none (resolved).

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.410.D.4 (D-82); 7 CFR 273.8(a), (e)(2) (D-12, D-191). Items: CO-5312, CO-4177, CO-5301.

Resolution (coverage pass 4 for SNAP resources, 2026-11-23, SR-1): the volume applies 7 CFR 273 where a Colorado rule is narrower (statute_override on CO-344, CO-119, CO-3704, CO-1382, CO-409), and 273.8(a) and (e) leave no State standard for this exclusion (the State option of (e)(19) adds TANF exclusions, it removes none). snap_countable_funeral_agreements (CO-5312) excludes the whole equity of one agreement per household member, with a statute_override citing (e)(2); the $1,500 figure (snap_funeral_agreement_exclusion, CO-4177) is no longer read. If the owner reads 4.410.D.4 as a standard the State may set, the override is removed and the cap read again.

### OQ-1306. Resolved: SNAP: the resources of a member who receives SSI or public assistance (7 CFR 273.8(e)(17), which 4.410 omits)

Body: 7 CFR 273.8(e)(17) (S4821; D-191): "The resources of a household member who receives SSI or PA benefits. A household member is considered a recipient of these benefits if the benefits have been authorized but not received, if the benefits are suspended or recouped, or if the benefits are not paid because they are less than a minimum amount." 10 CCR 2506-1 4.410 lists no such exclusion. A household where every member receives SSI or public assistance is categorically eligible (4.206.C.1.a) and not resource-tested; a mixed household that is not, or one barred from expanded categorical eligibility (a member serving an IPV disqualification), counts the recipient's resources under Colorado's text. Worked example (SR-2, SSI2): a father serving an IPV disqualification with $3,000 and his disabled daughter on SSI with $1,900: Colorado's text 4,900, over the $4,750 limit; (e)(17) 3,000, eligible.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner, Legal
Assumption: none (resolved).

Checked 2026-11-23: 7 CFR 273.8(a), (e)(17) (D-191); 10 CCR 2506-1 4.408, 4.410, 4.206.C.1.a (D-82). Items: CO-343, CO-1740.

Resolution (coverage pass 4 for SNAP resources, 2026-11-23, SR-2): applied as federal law over the narrower Colorado text, with a statute_override on snap_household_countable_resources (CO-343) and on snap_household_liquid_resources (CO-1740, the expedited test's liquid resources, which are resources of the household under (e)'s opening words). A recipient is a member who receives SSI, Colorado Works cash, or Old Age Pension, Aid to the Needy Disabled or Aid to the Blind (receives_ssi, receives_tanf_cash, receives_state_cash_assistance: the public assistance of 4.206.C.1.a); the facts' meanings include benefits authorized. If the owner reads the expedited test's "liquid resources, as declared by the household" (4.408.C) as every member's, CO-1740's override is removed.

### OQ-1307. SNAP: when a trust is inaccessible: Colorado's conditions joined by "or", the federal by "and"; annuities

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.410.F.3 (S2968) makes "Any funds in a trust or transferred to a trust" inaccessible if "a. The trust arrangement will not likely cease during the certification period, and no household member has the power to revoke ...; or b. The trustee administering the funds is either a court, an institution ...; or c. The trust investments ... do not directly involve or assist any business ... under the control ... of a household member; or d. The funds held in irrevocable trust are either established from household's own funds and are used solely to make investments ... or established from non-household funds by a non-household member". 7 CFR 273.8(e)(8) (S4824) joins the same four conditions with "and" ("(iii) ... ; and (iv) ..."), so under the federal text a trust is inaccessible only when all four hold. Under Colorado's "or" almost every trust is inaccessible (condition c alone holds for most). 273.8(c)(2) (S4820) counts "any other property" not excluded, so a revocable trust, and an annuity the person can surrender or assign, are resources unless (e) excludes them. The holdings record states whether a trust or annuity is revocable and the corpus that can be paid to the person (revocable, corpus_available_value), not the trustee or the trust's investments. Worked example: a revocable trust of $5,000 administered by a bank: under Colorado's "or" (b) it is inaccessible; under (e)(8) it is not ((i) fails), and $5,000 counts.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner, Legal
Assumption: A trust interest is counted at its value unless the county has found its funds inaccessible under 4.410.F.3 (SNAP's finding on the holdings record): the county applies F.3's conditions as the owner reads them, and the volume reads its finding, so a revocable trust with no finding cannot be determined (decision 4) rather than being left out. Annuities are not counted, as before; if the owner reads the federal "and" as governing, a surrenderable annuity's value is counted.

Field sweep fix round (2026-11-23; docs/reviews/field-sweep-snap-cash-nonmagi.md, FS-SNAP-4): before, the trust_interest row was not_counted_reading_open, so a revocable trust of $10,000 counted nothing under either reading (F.3.a fails for it, so under "or" it is inaccessible only if a trustee or investment condition holds, and under "and" it is never inaccessible). The row is now counted_unless_found_inaccessible; the open part is how the county applies F.3 ("or" or "and"), not whether the volume reads the trust.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.410.F.3 (D-82); 7 CFR 273.8(c)(2), (e)(8) (D-191). Items: CO-5301, CO-6900, CO-074, CO-4176, CO-5300.

### OQ-1308. SNAP: the measure of "income consistent with its fair market value" for income-producing property and installment contracts

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.410.E.1 (S3477): "Any property that is producing an annual income consistent with its fair market value in the community ... shall be an exempt resource"; E.2 (S3118): installment contracts "if the contract or agreement is producing income consistent with its fair market value"; 7 CFR 273.8(e)(4), (e)(6) (S4822) the same, and (e)(5) property "essential to the employment or self-employment of a household member" with no value test. No text on file sets the measure (a rate of return on the value, the return of comparable property in the community). Coverage pass 4 for SNAP resources (SR-5) reads the test: property used in the person's trade or business is exempt ((e)(5)); other income-producing property and installment contracts are exempt only when their net return over the last twelve months (net_annual_return) is more than 0 and the county has found it consistent with the value (snap_return_consistent_with_value, SNAP's finding, decision D9); a property that returned nothing is counted. Worked example (RENT): a $40,000 rental that produced nothing last year is counted; one returning $3,600 a year (9%) is exempt if the county finds that consistent.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: The county's finding decides a positive return; a return of 0 or less is never consistent with a positive value.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.410.E.1-2 (D-82); 7 CFR 273.8(e)(4)-(6) (D-191). Items: CO-5310, CO-6900, CO-5301, CO-5300.

### OQ-1309. SNAP: the first day of the three months before the date of application when the earlier month lacks the day

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.408.2 (S2961) reads transfers "within the three (3) month period immediately preceding the date of application". snap_disqualifying_transfer_value (CO-5314) and CO-5315 run back to the same day of the month three calendar months earlier, and a day the earlier month lacks runs into the next month: applied May 31, 2026, the window opens March 3 (February 31 read as March 3), not February 28 (the last day of the earlier month). Worked example: a transfer on March 1, 2026 to qualify, for an application on May 31, 2026: in the window if it opens February 28, outside it if it opens March 3.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: The day runs forward (March 3), as since the second final verification pass.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.408.2 (D-82); 7 CFR 273.8(h) (D-191). Neither says. Items: CO-5314, CO-5315.

### OQ-1310. SNAP: the day of a month other than the month of application on which resources are valued

Body: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.408.1 (S4823) values resources "at the application filing date", and 4.408.B uses resources "as anticipated to be available in the issuance month". Coverage pass 4 for SNAP resources (SR-9) values each month's resources on a day: the filing date in the month of application, and the first day of any other month, the day a holding disposed of later in the month is still held and the day a refund's twelve months are read against. No text on file names the day of a later month. Worked example (T1b): a household applies 2026-11-02 and is determined 2027-02-10; $4,000 it gives away (not to qualify) on 2027-02-20 is held on 2027-02-01 and counts in February; read on the determination date it would count too; read as anticipated at the month's end it would not.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: The first day of a month other than the month of application.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2506-1 4.408.B, 4.408.1, 4.402.1.A (D-82); 7 CFR 273.8 (D-191). Items: CO-4176, CO-5310, CO-5311, CO-5312, CO-5313, CO-6900, CO-343.
### OQ-1315. CCCAP: "all children in the home who are under eighteen" and a child whose own parent lives in the home

Body: Coverage pass 5, C5-4 (docs/reviews/coverage5-care-energy.md). 8 CCR 1403-1 3.103.LLL (S3636): "'Household' includes: all children in the home who are under eighteen (18) years of age; all children under nineteen (19) years of age who are still in high school and the responsibility of the adult caretaker(s); and the adult caretaker(s) or teen parent(s)." The responsibility test is written only for the eighteen-year-old student; the volume (cccap_household_members, CO-982) joins a child under eighteen to the household only through a relationship to a caretaker (parent, guardian, primary responsibility, financial contribution, step-relations), so a child in the home whose own parent also lives there and is not a caretaker of the applicant's child is outside the applicant's household. Worked example: two sisters share a home, each with a child of five; the first applies for her child. On the volume's reading the household is the applicant and her child (size 2, her income); on the literal reading it is every child in the home under eighteen with the applicant (size 3, the second sister's child counted, though not her income), which raises the entry limit. Neither reading is settled by the text.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: A child under eighteen is in the household through a relationship to a caretaker (the volume's reading).

Checked 2026-11-23: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.103.C (adult caretaker), 3.103.LLL, 3.103.OOO, 3.111.D (D-87, rule version 12658); none says whether "in the home" alone makes a child a member. Items: CO-982, CO-983.

### OQ-1316. CCCAP and LEAP: the income of jointly owned property that the share of ownership is taken of

Body: Coverage pass 5, X5-1. 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.H.4.e (S2704) and 9 CCR 2503-7 3.752.21.F (S4850) count income from jointly owned property "as a percentage at least equal to the percentage of ownership or, if receiving more than percentage of ownership, the actual amount received". The receipt record now states the owner's share (ownership_share) and the property's income for the record's period (property_income) (receipts, CO-5000), and the readers count the larger of share x property income and the amount received (CCCAP: cccap_counted_recurring_receipts CO-5721, cccap_one_time_receipts_averaged CO-5722; LEAP: leap_counted_recurring_receipts CO-5003, leap_one_time_receipts_in_period CO-5009). Neither rule says whether the property's income is taken before or after the property's expenses, nor whose expenses a co-owner deducts: CCCAP counts rent net of the landlord's expenses (I.12, I.17; rental_property_expenses_paid, taken against the counted rent, CO-4463), and LEAP counts unearned income gross (3.752.22.A), though 3.755.21.I.2.D limits a rental property's allowable expenses to the costs of its maintenance in the self-employment verification paragraph; whether that reaches a landlord's rent stated as unearned income is OQ-1365 (coverage pass 6, R6-3; this record before said only that LEAP counts gross unearned income).

Two co-owners in one household (coverage pass 6, R6-2). Each co-owner who is a person of the case states the property's income on the own list with what that co-owner received and names the other (co_owner, CO-5000). Applied to each co-owner's list alone, the share test counted the property twice: the co-owner who received nothing still counted the share the other had received and counted in full (probes CJ1, CCCAP: $4,400, ineligible_income, against $4,100; LJ1, LEAP: $5,500, ineligible, against $4,500). The volume applies the test once per property in the household: a record whose co-owner is another member of the household (cccap_household_members, leap_household_members_for_income) counts what the person received, so the household counts what its members received from the property, and the share is the floor only against owners outside the household (CO-5721, CO-5722, CO-5723, CO-4076, CO-4463; CO-5003, CO-5009, CO-4526). The text does not say how the floor applies when the household's co-owners together receive less than their combined shares because an owner outside the household keeps more than that owner's share (a property of three owners, two in the household): the volume then counts what the household's co-owners received, without the floor; the other reading takes the household's combined share times the property's income when it is larger. Worked example: a property with $3,000 of rent owned a third each by a couple in the household and a brother outside it, who keeps $2,000: the couple received $1,000 together; the volume counts $1,000, the combined-share reading $2,000. Worked example: a half-owned rental house with $2,000 of rent and $600 of expenses, the co-owner keeping everything: gross, the share is $1,000 (LEAP) and CCCAP takes the owner's own expenses off it; net of the property's expenses, the share is $700.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC), Energy assistance (LEAP) policy owner
Assumption: The property's income is stated as each program counts the owner's own rent: gross for LEAP; for CCCAP, the property's rent with the owner's own expenses deducted as for any rent. Two co-owners in one household: the share test is applied against owners outside the household only, each member counting what that member received.

Checked 2026-11-23: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.H.4.e, I.12, I.17 (D-87); 9 CCR 2503-7 3.752.21.F, 3.752.22.A (D-86). Checked again 2026-11-24 (coverage pass 6): 9 CCR 2503-7 3.755.21.I.2.D (D-86). Items: CO-5721, CO-5722, CO-5723, CO-4076, CO-4463, CO-5003, CO-5009, CO-4526.

### OQ-1317. LEAP: whether 3.752.23.EE's "income tax" governs refunds, rebates and credits alike

Body: Coverage pass 5, X5-2 (and coverage pass 4 X4-2). 9 CCR 2503-7 3.752.23.EE (S2208) excludes "Money received in the form of non-recurring lump sum payments for income tax refunds, rebates, or credits". The volume reads "income tax" as governing all three (an income tax refund, an income tax rebate, an income tax credit), so a refund of property or food taxes is counted under 3.752.21's "payments from any other source" (leap_receipt_treatment_table, CO-5001, property_or_food_tax_refund: counted). The other reading excludes any lump-sum refund, rebate or credit, of any tax or none (a utility's rebate). Worked example: a $400 property tax refund paid once in the income verification period with $3,500 of other income for one: counted, $3,900 > $3,774, ineligible; excluded, $3,500, eligible.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Energy assistance (LEAP) policy owner
Assumption: "Income tax" governs refunds, rebates and credits; a property or food tax refund is counted.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.752.21, 3.752.23 (D-86) and the amendments effective 2026-11-01 (D-173); no definition of a rebate or credit. Items: CO-5001.

### OQ-1318. CCCAP: whether the conversion of the person's own asset is income under 3.111.I's list

Body: Coverage pass 5, X5-2 (with OQ-873). 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.I (S3256, S4020) lists what is "considered countable income" and 3.111.J what is not; the volume counts a receipt neither names (OQ-873's reading; cccap_receipt_treatment_table, CO-5720, counted_no_rule_excludes). Three such kinds convert an asset the person already owns into cash: the proceeds of selling a personal resource (resource_liquidation), cash value taken out of one's own insurance policy (insurance_cash_value), and a distribution from one's own health, education or development savings account (savings_account_distribution where it is not a retirement account's, which I.8 names). 3.103.K (S1414) counts "money in checking or savings accounts" and "lump sum payments as specified in rule section 3.111(H)(4)" among assets, which the caretaker self-declares (H.5.c), and LEAP, Adult Financial and the SSI method treat such a conversion as no income (3.752.23.GG; 3.510; 20 CFR 416.1103). Worked example: a caretaker of two with $2,500 of wages sells her car for $4,000 in the thirty days: counted at one twelfth (H.4.i), $2,833.33; not income, $2,500.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: Counted because no rule excludes it, at one twelfth when paid once (the volume's reading of the closed list, as for a diversion that is not cash assistance).

Checked 2026-11-23: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.103.K, 3.111.H.4.i, I, J (D-87); no paragraph names the sale of a resource, a policy's cash value or a non-retirement savings account. Items: CO-5720, CO-5721, CO-5722.

### OQ-1319. LEAP: a distribution from one's own savings account and a Medicare premium refund

Body: Coverage pass 5, X5-2. The LEAP table counts savings_account_distribution and medicare_premium_refund because no rule excludes them (leap_receipt_treatment_table, CO-5001). For a distribution from the person's own health, education or development account, 3.752.23.GG (S3302) is a candidate ("Money obtained from the liquidation of personal resources"), as it now is for insurance_cash_value (L5-4); a retirement account's distribution is a candidate for 3.752.211.A-B (annuities, "Pension or retirement payments ... from any insurance or other public or private fund", S4100) as much as for GG. For a refund of Medicare Part B premiums, 3.752.23.S (S2208) is a candidate ("Reimbursement for past or future expenses, to the extent they do not exceed actual expenses, and do not represent gain or benefit to the household"). Worked example: $3,000 of wages for one and a $1,000 health savings account withdrawal in the period: counted, $4,000, ineligible; excluded under GG, $3,000, eligible.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Energy assistance (LEAP) policy owner
Assumption: Both counted because no rule excludes them; the rows carry the candidate paragraphs for the owner's signature.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.752.21, 3.752.211, 3.752.23 (D-86, D-173). Items: CO-5001.

### OQ-1320. LEAP: which retroactive payments are "public assistance payments" under 3.752.23.EE

Body: Coverage pass 5, L5-3. 3.752.23.EE (S2208) excludes "retroactive lump-sum social security, SSI, and public assistance payments".
