The volume reads "public assistance" as the public assistance income 3.751.1 defines (S3309: Colorado Works, OAP, AND, AB, NCRA, and SSDI for clients on another state program), so a retroactive payment of colorado_works_cash or adult_financial_cash is excluded with Social Security and SSI (leap_one_time_receipts_in_period, CO-5009), and any other retroactive payment received in the period is counted (3.752.22.A). 3.751.1's definition is written "For purposes of verifying income", so a county's general assistance or emergency assistance paid retroactively could also be read as public assistance for EE. Worked example: $3,000 of wages for one and $900 of retroactive county general assistance paid once in the period: counted, $3,900, ineligible; excluded, $3,000, eligible.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Energy assistance (LEAP) policy owner
Assumption: The public assistance payments of EE are the programs 3.751.1 defines as public assistance income.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.751.1 "Public Assistance Income", 3.752.21.L, 3.752.23.EE (D-86). Items: CO-5009.

### OQ-1321. CCCAP and LEAP: military retired pay paid directly to a former spouse under a court order

Body: Field sweep of coverage pass 5 (docs/reviews/field-sweep-care-energy.md). The receipt's paid_to_former_spouse (CO-5000) states the part of military retired pay a court order divides to a former spouse, which DFAS pays to the former spouse; the record's amount is the gross pay. CCCAP counts "Retirement or pension payments paid by defense finance and accounting services (DFAS) to retired members of the Armed Forces" (8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.I.7.a, S4020) and the income "received" in the prior thirty days (H.4, S2704); LEAP counts "Pension or retirement payments - payments to an applicant or recipient following retirement" (9 CCR 2503-7 3.752.211.B, S4100) and makes "Amounts withheld from unearned income because of a garnishment" countable (3.752.21.K). The part paid to the former spouse is not paid to the member, which supports leaving it out; read as an amount withheld from the member's pay, K and I.1's "before deductions" reading count it. Neither text names a court-ordered division. The volume reads neither program's figure net of it (the counting readers take the gross amount), as before. Worked example: a retiree with $2,000 of retired pay, $800 of it paid by DFAS to a former spouse, and $2,500 of wages for two in CCCAP: gross, $4,500; net, $3,700.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC), Energy assistance (LEAP) policy owner
Assumption: The gross retired pay is counted, the part paid to the former spouse included, until the owners say otherwise.

Checked 2026-11-23: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.H.4, I.1, I.7.a, I.8 (D-87); 9 CCR 2503-7 3.752.21.K, 3.752.211.B, 3.752.22.A (D-86). Items: CO-5721, CO-5003.

### OQ-1345. Medicaid long-term care: the community spouse's income, gross of an award's claim expenses

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.7.R.2 (S1756): "The community spouse's income shall be calculated by using the gross income less mandatory deduct ions for FICA and Medicare tax." The SSI method subtracts from an award the part that is an expense of getting it, and from a retroactive check the legal fees of the claim (20 CFR 416.1123(b)(3), S4756; ma_ssi_unearned_receipts, CO-5511), but R.2 names its own measure for the monthly income allowance and no other deduction. ma_community_spouse_income (CO-2467) reads the community spouse's receipts at their gross amount (ma_spousal_unearned_receipts, CO-5531), an award's claim_expense and a retroactive payment's legal fees included. Field-effect sweep (docs/reviews/field-sweep-snap-cash-nonmagi.md), CO-2467. Worked example: the community spouse receives 1,500 of workers' compensation, 1,200 of it for the claim's legal and medical expenses; R.2 gives 1,500 of income, so the monthly income allowance is 1,200 smaller than under the SSI method's 300.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The gross amount (R.2's words): claim expenses are not taken off the community spouse's income.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.7.R.1-2 (D-65); 20 CFR 416.1123(b)(3) (D-53). No text on file applies 416.1123(b) to the community spouse's income. Items: CO-2467, CO-5531.

### OQ-1346. Medicaid SSI method holdings: whose burial spaces are the immediate family's

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.M.2.h (S4504) exempts "burial spaces for the applicant/recipient, his/her spouse and any other members of his/her immediate family" and does not define the family; the SSI rule it follows by 42 CFR 435.601(b)(2) (S4754) defines it: "an individual's minor and adult children, including adopted children and step-children; an individual's brothers, sisters, parents, adoptive parents, and the spouses of those individuals" (20 CFR 416.1231(a)(4), S4943). The holdings field for_immediate_family (CO-5300) was written for Adult Financial (9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.77.I): the person, the spouse or another member of the immediate family, "or a brother or sister of one of them, or that person's spouse", which reaches the spouse's brothers and sisters, whom 416.1231(a)(4) does not name. Field-effect sweep, FS-MA-5: ma_ssi_holdings_countable_in_month (CO-5320) excludes a space for_immediate_family states. Worked example: a plot held for the applicant's wife's brother, 3,000: excluded as the field reads; counted under 416.1231(a)(4) (resources 3,000 more).

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The field's class (a space for the spouse's brother or sister is excluded).

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.M.2.h (D-65); 20 CFR 416.1231(a)(1), (a)(4) (D-54); 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.77.I (D-80). Items: CO-5300, CO-5303, CO-5320.

### OQ-1347. Medicaid SSI method holdings: a joint account held with another person of the case who is also a claimant

Body: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.M.3.d (S4941): "All funds in a joint account are presumed to be a resource of the applicant or client. If there is more than one applicant or client account holder, it is presumed that the funds in the account belong to those individuals in equal shares"; 20 CFR 416.1208(c)(1)-(2) (S4502, S4942) the same for SSI claimants and deemors. Field-effect sweep, FS-MA-7: ma_ssi_holdings_countable_in_month (CO-5320) reads a bank account held with someone outside the case, or with another person of the case who is not the spouse, at its whole value unless Medicaid finds the presumption rebutted (ma_joint_account_presumption_rebutted), and an account held with the spouse at the share stated (the couple counts it once; a deemed spouse's resources are counted with the person's). The case does not say whether another person of the case on the account is also an applicant or client for Medical Assistance; when both are, M.3.d gives each half, and the codex counts it whole on each list. Likewise a deeming parent on a child's account: 416.1208(c)(1) gives all the funds to the one claimant, and the codex counts the account whole on the parent's list too, so the parent's deemed resources include it again. Worked example: a 4,000 account held by two disabled sisters who both apply: 2,000 each under M.3.d; 4,000 each as read. A disabled child's 3,000 account held with her mother: 3,000 for the child under 416.1208(c)(1), and the mother's deemed resources 0 from it; as read, 3,000 also in the mother's resources above her deeming allowance.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: Whole value on the list of each person of the case on the account who is not the spouse (the presumption for one applicant account holder), until a fact says the other account holder applies.

Checked 2026-11-23: 10 CCR 2505-10 8.100.5.M.3.d (D-65); 20 CFR 416.1208(c) (D-4400). Items: CO-5300, CO-5320.

### OQ-1348. Resolved: Medicaid SSI method: a veterans benefit's amount for a dependent as the dependent's own income

Body: 20 CFR 416.1123(e)(2) (S4756): "If you are a dependent of an individual who receives a veterans benefit and a portion of the benefit is attributable to you as a dependent, we count the amount attributable to you as your unearned cash income if (i) You reside with the individual who receives the veterans benefit, or (ii) You receive your own separate payment". The veteran's side, (e)(1), is read (for_dependent, CO-5511); the dependent's is not. The field-effect sweep (FS-MA-3) asked for a field naming the dependent on the veteran's record, as for_person names the person a burial holding is for. The fix round found that the operators cannot read it: the dependent's figure would have to sum the records on another person's list (the veteran's, through `["of", ["P"], receipts]`) whose person field names the dependent, the person evaluated; inside a receipt sum a condition reads only the record's fields, the month, case facts, parameters and that person ["P"] (docs/operators.md, P55), and P is then the veteran. SNAP's burial reading (CO-5313) works because the reader is the holder and the member is P; here the reader is the person named. No field is added until a reader can read it. Text decides the reading; what is open is the operator. Worked example: p2, the veteran, receives 1,500 of which 300 is for p1, his wife, who lives with him: p1's SSI-method unearned income should be 300; the codex gives 0 (and 1,200 for p2).

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: none (resolved).

Checked 2026-11-23: 20 CFR 416.1123(e) (D-53); docs/operators.md "Receipt records" (P55, "A record that names a person"). Items: CO-5000, CO-5511, CO-5514, CO-6730.

Resolution (cross-list records round, 2026-11-24; docs/reviews/cross-list-records.md): the operators already read it. Inside a sum over another person's list (`["of", ["P"], receipts]`) this person is unchanged, so the record's person field is compared with this person as the persons joined to this person by no edge (`[reachable, [spouse], false]`, the person alone), the pattern Adult Financial and Colorado Works use for the same sentence since the field sweep fix round (CO-5017, CO-5651; the receipt's `for_person` field, CO-5000). ma_ssi_unearned_receipts (CO-5511), ma_ssi_gross_unearned_receipts (CO-5514) and ma_ssi_irregular_unearned_receipts (CO-6730) add, for the dependent, the part of each counted receipt of a person of the case the dependent lives with (lives_with, CO-1925) whose for_person names the dependent, (e)(2)(i); a dependent the VA pays separately states that payment on the dependent's own list, (e)(2)(ii). The worked example gives p1 300 and p2 1,200 (CO-5511-T26, T27). No operator was added.

### OQ-1335. Colorado Works: which twelve months irregular self-employment income is averaged over, and when receipts "vary significantly"

Body: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.2.E.3 (S4913): "If receipt of self-employment income is irregular or varies significantly from month to month, it shall be averaged over a twelve-month period." The paragraph names no period (Adult Financial's 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.783.F, S4914, names two: the previous tax year or the twelve months before the application date) and no measure of a significant variation. The field sweep fix round (FS-CW-5) averages, in each month, the net profit of the twelve months ending with that month, for a person with a business receipts record marked irregular in those twelve months (cw_self_employment_net_profit, CO-1706; cw_irregular_self_employment_averaging_months, CO-7100). Worked example: $2,400 of irregular receipts in February 2027: $200 in each month from February 2027 to January 2028 under the assumption; under the previous tax year, $200 in each month of 2028 and nothing in 2027; under the twelve months before an application in March 2027, $200 in each month of the certification. A business whose receipts are regular but vary from month to month has no field: it is stated irregular meanwhile.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: The twelve months ending with the month evaluated; "varies significantly" is stated as irregular on the business receipts record.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.1, 3.605.2.E (D-81); 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.783.F (D-80) for comparison. Neither names the Colorado Works period. Items: CO-1706, CO-7100, CO-5100.

### OQ-1336. Adult Financial: who chooses between the two twelve-month periods for irregular self-employment income

Body: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.783.F (S4914): "If receipt of self-employment income is irregular, it shall be averaged over a twelve-month period. The twelve-month period can either be the previous tax year (January to December) or the most recent twelve-months prior to the application date." The text allows either and does not say whether the county department or the client chooses, or by what test (the period that better represents expected income). The field sweep fix round (FS-AF-3) reads the period the determination used as a supplied fact (af_self_employment_averaging_period, CO-7102), required for a person with irregular business receipts. Worked example: $2,400 of irregular receipts in May 2026, a client applying in March 2027: $200 a month under either period; with the receipts in February 2027 instead, $200 under the twelve months before the application and nothing under the previous tax year.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: The county department states the period it used; no default.

Checked 2026-11-23: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.783.E-F (D-80). Items: CO-1708, CO-7101, CO-7102.

### OQ-1375. Adult Financial: whether a spouse approved in the same determination is "receiving Adult Financial grant payments"

Body: Coverage pass 6 CA6-8 (docs/reviews/coverage6-cash.md). 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.63.B (D-80): "If married, both spouses may apply for and/or receive Adult Financial programs. Each spouse shall have a separate case." 3.534.C (S1632): "If a client's spouse is receiving Adult Financial grant payments, SSI benefits, or Medicaid assistance and has income no greater than the OAP limit, his or her income shall not be considered as available to the client and shall not be deemed"; 3.544.F and 3.549.F say the same with the SSI limit (S1633, S1634). When both spouses apply together neither yet has a grant payment, so each spouse's income is deemed to the other; once both are paid, neither is. The text does not say whether a spouse approved in the same determination (or one whose application is pending) is "receiving". Worked example (probe A2): an OAP couple, the husband with $800 of wages and no other income, the wife with Social Security: read as payments actually received (the reading in force), the husband's income is deemed (800 - 65 = 735, halved, 367.5) and the wife's grant is $189; read as "a spouse who is an Adult Financial client", nothing is deemed and her grant is $442. Neither reading changes the husband's own grant ($669).

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: "Receiving" is read from the Adult Financial grant payments the case states for the spouse, so a spouse who applies in the same determination and has no payment yet is not receiving, and the spouse's income is deemed until a payment is made.

Checked 2026-11-24: 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.63.B, 3.534.C, 3.544.F, 3.549.F (D-80, rule version 12428, effective 03/02/2026). None defines "receiving" for a spouse whose own application is decided at the same time. The payments stated are the spouse's receipts of kind adult_financial_cash (af_grant_receipts, CO-5019). Items: CO-2384.

### OQ-1385. Medicaid SSI method: whether an aged, blind or disabled spouse who has not applied for SSI is an "ineligible spouse" for 20 CFR 416.1163(d)

Body: Coverage pass 6, non-MAGI F6-6 (docs/reviews/coverage6-medicaid-nonmagi.md). 20 CFR 416.1163(d)(1) (S5031) deems nothing from an ineligible spouse whose income after allocations is not more than the couple Federal Benefit Rate less the individual one, and tests the person's own income against the individual rate; 42 CFR 435.601(b)(2) (S4754) applies it where 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.5.H.1.a (S1746) names no standard. Colorado's H.2.c (S1506) defines the ineligible spouse as "a parent or spouse who is not applying or eligible for SSI". A spouse who is not aged, blind or disabled can never be eligible for SSI, and the codex applies (d) to such a spouse (ma_ssi_ineligible_spouse_income_is_not_deemed, CO-7353). A spouse who is aged, blind or disabled but has not applied for SSI and does not receive it is, on H.2.c's words, also an ineligible spouse; read so, an aged couple applying for Medical Assistance together, neither on SSI, would each be tested alone whenever the other's income is 497 or less, where SSI would treat two such spouses who both meet its tests as an eligible couple (tested together against the couple rate, 1,491). Worked example: p1 aged, Social Security 1,100; p2 aged, no income, neither applying for SSI: as an eligible couple 1,100 - 20 = 1,080 <= 1,491, aged for both; with p2 an ineligible spouse, p1 1,080 > 994, none. The codex reads such a spouse as a possible eligible spouse and keeps the couple's combined test, the reading that does not deny where SSI would grant.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: A spouse who is aged, blind or disabled in the month is not an ineligible spouse for 20 CFR 416.1163(d): the couple's income is combined and compared with the couple rate, as before coverage pass 6. Only a spouse who is not aged, blind or disabled, and is not applying for or eligible for SSI, has income that (d)(1) may leave undeemed.

Checked 2026-11-24: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.5.H.1.a, H.2.c (D-65); 20 CFR 416.1163 (D-53); 42 CFR 435.601(b)(2) (D-51). Colorado's definition of an ineligible spouse turns on applying for or receiving SSI, and no text on file says whether a spouse who would qualify but has not applied is treated as an eligible spouse for a Medical Assistance determination. Items: CO-7353.
### OQ-1365. LEAP: whether a landlord's rent stated as unearned income is counted net of the property's maintenance costs

Body: Coverage pass 6, R6-3. 9 CCR 2503-7 names rent in one place only: 3.755.21.I, "Verification of self-employment income shall include, at a minimum: ... 2. Receipts for business-related expenses are required in order to be considered as deductions: ... D. Rental property allowable expenses shall be limited to the costs of maintenance of the property" (D-86). Unearned income is "payments from any other source, which is considered to be a gain or benefit" (3.752.21), counted gross ("the countable gross unearned income received in the income verification period", 3.752.22.A); 3.752.21 and 3.752.211 name no rent and no expense against it. The volume states a landlord who does not run the property as a business as a `rental_income` receipt (CO-5000: "Rent or royalties from property the person owns and does not actively manage as a business") and one who does as a business (self_employment_receipts with cost records, LEAP reading I.2.D's limit on its costs, coverage pass 2 N-9). Whether I.2.D also reaches the rent of a landlord who is not self-employed, so that maintenance costs come off that rent too, the text does not say: it sits in the self-employment verification paragraph, and the unearned income paragraphs count gross. Worked example (review probe LRent): CO-712's adult with $3,000 of wages and $1,000 a month of rent from a house she owns, with $400 of receipted maintenance: gross, $4,000 > $3,774, ineligible; net of maintenance, $3,600, eligible.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Energy assistance (LEAP) policy owner
Assumption: Rent stated as unearned income (a landlord who does not run the property as a business) is counted gross, as the unearned income paragraphs count every other payment; the maintenance limit applies to rental property stated as a business. If the owner reads it as reaching every landlord, the rent's record needs a neutral maintenance figure (the property expenses the volume states today also hold taxes, insurance, interest and fees, which the paragraph does not allow), and LEAP counts the rent net of it.

Known limit (coverage pass 7, R7-3; docs/reviews/known-limits.md, CCCAP and LEAP): the assumption's "rental property stated as a business" is met only by leap_self_employment_allowable_costs (CO-1711) reading every cost record of the person's businesses, with a statement rule ("for self-employment from rental property a case states its maintenance costs as business_cost_operating and no interest or vehicle costs") that conflicts with CCCAP's (CO-7251: a small rental's costs are stated as rental_property_expenses_paid). LEAP does not read the business record's rental_property, so a rental business whose interest, taxes or insurance are stated as cost records has them deducted, which I.2.D does not allow (probe LRB1: $3,300, eligible, where I.2.D gives $3,900, ineligible); stated the CCCAP way, LEAP counts the rent gross (probe LRB3: $3,800, ineligible, where the rule gives $3,700, eligible). A household evaluated for both programs cannot be stated for both. The fix (cost records that name their business, with a neutral maintenance marker; LEAP deducting only maintenance records against a business whose rental_property is true) is a larger change than the fix round made.

Checked 2026-11-24: 9 CCR 2503-7 3.751.1, 3.752.21, 3.752.211, 3.752.22.A, 3.752.23, 3.755.21.I (D-86) and the amendments effective 2026-11-01 (D-173, 3.755.21.I.1): no other paragraph names rent, a landlord or a property's expenses. Items: CO-5001, CO-5003, CO-1711.

### OQ-1366. CCCAP: how the twenty hours a week of active management of rental property are averaged

Body: Coverage pass 6, R6-4. 8 CCR 1403-1 3.111.H.4.e (S2704): "For income from rental property to be considered as self-employment income, the adult caretaker(s) or teen parent(s) shall actively manage the property at least an average of twenty (20) hours per week. Income from rental property will be considered as unearned income if the adult caretaker(s) or teen parent(s) are not actively managing the property an average of at least twenty (20) hours per week." The text does not say over what period the weekly average is taken, nor whether two properties are averaged together. The earnings record states a business's hours for its month (CO-5100, hours) and whether it is rental property (rental_property). The volume averages the caretaker's hours in the month managing rental property (the records whose rental_property is true: cccap_rental_property_management_hours CO-7254), divided by the month's days over seven (cccap_rental_property_receipts_as_unearned CO-7251, cccap_self_employment_gross_receipts CO-5156, cccap_self_employment_hours CO-6404, cccap_rental_property_unearned_hours CO-7680), so one property stated in one record or two gives one answer, and two rental properties are read together. Coverage pass 7, R7-1 (docs/reviews/coverage7-care-energy.md): the hours of a business that is not rental property are not counted toward the line, since H.4.e asks whether the caretaker manages "the property" and no reading makes a cleaning business's hours management of it (probe CR3, stored household CO-1080: before, the two businesses' hours were pooled and the rent read as self-employment, ineligible_income where the rule gives eligible_low_income); that part of the earlier assumption is withdrawn as not a choice the text leaves. Other readings, still open: each rental property by itself ("the property"), so two properties are not averaged together; over the prior thirty days of H.4 (thirty days over seven, 4.29 weeks, the same line as a thirty-day month); over 4.33 weeks; over the twelve months H.4.e allows for rental income. Worked example: 86 hours in a 31-day month is 86 / 4.43 = 19.4 hours a week (unearned); over a 30-day period, 86 / 4.29 = 20.1 (self-employment).

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Child care policy owner (CDEC)
Assumption: The caretaker's hours in the month managing rental property, every rental property stated together, over the month's days divided by seven.

Known limit (coverage pass 7, R7-3; docs/reviews/known-limits.md, CCCAP and LEAP): one rental property managed under twenty hours a week cannot be stated so that CCCAP and LEAP both answer right. CCCAP takes a small rental's costs from rental_property_expenses_paid (CO-4458, through CO-4463) and not from the business's cost records; LEAP takes them from the business's cost records and allows maintenance only (9 CCR 2503-7 3.755.21.I.2.D; leap_self_employment_allowable_costs CO-1711). Probes CR2 (CCCAP, costs as a cost record: $3,400, ineligible_income, where the rule gives $3,100, eligible_low_income) and LRB1, LRB3 (LEAP). The fix (cost records that name their business, with a neutral maintenance marker, read by both programs) is a larger change than the fix round made.

Checked 2026-11-24: 8 CCR 1403-1 3.103 (no definition of active management or of a week), 3.111.G.2, H.4, H.4.e, I.2, I.12, I.17 (D-87). Checked again 2026-11-24 (coverage pass 7): H.4.e's "the property" settles that another business's hours are not counted; the averaging period and two properties together stay open. Items: CO-7251, CO-7252, CO-7254, CO-7680, CO-5156, CO-6404, CO-984.

### OQ-1425. MAGI and CHP+: a fact the case states for the determination date, read in a retroactive month (residence, Medicare, a child's coverage, immigration status, an earlier institution stay)

Body: Pattern sweep, MAGI (2026-11-24, docs/reviews/pattern-sweep-magi.md, PS-07). 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.3.E.1 (S217) gives a retroactive month to a person who "met all applicable eligibility requirements" in it, and § 8.100.4.G.1 (S3351) judges each month on its own facts; 42 CFR 435.915(a) (S218) covers the months in which the person would have been eligible. The case states some facts once, for the determination date, with no date at which they began: Colorado residence (state_of_residence, county_of_residence, read by ma_meets_residency CO-2250 and chp_meets_residency CO-7402), Medicare entitlement or enrollment (is_medicare_entitled_or_enrolled, CO-017, read by the Adults group's tests CO-548, CO-539 and CO-552), a dependent child's minimum essential coverage (dependent_child_has_minimum_essential_coverage, CO-085, read by the same three), the immigration status itself (immigration_status, CO-002, read by ma_meets_citizenship_requirement_in_month CO-7551) a live-in care provider's arrangement (lives_full_time_with_care_recipient, care_recipient_receives_hcbs_waiver_services and the counts of foster individuals, read by ma_excluded_difficulty_of_care_payments CO-5214), a parent's living apart on separation (ma_lives_apart_due_to_separation_or_pending_divorce, read by CO-1976) and the place the person lives (residence_setting_type, CO-3330: a stay in a jail that ended before the determination cannot be stated; ma_is_inmate_in_month CO-7555 places only the stay the case states, from institution_entry_date). A person who moved to Colorado, became entitled to Medicare, adjusted status or left a jail after a retroactive month is judged in that month on the determination date's fact. The sweep made per month every condition the case can date: the pregnancy (its start and end), the five years of qualified status (qualified_status_date), ages (date_of_birth), the home (dwelling_id with date_of_death) and the current institution stay (institution_entry_date). No text on file says from what the agency establishes a retroactive month's residence, Medicare entitlement, a child's coverage, immigration status or setting: on the application's statements for the determination date, or on a statement for each retroactive month.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The facts the case states for the determination date hold in each retroactive month, except where the case states the date of a change (when a pregnancy began and ended, when the person obtained qualified status, the date of birth, the date of death, the date a stay in an institution began).

Checked 2026-11-24: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.3.E.1-2 (D-65, S217, S1503), § 8.100.4.G.1 (S3351), 42 CFR 435.915(a) (D-35, S218), 42 CFR 435.403 (D-33, S171): each makes a retroactive month's requirements its own and none says how they are established for a month before the application or what the agency asks about them. CHP+ has no retroactive months (10 CCR 2505-3 § 430.1), so only its application month can precede a change. Items: CO-2250, CO-7402, CO-548, CO-539, CO-552, CO-7551, CO-7555, CO-5214, CO-1976. Narrowed by Medicaid status by month (2026-11-24, docs/reviews/medicaid-status-by-month.md): a move to Colorado is now dated (state_of_residence_start_date, CO-7651, read by ma_meets_residency_in_month CO-7650 for a month before the application month; OQ-1445), and the qualified status from qualified_status_date in every Medicaid category (CO-7551, CO-7652; OQ-1446); the assumption still governs the facts no date states (the status of a person who is not a qualified non-citizen, Medicare, a child's coverage, the care arrangements, a parent living apart, a stay that ended), for the non-MAGI categories too since every month-scoped Medicaid item reads status and residency for the month.
### OQ-1405. SNAP: the day of the month on which a dated change in membership or status counts for a month evaluated

Body: Three-pattern sweep (docs/reviews/pattern-sweep-snap-ce.md, T-1, T-3, T-4, T-6, T-7). SNAP determines each month "based on anticipated income and circumstances during the issuance month" (10 CCR 2506-1 4.402.1.A, S4734), and the application month on "the household's circumstances for the entire month of application" (7 CFR 273.10(a)(1)(i), S5091), so a change the case states with its date (a member's entry into an institution, a death, a birth, the fifth anniversary of qualified status, a sponsor's death, the ninetieth day of a stay in another's home) is read in the month it falls in, not on the determination date. No SNAP text on file says on which day of the month the change counts. The items take OQ-1262's reading of an age: a change that makes a person a member or eligible, or gives a deduction, counts for the month if it holds on any day of it (a child born during the month is a member of it; the five years reached by the month's last day; a stay in another's home within 90 days on the month's first day; a sponsor who dies during the month is not deemed for it), and a change that takes a person out of the household counts only if it holds on every day (a member who enters an institution, or dies, during a month is a member of that month and out from the next). Worked example (CO-944): a member who enters a nursing facility on 2026-11-10 is a member in November and out from December; before the sweep she was a member in every month, because the entry came after the 2026-10-15 determination. The other reading (out from the day of the change, prorated) needs a proration the text gives only for the application month (4.207.2.C).

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: A change that brings a person into the household or makes a status hold counts for the month if it holds on any day of it; a change that takes a person out of the household counts only for a month on every day of which it holds (entry into an institution or death before the first day of the month).

Checked 2026-11-24: 7 CFR 273.1(a), (b)(7)(vi) (S1, S3); 273.10(a)(1)(i) (D-14, S5091); 7 CFR 271.2 "Homeless individual" (S1950); 10 CCR 2506-1 4.304, 4.305.2.E.7 (S1619), 4.402.1.A (S4734) (D-82); 8 U.S.C. 1612(a)(2)(L), 1613(a) (S21, S22). None names the day of the month on which a change in membership or a dated status counts. Items: CO-304, CO-305, CO-307, CO-3736, CO-3738, CO-037, CO-2337, CO-7672.

### OQ-1415. Medicaid long-term care: whether a retroactive month before the Case Management Agency's assessment is an HCBS, PACE or level-of-care month

Body: Pattern sweep, non-MAGI Medicaid (docs/reviews/pattern-sweep-nonmagi.md, P1). The long-term care, HCBS and PACE rules read the functional findings as the Case Management Agency (or the Utilization Review Contractor) states them on the determination date: the waiver target group (meets_hcbs_waiver_target_group, CO-821), the waiver's level of care and condition criteria (CO-1178, CO-2270), the nursing facility level of care (CO-820), the PACE service area and community-safety findings (CO-2412, CO-2413) and the requested setting (CO-2411). 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.7.B.1 (S3408) provides HCBS or PACE "to persons who have been assessed by the Single Entry Point/Case Management Agency to have met the institutional level of care"; § 8.7100.C.9 (S375) has the agency certify waiver eligibility for those it determines to meet the target group and level of care; § 8.401 (D-55) requires the Level of Care determination "prior to admission to a Medicaid certified nursing facility". A nursing facility month before the admission is already not a long-term care month (ma_is_institutionalized, CO-640), so the question is the community settings: no text on file says whether a retroactive month (§ 8.100.3.E.1, S1503) before the agency's assessment or certification is an HCBS or PACE month. Worked example: a person assessed for the EBD waiver on 2026-10-20, applying 2026-11-02 for the retroactive months, meets every financial test in August: with the codex's reading August is an HCBS month (eligible_hcbs); if the certification governs, August is not_institutionalized_or_waiver.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The findings stated on the determination date are read for every month the determination covers, retroactive months included.

Checked 2026-11-24: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.7.A.1, B.1-2 (D-65), § 8.7100.C.9 and § 8.7101 (D-75), §§ 8.400-8.401 (D-55), § 8.7000-8.7001 (D-56); none dates the assessment's effect on a retroactive month. Items: CO-653, CO-654, CO-3310, CO-4630, CO-5204, CO-1631, CO-2267.


### OQ-1435. Cash programs: the day of an earlier month on which an absence or a stay is measured

Body: Cash pattern sweep PS-T8 and PS-T9 (docs/reviews/pattern-sweep-cash.md). A determination decides the months from the application month through the determination month (9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.1.A.1, 3.609.2.A.1; 9 CCR 2503-5 3.542.B, 3.532.B.2), and each month is judged on its own circumstances. An absence or a stay has a length that grows day by day: Colorado Works counts a member out of the State past ninety consecutive days (3.604.2.O.1), a resident of an institution after thirty (3.604.2.O.2), a parent or caretaker away past forty-five days with an intent to return (3.604.2.I.3), a child visiting under a parenting plan past six months or in voluntary foster care past three (3.604.2.G.2-3); Adult Financial presumes a client away thirty days from the country, or ninety (or one hundred and eighty) from the State, no longer a resident (3.520.64.E). Neither text says on which day of a month before the determination month the length is taken: on the month's last day (the absence as it stood when the month ended, so a month in which the limit is passed counts as a month away), on its first day (the month counts as at home if the limit had not been passed when it began), or day by day with a partial month. The determination month and later months are read on the determination date, the day the case's facts are stated as of, as before.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: The month's last day: a member or client whose absence or stay passed its limit by the last day of an earlier month is away for that month; the determination month and later months are read on the determination date.

Checked 2026-11-24: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.2.G, I, O, 3.605.1.A.1, 3.609.2.A.1 (D-81, rule version 12030); 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.64.E, 3.532.B.2, 3.542.B (D-80, rule version 12428). None names a day of the month for an absence's length, and neither volume prorates a month for a member who leaves within it; OQ-953 (whether the first day of the absence counts) and OQ-951 (the day an age is read on) are the nearest choices. Items: CO-7600, CO-7602, CO-904, CO-2351, CO-3804, CO-4401.

### OQ-1436. Cash programs: a status the case states for the determination date, read in an earlier month the determination covers

Body: Cash pattern sweep (2026-11-24, docs/reviews/pattern-sweep-cash.md, section 5). A Colorado Works determination decides the months from the month of application through the determination month, the application month's circumstances deciding eligibility (9 CCR 2503-6 3.605.1.A.1; the initial payment from the date of application or the later date the unit became eligible, 3.609.2.A.1), and Adult Financial eligibility begins with the date of application or the date the client meets all eligibility requirements, whichever is later (9 CCR 2503-5 3.542.B), the first month prorated (3.532.B.2). The case states some statuses once, for the determination date, with no date at which they began or ended: a strike (is_participating_in_strike), a fugitive or fleeing felon (is_fleeing_felon), an IPV or status disqualification, marriage (marital_status) and emancipation (is_emancipated_minor), immigration and sponsorship facts (immigration_status, is_sponsored_non_citizen, status_before_lpr_adjustment, qualifying_quarters, is_victim_of_battery_or_extreme_cruelty, the sponsor's dependents, household and spouse), disability and blindness (is_blind, meets_ssa_disability_criteria, af_and_so_disability_met, af_has_total_disability_six_months) and an SSDI award (title_ii_benefit_type), SSI receipt where a reader reads the award rather than the month's payment (receives_ssi in the Colorado Works unit's exclusion of SSI recipients and in the spouse's exemption of 3.534.C), receipt of Colorado Works cash (receives_tanf_cash) or Medicaid (is_enrolled_in_medical_assistance), Colorado residence (state_of_residence) and another State's assistance in the month (received_financial_assistance_from_another_state_this_month), the kind of setting the person lives in where no entry date is read (residence_setting_type and its features, nighttime_residence_type, lives_in_own_home and the home's shelter facts), school attendance, and the head of the unit. A status that began after the application month is carried by the eligibility dates the case states (af_eligibility_date; cw_unit_eligible_from_date); one that ended between the application month and the determination is judged in the application month on the determination date's statement. The sweep made per month every condition the case can date: ages (date_of_birth), death (date_of_death, of a member, a spouse, a parent or a sponsor), pregnancy (its start and end), an institution stay (institution_entry_date), an absence (temporarily_out_of_state_start_date, temporary_absence_start_date) and SSI received (the SSI receipts by month). No text on file says from what the county establishes the application month's status when it changed before the determination: on the application's statements for the determination date, or on a statement for each month.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: The statuses the case states for the determination date hold in each month the determination covers, except where the case states the date of a change (a birth date, a date of death, a pregnancy's start and end, the date a stay in an institution or an absence began, a receipt's month).

Checked 2026-11-24: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.3 (verification at application), 3.605.1.A.1, 3.609.2.A.1 (D-81, rule version 12030); 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.4-3.520.5 (application and verification), 3.532.B.2, 3.542.B, 3.554.C (D-80, rule version 12428): each makes the application month's eligibility its own and none says how a status that changed before the determination is established for it. MAGI's OQ-1425 records the same question for a retroactive month. Items: CO-921, CO-3808, CO-928, CO-2557, CO-906, CO-909, CO-4352, CO-2364, CO-2386, CO-4400, CO-1203, CO-950, CO-944, CO-2350, CO-2384, CO-942, CO-904, CO-3802, CO-1512, CO-1511.

### OQ-1445. Medicaid: residency in a retroactive month, at the time of application or in the month

Body: Medicaid status by month (2026-11-24, docs/reviews/medicaid-status-by-month.md, MS-1). 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.3.B.2 (S1518) defines a resident of Colorado as "a person that is living within the state of Colorado and considers Colorado to be their place of residence at the time of application", and § 8.100.3.E.1 (S1503) gives a retroactive month to an applicant who "met all applicable eligibility requirements" in it; 42 CFR 435.915(a)(2) (S218) makes a retroactive month eligible only if the person "would have been eligible for Medicaid at the time he received the services if he had applied", and 42 CFR 435.403(a) (S171) provides Medicaid to "residents of the State". B.2 measures residence at the time of application; 435.915(a)(2) asks whether the person was a Colorado resident in the retroactive month itself. Worked example (stored household CO-1060, MOVED-IN): a person who moved from Wyoming to Denver on 2026-09-20 and applied on 2026-10-15 with a retroactive request; under B.2's words alone July and August are months of a Colorado resident (she was one at the time of application); under 435.915(a)(2) she was a Wyoming resident in them.

Class: state policy choice, with Legal advising (both texts are on file; whether B.2's "at the time of application" reaches the retroactive months is the State's reading of its own rule against the federal rule a state plan follows, which HCPF settles by amending B.2 or confirming the reading in force)
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: A retroactive month is a month of residence only if the person lived in Colorado on a day of it (435.915(a)(2), the federal floor); the application month and after read the residence stated for the application (B.1-2). For a month before the application month the case states the date the person began living in Colorado, and without it the month cannot be determined.

Checked 2026-11-24: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.3.B.1-5 (D-65, S1518; D-39, S172), § 8.100.3.E.1 (S1503, S217), § 8.100.4.G.1 (S3351), § 8.100.5.C.1 (S3900); 42 CFR 435.403(a), (d), (h) (D-33, S171), 435.915(a) (D-35, S218). None says how residence is established for a month before the application. Items: CO-7650, CO-7651, CO-2250.

### OQ-1446. Medicaid: the immigration status held in a retroactive month before the qualified status the case states

Body: Medicaid status by month (2026-11-24, docs/reviews/medicaid-status-by-month.md, MS-2). The case states the status held on the determination date (immigration_status, CO-002) and the date the person obtained the qualified status held now (qualified_status_date, CO-003). In a retroactive month that ends before that date the person did not hold it (§ 8.100.3.G.1.g, S655 to S658), and the status held then decides the month: for a lawful permanent resident it is stated (status_before_lpr_adjustment, CO-2700); for an asylee, a person whose deportation is withheld, a battered non-citizen or a trafficking victim granted the status after the month, no fact states it, and § 8.100.3.G.1.g.vii counts some of the earlier statuses as lawfully residing (a pending asylum application with employment authorization, or by a child under 14 pending 180 days; deferred action) and not others. Worked example (CO-7551-T13, CO-7652-T5 and T6): a child granted asylum on 2026-09-20, in the United States since 2022, is read as a lawfully residing child in a retroactive August; an adult in the same position meets no category in August.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: Before the qualified status the case states, the qualified status and the exemptions it opens are not held; lawful presence is read as the case states it for the determination date (no date of a change to it being stated, the reading of the facts a case states once), so the lawfully residing pregnant woman and child (vii) and the ii arm are read on it; a month before entry into the United States meets no category.

Checked 2026-11-24: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.3.G.1.g (D-65, S655 to S658), § 8.100.3.E.1 (S1503); 8 U.S.C. 1613(a) (S176); 42 CFR 435.406 (D-33, S173); CMS SHO #26-001 (S1702, S1705). None says what status the agency establishes for a month before a grant. Items: CO-7551, CO-7652.
### OQ-1450. SNAP: a status the case states for the determination date, read in an application month before the determination month

Body: Coverage pass 7 for SNAP (2026-11-24, docs/reviews/coverage7-snap.md, C7-6). SNAP determines the application month on "the household's circumstances for the entire month of application" (7 CFR 273.10(a)(1)(i), S5091) and each issuance month "based on anticipated income and circumstances during the issuance month" (10 CCR 2506-1 4.402.1.A, S4734). A household that applies late in a month is often determined in the next, so the application month comes before the determination month. The case states some statuses once, for the determination date, with no date at which they began or ended: the nighttime residence other than a stay in another person's home (nighttime_residence_type, CO-2810; the stay is dated, CO-2811, CO-7674), the residence setting where only an entry is dated (residence_setting_type, CO-3330: a discharge from an institution between the application and the determination is not dated), receipt of TANF cash, SSI or State cash assistance where a reader reads the award rather than the month's payment (receives_tanf_cash, receives_ssi, receives_state_cash_assistance: categorical eligibility CO-336, the resource exclusions CO-343, CO-1740), immigration and disability statuses, and the home of a person who lives apart from the others on the determination date with no household edge to them. Coverage pass 7 made per month the conditions the case can date: who lives in the home (C7-1: the separation dates CO-7352, CO-7506, CO-7670 and the move-in date CO-7671), the energy assistance payments (C7-2, CO-7673), a stay in another's home that began after the month (C7-3, CO-7674), the expected completion of a student's program (C7-4) and the battered non-citizen's finding (C7-5); the earlier sweeps made per month ages, births, deaths, institution entry and the qualified non-citizen's five years. Two persons joined by a household edge who live apart on the determination date and state no separation date are read apart in the determination month (the determination date's homes), and cannot be determined for a month before it (decision 4). No text on file says from what the county establishes the application month's status when it changed before the determination: on the application's statements for the determination date, or on a statement for each month.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: SNAP policy owner
Assumption: The statuses the case states for the determination date hold in the application month and in every month of the certification, except where the case states the date of a change (a birth date, a date of death, an institution entry date, a separation or move-in date, a temporary stay's start and the residence before it, a payment's month, a program's expected completion date, a finding's date, a receipt's month); the determination month is read on the determination date where no date of a change is stated.

Checked 2026-11-24: 7 CFR 273.1(a) (S1), 273.10(a)(1)(i) (D-14, S5091); 10 CCR 2506-1 4.304, 4.402.1.A (S4734) (D-82): each makes the application month's eligibility its own and none says how a status that changed before the determination is established for it. The cash programs' OQ-1436 and MAGI's OQ-1425 record the same question for their earlier months. Items: CO-305, CO-7672, CO-037, CO-336, CO-343, CO-1740, CO-007, CO-304.

### OQ-1451. Colorado Works: the reach of "other financial assistance programs administered by the state of Colorado"

Body: Coverage pass 7 for the cash programs (2026-11-24, docs/reviews/coverage7-cash.md, F7-3). C.R.S. 26-2-706(1.5)(c) (S5210) allows no one to participate in Colorado Works who is "receiving financial assistance from other financial assistance programs administered by the state of Colorado", and 9 CCR 2503-6 3.609.1.D.6 (S5211) names, among the benefits that "may not be received concurrently with a Colorado Works grant", "Supplemental Security Income or Adult Financial programs". The items now leave out of the unit, in the month, a person who receives an Adult Financial grant (Old Age Pension, Aid to the Needy Disabled, Aid to the Blind; the receipt kind adult_financial_cash; cw_receives_adult_financial_grant_in_month, CO-7691), as 3.604.2.C.3 leaves out an SSI recipient. Three readings are open. (1) Which programs are "other financial assistance programs": the Adult Financial grants alone, or also the Home Care Allowance (9 CCR 2503-5 3.570.1, a cash payment of the Adult Financial rule), burial assistance, LEAP (a payment for heating costs, 9 CCR 2503-7), county general assistance, or a State diversion payment of another county. (2) A mandatory member who receives one: whether the parent is left out (a child-only unit, as for an SSI parent under C.3) or the unit is ineligible, since (1.5) is a condition "to participate" and 3.604.2.B makes a parent a mandatory member. (3) How the bar meets 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.71.F (an Adult Financial client with a dependent child in the home must apply for Colorado Works; 3.520.61.F bars Adult Financial for a Colorado Works recipient): whether the client chooses, or the Colorado Works grant comes first. Worked example (probe G2, stored household CO-1094): a grandmother of 66, Social Security $600 and an OAP grant of $452, caring for her granddaughter of 6 with no parent in the home, asks to be included; the unit is the granddaughter's alone, the child-only grant 173 x .65754 = 113.75, $113; before, the grandmother was a member and her $1,052 made the unit ineligible.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: The Adult Financial grants (OAP, AND, AB) are the "other financial assistance programs" (the programs 3.609.1.D.6 names); a person who receives one in a month is left out of the unit in that month, as an SSI recipient is under 3.604.2.C.3, whether an optional or a mandatory member; other State payments are read under the unit's income rules.

Checked 2026-11-24: C.R.S. 26-2-706(1.5) (D-59, S5210, S402); 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.2.B-C (S1625), 3.609.1.D.6 (D-81, S5211); 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.61.F, 3.520.71.F (D-80). None lists the programs (1.5)(c) reaches or says whether a mandatory member who receives one leaves the unit or ends it. Items: CO-7691, CO-7303, CO-921.

### OQ-1452. Colorado Works: a voluntary foster care placement "for a period not expected to exceed three months", measured by its elapsed length or by the county's expectation

Body: Coverage pass 7 for the cash programs (2026-11-24, docs/reviews/coverage7-cash.md, F7-1). 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.2.G.3 (S2203) considers living in the home "Child(ren) residing in voluntary foster care placement for a period not expected to exceed three (3) months", and C.R.S. 26-2-706(1)(a.5)(V) a child "in a voluntary foster care placement for a period not expected to exceed three months". G.2's parenting-plan visit is a length of time ("the visit not exceeding six (6) months unless otherwise specified"). The items measure both as the time elapsed from temporary_absence_start_date to the day each month is read (cw_month_reading_date, CO-7600; cw_is_considered_living_in_home_in_month, CO-7602): the child is in the home while the reading date is on or before the date three (six) months after the start. G.3's words test an expectation: a placement the county expects at its start to last five months is not one "not expected to exceed three months" in its first month, and one expected to last two months that runs to four is within it until the expectation changes. The case states no expected end of the placement.

Class: state policy choice
Owner: Cash programs policy owner
Assumption: A voluntary foster care placement is measured by its elapsed length, as a parenting-plan visit is: the child is in the home while the placement has lasted no more than three months on the reading date.

Checked 2026-11-24: 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.2.G.2-3 (D-81, S2203); C.R.S. 26-2-706(1)(a.5)(V) (D-59). Neither says how a county establishes the expected length, nor names a field of the placement that states it. Items: CO-7602.

### OQ-1475. Medicaid MAGI: "current or previous monthly household income" - the previous month only where the current month's income is not yet verified, or either month

Body: Coverage pass 7, C7-07 (2026-11-24, docs/reviews/coverage7-medicaid-magi-chp.md). 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.4.C.4 (S3356) determines the financial eligibility of applicants "based on current or previous monthly household income and family size", and C.4.a annualizes certain income for applicants "found financially ineligible based on current or previous monthly household income". § 8.100.4.B.1.c (S5270) makes the previous month a matter of verification: "Verification of earned income received during the month prior to the month of application shall be acceptable if the application month verification is not yet available." The volume reads the month evaluated only (ma_magi_household_income CO-503, ma_magi_monthly_household_income_pct_fpl CO-6452, ma_budget_period_household_income_pct_fpl CO-4567): the previous month's income stands in for the current month's as evidence of it, which B.1.c supports. Read instead as a choice between the two months, an applicant ineligible on the current month and eligible on the previous one would be eligible. Worked example: a single adult whose wages rose from $1,700 in October 2026 to $1,900 in November, the month of application (1,700 / 1,330 = 127.8%, less 5 = 122.8%, within 133%; 1,900 / 1,330 = 142.9%, less 5 = 137.9%, over it): ineligible_income in November as the volume reads C.4, eligible on the previous month's income under the other reading.

Class: state policy choice (the texts are on file: C.4's "current or previous" is the State's own budget-period rule, and whether it offers a choice of months or names the previous month as a stand-in for a current month not yet verified is the State's reading, which B.1.c supports but does not settle; HCPF confirms or amends C.4)
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The month evaluated's own income decides it; the previous month's income is evidence of the current month's where the current month's is not yet verified (B.1.c), not a second figure an applicant may choose.

Checked 2026-11-24: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.4.C.4, C.4.a (D-65, S3356), § 8.100.4.B.1.c (D-65:1641-1645, S5270), § 8.100.4.C.5 (S3357 and the volume's C.5 items), 42 CFR 435.603(h)(1) (D-34:196, S5271: "Financial eligibility for Medicaid for applicants, and other individuals not receiving Medicaid benefits at the point at which eligibility for Medicaid is being determined, must be based on current monthly household income and family size"). None says that an applicant ineligible on the current month's income is eligible on the previous month's. Items: CO-503, CO-6452, CO-4567.

### OQ-1485. Aid to the Needy Disabled: the five-year bar counted from the first qualified status (C.R.S. 26-2-111.8(2)(b)-(c)) or from the "date of admission ... for legal permanent residence" (9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.68.A)

Body: KL-CASH-2 fix round (2026-11-24, docs/reviews/known-limits.md). meets_public_assistance_citizenship_requirement (CO-905), read by Colorado Works and by Adult Financial for AND and AB, reads the five years through has_held_qualified_status_five_years (CO-508), which now counts them from the start of the waiting period (qualified_non_citizen_waiting_period_start_date, CO-7820: the first qualified status, or the status held now where the case shows it is the first), as 8 U.S.C. 1613(a) (S176) runs them: "beginning on the date of the alien's entry into the United States with a status within the meaning of the term 'qualified alien'". C.R.S. 26-2-111.8(2)(b) (S1151) bars a qualified alien who entered on or after August 22, 1996 from Colorado Works, the Old Age Pension, AND and AB "for a period of five years after the date of entry into the United States", and (2)(c) (S5300) says "After five years, a qualified alien described in paragraph (b) of this subsection (2) shall be eligible for benefits under this article". 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.68.A (S452) states the AND bar "for five years beginning on the qualified non-citizen's date of admission into the United States for legal permanent residence, as verified through SAVE". For a lawful permanent resident admitted directly the dates are the same; for one who held an earlier qualified status and adjusted (LPR-EX-PAROLEE: paroled 2019-05-01, lawful permanent resident 2023-03-01; stored household CO-1142), the statute's date of entry and 1613(a)'s first qualified status are 2019-05-01 (eligible for AND from 2024-05-01), and the rule's date of admission for permanent residence is 2023-03-01 (barred until 2028-03-01). The volume follows the statute, as it does for the Old Age Pension under OQ-430 (CO-929: a rule cannot narrow what the statute grants), and as C.R.S. 26-2-706(1.5)(b) (S402) and 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.1.B.3 (S407) state the bar for Colorado Works ("has been in a qualified alien status for a period of five years"). Items: CO-508, CO-905 (read for AND and AB by af_category CO-953 and af_and_so_eligible CO-1203).

Class: state policy choice (the texts are on file: the statute's "date of entry" and (2)(c)'s "After five years ... shall be eligible" against the rule's date of admission for permanent residence; CDHS confirms the reading or amends 3.520.68.A, with Legal advising on whether the rule may set a later date than the statute)
Owner: Cash programs policy owner, Legal
Assumption: The AND and AB bar is counted as the Colorado Works and Old Age Pension bars are, from the first qualified status (the date of entry in a qualified status, 8 U.S.C. 1613(a); C.R.S. 26-2-111.8(2)(b)); 3.520.68.A's date of admission for permanent residence is read as that date for a person admitted directly, the case it describes.

Checked 2026-11-24: C.R.S. 26-2-111.8(2)(a)-(c) (D-59:1339-1357, S1151, S5300); 9 CCR 2503-5 3.520.61.C, 3.520.67, 3.520.68.A (D-62:717, S449, S451, S452); C.R.S. 26-2-706(1.5)(b) (S402); 9 CCR 2503-6 3.604.1.B.3 (S407); 8 U.S.C. 1613(a) (S176). None says how 3.520.68.A applies to a person who held a qualified status before permanent residence.

### OQ-1495. Children with Disabilities Buy-In: which child's figure reports the household's one premium, and equal sharing among children born the same day

Body: Coverage verification 7 (2026-11-24, docs/reviews/verify-coverage7-fixes.md, section 3 and V7-4; coverage pass 7, non-MAGI F7-8). 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.6.Q.1.g.iii (D-65:3087, S1728) reads in full: "For households with two or more children eligible for the Medicaid Buy-In Program for Children with Disabilities, the total premium shall be the amount due for one eligible child." It fixes the household's total only and names no child who bears it, by age or otherwise. Q.1.g.ii ("Premiums are charged beginning the month after determination of eligibility. Any premiums for the months prior to the determination of eligibility will be waived.") is the only other sentence about whose premium is due. The volume reports the household's premium on one child's figure (ma_cbwd_premium, CO-621): the oldest child of the MAGI household whose own premium is due in the month (ma_cbwd_premium_is_due_in_month, CO-7861), shared equally among such children born the same day (twins: 35 each of a 70 premium), and 0 on the others. The order (oldest first) and the equal sharing are the volume's own presentation; another presentation (the whole premium on one child chosen by the county, or a household figure with no per-child amount) gives the same total. Worked example: twins born 2015-01-01, both eligible and both determined in January 2026, household at 140% of FPL with $2,000 of MAGI income: 70 for the household (the 70 band, under the 5% cap of 0.05 x 2,000 = 100); the volume reports 35 on each twin.

Class: state policy choice (the text is on file: Q.1.g.iii fixes the household's total and is silent on which child's record carries it; how the Department records one premium on a household of several enrolled children is its own administrative choice)
Owner: Medicaid policy owner
Assumption: The household owes one premium (Q.1.g.iii); it is reported on the oldest child whose premium is due in the month, shared equally among such children born the same day, and 0 on the others; the total of the children's figures is the household's premium.

Checked 2026-11-24: 10 CCR 2505-10 § 8.100.6.Q.1.g.i-vi (D-65:3081-3101, S1728); § 8.100.6.P.1.f (the adult Buy-In, D-65:3031-3051, which charges each individual). Neither says which child's record carries a household's single premium, nor how it is split among children born the same day. Items: CO-621, CO-7861.
